# Lyra UI — Project Summary (June 1, 2026)


## How to read this file (read this preamble; do NOT read the file in full)

This is an append-only history log (~650KB — it does not fit in a context window). To use it:

1. **Grep entry headings** for the component/file you're touching: `grep -n '^###.*<Name>' PROJECT_SUMMARY.md`, then read the matching entries only.
2. **Read ALL matches — newest wins.** Entries are chronological; later entries supersede or revert earlier ones (watch for "Follow-up", "Update", "Reverted"). Never act on a single old entry without checking for newer ones.
3. **Adding an entry** (required after any notable change): `### <Title naming the component/file>` under the right section below, dated (YYYY-MM-DD), **≤15 lines**, stating what changed and why. If it supersedes an earlier entry, name that entry's title explicitly ("Supersedes: ..."). Long sagas belong in `PROJECT_SUMMARY_ARCHIVE.md` with a short canonical entry here.

## Overview
Lyra is the NICE CXone design system component library built with React 19, TypeScript, Vite 6, Tailwind CSS 3.4, and Storybook 8.4. It uses Radix UI primitives for atoms and CSS custom properties for theming (light/dark via `[data-theme="dark"]`).

There are three repos:
- **lyra-ui** — the component library with Storybook
- **lyra-ux-templates** — lighter consuming page templates project
- **agent-next-gen-v1** — the primary/more feature-complete consuming app

## Architecture
- Tailwind preset (`src/tailwind-preset.ts`) maps CSS variables to utility classes
- Design tokens live in `src/styles/lyra-tokens.css` (light + dark themes)
- Storybook fallback tokens in `src/storybook.css`
- Tailwind config at `tailwind.config.js` (must mirror preset for Storybook)
- Components export from `src/index.ts`
- **Global Storybook dark mode toggle** (`.storybook/preview.ts`): a `Theme` toolbar control (sun/moon icons, Light/Dark) backed by `globalTypes.theme` + a `withTheme` decorator. The decorator sets `data-theme` on the preview iframe's `document.documentElement` — the exact same attribute every component already reads/writes (`AgentProfile`'s dark-mode toggle, `LoginCard`, `AgentNextGenPage`), so every story renders in real dark mode CSS, not just a background-color swap. It also syncs the canvas background to the live `--lyra-color-bg-surface-shell` value so the story canvas itself isn't left mismatched (stark white behind dark components). The toolbar is available globally in every story — no per-story opt-in needed. The existing `backgrounds` addon (`lyra-base`/`lyra-shell`/`dark` swatches) is unrelated and still independently selectable, though for real dark-mode testing use the new Theme toggle, not the `dark` background swatch (which only changes the canvas color, not the tokens).
  - **Gotcha**: the DOM mutation (`setAttribute("data-theme", ...)`) must happen directly in the decorator function body, not inside a `useEffect`. Storybook invokes decorators as plain functions rather than properly-mounted React components, so a `useEffect` called directly inside one isn't guaranteed to fire/re-fire when the toolbar global changes — the toolbar visibly toggled to "Dark" but nothing in the canvas actually re-themed. Fixed by making the `setAttribute`/background-sync call a synchronous side effect in the decorator body itself, which reruns every time Storybook re-invokes decorators (i.e. every toolbar change). If a future dark-mode-adjacent decorator needs to be added here, keep this pattern — don't reach for hooks inside `decorators` entries.
  - **Not covered**: the `Colors` story's `Swatch`/`AliasRow` helpers render hardcoded hex strings via inline `style={{ backgroundColor: value }}`, not CSS variables — they don't (and structurally can't) react to the theme toggle. That page already shows both light and dark hex values side-by-side as static reference chips instead. Use any real component story (Button, Container, Accordion, Toast, etc.) to verify the toggle is working.
- **Storybook never actually loaded the Inter font**: `storybook.css`'s `body` rule always said `font-family: "Inter", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif`, but nothing in `.storybook` ever fetched an Inter font file — no Google Fonts link, no self-hosted `@font-face` (the only `@font-face` rules anywhere in this repo are Storybook's own "Nunito Sans" chrome font, unrelated to component rendering). Consuming apps (`lyra-ux-templates`, and any repo scaffolded from it) separately add a Google Fonts `<link>` for Inter in their own `index.html`, so they've always rendered true Inter — Storybook was silently falling back past `"Inter"` to `ui-sans-serif`/`-apple-system` (San Francisco on Mac), confirmed via Chrome DevTools' "Rendered Fonts" panel showing `.SF NS` / `Font origin: Local file` on a `Button` in Storybook. Fixed by adding `.storybook/preview-head.html` with the same Google Fonts `<link>` markup the consuming apps use, so Storybook's own canvas now matches what every real app has been rendering all along. If comparing a component's typography against Storybook in the future, this is now consistent — but for anything touching fonts specifically, sanity-check with DevTools' Rendered Fonts panel rather than assuming a CSS `font-family` value took effect.

## Cross-Repo Sync

This repo is consumed live (no build/version step) by sibling app repos —
currently `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` — via a Vite alias
(`@nicecxone/lyra-ui` → `lyra-ui/src/index.ts`). Those apps always see
whatever is in this source tree the instant it changes; there's no package
version to bump. That means "staying in sync" isn't automatic — it depends
on whether each app's own pages have actually been updated to *use* new or
improved components as they land here, not on any install/build step. A
component can sit unused in this library for a long time after it's added
while consuming pages keep working fine on an older hand-rolled pattern.

**Whenever you're working in this repo** — including right after the user
pulls new changes from GitHub — and you notice a component was added,
replaced, or significantly reworked (check recent entries in this file, or
recent git history), check whether the mounted sibling app folders
(`agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, if present in this session) have
pages still using the older pattern that component replaces. If so,
proactively tell the user what's outdated there and ask whether they'd
like those apps updated — don't silently rewrite their pages. Concrete
examples of this exact situation, already found and fixed once (worth
checking for again after future changes here):
- Hand-rolled `Panel` + `PageHeader` + `TreeMenu` composition where
  `AdminShell` now exists and does the same thing with less code and more
  consistent pin/hover/resize behavior across pages.
- A custom width-animated wrapper (e.g. a `SlidingPanel`-style helper)
  around `AiPanel` instead of using `AiPanel`'s own `draggable` /
  `draggableVariant` prop, which supports docking/undocking and resizing
  out of the box.
- Any component reimplemented locally under a consuming app's
  `src/components/ui/` that now has a shared, more complete equivalent
  exported from `src/index.ts` here.
- A page that never wires up `LeftNav`'s `overlay` prop / narrow-viewport
  handling that other pages in the same app already have.
- Mock "database" fixtures duplicated by hand in a consuming app instead of
  imported from this repo's own fixture files. `src/components/__stories__/
  create-new-agents-data.ts` and `create-new-customers-data.ts` are the
  single source of truth for the CreateNew → Outbound demo's agent/customer
  records; they aren't re-exported from `src/index.ts` (story-only fixtures,
  not real design-system API), so each consumer's `vite.config.ts` adds its
  own explicit `@nicecxone/lyra-ui/agents-data` / `@nicecxone/lyra-ui/
  customers-data` aliases pointing straight at those two files (same pattern
  as the existing `/styles` alias). If a consumer app's `OUTBOUND_AGENTS`/
  `OUTBOUND_CUSTOMERS` are ever hand-written again instead of mapped from
  `CREATE_NEW_AGENTS`/`CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS`, that's this same drift
  happening again — fix it the same way.
- **`tailwind.config.js`'s color token list — now a single shared file,
  `tailwind-tokens.cjs`, not hand-duplicated per consumer.** Historically each
  consuming app kept its own full copy of this repo's `theme.extend.colors`
  map (Tailwind configs aren't importable across the plain Vite source alias
  the way components are — `tailwind.config.js` is loaded by Tailwind's own
  config loader, outside Vite, so `@nicecxone/lyra-ui`'s `resolve.alias`
  doesn't apply to it). Adding a new `--lyra-color-*` CSS variable here and
  using it in a component didn't automatically make the matching
  `bg-`/`text-`/`border-` utility class exist in a consumer's build —
  Tailwind silently emits no rule for a class name it doesn't recognize as a
  configured color, so the class sits inert in the DOM with zero visual
  effect. **Found exactly this way**: `agent-next-gen-v1`'s
  `tailwind.config.js` was missing `lyra-status-info-medium`, `lyra-status-
  info-subtle`, and `lyra-status-warning-medium` (present here since long
  before this session) — `channel-row.tsx`'s "highlighted" channel row uses
  `bg-lyra-status-info-subtle`, which therefore rendered with *no background
  at all* in that app, looking like a broken/missing feature (reported as
  "the selected interaction isn't showing") when the actual component logic
  was already correct. A follow-up audit found 31 tokens missing in total,
  including 8 of 10 customer-avatar accent color pairs.

  **Fix (structural, not just a one-time patch)**: the full color map now
  lives in `lyra-ui/tailwind-tokens.cjs` (plain CommonJS, `module.exports`),
  and both `lyra-ui/tailwind.config.js` and `agent-next-gen-v1/
  tailwind.config.js` pull from it via a plain relative `require(...)` —
  `require("./tailwind-tokens.cjs")` here, `require("../lyra-ui/
  tailwind-tokens.cjs")` there — then spread it into their own
  `theme.extend.colors` (each still appends its own one-off literals, e.g.
  `cxone-navy`, locally). There is now exactly one object to keep correct;
  a new token added to `tailwind-tokens.cjs` (alongside its `--lyra-color-*`
  variable in `src/styles/lyra-tokens.css`) is picked up by every consumer
  automatically, with no per-consumer copy step to forget. **Any new
  prototype cloned from `lyra-ui` should do the same** — `require()` this
  file from its own `tailwind.config.js` rather than hand-copying the color
  list — see the "Sync check" section of that new project's own `CLAUDE.md`
  (copied from `agent-next-gen-v1`'s, per the note at the bottom of this
  repo's `CLAUDE.md`).

This file is the single source of truth for *what changed and why* — the
actual check procedure (a `.lyra-ui-sync` marker file recording the last
lyra-ui commit hash each consumer checked against, diffed via `git log`
against this repo's current HEAD) lives in each consumer's own `CLAUDE.md`,
since that's where it needs to run and where it must keep working even if a
consumer repo is opened on its own, without this repo mounted alongside it.
Don't duplicate that procedure here — if it changes, update it in the
consumer `CLAUDE.md`s directly. A repo duplicated from `lyra-ux-templates`
to start a new project inherits both `.lyra-ui-sync` and `CLAUDE.md`
automatically, as long as it keeps `lyra-ui` as a sibling checkout (or its
`vite.config.ts` alias is updated to point at wherever `lyra-ui` lives).

Consuming repos found so far, beyond the two named at the top of this file:
**`Outbound-Campaigns`** and **`Agent Nav Testing`** (same live-alias/
`.lyra-ui-sync`/hand-copied-`tailwind.config.js` setup as the others).
Neither has "lyra" in its folder name — **the repo name is not a reliable
signal for "is this a lyra-ui consumer."** The reliable check is
`grep -rl "nicecxone/lyra-ui" */vite.config.*` across every sibling folder
mounted alongside this repo — that's what actually surfaced `Agent Nav
Testing`, which a name-based search missed entirely. If you're asked to
check "everything with lyra in the name" or similar, do the name search if
asked, but also run the alias grep and report anything it finds that the
name search didn't — this already happened once. Any new sibling folder
that alias-greps positive should get the same treatment as the others: its
`tailwind.config.js` checked/patched for the `accordion-down`/`accordion-up`
keyframes block, and noted here.

## Agent Sandbox vs. User's Machine — macOS, not Linux

**The user's actual machine is macOS (Apple Silicon).** These repos
(`lyra-ui` and every sibling consumer) are live-mounted folders — an agent
working in a sandboxed environment (e.g. Claude's Cowork sandbox, which
runs Linux) is editing the user's real files directly, not a disposable
copy. This matters specifically for anything that touches `node_modules`
or `package-lock.json`:

**Do not run `npm install` (bare, or to add/update a dependency) against
these repos from a Linux sandbox without flagging it first.** npm resolves
platform-specific optional dependencies (e.g. `@rollup/rollup-darwin-arm64`,
`@esbuild/darwin-arm64`) based on the OS/arch of whatever machine runs the
install. An install run from a Linux sandbox can silently rewrite the real,
shared `package-lock.json`/`node_modules` on the user's Mac to reflect a
Linux resolution instead — the user then hits `Cannot find module
@rollup/rollup-darwin-arm64` (or similar) the next time they run `npm run
dev`/`vite build` themselves. This is not a code bug and re-diffing the
component source won't find it — it's this exact platform mismatch. **Found
and fixed once already**: happened to `Outbound-Campaigns` after an agent
ran `npm install` there from its own sandbox to add `@radix-ui/react-
accordion` and populate a from-scratch `node_modules`.

**Going forward**: prefer read-only verification (`vite build`, `tsc
--noEmit`) over anything that touches the lockfile. If a new dependency
genuinely needs installing, edit `package.json` directly and tell the user
to run `npm install` themselves (or, if running it in the sandbox is
unavoidable for verification purposes, say so explicitly and warn that the
user should re-run `rm -rf node_modules package-lock.json && npm install`
on their own machine afterward to restore the correct platform-specific
binaries) — don't assume a sandbox-run install is safe to leave in place
silently.

## Key Components

### Icon (`src/components/icon.tsx`)
- New `"info"` value added to `IconBackground` (existing: none/primary/active/success/warning/critical/neutral/surface) — renders `bg-lyra-accent-purple-soft text-lyra-accent-purple-strong`, giving a purple icon-badge option alongside the existing blue (`active`)/green (`success`)/amber (`warning`)/red (`critical`) badges.
- The `--lyra-color-accent-purple-soft`/`-strong` tokens already existed in `lyra-tokens.css` and were already mapped in `tailwind.config.js`, but were missing from `tailwind-preset.ts` (the file Storybook itself uses) — added there too so the utility classes resolve identically in both the templates app and Storybook. Always add new Tailwind color utilities to **both** files, per Important Patterns below — this was a pre-existing gap in that rule being followed for this token pair specifically.
- Story: `Custom Primitives/Icon` → `Background variants` now includes `info` in both the Rounded and Circle galleries; `background` argType options list updated too.
- Used in `lyra-ux-templates`' `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` home-screen summary cards (Schedule = `active`, Performance = `success`, Messages = `info`) via `<Icon icon={LucideIconComponent} background="..." shape="rounded" decorative />` as a `Container`'s `headerIcon`.
- **New `xs` size** (`IconSize`, 24px container / 14px glyph) and **new `shell` background** (`IconBackground`, `bg-lyra-bg-surface-shell` — visibly grayer than the existing near-white `neutral`, `bg-lyra-bg-surface-container-subtle`) — both added specifically to let `AIProcess`'s step icons (see that component's own entry below) move onto `Icon` instead of staying a separate hand-rolled implementation.
- **New `strokeWidth?: number` prop** (default `1.5`, matching every other icon in the library) and **new `spin?: boolean` prop** (adds `animate-spin`) — also added for `AIProcess`: its "done"/"active"/"error" steps need a heavier stroke (2/2.5) than the library default to stay legible at 14px, and "active" needs a spinning `Loader` glyph.
- Story: `Custom Primitives/Icon` → new `Status icons (AIProcess steps / Queue agent status)` story demonstrates both real "colored circle + glyph" status-indicator call sites side by side — `AIProcess`'s 4 step states at `size="xs"` and the "Queue Agent status" icons from `agent-dashboard.tsx`'s `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown` at `size="sm"` — since both are the same `Icon` pattern at different sizes/glyphs, not two different components. `Sizes`/`BackgroundVariants` stories and the `size`/`background` argTypes updated to include `xs`/`shell`.

### Popover (`src/components/popover.tsx`) — `bodyPadding` gotcha documented
2026-08-10: No code change — documenting a recurring usage mistake so it stops recurring. `Popover`'s `content` slot defaults `bodyPadding` to `true`, adding its own 20px `px-5` inset; passing a `Menu`/listbox (which already carries its own `p-1` row padding, or is rendered `bare` specifically to rely on the *parent's* surface) as `content` without also setting `bodyPadding={false}` stacks both insets — renders fine, no error, just reads as an oddly over-padded, shrunken-looking list. Caught from a screenshot on agent-next-gen-v2's Interactions-tab bulk-actions bar ("Change Status"/"Assign to Others" pickers, both `<Menu items={...} bare />` inside a `Popover`). This exact pattern (and the fix) was already implicitly documented once before, buried inside agent-next-gen-v2/CLAUDE.md's rule 28 (the session-status pill's popover, which toggles `bodyPadding` per-view since only one of its two `content` states is a full-bleed `Menu`) — but not stated as a general, greppable rule, which is why it recurred. Now stated plainly in both `CLAUDE.md`s' "Panels, menus & overlays" sections: any `Popover` whose `content` is a `Menu`/listbox needs `bodyPadding={false}`.

### AIProcess (`src/components/ai-process.tsx`)
- Renders a collapsible "Thought process" step list — each step has a `status` (`pending`/`active`/`done`/`error`), an optional `description`, and is connected to the next by a short vertical line.
- **Step icons now built on `Icon`** (`size="xs"`, `background` + `shape="circle"`, mapped per status: `done`→`success`+`Check`, `active`→`active`+`Loader`+`spin`, `error`→`critical`+`AlertCircle`, `pending`→`shell`+`Clock`) instead of a bespoke `StepIcon` span that duplicated `Icon`'s own circle-background rendering with its own hardcoded classes. This was one of three parallel "colored circle + icon" implementations found in the library (alongside `Icon`'s own `background`+`shape="circle"` mode, already used by `agent-dashboard.tsx`'s Queue Agent status icons, and `Badge`'s `shape="circle"` icon-content mode, used by `agent-profile.tsx`'s `StatusIcon`) — this refactor collapses `AIProcess`'s copy into the first of those, leaving `Badge`'s icon-content mode as the one genuinely different case (a corner-overlay marker, not a standalone content icon — see `Icon`'s own doc comment for that distinction). No visual change: `Icon` gained `xs`/`shell`/`strokeWidth`/`spin` (see `Icon`'s entry above) specifically to reproduce this component's exact prior dimensions/colors/stroke weights.
- Story: `UI/AIProcess` unchanged (no story-level references to the old `StepIcon` internals).

### ClearButton (`src/components/clear-button.tsx`)
- New shared internal atom — not exported from `src/index.ts` (an implementation detail, like `ai-process.tsx`'s `StepIcon`), just a building block both `SearchInput` and `Autocomplete` import directly. Extracted because those two had drifted into two different hand-rolled "clear text" buttons: `SearchInput`'s had a `hover:bg-lyra-state-hover` background but no tooltip; `Autocomplete`'s had neither the hover background nor a tooltip (just a color-only icon hover). One shared component fixes both gaps at once instead of patching each copy separately.
- 20px box (`h-5 w-5`), `rounded-lyra-xs`, `text-lyra-fg-secondary` → `hover:text-lyra-fg-default` + `hover:bg-lyra-state-hover`, wrapping a 14px `X` glyph. Wrapped in `Tooltip` — `tooltipLabel` prop controls the *visible* tooltip text (default `"Clear"`), kept independent of `aria-label` (a plain passthrough prop) so each consumer can keep as specific an accessible name as it wants (`SearchInput` passes `aria-label="Clear search"`, `Autocomplete` passes `aria-label="Clear"`) without changing what the tooltip actually displays.

### SearchInput (`src/components/search-input.tsx`)
- Clear ("×") button is now `ClearButton` (see above) instead of a hand-rolled `<button>` — same visual position/sizing (`absolute right-2 top-1/2 -translate-y-1/2`), now with a "Clear" tooltip on hover it didn't have before.

### Autocomplete (`src/components/autocomplete.tsx`)
- Clear ("×") button is now `ClearButton` (see above) instead of a hand-rolled inline `<button>` — this is the fix, not just a refactor: the old version had no hover background at all (only a color change on the icon), unlike `SearchInput`'s always-present `hover:bg-lyra-state-hover`. Now both share the exact same clear-button treatment, including the new "Clear" tooltip. Positioned with `mr-2` (was `pr-3` on the old wider hand-rolled hit target) to sit the same ~8px from the input shell's right edge as `SearchInput`'s `right-2`.
- Disabled-state hover-border bug fixed — see "Disabled fields still showed the hover border" below.

### PhoneInput (`src/components/phone-input.tsx`)
- New `hideCountrySelector` prop (default `false`): renders a plain single-country number field with no flag/dial-code button or country popover. `defaultCountry` / `value.countryCode` still determine the mask and formatting — it just can't be changed from the UI.
- Implementation shares one `numberInput` JSX node between the `hideCountrySelector` shell and the full popover-based shell, so the two layouts can't drift apart.
- Story: `Custom Primitives/PhoneInput` → `Without country selector`
- Disabled-state hover-border bug fixed — see "Disabled fields still showed the hover border" below.

### Label (`src/components/label.tsx`) — now built on Radix
- Was a hand-rolled `<label>` element, miscategorized under `Custom Primitives` in Storybook even though Radix has a real Label primitive (`@radix-ui/react-label`) — unlike `Autocomplete`/`DatePicker`/`DateTimePicker`/`PhoneInput`/`Table`/`TimePicker` (moved the other direction, out of `Headless Primitives`, since those only use Radix internally as an implementation detail and aren't themselves Radix primitives — see that entry). Rebuilt on `LabelPrimitive.Root` (same "swap internals, keep the same export API" playbook as `Select`/`Accordion`/`Separator`) — `label`/`labelFor`/`labelHelpText`/`required`/`disabled`/`readonly`/`className` are all unchanged, so no consumer needed to change.
- Radix's `Root` adds one real behavior improvement over the old hand-rolled version: it guards against a Safari quirk where double-clicking a label selects text in a sibling element instead of just interacting with the label's associated control.
- New dependency: `@radix-ui/react-label` (bundled into `dist` by `tsup` like every other Radix package this library uses — external consumers don't need to install it themselves).
- Story: `Custom Primitives/Label` → `Headless Primitives/Label` (same stories, just recategorized).

### PasswordInput (`src/components/password-input.tsx`) — now built on Radix
- Was a fully hand-rolled show/hide field — local `useState` for `visible` + a plain `<button tabIndex={-1}>` flipping the input's `type` between `"password"`/`"text"`. Rebuilt on Radix's real Password Toggle Field primitive (`@radix-ui/react-password-toggle-field` — `Root`/`Input`/`Toggle`/`Icon`), same "swap internals, keep the export API" playbook as `Select`/`Accordion`/`Separator`/`Label`. `PasswordInputProps` is unchanged except `autoComplete` narrowed from `string` to `"current-password" | "new-password"` (matching `PasswordToggleField.Input`'s own type — no consumer passed anything else).
- **Real behavior gained, not just an internal refactor**: the toggle button is now a normal tab stop with a real, auto-generated `"Show password"`/`"Hide password"` `aria-label` — the old `tabIndex={-1}` pulled it out of the tab order entirely, so keyboard-only users had no way to reach it at all. Clicking the toggle now also refocuses the input and restores the exact text cursor/selection position (Radix's own built-in behavior), the field auto-re-masks on the surrounding `<form>`'s `submit`/`reset` events, and the input gets `autoCapitalize="off"`/`spellCheck={false}` by default (the hand-rolled `<input>` never set either, so a password field's contents could get auto-capitalized or spellchecked on mobile).
- Added a `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-lyra-border-focus` treatment to the toggle button, since it's a real focusable element for the first time — the old version never needed one (it wasn't reachable by keyboard to begin with).
- No external consumer uses `PasswordInput` directly (only lyra-ui's own `PasswordInput.stories.tsx`/`ChangePassword`), so this swap had zero cross-repo footprint.
- Story: `Custom Primitives/PasswordInput` → `Headless Primitives/PasswordInput` (same stories, just recategorized).

### Toast / ToastContainer (`src/components/toast.tsx`) — now built on Radix
- Was a fully hand-rolled slide-in/out toast: `useState`+`useLayoutEffect`+`requestAnimationFrame` computing inline `transform`/`opacity` styles by hand, a manual `setTimeout` auto-dismiss timer with no pause-on-hover, and a plain `role="alert"` div. Rebuilt on Radix's real Toast primitive (`@radix-ui/react-toast` — `Root`/`Title`/`Description`/`Close`, plus `Provider`/`Viewport` folded into `ToastContainer`), same "swap internals, keep the export API" playbook as `Select`/`Label`/`PasswordInput`/`Separator`. `ToastProps`/`ToastVariant`/`useToast()`'s return shape are all unchanged.
- **Real behavior gained**: auto-dismiss timers now pause while a toast is hovered or focused (Radix's built-in behavior for `type="foreground"`, the default — the old timer fired regardless of whether the user was still reading it); swipe-to-dismiss (drag right) works out of the box via `data-swipe-*` attributes, no hand-rolled gesture code; enter/exit animation now uses the same `tailwindcss-animate` `data-[state=...]:animate-in`/`animate-out` + `slide-in-from-right-full`/`slide-out-to-right-full` convention every other Radix-based component here already uses, instead of a one-off hand-rolled transform.
- **Architecture note — unlike `Tabs`**: `ToastContainer` and the `Toast`s it wraps are always DOM/React siblings in every real usage (confirmed via the `ToastPlayground` story — one `ToastContainer` directly wraps a `.map()` of `Toast`s in the same render), so folding Radix's `Provider`+`Viewport` into `ToastContainer` internally was a clean fit with zero external API change — not the same "shared Root across disconnected pieces of the tree" tension `TabList`/`TabPanel` have (that rebuild is on hold — see `CONTRIBUTING.md`/session notes).
- **`open`-state bridging**: `Toast` keeps a local `open` boolean (starts `true`) fed into `RadixToast.Root`. When Radix wants to close (auto-dismiss timer, swipe, Escape, or the Close button) it calls `onOpenChange(false)`; `Toast` flips its local `open` to `false` (letting Radix's Presence play the `data-state="closed"` exit animation while still mounted) and only calls the *real* `onDismiss` prop — the one that actually removes the toast from the consumer's own list — after `ANIMATION_MS` (200ms, matching the `duration-200` Tailwind class). This is the same "delay the real removal so the exit animation isn't cut off" contract the old hand-rolled version had, just re-pointed at Radix's `open`/`data-state` instead of a hand-computed inline transform.
- **`duration={0}` → `Infinity`**: this component's own contract is "0 = never auto-dismiss", but Radix's `Root` defaults to 5000ms if no `duration` is given and has no explicit "never" value — passed `Infinity` explicitly instead (same idiom other Radix Toast implementations, e.g. shadcn/ui's, use; `setTimeout` clamps an `Infinity` delay to its ~24.8-day max rather than erroring, which is indistinguishable from "never" for a UI toast).
- **DOM structure**: `Toast.Viewport` renders a real `<ol>` (Radix's own semantics — a toast region is a list) and `Toast.Root` renders an `<li>`, replacing the old plain `<div>`s. The old "outer div ignores pointer events, inner div re-enables them" trick doesn't carry over as-is (a bare `<div>` isn't a valid direct child of `<ol>`) — same visual effect preserved by moving `pointer-events-none` onto the `<ol>` (`ToastContainer`) itself and `pointer-events-auto` onto each `<li>` (`Toast`) instead. Visually identical either way.
- No external consumer uses `Toast`/`ToastContainer`/`useToast` directly (only this library's own `ToastrNotification.stories.tsx`), so this swap had zero cross-repo footprint.
- **Real behavioral change to be aware of**: `Toast` now requires a `ToastContainer` ancestor to render *anything* — Radix's `Toast.Root` portals its content into whatever `Toast.Viewport` is registered on the nearest `Toast.Provider`, and renders nothing at all if none exists (the old hand-rolled version was a plain div and worked completely standalone). Caught this immediately via `ToastrNotification.stories.tsx`'s 5 static single-variant stories (`ToastAll`/`ToastWarning`/`ToastError`/`ToastInfo`/`ToastSuccess`), which used to wrap each `Toast` in a bare layout `<div>` — fixed by wrapping them in `ToastContainer className="static inset-auto w-[380px]"` instead (overriding the fixed bottom-right positioning back to normal document flow, since these are static documentation examples, not the real floating widget). Any future demo/story that renders a bare `Toast` needs the same treatment.
- **Top-left status icon de-hardcoded — new `*IconSolid` glyph set** (`src/components/icons/warning-icon-solid.tsx`, `error-icon-solid.tsx`, `info-icon-solid.tsx`, `success-icon-solid.tsx`): the icon was initially routed through `Icon size="md"` but still used the hand-drawn `WarningIcon`/`ErrorIcon`/`InfoIcon`/`SuccessIcon` assets (shared with `InlineNotification`, form-field error rows, etc.), which bake a literal hex `fill` into the SVG path — no CSS/theme can recolor them, so they'd never respond to dark mode. An intermediate attempt swapped to outline Lucide glyphs (`AlertTriangle`/`XCircle`/`Info`/`CheckCircle2`) plus a new `Icon` `tone="solid"` background system, but that changed the icon's whole visual language (an outline glyph instead of the intended solid colored-circle-with-white-mark look) — reverted per user feedback. The actual fix: 4 new sibling files, each the exact same silhouette as the original hand-drawn asset (colored circle or triangle + white checkmark/exclamation/"i"), but with the shape drawn as `fill="currentColor"` instead of a hardcoded hex. Rendered via `Icon`'s existing `color` prop (`iconColorMap`, mapped to the same `status-warning`/`status-critical`/`status-info`/`status-success` tokens `titleColorMap` already uses for the title text) — icon and title are always in exact color lockstep, in both themes, with zero hardcoded hex anywhere in Toast. The inner white mark stays literal `fill="white"`/`stroke="white"` (intentional — it's contrast against the solid color circle, not something that should vary with theme). Verified via a static-markup render: every icon's `<svg>` class list includes the correct `text-lyra-status-*-strong` token class, the shape path uses `fill="currentColor"`, no literal hex anywhere. Deliberately new files rather than editing the original 4 in place — those are still used, unchanged, by ~13 other components (`InlineNotification`, `EmailInput`, `Select`, etc.) where flipping to `currentColor` would need an audit of every call site's ambient text color to avoid a regression; out of scope here. Exported from `index.ts` and demoed in `Icon.stories.tsx`'s new `SolidStatusIcons` story.
- Story: `Custom Primitives/Toastr Notification` → `Headless Primitives/Toastr Notification` (same stories, just recategorized).
- **Bug fix — drop shadow clipped**: `ToastContainer`'s `Viewport` carried an `overflow-hidden` class over from the original hand-rolled version (predates this Radix rebuild). Each `Toast` has its own `shadow-lg`, whose blur/spread extends past its own border box; since the container is sized to fit its children exactly with no padding, `overflow-hidden` clipped that shadow flush at the container's edge instead of letting it bleed outward. Fixed by removing `overflow-hidden` entirely (rather than adding padding, which would have shifted the effective `bottom-4 right-4` viewport-edge offset) — there's no layout reason to clip a `fixed`, viewport-relative overlay region anyway.
- **Title typography bumped to `lyra-heading-md`**: `RadixToast.Title` previously used `lyra-body-md-emphasis` (14px/20px/500), which is a bold-body treatment, not an actual heading style. Now uses `lyra-heading-md` (16px/24px/500/-0.01rem letter-spacing) — the same preset `ContainerHeader`/`Breadcrumb`/`DashboardCard` use for their own titles — while keeping the per-variant color from `titleColorMap` layered on top.
- **Bug fix — `lyra-status-info-subtle`/`lyra-status-info-medium` were never wired into Tailwind**: found while working on the icon color fix above. `tailwind-preset.ts` only mapped `lyra-status-info-strong`, even though `lyra-tokens.css` defines `--lyra-color-status-info-subtle`/`-medium` in both themes — so `toastVariants`'s own `info` variant (`bg-lyra-status-info-subtle`) was silently rendering with no background fill at all (a pre-existing bug, not introduced by this session's Toast rebuild). Fixed by adding both missing color mappings to `tailwind-preset.ts`.
- **Width bumped 380px → 400px**: `toastVariants`'s base width class (`w-[380px]` → `w-[400px]`), plus the matching `w-[380px]` → `w-[400px]` override on the 5 static single-variant demo stories in `ToastrNotification.stories.tsx` (the interactive playground story has no explicit width override — it's governed entirely by `toastVariants`, so it picked up the new width automatically).
- **Top-left status icon now routed through the shared `Icon` component**: previously a bare custom SVG (`WarningIcon`/`ErrorIcon`/`InfoIcon`/`SuccessIcon`, picked via `iconMap`) rendered directly with a manual `className="h-5 w-5"`. Now wrapped as `<Icon icon={StatusIcon} size="md" decorative />` — `size="md"` resolves to the same `h-5 w-5` (20px), so this is a no-op visually, but it routes the icon through the same sizing/accessibility plumbing (`decorative` sets `aria-hidden`/`focusable={false}` on the `<svg>` itself) every other icon in the library uses instead of a one-off manual wrapper. Note these 4 status glyphs have hardcoded literal `fill` colors baked into their own SVG paths (not `currentColor`), so `Icon`'s `color`/`background` props have no visual effect on them — they were left at `Icon`'s defaults (`color="inherit"`, `background="none"`) since the correct color is already baked into each glyph. The local variable that used to be named `Icon` (shadowing the real component import) was renamed to `StatusIcon` to avoid the collision. `InlineNotification` (`inline-notification.tsx`) uses the identical bare-SVG pattern and was intentionally left as-is — out of scope for this change, which was Toast-specific.
- **Follow-up bug fix — removed vestigial inline sizing from the 4 status-icon assets themselves** (`warning-icon.tsx`, `error-icon.tsx`, `info-icon.tsx`, `success-icon.tsx`): user inspection via DevTools caught that even after routing through `Icon size="md"`, the rendered `<svg>` still carried a literal hardcoded `width="16" height="16"` HTML attribute plus each icon's own internal default `className={cn("h-5 w-5", className)}` fallback — both vestigial from when these icons were always rendered bare. The Tailwind `h-5 w-5` class from `Icon` visually overrode the 16×16 intrinsic size, so it wasn't a visible layout bug, but it meant the icon's real size was still "inline coded" into the leaf SVG rather than being solely a function of the class the caller passes in. Fixed at the source (all 4 files): removed `width="16" height="16"` and the default `"h-5 w-5"` className fallback, so `className={className}` — sizing is now purely whatever the caller (in every real case, an explicit `className` already passed at every call site — verified via full-codebase grep, no consumer relied on the removed default) supplies. Zero visual change (every existing call site already passed its own explicit size class), verified via a static-markup render of `Icon`-wrapped `WarningIcon`/`ErrorIcon` plus a full `tsc` pass.

### Storybook category renamed: "Radix Primitives" → "Headless Primitives"
- The category existed to mark components whose defining behavior comes from a headless (unstyled, behavior-only) library rather than being hand-rolled — but the name "Radix Primitives" implied *only* Radix qualified, which stopped being accurate once `Calendar` (`src/components/calendar.tsx`, built on `react-day-picker`'s `DayPicker` for day-grid generation, keyboard nav, and range-selection math) was checked against the rule and found to fit the same pattern with a different vendor underneath. Renamed the category (all 14 story `title`s, plus the explicit `"Radix Primitives"` entry in `.storybook/preview.ts`'s `storySort.order`) to the vendor-neutral "Headless Primitives" so it accurately covers any behavior-library-backed component, not just Radix-specific ones.
- `Calendar.stories.tsx` moved `Custom Primitives/Calendar` → `Headless Primitives/Calendar` to match — it was previously miscategorized under `Custom Primitives` despite not being hand-rolled HTML (that category is for components with *no* library underneath, e.g. `Badge`/`Tag`/`Spinner`, plain native elements + `React.forwardRef` + `cva`/`cn`).
- The existing rule for what belongs in this category is unchanged, just reworded to not name Radix specifically: a component only qualifies if the library provides the component's *defining* interaction logic (e.g. `react-day-picker`'s day grid, Radix's focus-trap/positioning/dismissal for Popover/Select/Tooltip) — not if it merely uses a headless library internally as one implementation detail for a minor piece. `Autocomplete`/`DatePicker`/`DateTimePicker`/`PhoneInput`/`Table`/`TimePicker` still correctly stay under `Custom Primitives` under this same rule (see the `Label` entry above) — checked package.json and confirmed `react-day-picker` is the only non-Radix headless UI dependency in the project, so `Calendar` was the only other component needing to move.

### Field label casing (content convention, `CONTRIBUTING.md` §18)
- New rule: prototype form-field labels should be Title Case ("Email Address", not "Email address") unless the user explicitly specifies otherwise — added after `EmailInput`'s "Email address" label was flagged via screenshot. Doesn't apply to `aria-label` strings (stay sentence case) or lyra-ui's own internal Storybook placeholder labels (e.g. "Radio label").
- Fixed at the flagged call sites: `Label.stories.tsx`'s `RequiredLabel` story and `EmailInput.stories.tsx` (3 occurrences) — both real lyra-ui files, `"Email address"` → `"Email Address"`. Scoped to just these per explicit user direction — the ~60 other pre-existing label strings across lyra-ui's own Storybook stories (mostly generic placeholders like "Radio label", "New password") were intentionally left as-is, not retroactively rewritten.

### Input (`src/components/input.tsx`)
- Disabled-state hover-border bug fixed — see "Disabled fields still showed the hover border" below (this was the component the bug was originally reported against).

### Disabled fields still showed the hover border (bug fix, multiple files)
- **Bug**: `Input` (`input.tsx`) — reported via a screenshot of the `Disabled` story showing a visible border on a grayed-out field. Root cause: the field's base (non-error, non-readonly) class branch always includes `hover:border-lyra-state-border-hover-neutral`, and the separate `disabled && "..."` override (applied on top, not as an exclusive ternary branch) set `border-transparent` but — unlike the `readonly` branch right above it in the same file — never added `pointer-events-none`. A browser's `:hover` pseudo-class still matches a disabled form control (native `disabled` only blocks focus/typing/click, not hover), so the plain `hover:` rule kept winning whenever the cursor sat over a "disabled" field.
- **Same bug, same root cause, found in 7 more components** by grepping every other place `hover:border-lyra-state-border-hover-neutral` appears and checking whether `disabled` was an exclusive ternary branch (safe) or a non-exclusive `&&` override missing `pointer-events-none` (buggy): `password-input.tsx`, `email-input.tsx`, `time-picker.tsx`, `date-time-picker.tsx` (both `DateTimePicker` and `DateRangePicker` share the same `inputClass`), `phone-input.tsx`, and `autocomplete.tsx`'s `inputShell` all had it. `date-picker.tsx` had the equivalent gap using `data-[disabled=true]:` attribute selectors instead of a JS boolean (its trigger is a `<div>`, not a native disableable element, so it drives state off a `data-disabled` attribute rather than the real `disabled` prop) — same missing `pointer-events-none`, same fix.
- **Fix**: added `pointer-events-none` to each affected `disabled` branch (matching the `readonly` branch's existing convention in every one of these same files — `readonly` already had this right everywhere). Blocking pointer events outright means `:hover` can never match in the first place, rather than trying to out-specificity the existing `hover:` rule with a second override class.
- **Also fixed while auditing**: `radio.tsx`'s unchecked+disabled state was missing the equivalent override its own checked+disabled state already had (line-for-line: `disabled:data-[state=checked]:hover:border-lyra-border-disabled` existed, `disabled:data-[state=unchecked]:hover:...` didn't) — added the missing rule so both states are consistent.
- **Confirmed already correct, left untouched**: `textarea.tsx`, `number-field.tsx`, `tags-input.tsx` (all three treat `disabled` as its own exclusive ternary branch, so the hover class is never present in the disabled case at all); `select.tsx`'s trigger and `checkbox.tsx` (both already had `pointer-events-none`/`disabled:hover:` overrides); `search-input.tsx` (already has `disabled:hover:border-transparent` as a real Tailwind `disabled:` pseudo-class variant — a different but equally valid fix, since its border lives directly on the native `<input>` rather than a wrapping `<div>`).

### FilterChip (`src/components/filter-chip.tsx`)
- Uses the `Select` component internally (not a custom dropdown)
- Props: `label`, `options`, `selectedValues`, `onSelectionChange`, `error`, `disabled`, `onRemove`, `className`
- Four variants: default, active, error, disabled
- Removable variant: pass `onRemove` — renders X button with split border-radius
- Chip height: fixed 32px (`h-8`)
- Default bg: `control-subtle`, active bg: `active-subtle`
- Hover/pressed states use proper tokens (hover-active-subtle, pressed-active-subtle for active; hover-critical-subtle, pressed-critical-subtle for error)
- Values truncate at 200px max-width
- Select dropdown uses `portalDropdown` prop to escape overflow containers
- **Error-variant color fix (on request)**: two spots previously ignored the `error` variant's intended color. (1) `filterChipRemoveButtonVariants`'s `error` entry set the whole remove button's text to `text-lyra-status-critical-strong`, which the "x" (`<X>`, no color class of its own) inherited via `currentColor` — reads as a second red element competing with the chip's own error icon/label, so it now uses the same neutral `text-lyra-fg-secondary` (hover `text-lyra-fg-default`) as the `default` variant's remove button; only the border/background stay red-tinted. (2) the label span shown when a chip has no operators and no selected values hardcoded `text-lyra-fg-default` regardless of variant, so it never actually turned red in the error state — removed the hardcoded class so it inherits the trigger button's own `filterChipVariants({variant})` color, same as every other span in the component.

### Select (`src/components/select.tsx`) — now built on Radix
- Internals swapped onto Radix primitives (same "swap internals, keep the same export API" playbook used for `Accordion`): single-select uses `@radix-ui/react-select` directly; multi-select composes `Popover` (Radix-based) + `Checkbox` (Radix-based) since Radix's own Select is single-value only. Exported `Select`/`SelectProps`/`SelectOption` are unchanged — no consumer (`FilterChip`, `table.tsx`'s `ColumnToggle`, `CreateNew`, or any external app) needed to change.
- Supports: `multiple`, `searchable`, `showSelectAll`, custom `trigger` (button element or icon)
- `dropdownAlign`: `"left" | "right"` — now a Radix `align` *preference* (`"left"`→`"start"`, `"right"`→`"end"`), not a hard pin. Radix still auto-flips/shifts if the preferred side would overflow the viewport, so the old "no collision awareness, must set this explicitly near an edge" caveat no longer fully applies — the prop is kept for API compatibility and as a starting preference.
- `portalDropdown`: accepted but a no-op — Radix's Select/Popover primitives always portal their content. A repo-wide grep found no real caller anywhere passing `portalDropdown={false}` before the swap, so the old inline/non-portal rendering path wasn't carried over.
- Custom `<button>` trigger: single-select can't use Radix's `asChild` on `Select.Trigger` (Radix's own type doesn't support it — the Trigger must stay the real DOM node carrying the combobox's aria/data-state wiring), so a button trigger's className/children are read off and rendered *through* Radix's real Trigger instead of swapping the element out. Multi-select's `Popover` trigger does support `asChild`, so a button trigger is cloned in directly there.
- "Select All" row is a `<button>` so clicking text toggles selection
- See `CONTRIBUTING.md`-style component comments at the top of `select.tsx` for the full before/after writeup.

### TableToolbar (`src/components/table.tsx`)
- Declarative filter API: `filterDefs`, `filterValues`, `onFilterChange`, `onFilterClear`
- Also accepts `filters` as ReactNode for custom filter content
- Filters render inline to the right of Quick Search (same row)
- Record count props still available but not used in Data Management
- Renders FilterChip components and a ghost "Clear" button internally

### Table (`src/components/table.tsx`)
- `useAutoFitRows` hook with ResizeObserver for dynamic pagination
- AutoFit sets `overflow-y: hidden` via inline style
- Non-autofit uses `overflow-auto` class conditionally
- `useTableGrouping` hook for grouping — must receive filtered data, not raw data
- `useColumnReorder` for drag-and-drop column reordering
- TableFooter uses ChevronDown icon element (not background-image) for accessibility

### Button (`src/components/button.tsx`)
- Sizes: sm (24px), default/md (32px), lg (36px), xl (40px), icon-sm (24px), icon/icon-md (32px), icon-lg (36px), icon-xl (40px), **icon-2xl (44px)**
- Variants: default, destructive, outline, ghost, icon
- Data Management uses lg size (36px) for page header buttons; toolbar icon buttons use icon/icon-md (32px)
- **`icon-2xl` (44px) is the canonical AppHeader icon-button size** — added after two hand-rolled implementations of "AppHeader icon button" had drifted apart: `ActionIconButton` (`actions.tsx`) previously had its own separate `cva` (44px `xl`, `rounded-lyra-sm`), while `notifications-bell.tsx`'s trigger and several consuming-app buttons (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s Screen Pop/Conversations/Schedule/Ask AI, in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates`, plus the same pattern in `Agent Nav Testing`'s `Option01-04Page.tsx`) hand-rolled a *different* shape (40px, `rounded-lyra-lg`) rather than using `ActionIconButton`. `Button` is now the single source of truth: `ActionIconButton` composes it internally (mapping its own legacy `sm`/`default`/`lg`/`xl` size names onto `Button`'s `icon-md`/`icon-lg`/`icon-xl`/`icon-2xl`), and every hand-rolled copy above was switched to `<ActionIconButton size="xl">` instead. `Option05Page.tsx` (Agent Nav Testing) has since evolved a persistent active-state/drag-to-reorder icon row `ActionIconButton` doesn't support — left hand-rolled there, but its size/radius were still corrected to 44px/`rounded-lyra-sm` to match everything else.
- **`badge?: number` prop** — renders a count `Badge` (`shape="circle"`, `variant="critical"`, `size="sm"`, absolute-positioned `-top-2 -right-2` on the button's icon content) when set and truthy; only meaningful on an icon-shaped button (`variant="icon"` or an `icon-*` size). This replaced `ActionIconButton`'s own previously-separate hand-rolled badge span, so there's one badge implementation for every icon button in the library, not two. Position note: an earlier version of this offset was flush (`top-0 right-0`, sitting fully inside the button's corner) — corrected to `-top-2 -right-2` (pushed out past the corner) after a side-by-side comparison against the real AppHeader showed the flush version looked wrong.

### ActionIconButton (`src/components/actions.tsx`)
- Thin wrapper around `Button` (`variant="icon"`) — see `Button`'s own entry above for the consolidation history. Its own `size` prop (`"sm" | "default" | "lg" | "xl"`, 32/36/40/44px) maps onto `Button`'s `icon-md`/`icon-lg`/`icon-xl`/`icon-2xl` respectively; external API (including `title`, `badge`) is unchanged, so no consuming code needed to change when this was refactored.
- `ShellIconButton` is a back-compat alias of the same component (`ShellAvatarButton` for `ActionAvatarButton`) — both still exported from `src/index.ts`.
- `actionIconButtonVariants` (the old standalone `cva` instance) no longer exists / isn't exported — it rendered `ActionIconButton`'s markup directly before the `Button` consolidation; nothing in either consuming app imported it directly, so removing it wasn't a breaking change in practice.

### Badge (`src/components/badge.tsx`)
- **Merges what used to be two separate components — `Chip` and `StatusBadge` — into one, discriminated on a `shape` prop** (`"pill" | "circle"`), the same discriminated-union pattern already used elsewhere (`Select`'s `multiple`, `Calendar`'s `mode`).
- `shape="pill"` (default) is the old `Chip`: `color` (10 accent families: slate/red/orange/yellow/lime/green/teal/blue/purple/pink) + `variant` (`"subtle"` soft-bg/strong-text or `"solid"` strong-bg/white text).
- `shape="circle"` is the old `StatusBadge`: `variant` (6 semantic roles: default/info/success/warning/critical/neutral) + `size` (`sm`=16px/`md`=20px/`lg`=24px diameter) + `dot` (plain circle, no content) + `count`/`max` (auto-formats `"{max}+"` past the cap) or arbitrary `children` (e.g. an icon glyph).
- `chip.tsx`/`status-badge.tsx` are deleted; `Chip`/`ChipColor`/`ChipVariant`/`StatusBadge` are no longer exported from `src/index.ts` — only `Badge`/`BadgeProps`/`BadgeShape`/`BadgeColor`/`BadgePillVariant`/`BadgeCircleVariant`/`BadgeSize`.
- **Real call sites migrated**: `page-header.tsx` (`chip`/`chipColor`/`chipVariant` props renamed `badge`/`badgeColor`/`badgeVariant`), `admin-shell.tsx` (`pageChip` → `pageBadge`), `agent-profile.tsx`'s `StatusIcon` + connected-apps count, `agent-notifications.tsx`'s unread-count `titleBadge`, `notifications-bell.tsx`'s count badge (now rendered via `Button`'s own `badge` prop instead — see `Button`'s entry above), and `interaction-nav-item.tsx`'s awaiting-response dot.
- **Cross-repo**: `pageChip`/`showChip` renamed to `pageBadge`/`showBadge` in `lyra-ux-templates`, `agent-next-gen-v1`, and `Outbound-Campaigns` (`DesktopDesignsPage.tsx`, `OutboundEngagementPage.tsx`, `OutboundCampaignsPage.tsx`). `Agent Nav Testing`'s live app had no `Chip`/`StatusBadge`/`pageChip`/`showChip` references; its `vendor/lyra-ui/` snapshot is stale (pre-dates this merge) but is only consulted by CI/Pages builds when the sibling `lyra-ui` checkout is absent — see that folder's own README for the re-sync process (never hand-edit the vendored copy).
- Story file: `Custom Primitives/Badge` (`Badge.stories.tsx`, merged from the old `Chip.stories.tsx` + `StatusBadge.stories.tsx`) — `Default`/`PillAllVariants` (pill shape), `CircleDefault`/`CircleAllVariants`/`CircleSizes`/`CircleOverflow`/`CirclePositionedOnIcon`/`CircleTextContent` (circle shape). `CirclePositionedOnIcon` demonstrates the real icon-button corner offset (`-top-2 -right-2`, matching `Button`'s own `badge` prop) across all five `Button` icon sizes, plus the avatar corner-dot convention (`bottom-[-2px] right-[-2px]`, both a plain dot and an icon-content variant matching `StatusIcon`).
- Naming-audit note: the previously-proposed `Tag ↔ Chip` naming swap (`lyra-ui-naming-audit.md`) is now moot, since `Chip` no longer exists as a separate component to swap names with.

### NotificationsBell (`src/components/notifications-bell.tsx`)
- Trigger now composes `ActionIconButton` (`size="xl"` → 44px) instead of hand-rolling its own `<button>` + `Tooltip` + `Badge` — this was the other half of the "two different AppHeader icon-button shapes" drift documented under `Button`'s entry above (this trigger's old shape was 40px/`rounded-lyra-lg`). External props (`notifications`, `open`/`onOpenChange`, `renderPanel`, etc.) are unchanged.

### Separator (`src/components/separator.tsx`)
- **Renamed from `Divider`** and rebuilt on Radix's `@radix-ui/react-separator` primitive (`SeparatorPrimitive.Root`) instead of a hand-rolled `<div role="separator" aria-orientation={...}>` — matching every other structural atom in the library that already wraps a Radix primitive (`Accordion`, `Select`, `Popover`, `Tooltip`, etc.) rather than hand-rolling its own ARIA semantics. Radix's `Root` sets `role`/`aria-orientation` itself (`role="none"` when `decorative`, which most real call sites are — these are visual dividers, not semantic content boundaries — vs. the old version's unconditional `role="separator"`), and adds a `data-orientation` attribute other selectors could key off if ever needed. Visual output (the `orientation`-driven border classes) is byte-for-byte unchanged, so every existing call site updated as a pure rename: `Divider`→`Separator`, same `orientation`/`className` props, no behavior change.
- New dependency: `@radix-ui/react-separator` (added to `package.json`, bundled into `dist` by `tsup` like every other Radix package this library uses — external consumers don't need to install it themselves, same as `Accordion`'s `@radix-ui/react-accordion`).
- `orientation` prop: `"horizontal"` (default, `w-full border-t`) or `"vertical"` (`h-full border-l`) — mirrors the ad hoc separator patterns already used internally in `app-menu.tsx`, `page-header.tsx`, `table.tsx`, etc., but as a single reusable atom
- Vertical orientation requires a parent with a defined height (e.g. `flex items-center h-6`) to be visible, same constraint as the existing internal `w-px`/`h-full` separator divs
- **Cross-repo**: renamed at every real call site — `login-card.tsx`, `agent-welcome-message.tsx`, `agent-dashboard.tsx`, `dashboard-card.tsx` (lyra-ui itself), `agent-next-gen-v1`'s `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, and all 5 of `Agent Nav Testing`'s `Option01-05Page.tsx`. `Agent Nav Testing`'s `vendor/lyra-ui/` snapshot still has the old `divider.tsx`/`Divider` — stale, out of scope (see that folder's own README on re-syncing; never hand-edit it directly).
- Story: `Custom Primitives/Divider` renamed to `Custom Primitives/Separator` (`Divider.stories.tsx` → `Separator.stories.tsx`) → `Horizontal`, `Vertical`

### Accordion (`src/components/accordion.tsx`)
- `AccordionItem.title` and `.subhead` are typed `React.ReactNode` (widened from `string`) so an item's header can hold richer content — e.g. a name + status `Tag` pill on one line and a multi-line summary on the next — while staying backward compatible with existing plain-string usage.
- Built on the same `CollapsiblePanel` (from `tree-menu.tsx`) used internally elsewhere for smooth open/close; `item.content` can be any React node, including a full `Table`.
- Used in `lyra-ux-templates`' `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` for the home screen's "Latest Contacts" list: each contact is an accordion item (rich title/subhead built from the same fields the old `ListItem`-based version used) whose content is a per-contact interaction-history `Table` (Default `Table` story markup: `TableHeader`/`TableRow` with `hover:bg-transparent`, `flex-[n]` sized `TableHead`/`TableCell` pairs, kebab "More options" button in the last column) wrapped in a fixed-height bordered/rounded container sized to its row count.
- Story: `Custom Primitives/Accordion` → `Rich Header + Table Content` demonstrates the ReactNode title/subhead + embedded `Table` pattern for future reference — check this story before building another "list of expandable rows with a table inside" pattern instead of re-deriving it from scratch.

### Tag (`src/components/tag.tsx`)
- **Bug fix**: every variant's border (`default`, `success`, `warning`, `critical`, `info`) used Tailwind's slash opacity-modifier syntax on a design-token color, e.g. `border-lyra-status-success-strong/30`. Tailwind can only generate opacity-modified utilities for theme colors defined as raw RGB/HSL channel triples — our tokens are CSS custom properties holding full hex/rgba strings (see `lyra-tokens.css`), so that modifier silently produced *no matching CSS rule at all*. The border then fell back to Tailwind Preflight's default (`#e5e7eb` gray) instead of a tinted status color, making every Tag render with a washed-out gray border regardless of variant — visually generic enough that it read as "not a real design-system component" even though it was.
- Fixed by switching those five border classes to a `color-mix()` Tailwind arbitrary value instead, e.g. `border-[color-mix(in_srgb,var(--lyra-color-status-success-strong)_30%,transparent)]` — this works with any color value (not just RGB/HSL triples) and doesn't depend on Tailwind's theme-opacity plumbing.
- This bug affected every consumer of `Tag` (both `lyra-ui` Storybook and `lyra-ux-templates`), not just one app — confirmed by direct Tailwind CLI compilation showing the `/30` classes never emitted a rule while the plain (no-modifier) equivalents did.
- **Takeaway for future components**: don't use Tailwind's `/opacity` modifier on any `lyra-*` color utility — none of our design tokens support it, since they're all plain `var(--lyra-color-*)` references to hex/rgba strings. Use the `border-[color-mix(in_srgb,var(--lyra-color-x)_N%,transparent)]` (or `bg-[color-mix(...)]`) arbitrary-value pattern instead whenever a translucent tint of a token is needed.

### AgentProfile (`src/components/agent-profile.tsx`)
- **Bug fix**: the status-picker popover's "Search statuses" field was a hand-rolled `<input>` (not the real `Input` component), styled with `focus:ring-2 focus:ring-lyra-border-focus`. `lyra-border-focus` maps to `--lyra-color-border-focus-default`, which is intentionally *swapped* between light and dark themes (`#185ba4` blue in light, `#facb33` gold/amber in dark — see the matching `[data-theme="dark"] .focus-visible\:ring-lyra-border-focus` override in `lyra-tokens.css`) because it's meant as a general keyboard-focus-visible indicator for interactive controls (buttons, menu items, tabs), not a text-field focus style. Every real text input (`Input` component) instead focuses with `border-lyra-border-active` + `ring-lyra-border-active/20`, which stays blue in both themes. Using the wrong token made this one search box flash an unexpected gold/orange ring in dark mode while every other input in the app stayed blue.
- Fixed by replacing the hand-rolled `<input>` + absolutely-positioned `Search` icon with the real `Input` component and its `startIcon` prop (`<Input startIcon={<Search .../>} .../>`), which both matches every other text input's focus styling automatically and removes ~10 lines of duplicated markup.
- **Takeaway**: `lyra-border-focus` / `ring-lyra-border-focus` is for keyboard-focus-visible indicators on non-field interactive elements, not text inputs — a hand-rolled input reaching for "the focus color" will likely grab the wrong one. Always use the real `Input` component (with `startIcon`/`endIcon` for icons) for any text field rather than a bespoke `<input>`.
- **Follow-up, from a screenshot of the status menu**: "move Help into the agent profile menu, below Agent Leg Disconnected." Added a new `id: "help"` menu row (label "Help", `CircleHelp` icon) right after the existing `agent-leg` row and before the trailing separator + `Log Out`, plus a new optional `onHelpClick?: () => void` prop wired to that row's `onClick` (same optional-callback shape as `onDarkModeToggle`). Demonstrated in `AgentProfile.stories.tsx`'s `Interactive` story (`onHelpClick={() => alert("Help")}`).
- **Cascaded**: both consumers previously surfaced Help as a separate icon-only affordance with no `onClick` of its own (agent-next-gen-v1: an `ActionIconButton` in the `AppHeader`; lyra-ux-templates: a `NavItem` in the LeftNav) — both were removed (along with their now-unused `HelpCircle`/icon imports) since the new `AgentProfile` row replaces them. Neither consumer passes `onHelpClick` yet, matching the fact that the removed affordances had no real behavior behind them either — just relocated, not newly wired up.

### InteractionNavItem (`src/components/interaction-nav-item.tsx`)
- Represents an active customer interaction as a LeftNav item — a compact colored-initials avatar tile when the rail is collapsed, a full detail card (name, kebab + collapse buttons, message preview, channel + elapsed-time footer) when the rail is expanded.
- `expanded` mirrors `AddChannel`'s prop of the same name/purpose: false → compact avatar tile (rail collapsed), true → full card (rail expanded). Wire it to the same `open` state used for the parent `LeftNav`.
- Initials are derived from `customerName` (first + last name initial, e.g. "Sofia Martinez" → "SM"); with no `customerName`, both the initials and the expanded card's title fall back to `"C"` / `"Customer"`.
- `awaitingResponse` (customer sent a message the agent hasn't replied to yet) switches the avatar/card accents from primary (`bg-lyra-status-info-subtle` / `text-lyra-status-info-strong`) to critical (`bg-lyra-status-critical-subtle` / `text-lyra-status-critical-strong`) and adds a small red badge dot on the compact avatar. No `awaitingResponse` → primary, no badge. Uses the existing `status-*-subtle`/`status-*-strong` token pairing (same convention as `Tag`'s variants), not one-off colors.
- `active` (currently-open interaction) gets an elevated white background + shadow in compact mode, and a `border-lyra-border-active` blue border + elevated background in expanded mode.
- The outer element is a `<div role="button" tabIndex={0}>`, not a real `<button>`, because the expanded card contains its own nested `<button>`s (each channel row's kebab) — buttons can't legally nest inside buttons in HTML, and the compact tile uses the same element type for consistency even though it has no nested controls. (A chevron/`onCollapseClick` button was removed from the expanded card's header row early on; the header row itself no longer has any button at all — see the kebab-menu bullet below.)
- **`LeftNav` gained a new `header` prop** (`src/components/left-nav.tsx`) to host a list of these above the regular nav items — mirrors the existing `footer` prop (used for `AddChannel`) exactly, including the same overlay-mode `React.cloneElement(..., { expanded: hoverOpen })` auto-injection; in inline mode, the consumer passes its own `expanded={open}` on each item, same as `footer` already does for `AddChannel`. Added `items-center` to both `header`/`footer` wrappers so compact tiles center correctly in the 52px collapsed rail width.
- Demonstrated in `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story via `header`: an active + awaiting-response item, an inactive + awaiting-response item, and a no-`customerName` + not-awaiting item (showing the `"C"` fallback and primary/no-badge state).
- **Also has its own dedicated `InteractionNavItem.stories.tsx`** (`UI/InteractionNavItem`, 8 stories: Compact/Expanded × active/inactive/no-customer, plus a stacked preview of each mode). Being embedded inside another component's story (here, "Agent Next Gen Left Nav") does **not** make a component browsable on its own in Storybook's sidebar — every standalone component needs its own `ComponentName.stories.tsx` too, same as `AddChannel` has both its own stories file *and* an appearance inside that same Agent Next Gen story. Forgetting the dedicated file was reported back as "I don't see it in the storybook."
- Compact tile follow-up: added a `border` matching the avatar's tone (`border-lyra-status-critical-strong` / `border-lyra-status-info-strong`) around the avatar square, sized to 32×32px (`h-8 w-8`, down from an original 44px), radius `rounded-lyra-sm` (6px, matches every corner in the component — outer wrapper, avatar square, expanded card, kebab button), initials at `lyra-body-sm-emphasis` (12px).
- **Awaiting-response badge dot — since consolidated onto the shared `Badge` component.** Was originally a hand-rolled `h-3 w-3` span fixed to the top-right corner (`-right-1 -top-1`) — a `h-3.5`/`w-3.5` mixup (14px, not the intended 12px) at that stage is documented in Important Patterns below. Per a later request, this dot now: (1) renders via `<Badge shape="circle" dot variant="critical" size="sm" .../>` (Badge's own `sm` dot = 8×8px) instead of a bespoke span, and (2) sits at the **bottom**-right corner (`bottom-[-2px] right-[-2px]`, matching `agent-profile.tsx`'s `Avatar`/`StatusIcon` corner-dot convention) instead of top-right — do not reintroduce the old top-right/hand-rolled version.
- **`InteractionNavItem.stories.tsx` gained a "Compact — Channel Icon Badge" story** — a Storybook-only concept demo (not a new real prop) showing the top-left count badge's position instead holding a channel-type icon (chat/email/voice/WhatsApp) rather than a number, for a single-channel tile. Lucide's default thin stroke disappears at this size and a full solid `fill` turns line icons into blobs, so `MessageSquare`/`Mail`/`Phone` use a heavier `strokeWidth={3}` outline instead (`WhatsAppIcon` is already a solid glyph, unchanged); icon size is `h-2 w-2` (8px, matching `StatusIcon`'s Check/Minus glyphs) inside a standard `Badge size="md"` (20×20px) — 8px content + `md`'s 6px-per-side padding lands exactly on `md`'s own 20px minimum, so it renders as a true circle rather than an oval. `WhatsAppIcon` was additionally exported from `channel-row.tsx` (previously module-private) so this story could reuse the real glyph instead of a duplicated inline SVG.
- **Multi-channel support**: replaced the single `channelIcon`/`channelLabel`/`preview` props with `channels?: InteractionChannel[]` (`{ type, elapsed, preview?, current?, awaitingResponse?, removable?, menuItems? }` — see `ChannelRow` below for what `type` does and why icon/label were dropped from this shape). A customer can have more than one open conversation (e.g. Chat + Email + SMS + WhatsApp simultaneously); the compact tile shows a numeric count badge (top-left, only when `channels.length > 1`, reusing `ActionIconButton`'s exact 18px badge classes since it needs to fit a legible digit — unlike the 12px awaiting-response dot, which has no text) and the expanded card dispatches each channel to its matching per-type row component, with a `border-t` divider between rows (handled via each row's own `isFirst` prop, not the parent's `i > 0` check).
- **`current` + `active` interaction**: a channel row only gets the blue highlight (`bg-lyra-status-info-subtle`) when `active && channel.current` — i.e. an inactive `InteractionNavItem` never highlights any of its channel rows, even if one is flagged `current`, matching an explicit requirement ("the background of the active channel should only be blue on the active cards, the other cards should look plain").
- The expanded card's preview text moved from a single top-level paragraph to living inside each channel's own row (`ch.preview`), since each channel can have a different message/preview once there's more than one.
- `InteractionChannel` also gained its own `awaitingResponse?: boolean` (separate from the card-level `awaitingResponse` prop): the specific channel awaiting a response gets a `critical` (red) chip via `tagVariants({ variant: ch.awaitingResponse ? "critical" : "success", shape: "pill" })` instead of `success` (green), and the expanded card's outer border is `border-lyra-status-critical-strong` whenever the card-level `awaitingResponse` is true — taking priority over the `active` blue border (an active card that's also awaiting response still shows the red "needs attention" border; `active` only continues to control the elevated shadow).
- Hover behavior: the outer expanded card no longer has its own `hover:bg-lyra-state-hover` — hover is scoped to each individual channel row instead (skipped on whichever row is already highlighted `current`+`active`), while the whole card remains one clickable unit — channel rows don't call `stopPropagation`, so clicking anywhere (including inside a specific channel row) still bubbles up to the single `onClick`, which should navigate to that customer's last-active channel, not a per-row destination.
- The compact tile's elapsed-time label now uses the new `lyra-body-xs` preset (see below) instead of `lyra-body-sm`.
- `elapsed` is always a 4-digit `MM:SS` string (e.g. `"00:02"` = 2 seconds since the customer's last response), consistently in both compact and expanded modes — all story examples (`InteractionNavItem.stories.tsx` and `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav") use this format for both modes. An earlier pass had compact using `MM:SS` (`"08:27"`) and expanded using a relative label (`"8m"`) — that inconsistency was corrected; don't reintroduce a relative-label format for either mode.
- **Kebab (⋮) dropdown menu**: each channel row's trailing button (formerly a `Trash2` remove icon, gated by `ch.removable !== false`) is a real dropdown trigger built on the shared `Menu` component. This — along with the row markup itself, the per-type icon/label, and the default menu items — now lives entirely in `channel-row.tsx`, not `interaction-nav-item.tsx`. See the dedicated `ChannelRow` section below.
- `onRemove` was removed from `InteractionChannel` entirely (it was `@deprecated` and unused after the trash→kebab change; the type was rebuilt from scratch for the `type`-based channel-row split, so there was no reason to keep the dead field around).
- **Follow-up, from a screenshot**: "add a drop shadow to the hover interactionNavItem cards." The expanded card had no hover treatment at all (only its per-channel-row hover, per the bullet above) — an inactive card sitting flat with `shadow-sm`'s absence and no lift on hover. Added `hover:shadow-md` to the card's shared `expandedCardClassName` (used by both the real expanded card and the compact tile's hover-popover preview), scoped to inactive cards only — an `active` card already carries a permanent `shadow-md` (the "elevated card" token per `Shadows.stories.tsx`) regardless of hover, so hovering it stays at that same elevation rather than trying to stack a second shadow on top.
- **Follow-up, from a second screenshot**: "not when they are in the expanded leftNav, just the hover states on the collapsed leftNav." The above had applied uniformly to `expandedCardClassName`, so the real expanded (rail-expanded) card picked up the same hover shadow — not what was wanted. Reverted `expandedCardClassName` itself to no hover shadow (`active && "shadow-md"`, active-only, `transition-colors` restored); the shadow now lives in exactly two places instead, both scoped to the collapsed rail: the compact avatar tile's own class list, and, appended on top of `expandedCardClassName` only at that one render site, the compact tile's hover-popover preview — since that preview only ever appears from a collapsed-rail hover in the first place.
- **Follow-up, from a third screenshot**: "I want the dropshadow on the cards when they are being displayed, not only when they are hovered on... this should always be there." Both of the two spots above were still gated on CSS `:hover` (`hover:shadow-md`); dropped the `hover:` prefix on both — the compact tile's inactive branch was made plain `shadow-md` (permanent), and the hover-popover preview's added class became `!active && "shadow-md"` with no `hover:` gate at all.
- **Follow-up, from a fourth screenshot**: "no dropshadow on the collapsed interactionNavItem — JUST the card being displayed on hover." The previous fix had put the permanent shadow on *both* spots; pulled it back off the small icon-rail tile itself (reverted to no shadow at all, `transition-colors` restored, `hover:bg-lyra-state-hover` unchanged) — the shadow now lives only on the hover-popover preview card (the floating "Jamie Torres"-style detail card), which keeps its permanent `!active && "shadow-md"` from the bullet above.
- **Follow-up bug, from a fifth screenshot**: with the tile's background still on plain `hover:bg-lyra-state-hover`, moving the pointer off the tile and onto the preview popover next to it (to actually read/click something in it) dropped the tile's `:hover` background entirely — the tile's own DOM node is no longer under the pointer once it's over the popover, even though the preview it triggered is still open. Fixed by driving the tile's background off the component's existing `hoverCardOpen` state instead of CSS `:hover` (`active ? "bg-lyra-bg-surface-base shadow-sm" : hoverCardOpen && "bg-lyra-state-hover"`) — that state already stays true across the move (the preview's own `onMouseEnter` re-arms it, same mechanism keeping the popover itself open), so the tile now keeps reading as "the one being shown" for as long as its preview is open, not just while the pointer happens to still be sitting on the tile itself.
- **Follow-up, from a sixth screenshot**: "use blue badges unless the interaction is waiting for an agent response" — the compact tile's channel-count badge (top-left circle, e.g. "2") was hardcoded `bg-lyra-bg-destructive` (red) regardless of `awaitingResponse`, even though it's just a count, not itself a "needs attention" signal — that's already conveyed separately by the small awaiting-response dot (top-right, only rendered `awaitingResponse && ...`) and the avatar/border tone. Made the count badge conditional: `awaitingResponse ? "bg-lyra-bg-destructive" : "bg-lyra-bg-primary"` — blue (the same solid-primary token buttons/active-states use) by default, red only once the interaction is genuinely awaiting a response.

### ChannelRow (`src/components/channel-row.tsx`)
- **Extracted from `interaction-nav-item.tsx`** so kebab-menu behavior can be owned per channel type at the component level, instead of being configured generically from `InteractionNavItem`'s props or from within a story. Direct trigger: "there will be different kebab menu items and functionality depending on channels and I want to control that at a component level not merely within the InteractionNavItem stories."
- `InteractionChannel.icon`/`.label` (free-form `ReactNode`/`string`, previously supplied by each story) were replaced with a single required `type: "chat" | "email" | "sms" | "whatsapp" | "voice"` (`ChannelType`). The icon and label are no longer story/consumer-supplied — they're baked into each type's row component, so every `InteractionNavItem` consumer renders visually identical Chat/Email/SMS/WhatsApp/Voice rows without having to re-supply the same icon+label every time.
- Five exported per-type components — `ChatChannelRow`, `EmailChannelRow`, `SmsChannelRow`, `WhatsAppChannelRow`, `VoiceChannelRow` — each a thin wrapper around an internal (unexported) `ChannelRow` base that does the actual rendering (chip, `Clock` + elapsed time, trailing kebab, preview text). Each wrapper supplies its own icon, its own label, and its own **default kebab menu**:
  - Chat/Email/SMS/WhatsApp (all share `DIGITAL_MENU_ITEMS`): Unassign & Dismiss (`TriangleAlert`), Consult/Transfer (composite `User` + `ArrowUpRight` — no single Lucide icon covers "transfer"), Outcome (`CircleCheck`, tinted blue), Send Transcript (`Send`), Download Transcript (`FileDown`), Translate Messages (`Languages`).
  - Voice (`VOICE_MENU_ITEMS`) swaps the transcript/translate items for recording-appropriate ones: Unassign & Dismiss, Consult/Transfer, Outcome, **Listen to Recording** (`PlayCircle`), **Download Recording** (`FileDown`) — demonstrates that menu contents genuinely differ per channel type, not just in theory.
  - A specific row's menu can still be overridden via `InteractionChannel.menuItems` (e.g. one particular Chat row with extra actions) without affecting every other row of that type.
- `InteractionNavItem` dispatches each channel to its row component via a lookup table, `CHANNEL_ROW_COMPONENTS: Record<ChannelType, ...>`, keyed by `ch.type` — adding a 6th channel type later means adding one entry to this map plus one new wrapper component, not touching `InteractionNavItem`'s render logic at all.
- The base `ChannelRow`'s `highlighted` and `isFirst` props are computed by `InteractionNavItem` (from `active && ch.current` and `i === 0` respectively) and passed down — the row components themselves stay stateless about their position/highlight status.
- `KebabMenuButton` (the portal-based dropdown trigger — see the "Portal positioning is required" note that used to live in the `InteractionNavItem` section) also moved here, since it's now owned by the row, not the card.
- `src/index.ts` exports `ChannelRow`, `ChatChannelRow`, `EmailChannelRow`, `SmsChannelRow`, `WhatsAppChannelRow`, `VoiceChannelRow`, and the `ChannelType`/`InteractionChannel`/`ChannelRowInstanceProps` types (the last two re-exported, for convenience, from both `channel-row.tsx` directly and via `interaction-nav-item.tsx`).
- **Portal positioning is still required, not optional**: the dropdown renders via `createPortal(..., document.body)` with `position: fixed` coordinates computed from the trigger button's own `getBoundingClientRect()` (`{ top: rect.bottom + 4, left: rect.right }`, plus `transform: translateX(-100%)` to right-align without knowing the menu's width ahead of render).
