Rendering it inline gets clipped by the card's own `overflow-hidden`/rounded corners — confirmed by a user screenshot showing the dropdown cut off mid-item. `overflow-hidden` on an ancestor clips any descendant positioned relative to a containing block inside that ancestor; `position: fixed` escapes it (its containing block is the viewport, not the clipped ancestor) — the standard technique for popovers/dropdowns/tooltips that must escape a scrolling or overflow-clipped card.
- All existing stories (`InteractionNavItem.stories.tsx`, `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav") were updated to pass `{ type: "chat" | ... }` instead of `{ icon, label }` — the previously-"Call"-labeled channel is now `type: "voice"` (renders as "Voice"), matching the 5-type taxonomy (Chat, Email, SMS, WhatsApp, Voice) the component is built around.

### CXoneSmiley (`src/components/cxone-smiley.tsx`)
- Added an optional `monochrome?: boolean | string` prop: renders both eyes and all four smile-arc segments in a single color instead of the default multicolor (blue/green/orange/magenta) scheme. `monochrome` (`true`) uses the brand blue `#2196F3`; any other string is used as-is as a CSS color. Kept for any future use, but **this is a different, unrelated mark from the "Agent Next Gen" app icon** — see the correction below and the Scope Rules entry.
- **Correction**: this prop was first added, and briefly used, for `lyra-ux-templates`' home-screen "welcome" modal smiley. That was wrong — the reference design was actually the existing `assets/app-icon.svg` mark (two dot "eyes" + a single blue gradient swoosh "smile", the same icon already used for the app logo in `AppName`/`AppHeader` and `LoginCard`), not `CXoneSmiley`'s rainbow multicolor face. The welcome modal now renders `<img src={appIcon} .../>` directly, matching the header logo. This is the same "wrong icon" mistake documented in the `LoginCard` entry below — see the Scope Rules note.

### LoginCard (`src/components/login-card.tsx`)
- Promoted from `lyra-ux-templates`' `LoginPage.tsx` into a real lyra-ui template component, since the whole card (branding, Phone Setup radio group, dark-mode toggle, conditional Phone Number / Station ID reveal, Launch button, Save Preferences, "Compiling Experience" launch sequence) is reusable UI, not page-specific logic.
- Fully self-contained: manages its own phone setup selection, phone/station-id validation ("12345" is the only valid Station ID — a placeholder rule for the demo), dark-mode toggle (writes `data-theme` to `document.documentElement`, same mechanism as `AgentProfile`), and the launch sequence (3 sequential steps via `ConversationMessage`'s `process` prop, then a card fade-out).
- Props: `appName` (default "Agent Next Gen"), `appIcon` (default: the shared `assets/app-icon.svg` mark — **not** `CXoneSmiley`, which is a different, unrelated icon; this was wrong in an earlier pass and made the card render the wrong logo whenever `appIcon` wasn't overridden), `defaultPhoneSetup`, `defaultLaunching` (auto-starts the launch sequence on mount — used for the `Launching` story), `onLaunch` (fired after the fade-out completes; the consuming template does routing from here, so this component has no knowledge of app-specific page/route types)
- `app-icon.svg` already exists identically in both `lyra-ui/src/assets/` and `lyra-ux-templates/src/assets/` — same convention as `AppHeader.stories.tsx`/`AppName.stories.tsx`/`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, which all import it directly rather than using an icon component. `lyra-ux-templates`' `LoginPage.tsx` no longer needs to pass its own `appIcon` override — the default already matches.
- The templates app's `LoginPage.tsx` is now just a thin full-screen wrapper: `<LoginCard onLaunch={() => onNavigate?.("agent")} />`
- Stories: `Templates/Login Card` → `Phone Setup / Integrated Soft Phone`, `Phone Setup / Phone Number`, `Phone Setup / Station ID`, `Launching`
- Sized as a small modal: renders via `Container variant="modal"` with `headerTitle`/`headerIcon` (instead of a hand-rolled bordered/shadowed div) at `w-[360px]` — matches the "Small (360px)" convention from `Custom Primitives/Modal`'s width variants (`sm`/`md`/`lg` → `360px`/`480px`/`640px`). Body content sits in an inner `px-5 pb-5 pt-4` wrapper since `Container` itself only pads its header, matching the `px-5` body pattern in `Modal.stories.tsx`'s `FormContent`.
- Header title (`appName`) renders at `lyra-heading-lg` via `headerTitleClassName="lyra-heading-lg"`, not `Container`'s default `lyra-heading-md` — added because `Container`/`ContainerHeader` had no way for a consumer to size the title up. This required adding a new `headerTitleClassName?: string` passthrough prop to `Container` (forwarded to `ContainerHeader`'s existing `titleClassName` prop, which previously could only be set by components using `ContainerHeader` directly, not through `Container`). Backward compatible — omitting it keeps the `lyra-heading-md` default.

### TreeMenu (`src/components/tree-menu.tsx`) gains per-child icons
- **`TreeMenuChild` gained an optional `icon?: React.ReactNode`**, for a reference screenshot (a "Call Centers" nav tree) showing a small icon on every leaf row (individual call centers), not just top-level `TreeMenuItem`s (which already had `icon`). Renders in a `flex-shrink-0` span ahead of the label, same position as the top-level icon.
- **Deliberately does NOT recolor the child icon based on active state**, unlike the top-level `item.icon` (which TreeMenu tints via `isParentActive`/`isLeafActive`). The reference screenshot shows every leaf's icon the same fixed color regardless of which row is selected — a per-item "type" glyph, not a selection indicator — so the child icon renders exactly as the consumer passes it (color included), no wrapping color logic.
- **The "Call Centers" root label + gray header band from the same screenshot is NOT a `TreeMenu`/`TreeMenuItem` feature** — that gray band is a distinct section header sitting above the tree, not a tree row itself. `TreeMenu.stories.tsx`'s "Call Centers" story (`CallCentersTree`) demonstrates one light way to add a section header above a standalone `TreeMenu` (a plain button + the already-exported `CollapsiblePanel`) for a case that's just a tree, nothing else.

### TreeMenu supports arbitrary nesting depth
- **`TreeMenuChild` gained an optional `children?: TreeMenuChild[]` (+ `defaultOpen?`)**, making any row — not just a top-level `TreeMenuItem` — able to expand/collapse its own nested children, recursively. Request: an "Enterprise" root added above an existing flat list of call-center categories (Financial Services, Hospitality, Insurance, ...) — the ask was for "Enterprise" to *contain* that whole list as its own expandable children, not sit as a sibling beside them, which needs a genuine third nesting level (Enterprise → category → individual call center) that `TreeMenu` couldn't do before (`TreeMenuChild` was a flat leaf: no icon-recoloring-on-active, no chevron, no further children).
- **`TreeMenuRow` (internal) was previously two separate code paths** — a depth-0 expandable row, and a second, inline, non-expandable rendering block just for flat children. Unified into one recursive function parameterized by `depth`, called by itself for each level of `children`, since a nested row now needs the exact same expand/chevron/active-recolor behavior a top-level row always had.
- **Indentation scales by `depth` instead of being two fixed, hand-picked values.** Depth 0 keeps the original 10px (`px-2.5`); every deeper level adds the same 30px step the original depth-1 indent already implied (`pl-10` = 40px = 10 + 1×30) — so depth 2+ continues that step consistently rather than introducing a new, arbitrarily-chosen increment.
- **Guide-line connector: one continuous line per expanded children list, not a short segment per row.** First version scoped the line to each individual child row's own height (a small `relative` wrapper around just that row's button) — caught from a screenshot as wrong: with 3 levels now possible, that rendered as disconnected dashes, one broken stub per row, instead of a single line running from "Financial Services" down through "Hospitality" down to "Insurance" the way a real tree connector should. Fixed by moving the line to the PARENT's own `relative` wrapper around the whole `<ul>` of children (one line per expanded list, sized to that wrapper's natural height) — it now runs continuously through every sibling at that level, including down through any of those siblings' own expanded descendants, since the wrapper's height already includes that nested content.
- **Icon recoloring is now keyed off "does this row have children," not "is this a top-level item."** A row with `children` (any depth) recolors its icon based on active/parent-active state, same as a top-level item always did; a childless leaf row's icon renders exactly as passed with no recoloring (unchanged from the per-child-icon behavior above — the reference screenshot's call-center icons stay one fixed color regardless of which row is selected).
- **Backward compatible for every existing 2-level consumer** (`LeftNav`, `AdminShell`, and every other `TreeMenu` story) — none of them ever pass a `children` array on a `TreeMenuChild`, so they keep rendering through the exact same depth-0/depth-1 code paths and pixel values as before, just now expressed as `depth === 0`/`depth === 1` instead of two separate blocks. Verified via `tsc`/`vite build` across lyra-ui, Outbound-Campaigns, agent-next-gen-v1, lyra-ux-templates, and lyra-form-generator.
- Consumer usage: Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard `CallCentersSideMenu.tsx` — "Enterprise" (depth 0) → categories like "Financial Services" (depth 1, own `defaultOpen`/children) → individual call centers (depth 2, leaf, icon).
- **For a real app-level side panel (a tree that toggles from a page header, can be pinned/unpinned, hover-peeks, etc.), use `SidePanel` (side-panel.tsx) instead of that lighter composition** — that's the actual established pattern for this, already used by `AdminShell`'s own left nav tree (`<SidePanel side="left" headerTitle={navTitle}><TreeMenu items={...} /></SidePanel>`, wired to `PageHeader`'s `panelToggle="left"`/`panelPinned`/`onPanelToggle`/`onPanelHoverStart`/`onPanelHoverEnd`). Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard side menu (`CallCentersSideMenu.tsx`) originally used the lighter `TreeMenu.stories.tsx`-style composition in a plain fixed `<div>` — corrected to use real `SidePanel` + `PageHeader`'s built-in toggle once it was pointed out that this bypassed the established pattern.
- **`CollapsiblePanel` (already used internally by `TreeMenu` for each expandable row) is now also exported from `index.ts`** — it wasn't before, so a consumer wanting the lighter "section header wrapping a standalone tree" composition had no way to reuse it and would have had to hand-roll a second animated-height implementation.
- Story: `Custom Primitives/TreeMenu` → new `Call Centers` (alongside `Default`, `All Collapsed`, `Chevron Left`, `No Icons`).

### TreeMenu gains `exactSelection`
- **New `exactSelection?: boolean` prop (default `false`), threaded through `TreeMenuRow`'s recursion.** Request: Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard side menu needed "click any row — parent or leaf, any depth — and exactly that row reads as selected," with the SAME background-pill treatment regardless of depth. `TreeMenu`'s existing default behavior doesn't do either of those: only a childless leaf gets the background pill (`isLeafActive`); a parent instead gets a plain-text blue treatment (`isParentActive`) that also *cascades* — it fires whenever any of that parent's direct children has `active: true`, on top of its own `active` flag. Right behavior for `LeftNav`/`AdminShell`'s own nav trees (a whole ancestor chain reads as "in the current section"), wrong for a tree meant to have exactly one selected row at a time.
- **Implementation is a two-line redefinition, not a rewrite**: with `exactSelection` on, `isParentActive` is forced to `false` (no cascade, ever) and `isLeafActive` becomes just `item.active` (regardless of `hasChildren`) instead of `item.active && !hasChildren`. Every other line of `TreeMenuRow` — the className branches, the accent bar, the icon recolor condition — reads those same two variables unchanged, so a selected row (leaf OR parent) automatically gets the full background/text/icon treatment a leaf always got, with zero other code changes.
- **Off by default, so every existing consumer is unaffected** — `LeftNav`, `AdminShell`, and every other `TreeMenu` story/usage keep the exact same cascading-parent behavior they always had. Only Outbound-Campaigns' `CallCentersSideMenu.tsx` passes `exactSelection` (see that file's own doc comment). Not yet adopted as the default anywhere — explicitly requested to stay scoped to that one consumer for now.
- Verified via `tsc`/`vite build` across lyra-ui, Outbound-Campaigns, agent-next-gen-v1, lyra-ux-templates, and lyra-form-generator.

### SidePanel (`src/components/side-panel.tsx`) gains `headerTitleBadge`
- **New `headerTitleBadge?: React.ReactNode` prop, forwarded to `PanelHeader`/`ContainerHeader`'s existing `titleBadge` slot** (which already renders inline immediately after the title text, same row — `ContainerHeader` already had this seam for something like a `Badge`, it just wasn't exposed through `SidePanel`). Request: a chevron right after a side panel's title, opening a small menu that switches both the header text and the panel's own tree content — `headerActions` wasn't right for this since that slot renders at the header's *far* right, not glued to the title itself.
- **Built from `Select` (select.tsx), not the experimental/unexported `MenuRadix`/`Menu` primitives.** `Select` already supports a fully custom `trigger` (a bare icon, per `Select.stories.tsx`'s "Custom Trigger (Icon, Single-Select)" story) plus controlled single-select (`value`/`onValueChange`) — exactly "bare chevron opens a short list of mutually-exclusive options," and it's real, exported, established API, unlike `MenuRadix` (still Storybook-only, explicitly not wired into `index.ts` per its own doc comment).
- Story: `Custom Primitives/SidePanel` → new `Side Panel — View Switcher (headerTitleBadge)`, alongside `Side Panel — Left/Right`, `PanelPinButton`, `PanelPinButton — Custom Icon`.
- Consumer usage: Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard `CallCentersSideMenu.tsx` — switches between a "Call Centers" tree (the "Enterprise" one described above) and a flat "Service Groups" category list, matching a second reference screenshot.

### AdminShell (`src/components/admin-shell.tsx`)
- Promoted from `lyra-ux-templates`' `OutboundEngagementPage.tsx`, which had a left nav-tree side `Panel`, a `PageHeader`, an interior detail `Panel`, and a right side `Panel` — scaffolding that turned out to be ~90% duplicated verbatim in `DesktopDesignsPage.tsx` (Agent Workspace Premium / Designer) too. Unlike `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` (a Storybook-only reference implementation that the real apps re-implement separately), `AdminShell` is a genuine reusable component exported from `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` and imported directly by both pages — this is what the user asked for ("use this template in the lyra-ux-templates for both Outbound Engagement and Agent Workspace Premium").
- Owns: the left nav `Panel` (`TreeMenu` + pin/hover/cookie state), the `PageHeader` row (optional via `showPageHeader`, off for the bare "Shell" story), and the right side `Panel`. Cookie key for the left panel's pinned state is `${storageKeyPrefix}_panel_pinned` — pass the same prefix the page used before promotion to preserve users' existing pinned preference (e.g. `"lyra_outbound"` → `lyra_outbound_panel_pinned`; Designer's pre-promotion cookie was literally `lyra_panel_pinned`, so its prefix is `"lyra"`, not `"lyra_panel"`).
- Does NOT own the interior (right, in-content) detail panel's open state — that stays fully controlled by the consumer (`interiorPanelOpen`/`onInteriorPanelClose` props) since it's typically toggled from a button living inside `children` (e.g. `DesktopDesignsPage`'s `TableToolbar` `onRightPanelToggle`), not by the shell itself.
- `children` renders below the `PageHeader`, to the left of the interior panel — anything a page needs there (tabs, toolbars, tables, or nothing) goes here. Outbound Engagement currently passes just `<div className="flex-1" />` (its content area is still a placeholder), matching its current empty state.
- Props: `storageKeyPrefix`, `navTitle`, `navItems` (`TreeMenuItem[]`), `defaultLeftPinned`, `showPageHeader`, `pageTitle`, `pageBadge`, `pageActions`, `interiorPanelTitle`, `interiorPanelOpen`, `onInteriorPanelClose`, `interiorPanelContent`, `rightPanelContent`, `children`.
- Stories: `Templates/Admin UIs` → `Admin UIs – Shell` (bare shell, no PageHeader), `Admin UIs – With Page Header` (mirrors the Outbound Engagement "Campaigns" configuration, like the `Templates/Agent Next Gen` story's Shell/With Page Header split).
- The demo chrome's `LeftNav` icon rail (`AdminShellDemo`/`AdminShellWithAiDemo` in the stories file) now uses the same "Outbound Engagement Left Nav" set as `LeftNav.stories.tsx`: Monitor (`Gauge`, active) / Configure (`Settings`) / Review (`BarChart3`) — previously it showed an unrelated Monitor/Dashboard/Configure/Designer/Examples set left over from before the demo's `AppHeader` said "Outbound Engagement," so the icon rail didn't match what the header claimed.
- Same demo chrome's `AppName` was also still static/non-interactive after `AppHeader.stories.tsx` was fixed to open a real `AppMenu` on click — this didn't cascade automatically because the click-to-open behavior is Radix `Popover` wiring hand-duplicated in each consuming story file, not something `AppName`/`AppHeader` provide themselves. Fixed locally here the same way (own `AppNameWithMenu` helper + `APP_MENU_GROUPS`), using the same Agent Next Gen / Agent Workspace Premium / Outbound Engagement (active) content as `lyra-ux-templates`' real `Header.tsx`, since that's what this demo's header actually displays. **This class of fix will keep needing to be repeated per file** until the menu-open behavior lives inside `AppName`/`AppHeader` itself instead of being copy-wired by every consumer — see Known Debt.
- After promoting, confirmed via `grep` that no inline `Panel variant="side"`/`TreeMenu` markup remained in either `OutboundEngagementPage.tsx` or `DesktopDesignsPage.tsx`, and rebuilt both `lyra-ux-templates` and `agent-next-gen-v1` (which also consumes `lyra-ui`) plus `lyra-ui`'s Storybook to confirm nothing broke.

### Chart / DonutChart (`src/components/chart.tsx`, `src/components/donut-chart.tsx`)
- Added to back `agent-next-gen-v1`'s home-screen Activity card (Available/Working/Unavailable ring, replacing the old Schedule summary card). First tried `recharts`, then `c3` (rejected: c3's `d3@^5` dependency carries 9 high-severity vulnerabilities with no non-breaking fix, and c3 itself is effectively unmaintained) before settling on **ECharts** via `echarts-for-react`, per explicit user direction since more chart types are coming and engineering may revisit the choice later — picked for being actively maintained with a wide chart-type catalog, so the next chart type needed is very likely already covered rather than requiring another library swap.
- **`Chart`** is a deliberately dumb wrapper around `echarts-for-react` — takes a raw ECharts `option` object and renders it, nothing else. This is the shared base every future ECharts-backed component should build on rather than each hand-rolling its own `ReactECharts` usage.
- **`DonutChart`** is the first concrete chart built on `Chart`: a ring given `data: { label, value, colorVar }[]`. Deliberately renders only the ring, no built-in legend — a consumer that wants a legend (like the Activity card's colored-dot + label + percent rows) builds it from the same `data` array alongside the chart, so `DonutChart` stays reusable for any "ring + external legend" layout rather than being locked to one card's exact look. See `Chart.stories.tsx`'s `DonutVariants` ("Donut - All Variants") → "Ring + external legend" for the reference composition (`showTooltip={false}` on the chart since the external legend already shows every value). (`DonutChart.stories.tsx` was later folded into `Chart.stories.tsx` as a Storybook-only reorganization — same component/props, just grouped under one `Custom Primitives/Chart` story file instead of a separate top-level entry.)
- **Canvas color gotcha (read before adding any other canvas-based chart)**: ECharts renders to `<canvas>`, and the Canvas 2D API cannot resolve CSS custom properties — `ctx.fillStyle = "var(--lyra-color-status-success-strong)"` is silently invalid and paints black instead of erroring. Every lyra token color handed to ECharts has to be resolved to a concrete value via `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue(...)` first. `DonutChart` does this itself (`resolveCssColor`) and re-resolves whenever `document.documentElement`'s `[data-theme]` attribute flips (via a `MutationObserver`, exposed internally as `useThemeVersion`) — without that, a chart's colors would go stale the instant a user toggled dark mode, since the resolved hex values were only ever computed once at first render. Any future Chart.js/ApexCharts/etc. component needs this same treatment; it is not automatic the way it is for every DOM-based component's `text-lyra-*`/`bg-lyra-*` classes.
- `data.colorVar` takes the full `var(--token)` string (not a bare token name) so callers can pass any valid CSS color expression, not just lyra tokens, if a future consumer ever needs a one-off color.
- Adds `echarts` + `echarts-for-react` as new dependencies. No new vulnerabilities introduced (confirmed via `npm audit` before and after — the only vulnerabilities present are pre-existing dev-tooling ones in Storybook/vite/concurrently, unrelated to this change).
- Stories: all under `Custom Primitives/Chart` — `Default` (bar chart), `AllVariants` (bar + line, proves the wrapper is chart-type-agnostic), `Donut` (basic ring), `DonutVariants`/"Donut - All Variants" (default ring / thinner ring / ring + external legend). `DonutChart` used to have its own top-level `Custom Primitives/DonutChart` story file; folded into `Chart.stories.tsx` since it's a chart-type built on `Chart`, not a separate primitive.
- **Hover state, and a clipping bug it caused**: added `emphasis`/`blur` to `DonutChart`'s pie series so hovering a slice gives real feedback (dim the other slices) instead of nothing. First attempt also used ECharts' `emphasis.scale` (grows the hovered slice outward) — this hard-clipped the slice's outer arc against the canvas edge, because `scale` grows the *actual drawn radius*, not a CSS transform, and the default `outerRadius` ("95%") already left almost no headroom inside the canvas. Fixed by dropping `scale` entirely and using a shadow glow (`itemStyle.shadowBlur`) instead, which reads as "this one's active" without growing the geometry past the canvas bounds. Any future chart with a hover-grow effect needs to leave real radius headroom (well under 95%) if it's going to use `emphasis.scale`.

### Chart library expanded: LineChart, ScatterChart, HeatmapChart, GaugeChart, FunnelChart, TreemapChart, SunburstChart, BoxPlotChart + DonutChart center label + BarChart stacking
**Context**: the user shared a Claude-design prototype ("NiCE Data Viz — Reporting Platform Library," a hand-rolled SVG/React chart-spec app, explicitly documented in its own source as design-only — "R&D will pick the actual engine (Recharts/ECharts/D3)") with a 34-item chart catalog. Asked to assess which catalog items were missing from lyra-ui and whether ECharts (already lyra-ui's chart engine, via `echarts` 6.1.0 + `echarts-for-react`, the full bundle so every series type is available with no separate registration) could build the missing ones natively, then — on a follow-up request — to actually build stories for the ones assessed as "genuinely missing, ECharts-native."
**New chart-type components** (`line-chart.tsx`, `scatter-chart.tsx`, `heatmap-chart.tsx`, `gauge-chart.tsx`, `funnel-chart.tsx`, `treemap-chart.tsx`, `sunburst-chart.tsx`, `box-plot-chart.tsx`) all follow `bar-chart.tsx`'s established architecture exactly: built on the shared `Chart` (ECharts) wrapper, every color resolved via `resolveCssColor`/re-run on `useThemeVersion` (the canvas color-resolution gotcha — see `chart.tsx`'s own doc comment), axis-bearing charts (`LineChart`/`ScatterChart`/`BoxPlotChart`) resolve axis label/line/split-line color to `--lyra-color-fg-secondary`/`--lyra-color-border-subtle` the same way `BarChart` does, never a bare `var(...)` handed straight to ECharts.
- **`LineChart`** — same `categories`/`series` (`label`/`data`/`colorVar`) shape as `BarChart`, so switching between "compare categories" and "track over time" reuses the same data prep. `area`/`stacked` props; `stacked` implies `area` (a stacked-area chart is a stacked line chart with fill on, not a separate chart type) rather than requiring both set by hand.
- **`ScatterChart`** — also series-based (one series per color-coded group, e.g. one per team), points as `{x, y, size?}`. Bubble mode isn't a separate prop — a point's own `size` (falling back to `pointSize`) drives `symbolSize`; a "plain" scatter is just every point's `size` left unset.
- **`HeatmapChart`** — the one chart in this library where color is a continuous magnitude scale, not a per-datum category color: `minColorVar`/`maxColorVar` (default `accent-teal-{soft,strong}`) feed ECharts' `visualMap`, which interpolates every cell's value between them. A visible `visualMap` slider is intentional, not optional — "always include a color legend" per this chart family's own dos.
- **`GaugeChart`** — `value`/`min`/`max`/`label`, `pointer: { show: false }` (a numeric readout beats a needle for a dashboard tile), `colorVar` for the progress arc defaulting to an accent color, with the same "semantic status color" exception as everywhere else in this rule (a gauge whose value is genuinely a status read may use a `status-*` token instead).
- **`FunnelChart`** — ordered `data` (widest/first stage to narrowest/last), each stage takes an explicit `colorVar` or falls back to a new shared cycling default (see below).
- **`TreemapChart`/`SunburstChart`** — recursive `{name, value?, colorVar?, children?}` nodes. Color inheritance is the interesting part: rather than deriving a lighter shade mathematically, a **new shared `DEFAULT_ACCENT_HUES`** constant in `chart.tsx` pairs each accent hue's existing `-soft`/`-strong` tokens — an uncolored top-level node gets that hue's `-strong` stop, and its own uncolored children automatically get that *same* hue's `-soft` stop (via a recursive `mapNode` helper local to each component), so a branch reads as "one category, one shade lighter per level down" using tokens that already existed rather than adding any new ones or a color-manipulation dependency. A node at any level can still set its own `colorVar` to opt out (e.g. the semantic-status-color exception).
- **`BoxPlotChart`** — takes pre-computed five-number summaries (`min`/`q1`/`median`/`q3`/`max`) per category, not raw samples (ECharts' own `boxplot` series expects the same) — outliers render as a companion `scatter` series overlay, the standard ECharts recipe, since `boxplot` itself only draws the box-and-whiskers. Every box shares one `colorVar` (unlike `BarChart`'s per-series colors) since categories here are the groups being compared, not different series.
- **New shared helpers in `chart.tsx`**: `DEFAULT_ACCENT_COLOR_VARS`/`getDefaultAccentColorVar` (a flat 8-color cycle, `FunnelChart`'s default) and `DEFAULT_ACCENT_HUES`/`getDefaultAccentHue` (the soft/strong pairs above, `TreemapChart`/`SunburstChart`'s default) — both deliberately exclude `accent-blue-strong` per the existing dashboard color rule, and both exist only for "no per-item color supplied" cases; a consumer with actual meaning to encode should still pass real `colorVar`s per item rather than relying on either cycle.
- **`DonutChart` gained `centerLabel`/`centerValue`** — the catalog's "counter donut"/"Service Level donut" pattern (a headline figure inside the ring). Implemented as a plain absolutely-positioned (`pointer-events-none`) DOM overlay, not an ECharts `graphic` element — real text picking up theme/typography CSS for free beats re-deriving canvas color resolution just to draw two lines of centered text. Omitting `centerValue` keeps the original bare-ring behavior exactly as before (fully backward compatible).
- **`BarChart` gained a `stacked` prop** — `stack: "total"` per series; segment corners only round on the outermost segment in stacked mode (every other segment stays square) so a stack reads as one continuous column instead of several pill shapes glued together, unlike grouped mode where every bar rounds independently.
- **Catalog items assessed as NOT part of this batch** (either already covered by an existing component, or not really an ECharts/chart-library question at all): grouped bar and horizontal bar (`BarChart` already supports both), pie (`DonutChart`'s existing `innerRadius="0%"` already collapses the ring to a pie), KPI/big-number and KPI trend tile (`DashboardCard`'s `Metric` sub-component already covers value+trend+sparkline), histogram (added as a *story*, not a component — a `BarChart` over pre-binned data, since ECharts has no binning of its own), calibration bars (also just a single-series `BarChart`, no new component), violin (no native ECharts series — would need a hand-rolled `custom` series with manual `renderItem`, meaningfully more work, left for a future ask), progress/bullet (no native ECharts "bullet" series either — the design file's own mock doesn't use a chart library for this at all, just CSS width-percentage divs, arguably the simpler and equally valid path), and every table/list/badge composite widget (data table, agent status board, dispositions table, leaderboard, top categories, plan status, challenge card) — not chart types, already covered by `table.tsx` plus ordinary layout.
**Storybook**: 11 new/updated stories, all added to the existing `Custom Primitives/Chart` file (`Chart.stories.tsx`) rather than new top-level files, matching that file's established "one file for every Chart-type-component" convention — `BarChartStacked` ("BarChart - Stacked"), `Histogram`, `DonutCenterLabel` ("Donut - Center label (counter donut)"), `LineChartStory`, `ScatterChartStory`, `HeatmapChartStory`, `GaugeChartStory`, `FunnelChartStory`, `TreemapChartStory`, `SunburstChartStory`, `BoxPlotChartStory`. All use CX/contact-center sample data (teams, channels, handle time, sentiment) consistent with every other chart story in this file, and accent (never `accent-blue-strong`) or real status colors per the existing dashboard color rule, with the status-color exception applied explicitly to the sentiment/SLA donut examples and the "SLA attainment (below target)" gauge example.
**Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` clean at the existing 2-error baseline (`admin-shell.tsx` ×2, `list-item.tsx`) after every new file; a direct `esbuild` bundle of `Chart.stories.tsx` (platform=browser, jsx=automatic, externals for react/react-dom/@storybook/echarts/echarts-for-react) also succeeded, the same Storybook-Vite-equivalent check this repo's standing "premature `*/`" rule requires for any doc-comment-heavy story edit.

### DashboardCard (`src/components/dashboard-card.tsx`)
- The general dashboard/home-screen widget shell, built on top of `Container` (composition, not reimplementation — `Container` already owns the card surface/border/radius/header). Same component covers three real usages:
  1. **Chart widget** — `children` holding a chart (e.g. `DonutChart`) + legend, same composition as `agent-next-gen-v1`'s Activity card. See `DashboardCard.stories.tsx` → `Chart Widget`.
  2. **Data card** — `children` holding rows/bars, no chart, same composition as the Productivity breakdown card. See `Data Card`.
  3. **Metric card** — a new `metrics: DashboardCardMetric[]` prop (`{ value, label, selected? }`, up to 4, extras silently dropped via `.slice(0, 4)`) takes over the card body in place of `children`. Two layouts via `metricVariant`: `"divided"` (plain columns separated by a vertical `Divider`, no per-item border — the plain reference screenshot) and `"contained"` (each metric in its own nested `Container`, `variant="info-strong"` when `selected` for the blue "active card" treatment from the second reference screenshot, `variant="default"` otherwise). Reused `Container`'s existing `info-strong` variant (`bg-lyra-bg-active-subtle` + `border-lyra-border-active`) for the selected look rather than inventing new blue tokens — it was already exactly the right color pair.
- **New typography preset added for this: `.lyra-heading-2xl`** (28px/32px, weight 700) — the metric value in both reference screenshots is visibly larger and bolder than any existing heading tier (`lyra-heading-xl` tops out at 24px/weight 500, tuned for section/card titles, not a standalone stat figure). Added to `lyra-tokens.css` and `storybook.css` together (per the cross-file sync convention) and registered in `Typography.stories.tsx`'s `presets` table.
- `metrics` and `children` are mutually exclusive by design (`metrics`, when present, replaces the body instead of `children`) — keeps the prop surface small instead of a separate `mode` flag that could disagree with which content prop was actually passed.
- The outer `DashboardCard`/`Container` shell itself is fully passthrough-controlled (`variant`, `className`, etc. all flow straight to the underlying `Container`) — the Metric Card stories pass `className="border-0 bg-transparent shadow-none"` to strip the outer card chrome entirely, matching what's actually visible in both reference screenshots (no outer border, just the metric row itself — for "contained," only the *per-metric* boxes are visible, not an outer wrapper box). A consumer that wants a bordered/shadowed outer card around a metric strip can just omit that override.
- Stories: `Custom Primitives/Dashboard Card` → `Default` (bare divided strip), `Chart Widget`, `Data Card`, `Table Card`, `Card Controls` (renamed from `Header Controls` once it started covering the footer too — see the dedicated entry below), `Metric Card` (divided / contained-with-selection / 2-column / with trend+sparkline / surface-container-subtle background), `AllVariants`.
- **`Metric Card`'s "surface-container-subtle background" example** — `variant="neutral-subtle"` (`container.tsx`'s `containerVariants`: `bg-lyra-bg-surface-container-subtle` + `border border-lyra-border-subtle`), same divided metrics as the other examples but with a real card surface showing instead of `border-0 bg-transparent shadow-none`. `neutral-subtle` (not `neutral-none`) specifically because only the background was asked to change — `neutral-none` drops the border entirely, which the request never asked for; `neutral-subtle` keeps the exact same `border-lyra-border-subtle` the default card variant already has, just swapping the background color underneath it. Added on request and applied directly to Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard (`MonitorDashboardPage.tsx`) — every `DashboardCard` there (the 6 metric cards + the full-width summary card) now passes `variant="neutral-subtle"` instead of the plain white `default` surface.
- **`showHeaderIcon`/`showHeaderSubhead` are story-only toggles, not new component props.** Both are just booleans in `Header Controls`' `args`, used by that story's `render` to decide whether to pass a real `headerIcon`/`headerSubhead` value or `undefined` — `DashboardCard` itself needs no new prop for this, since a consumer already controls icon/subhead visibility by whether they pass those (already-existing, `Container`-inherited) props at all. Same reasoning as before for `showFilterChip`/`showKebabMenu`, which *are* real props (they gate genuinely new rendering logic DashboardCard owns) — the distinction matters when deciding whether a "show/hide X" ask needs a component change or just a story-level `args` toggle.
- **`Header Controls`' `contentType` select** (`"chart" | "data" | "metric"`, Storybook `select` control) swaps the same story's card body between the three real content shapes `DashboardCard` supports, so any header-control combination (icon/subhead/filter chip/kebab, all independently toggleable) can be checked against any content type in one story instead of three separate fixed ones. Extracted `ChartWidgetBody`/`DataCardBody` into their own small components specifically so `Header Controls` and `AllVariants` could reuse the exact same markup as the dedicated `Chart Widget`/`Data Card` stories rather than a second hand-copied version drifting from the first.
- **`Table Card`** — a `Table` using `useAutoFitRows` (see `table.tsx`; same hook `DataManagement.stories.tsx`'s `AutoFit` story uses at full toolbar/pagination scale) as `DashboardCard` `children`, proving the card shell works for a real interactive `Table`, not just static rows/charts. `useAutoFitRows` only measures its container and returns a row count — the consumer still slices their own data to that count (`TABLE_CARD_ROWS.slice(0, rowsPerPage)`), same as the full-scale example. `AutoFitTableCardBody` is reused as-is by `Header Controls`' `table` content type (see below) rather than a second copy.
  - **Bug (caught from an actual screenshot, not just a clean build): the table overflowed and scrolled instead of fitting the card.** `useAutoFitRows(40, 40, ...)`'s defaults assume every row is exactly 40px, matching `TableHead`/`TableCell`'s `h-10` — but `TableRow` (the `<tr>`) also carries its own `border-b`, *outside* that box, and `box-sizing: border-box` only keeps a fixed-height element's own border inside its box, not a child's. So every row except the last (`TableBody`'s `[&_tr:last-child]:border-0` strips it there) is actually 41px, and the header row is too. With a container sized to exactly `rows × 40 + 40`, the hook's `floor(available / rowHeight)` landed on a row count that measured as "fits" under the 40px assumption but needed a few pixels more in reality — clipping the last row and forcing a scrollbar. Fixed by passing the hook the real `41`/`41` and giving the container real slack above the theoretical minimum (`h-[320px]`, comfortably over the true ~286px needed for 6 rows) instead of sizing it to a knife-edge exact multiple of an assumed row height. Any future `useAutoFitRows` consumer needs to pass the *actual* rendered row/header height (check for a `border-b` on the row itself, not just the cells) rather than trusting the hook's 40/40 defaults blindly.
- **`Header Controls`' `contentType` select gained a fourth option, `"table"`** (now `"chart" | "data" | "table" | "metric"`), rendering `AutoFitTableCardBody` with the same "Campaigns"/`List` header as the dedicated `Table Card` story. Per-content-type header title/icon/background moved into one `CONTENT_TYPE_HEADER` lookup (keyed by content type) instead of a growing chain of ternaries, since a 4th branch was the point where ternary-chaining stopped being the more readable option.
- **All stories in this file switched from fixed pixel-width wrappers (`w-[360px]`/`w-[720px]`) to `w-full` cards inside a `layout: "fullscreen"` + `p-6` frame**, per an explicit "all cards should stretch to 100% width of the screen" request — `padded` (Storybook's default layout) caps the canvas at a fixed max-width itself, which would silently reintroduce a pixel ceiling underneath any `w-full` card, so the layout parameter had to change too, not just the card's own className.
- **Header controls: FilterChip + kebab menu, both off by default.** `showFilterChip`/`showKebabMenu` add a `FilterChip` and, at the far right of the header, a kebab (⋮) menu — composed alongside any custom `headerActions` a consumer already passes (`[headerActions] [filter chip] [kebab menu]`, in that order). Both exposed as boolean Storybook controls (`Header Controls` story wires them to live `args`, so they're literally on/off switches in the Controls panel, not just documented prop names) — direct response to "add a control that turns them on/off," not a figure of speech.
  - The kebab defaults to a generic Edit / Refresh / Remove menu (`kebabMenuItems` overrides it) — reasonable defaults for "some generic menu options" without forcing every consumer to redefine the same three actions.
  - The filter chip defaults to a generic "Filter" / Option 1-3 placeholder (`filterChipProps` overrides any part of it) and manages its own selection internally unless the consumer passes `selectedValues`/`onSelectionChange` — same "internal state + optional external callback" pattern `agent-next-gen-v1`'s own `DateFilterChip` already uses, not a new one invented for this.
  - **Bug (caught from a screenshot, twice): the header filter chip's dropdown opened off the right edge of the screen.** `FilterChip` (and `Select`, which it wraps) positions its dropdown by hand-computing `left`/`right` CSS from the trigger's bounding rect — there's no Radix-style `avoidCollisions`/auto-flip, so a chip near the right edge of a wide card always overflowed, every time, with the previous hardcoded `dropdownAlign="left"`. `FilterChip` gained a `dropdownAlign?: "left" | "right"` prop (default `"left"`, so every existing `FilterChip` consumer elsewhere is unaffected), and `DashboardCard`'s `HeaderFilterChip` defaults specifically to `dropdownAlign="right"` since the header chip sits at the far right of the card (right before the kebab menu) and opening leftward is the only direction with room there — still overridable per-instance via `filterChipProps.dropdownAlign`.
    - **First attempt at threading the prop through missed one of the three internal `<Select>` call sites.** `FilterChip` renders `Select` from three different code paths — the `hasOperators` branch's operator selector, that branch's value selector, and a separate no-operators branch's value selector. The `replace_all` edit that swapped hardcoded `dropdownAlign="left"` for the prop-driven `dropdownAlign={dropdownAlign}` only landed on the first two (both inside the `hasOperators` branch). The third — the plain `return` at the bottom of the component, used whenever no `operators` are passed — kept its literal `dropdownAlign="left"`, silently ignoring the prop entirely. That third branch is exactly the one every current consumer hits: `DEFAULT_FILTER_OPTIONS` (DashboardCard's placeholder) has no `operators`, and no other `FilterChip` usage in either repo passes them either. So the `dropdownAlign="right"` default on `DashboardCard`'s header chip compiled clean, built clean, and did *nothing* visually — the exact case being fixed never reached the prop. Lesson: when a multi-instance prop-threading `replace_all` looks done because it "compiles," grep the file afterward for the literal old value to confirm zero occurrences remain, rather than trusting instance count from memory.
    - **Any future `FilterChip`/`Select` usage positioned near a container's right edge needs the same explicit `dropdownAlign="right"`** — true collision-aware auto-flipping would need real changes to `Select`'s hand-rolled positioning logic (affects every `Select` consumer in the library), which is a bigger, riskier change than this scoped fix and wasn't what was asked for here.
    - **Update, later session: `Select` was subsequently swapped onto Radix internals** (see "Select" entry above) — its `Content`/`Popover` now do auto-flip if the preferred `dropdownAlign` side would overflow. Explicitly setting `dropdownAlign="right"` near an edge is still the recommended, correct choice (it's the actual preferred side, not a workaround for a missing feature anymore), but it's no longer the *only* thing standing between a chip and an off-screen dropdown — Radix's own collision handling is now a real safety net underneath it.
- **`ContainerHeader` gained a `topSlot` prop** (forwarded through `Container` as `headerTopSlot`) — a generic slot rendered on its own line above the icon+title row, left-aligned with the *icon* (flush with the header's own left edge — a follow-up screenshot corrected an initial version of this that indented the slot to the title's start instead, past the icon). Added for a screenshot showing a small red pill `Tag` ("Anomaly") sitting above a card's headline ("Voice queue depth 3.4× baseline on Tier 2 Billing"). This is deliberately a `Container`/`ContainerHeader`-level addition, not a `DashboardCard`-only one: `topSlot` accepts any `ReactNode`, so any future consumer needing something above a header title (a status tag, a badge, anything) gets it too, and — because `DashboardCardProps extends ContainerProps` — `DashboardCard` inherits `headerTopSlot` automatically with zero new code of its own; no redundant `headerTag` wrapper prop was added. Distinct from the pre-existing `titleBadge`/`headerTitleBadge`, which renders inline *next to* the title rather than on the line above it.
  - `DashboardCard.stories.tsx`'s `Header Controls` story got a `showHeaderTag` boolean control (`headerTopSlot={args.showHeaderTag ? <Tag label="Anomaly" variant="critical" shape="pill" /> : undefined}`) — same synthetic story-only-toggle convention as `showHeaderIcon`/`showHeaderSubhead`, since the real prop (`headerTopSlot`) already exists and doesn't need its own boolean twin on the component itself. Reused the existing `Tag` atom's `critical`/`pill` combination (see `Tag.stories.tsx`'s "Pill Shape" story) rather than inventing new styling. Note: the reference screenshot showed the tag label in all-caps ("ANOMALY"), but nothing in the request explicitly called for all-caps, so the demo uses sentence case ("Anomaly") instead — per standing guidance, all-caps text should only be used when a user explicitly asks for it, not inferred from a screenshot's rendering alone. `Tag` itself renders `label` verbatim with no uppercase transform either way, so a consumer who does want all-caps can still just pass that string.
- **Show/hide header text, and show/hide the card's own chrome — two more `DashboardCard.stories.tsx` "Header Controls" toggles, one synthetic and one a real new prop.**
  - **`showHeaderText`** (synthetic, story-only — same convention as `showHeaderIcon`/`showHeaderSubhead`) toggles `headerTitle` itself, independent of icon/subhead/tag. This exposed a real gap in `Container`/`ContainerHeader`: the header row's existence was gated entirely on `headerTitle` being truthy (`{headerTitle && <ContainerHeader .../>}`), so an icon-only or tag-only header with no title text couldn't render at all — turning `headerTitle` off silently dropped the icon/subhead/tag along with it. Fixed at the source rather than special-cased in the story: `ContainerHeaderProps.title` is now optional (the `<h2>` simply doesn't render when absent, and the `onClose` tooltip/aria-label fall back to `"Close"` instead of `"Close undefined"`), and `Container` now renders the header whenever *any* header-related prop is present (`headerTitle || headerIcon || headerActions || headerTitleBadge || headerTopSlot || headerSubhead`), not just `headerTitle` alone. All existing direct `ContainerHeader` consumers (`ai-panel.tsx`, `agent-notifications.tsx`, `panel-header.tsx`'s alias) already pass a real title string, so this is purely additive — nothing that used to render stops rendering.
  - **`showContainer`** (default `true`) is a genuine new `DashboardCard` prop, not a synthetic toggle, since it needs to merge onto `Container`'s own `className` rather than just conditionally passing a prop. Off strips the card down to background-less/border-less/shadow-less (`border-0 bg-transparent shadow-none`, merged via `cn`'s `twMerge` so it wins over whatever `variant`/`className` a consumer also passed).
    - **First version also zeroed padding, and a screenshot showed why that was wrong.** Along with stripping the surface, `showContainer=false` originally also zeroed the header's own padding (via a new `Container` `headerClassName` prop forwarded to `ContainerHeader`'s `className`) and the metrics row's outer padding (via a `compact` flag on the internal `MetricRow`) — the reasoning being that a border-less/bg-less card reads as "not a card," so its internal padding shouldn't look card-shaped either. A screenshot of the Table Card content type immediately showed the problem: with no border separating the header from the table below it, zero padding meant the table's own header row sat completely flush against "Jan 26, 2026 at 4:00 PM," with no breathing room at all — worse, not better, without the surface. Reverted the padding side of it entirely: `showContainer` now only ever touches `className` (surface), never spacing. `Container` keeps the `headerClassName` prop (still a reasonable generic escape hatch for any future consumer who does want to override header padding directly), but `DashboardCard` itself no longer drives it, and the `compact` flag was removed from `MetricRow` outright rather than left as unused dead code.
  - **`metricCount`** (synthetic, select control with options `1`/`2`/`3`/`4`) — only meaningful when `contentType` is "metric"; slices `METRICS` down to that many columns (`METRICS.slice(0, metricCount)`) and passes the result as the real `metrics` prop, the same `slice` mechanism the dedicated "Metric Card" story below already uses for its 4-column/2-column examples, just wired to a live control instead of separate hardcoded variants.
- **`KebabMenuButton` promoted to its own shared atom** (`src/components/kebab-menu-button.tsx`) — it only existed as an unexported helper inside `channel-row.tsx` before this (one per channel row); `DashboardCard`'s header kebab needed the exact same trigger+portal+positioning behavior, so it was extracted rather than rebuilt a second time, same rationale as the earlier `FavoriteButton` extraction. `channel-row.tsx` now imports it instead of defining its own copy. Also fixed a real (pre-existing, moved-with-the-code) type bug in the process: the internal `entries.map` assumed every non-`"separator"` `MenuEntry` was a `MenuItemDef` with an `onClick`, but the union also includes `MenuSectionLabel` (no `onClick`) — now excludes `"sectionLabel" in entry` from the wrapping branch too, not just `"separator"`.
- **New `Sparkline` atom** (`src/components/sparkline.tsx`) — a minimal inline line+gradient-area chart for a metric's trend graphic (e.g. the small shape next to "+12.4% vs last week"), built on the same shared `Chart`/echarts wrapper `DonutChart` uses. Deliberately bare: axes, grid lines, and tooltip are all switched off (`show: false` / collapsed `grid` / `tooltip: { show: false }`) — a sparkline reads as a shape at a glance, not something a user hovers or reads exact values off of. `symbol: "none"` keeps only the line itself visible, no per-point dots. Sharp/linear points by default (`smooth: false`, a real `smooth?` prop) rather than a smoothed curve — an explicit follow-up correction, not the ECharts-typical default left in place. The gradient fill needed an actual rgba string for its alpha-faded stop, not just the resolved hex `resolveCssColor` returns, so a small local `withAlpha(hex, alpha)` handles that — deliberately narrow (only the `#rrggbb` shape every lyra color token is authored in, not the general CSS color grammar) since that's the only shape it ever needs to handle.
  - **`resolveCssColor`/`useThemeVersion` extracted out of `donut-chart.tsx` into the shared `chart.tsx`** ahead of building `Sparkline`, since it needed the exact same canvas-color-resolution + dark-mode-revalidation logic `DonutChart` already had — rather than copy-pasting a third canvas color helper, both now import one shared implementation from the base `Chart` wrapper. `donut-chart.tsx`'s own doc comment updated to point at the new shared location instead of describing helpers that used to live in that same file.
- **`DashboardCardMetric` gained `trend`/`sparkline`/`sparklineColorVar`**, for a screenshot showing an inline colored arrow next to a metric's label ("+12.4% vs last week") plus a small sparkline alongside it. Went through one real correction after the first version, both captured below.
  - **`trend` is a structured object** (`{ direction: "up" | "down" | "flat"; percent: number; comparisonPeriod?: string }`), not a bare direction string — it needs to carry the percent value and comparison period too, not just up/down/flat, to build the full "{arrow} {percent}% vs. last {period}" line. Renders a `TrendingUp`/`TrendingDown` icon (or a literal em dash "—" for "flat," not an icon) colored success/warning/critical (`METRIC_TREND_META`, now also carrying each direction's `colorVar` for the sparkline — see below), followed by the signed percent (`formatTrendPercent`: `+12.4%` / `-3.1%` / `0%`, sign taken from the number itself) and "vs. last {comparisonPeriod ?? "week"}".
  - **`trend` *replaces* the `label` subhead entirely — it doesn't render next to it.** First version put the arrow inline before the existing `label` text and separately renamed the demo's `label` string to "vs. last week," which technically worked but wasn't what was asked: the request was to put the whole "{arrow} {N}% vs. last {week}" line *where the "Metric Name" subhead currently is*. `MetricContent` now branches: `trend` present → render the trend row; absent → render `label` as plain text, same as before trend existed at all. `label` stays a required field (still shown when there's no `trend`), it's just not rendered when `trend` is set.
  - **The main `value` figure is explicitly untouched by any of this** — first version had accidentally folded the percent into `value` itself (e.g. `value: "+12.4%"` replacing the metric's actual number). Corrected per an explicit "do not modify the main metric": `METRICS_WITH_TREND`'s `value`s are the exact same `70`/`10`/`58`/`2` as plain `METRICS`; `trend.percent` is a separate field carrying the "12.4" shown in the subhead line.
  - **Sparkline color now matches `trend`'s own color, not an independent default.** First version deliberately kept the sparkline blue regardless of `trend`'s color (reasoning: shape-over-time and up/down-vs-comparison are different signals). Corrected on explicit instruction to match instead: `sparklineColorVar` now defaults to `METRIC_TREND_META[trend.direction].colorVar` when a `trend` is present, still overridable per-metric, and falling back to `Sparkline`'s own default blue only when there's a `sparkline` with no `trend` at all.
  - Both variants' per-metric markup (`MetricRow`'s "divided" and "contained" branches) share one `MetricContent` sub-component so trend/sparkline/plain-label markup can't drift between the two variants — each branch just supplies its own outer wrapper (bordered `Container` for "contained," plain divided column for "divided") around the same `MetricContent`.
  - Wired into `DashboardCard.stories.tsx`'s "Header Controls" story as a `showTrend` boolean control (synthetic — swaps `METRICS` for `METRICS_WITH_TREND`, only meaningful when `contentType` is "metric," composes with the existing `metricCount` slice), plus a permanent "Divided — with trend + sparkline" example in the dedicated "Metric Card" story so the feature isn't only visible from inside the Header Controls sandbox.
  - **Gotcha hit while fixing this: `tsconfig.json` excludes `src/components/__stories__` from `tsc` entirely**, so the standard `npx tsc --noEmit` verification used throughout this file's history never actually type-checked story files — it silently passed even with `METRICS_WITH_TREND` still using the old `trend: "up"` string shape against the new object type. Confirmed via a one-off temporary tsconfig (`extends` the real one, drops the `__stories__` exclusion) that this specific mismatch was a real bug, not a tolerated CSF3 quirk — the same temp check also surfaced a long list of pre-existing "missing `args`"/"property does not exist" errors across nearly every other story file in the repo (`Icon.stories.tsx`, `Label.stories.tsx`, `DataManagement.stories.tsx`, etc.), which is presumably *why* the exclusion exists in the first place, and isn't something to chase down here. **Takeaway: a "clean `tsc --noEmit`" claim for anything in `__stories__` was never actually verifying story-file types** — real story-file bugs need either a temporary tsconfig override like this one, or eyeballing the diff directly.
- **`DashboardCard` gained a real `footer` prop, and its `"Header Controls"` story was renamed to `"Card Controls"`.** `footer` (`React.ReactNode`, default none) renders below the body in its own row, separated by a top border (`border-t border-lyra-border-subtle px-4 py-3`) — independent of `showContainer`, so even a bare/surface-less card still gets that border between body and footer. Wired into the renamed story as a new `showFooter` boolean control (a real prop, not a synthetic toggle, same category as `showContainer`) showing a `<Button variant="ghost" size="sm">View All</Button>` when on. **The rename**: once this story covered a footer, "Header Controls" no longer described what it exercised (a footer isn't a header piece) — renamed the exported story (`HeaderControls` → `CardControls`) and its local content-type type (`HeaderControlsContentType` → `CardControlsContentType`) to match, confirmed via grep that neither identifier was referenced anywhere outside this one story file before renaming (a couple of unrelated `hasHeaderControls` local variables and `renderHeaderControls`-style props elsewhere in the repo are different concepts, not naming collisions).
- **Metric rows (`DashboardCard`'s "divided" and "contained" `metricVariant`s) adapt in three ordered stages as the card's own available width gets tight — via a CSS container query, not a `sm:`/`lg:` Tailwind breakpoint.** A metric card can end up narrow for reasons that have nothing to do with the browser window (a sidebar widget, a dense dashboard grid, a docked panel), and a container query reacts to the actual ancestor element's width where a `@media` query can't. This repo already had exactly this general pattern for a different case — `.lyra-container-grid-wrap`/`.lyra-container-grid` in `lyra-tokens.css`, for a row of cards collapsing to one column — so the metric-row version reuses its 761px (rounded to 760px) as the *widest* of its own three thresholds, explicitly requested in this order:
  1. **(≤760px) Sparkline moves below the text.** Each metric's own value/trend block and its `Sparkline` sit side by side by default (`.lyra-metric-content { flex-direction: row }`); at this threshold they stack (`column`), and the sparkline's fixed `w-20` width becomes `100%` (`.lyra-metric-sparkline`). The row of metrics itself is untouched — still one flex row, however many metrics there are — this stage only reclaims the width a side-by-side sparkline was taking.
  2. **(≤560px) 2-up grid.** `.lyra-metric-row` switches from `display: flex` to a strict `display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0,1fr))`, wrapping 3-4 metrics onto a second row. A flat `Divider` sibling can't represent "between column 1 and 2" *and* "between row 1 and 2" at once, so both dividers hide here and a real `gap: 0.75rem` takes over spacing instead — the same convention "contained" metrics (bordered boxes, no dividers) already used at every width via their own `gap-3`.
  3. **(≤360px) 1-up grid.** `grid-template-columns` drops to `1fr` — every metric fully stacked. Unlike stage 2, a single column has no "which pair of neighbors" ambiguity, so `.lyra-metric-row-divider--horizontal` comes back ("divided" only; "contained" metrics are already separated by their own border).

  `Divider`'s own `orientation` prop is a JS-time decision with no way to flip it purely from a CSS breakpoint, so `MetricRow`'s "divided" branch renders *two* `Divider`s per gap (`orientation="horizontal"` and `="vertical"`), each carrying a class that toggles `display: none/block` at the right stage — vertical visible only in the unconstrained row (stage 0), both hidden at stage 2, horizontal visible again at stage 3. Added to both `lyra-tokens.css` and `storybook.css` per the standing "new tokens/CSS need to land in both files" convention.
  - **Padding fixes layered on top of the same container-query system**, both from a follow-up screenshot of the stacked (pre-3-stage, single-breakpoint) state: the space above the metrics was 0 with no header, and the first stacked metric had less left padding than the rest.
    - **Padding above the metrics**, on the explicit suggestion to invert which side owns it: rather than conditionally adding top padding only when there's no header (which would need threading a `hasHeader` boolean down from `DashboardCard` to `MetricRow`), `MetricRow`'s row now *always* carries `pt-4`, and `DashboardCard` passes `headerClassName="pb-0"` to `Container` whenever `metrics` is set (merged via `cn`/`twMerge`, so `py-5 pb-0` correctly resolves to just the top half staying). Net effect: no header → `pt-4` (16px) matches the rest of the row's own padding, where there used to be none; header present → the total header-to-metrics gap becomes 16px instead of the previous 20px (header's `pb-5` removed, `pt-4` added) — strictly *less*, never more, satisfying "don't increase the gap when a header is shown" by construction rather than by a conditional. Scoped to `metrics` only (`headerClassName` untouched for `children` content types — only metrics were reported tight).
    - **First-item left padding**: `.lyra-metric-row-item` replaces the divided variant's `px-4 first:pl-0 last:pr-0` with the equivalent behavior as real CSS (`padding-left/right: 1rem`, `:first-child`/`:last-child` zero out one side) instead of Tailwind's utility classes, specifically so a container query can reset it — in the final 3-stage version, that reset happens at stage 2 (≤560px), the same threshold where item padding gets zeroed out entirely in favor of `gap`, rather than at the single old threshold.
  - **The vertical `Divider` between metrics wasn't rendering at all (stage 0, unconstrained row).** `Divider`'s own base styling for `orientation="vertical"` is `h-full` (`height: 100%`) — fine inside a container with a definite height, but `.lyra-metric-row` is a plain `display: flex` row with no explicit height of its own; its height is intrinsic, driven by its tallest child. A percentage height has nothing definite to resolve against in that case, so the Divider collapsed to zero height instead of stretching, and the line was invisible even though `display: block` and the border color were both correct. Fixed in `dashboard-card.tsx` by appending `h-auto self-stretch` to the vertical Divider's own `className` (which wins over Divider's base `h-full` via `cn`'s twMerge, last-argument-wins on the same class group) — `self-stretch` fills the row's height purely through the flex algorithm, which has no percentage-resolution ambiguity. No CSS token changes needed; this was a component-usage fix, not a new pattern.
- **`DashboardCard`'s per-metric value+label block is now also exported standalone as `Metric` (`src/components/dashboard-card.tsx`)** — first requested as a brand-new "StatCard" atom (bordered box, label above a big blue number, both centered) to put a couple of them at the end of an `agent-next-gen-v1` home-tab queue row, next to "Contacts"/"Skills." Built and shipped that new component, then corrected: the actual reference was `DashboardCard`'s own existing metric look (bold value on top, gray label below, left-aligned, no border) — the "StatCard" mockup and the real ask looked superficially similar ("a box with a value and a label") but weren't the same component, and the fix wasn't a new component at all, just exposing the one that already existed. `stat-card.tsx`/`StatCard.stories.tsx` were deleted; the formerly-private `MetricContent` (renamed `Metric`) now takes an optional `className` so a standalone usage can override its `flex-1` default (which only makes sense inside `MetricRow`'s equal-width columns) with e.g. `"flex-none"` to size to its own content instead. `MetricRow` itself is unaffected — same function, just renamed and given one more prop no existing caller needed to pass. See the "endSlot" doc comment on `Accordion` below and the "With End Slot (Metrics)" story in `Accordion.stories.tsx`.
- **`Accordion`'s new `endSlot` item prop (`src/components/accordion.tsx`)** — renders arbitrary content at the end of the trigger row, between the title/subhead block and the chevron (e.g. a `Metric` pair). Added specifically to unblock the use case above: `Accordion` previously had no slot between its title/subhead column and its fixed chevron, and per CONTRIBUTING.md "never modify a lyra-ui core component from [a consuming app]," adding the slot belonged here, not as a locally hand-rolled replacement trigger row in `agent-next-gen-v1`. Rendered inside the same `<button>` as the rest of the row (no separate click target), so its doc comment calls out that anything passed in should stay display-only — a nested interactive control would be a button-inside-a-button. See the "With End Slot (Metrics)" story in `Accordion.stories.tsx`.
  - **Icon no longer centers against the whole title+subhead+endSlot block.** Once `agent-next-gen-v1` gave its queue rows both a per-type `icon` and the `endSlot` metric pair above, the row's overall height grew (driven by the tall metric boxes), and the button's own `items-center` — with `icon` as a plain sibling of the title/subhead column — centered the icon against that new taller row instead of against the title text it's meant to sit next to. Restructured so `icon` and `title` share their own inner `flex items-center` row (aligning against each other only), with `subhead` as a separate line below that inner row, indented `pl-7` (icon width `h-5`/20px + `gap-2`/8px) to land under the title text rather than under the icon — same "icon+title row, subhead below" shape `ContainerHeader` already uses, applied here for the same reason.
- **`DashboardQueue` (`src/components/dashboard-queue.tsx`)** — everything `agent-next-gen-v1` had hand-assembled in its home tab for a row of queue-type widgets (Digital/Inbound Voice/Voicemail/Work Item, each with Skills/Contacts metrics) plus the queue-as-Accordion alternative it had briefly used, extracted into one shared component with two variants on the same `items` data:
  - **`variant="cards"` (default)** — each queue is its own standalone `DashboardCard` (a plain `Clock` icon + name + "Wait Time: {wait}" header, Skills/Contacts as "contained" metrics), laid out in a plain gapped grid. Clicking a card selects it; the selected card gets `DashboardCard`'s `info-strong` (blue) treatment, unselected cards get `neutral-subtle` (a quiet gray surface) — no shared enclosing backdrop carries a background of its own; each card owns its own surface. (First version wrapped the whole row in one bordered/gray backdrop with plain white cards inside; a follow-up reference screenshot showed standalone gray cards with no shared border instead — corrected to match.) Selection is controlled/uncontrolled like `FilterChip`'s own value prop — `selectedId`/`onSelect` are both optional, and `onSelect` fires with the already-*resolved* next value (clicking the selected card again resolves to `null`), so a consumer can wire it straight to a `useState` setter without writing its own toggle logic. `DashboardQueue` only reports the selection; it has no opinion on what a consumer does with it (e.g. `agent-next-gen-v1` uses it to decide what the interior side panel shows — that wiring lives entirely in the app, not here).
  - **`variant="accordion"`** — the same queue list as `Accordion` rows instead: icon + name + "{contactsCount} contacts in queue"/"Wait: {wait}" subhead, with the Skills/Contacts metrics moved into `endSlot` (the exact same `Container`+`Metric` composition as "cards," just smaller and inline — see the `endSlot` entry above). Each item's optional `content` becomes what shows when its row expands; omitted, the row just has nothing to expand into.
  - Both variants compose existing atoms (`DashboardCard`/`Metric`, `Accordion`, `Container`) rather than re-deriving card/row markup — this component is genuinely just "the same `items` data, rendered two ways," not two different pieces of logic that happen to share a name.
  - **`MetricRow`'s container-query stacking gained a `lyra-metric-row--compact` override for ≤2-metric rows**, added specifically because of this component: the existing 3-stage collapse (see the entry above) was calibrated around up to 4 metrics needing to progressively give way, collapsing to a single stacked column at ≤360px. A DashboardQueue card's Skills/Contacts pair is only ever 2 metrics, sitting inside a card that's already narrower than a full-width `DashboardCard` typically is (one of several in a row) — the same 360px threshold that's correct for 4 metrics stacks a 2-metric row needlessly early, when two items still fit comfortably side by side. `MetricRow` now adds `lyra-metric-row--compact` whenever `metrics.length <= 2`; a same-specificity-beating override in both `lyra-tokens.css` and `storybook.css` keeps a compact row at Stage 2's 2-up grid instead of collapsing further, no `!important` needed since `.lyra-metric-row.lyra-metric-row--compact` (two classes) already outranks the plain `.lyra-metric-row` (one class) Stage 3 rule.
  - `agent-next-gen-v1`'s home tab now renders `variant="cards"` only; the app had been using the `accordion` variant's shape directly, and pulled it out in favor of this shared component specifically so the accordion layout stays available (as `variant="accordion"`) if/when the app wants it back, without having to re-derive it from scratch a second time. See the "QueueCards" and "QueueAccordion" stories in `DashboardQueue.stories.tsx` (moved to the "UI" Storybook category alongside other composed widgets like `AppHeader`/`LeftNav`, not "Custom Primitives" — this is a page-level assembly of several atoms, not an atom itself), each modeled on its own reference screenshot.
- **`.lyra-container-grid` (the "main container content grid" pattern, `lyra-tokens.css`) switched from CSS Grid to Flexbox, and its collapse threshold moved from 761px to 991px** — used by `agent-next-gen-v1`'s home tab for its Activity/Performance/Productivity row (three `DashboardCard`s). Explicitly requested as `flex-direction: row` → `flex-direction: column`, not a `grid-template-columns` collapse — the two read as the same result visually (three equal-width cards collapsing to one stacked column) but the request was specifically about the row *becoming* a column, which is what `flex-direction` actually models; a grid's column *count* changing is a different mechanism arriving at a similar picture. Child cards get `flex: 1 1 0%; min-width: 0` (added alongside, on `.lyra-container-grid > *`) so they still split the row evenly the way the grid's `minmax(0, 1fr)` tracks used to — plain flex children with no explicit sizing would otherwise size to their own content instead of sharing space equally, and `min-width: 0` overrides flex's own `min-width: auto` default so a card with unbreakable wide content can't push the row wider than its container. Note this decouples it from `MetricRow`'s own 3-stage collapse (see the "Metric rows" entry above), whose narrowest 760px threshold was originally set to *echo* this pattern's old 761px for consistency — that echo is now stale (760px/grid vs. 991px/flex, two independent systems), which is fine since they were always two separate CSS rules that happened to share a number, not one shared value.
  - **Follow-up: gained a 2-column intermediate stage.** The single row→column jump at 991px was replaced with two ordered breakpoints: (≤991px) `display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr))` — a 3rd/4th card wraps onto its own row instead of squeezing three-across into a container that's already tight — then (≤767px) back to `display: flex; flex-direction: column` for the final single-stacked-column stage, same as before. The unconstrained wide state is untouched (`flex-direction: row`). `.lyra-container-grid > *`'s `min-width: 0` needed no change — grid items default to `min-width: auto` too, so the same override that was already there for the flex stages covers the new grid stage as well.
  - **`DashboardQueue`'s "cards" row picked this pattern up too, replacing its own bespoke layout.** It had been laid out with an inline `style={{ gridTemplateColumns: `repeat(${items.length}, minmax(0, 1fr))` }}` (dynamic because `items.length` varies) and no responsive behavior at all — a fixed N-column grid regardless of how narrow its container got, which a follow-up screenshot showed squeezing each card's Skills/Contacts metric boxes down until their numbers and "Skills"/"Contacts" labels were truncating ("2.", "S…"). Rather than bolt a second, separate container-query system onto `dashboard-queue.tsx` to fix that, it now renders inside `.lyra-container-grid-wrap`/`.lyra-container-grid` directly — the flex-based equal-width-children-collapsing-to-a-column behavior already works for *any* child count, not just three, so there was no reason to keep a bespoke dynamic-grid implementation next to a shared one that already does the same job. Both rows now collapse the same way, at the same 991px threshold, through the same two classes.

### LeftNav (`src/components/left-nav.tsx`) — overlay mode's `header`/`footer` didn't actually expand on hover
- **Bug, caught from a screenshot**: in overlay mode (narrow screens — the aside stays a fixed 52px rail with the expanded panel sliding out as an absolutely-positioned overlay on hover), the nav's own item list correctly expanded on hover, but consumer-supplied `header`/`footer` content (`agent-next-gen-v1` passes a `CreateNew` button as `footer` and a `<>` fragment of `CreateNew` + `InteractionNavItem` cards as `header`) stayed visually collapsed the whole time.
- **Root cause**: the overlay branch auto-injects hover state via `React.isValidElement(header) ? React.cloneElement(header, { expanded: hoverOpen }) : header` — this is correct for a *single* element, but `agent-next-gen-v1`'s `header` is a `<>...</>` fragment wrapping multiple elements. `React.isValidElement` returns `true` for a Fragment too (it's a real element, just of type `React.Fragment`), so the clone actually happened — but `cloneElement` on a Fragment attaches the new prop to the Fragment itself, and `React.Fragment` doesn't forward arbitrary props down to its children. The injection silently no-op'd, and each child kept whatever `expanded` value the consumer had already hardcoded on it directly (`expanded={navOpen}`, the *inline*-mode toggle state per the doc comment's "consumers should pass their own `expanded` prop tied to `open` in inline mode" contract) — so in overlay mode the cards tracked `navOpen`, never `hoverOpen`.
- **Fix**: new `injectExpanded(node, expanded)` helper — unwraps one level of `React.Fragment` first (checking `node.type === React.Fragment` and reading `.props.children`) before running `React.Children.map` + `cloneElement` over whatever's left, so it clones every real top-level child whether `header`/`footer` was passed as one element or a fragment of several. Both the `header` and `footer` blocks in the overlay branch now call this instead of the old bare `isValidElement`/`cloneElement` pair. The inline-mode branch is untouched — it never auto-injects `expanded` at all, by design, since inline mode's `header`/`footer` should already carry the consumer's own `open`-driven `expanded` value.
- Scoped entirely to `left-nav.tsx`; no change needed in `agent-next-gen-v1` — the fragment-of-elements pattern it already uses for `header` is a completely reasonable, common React shape, and the bug was in `LeftNav` failing to handle that shape, not in the app doing something unusual.

### LeftNav (`src/components/left-nav.tsx`) — `header` and the item list now share one scroll region; new `pinnedHeader` prop
- **Request, from two screenshots**: in expanded mode, `header` (the `CreateNew` button + `InteractionNavItem` cards) never scrolled at all — only the nav item list below it had its own `overflow-y-auto`, so a long list of interaction cards had nowhere to go. In collapsed/icon-rail mode, the opposite symptom: the rail's icons visibly compressed their own spacing to fit before a scrollbar ever kicked in, instead of just scrolling. In both cases the one thing that should stay fixed — the `CreateNew` trigger itself (the "+" button/pill) — needed to stay put while everything else scrolled.
- **Root cause**: `header` was rendered as its own `flex-shrink-0` block *outside* the scroll wrapper entirely, so nothing inside it could ever scroll — that's the same block the `CreateNew` button lived in, with no way for `LeftNav` to treat the button differently from the cards beneath it since both arrived as one opaque `header` fragment. The icon rail's `iconOnlyNav` had its own separate `overflow-y-auto` region *and* no `flex-shrink-0` on its `<nav>`/items, so default flex shrink behavior let it compress before the (separate, item-list-only) scrollbar engaged.
- **Fix**: split the old single `header` prop into two: **`pinnedHeader`** (new) — content that must never scroll, rendered above the scroll wrapper, same as `footer`'s existing "pinned" framing but at the top; and **`header`** (redefined) — now renders *inside* the scroll wrapper, stacked above the nav item list, so both scroll together as one continuous region. Applies identically to expanded (`TreeMenu`) and collapsed (`iconOnlyNav`) modes, and to both the inline and overlay render branches. `iconOnlyNav`'s `<nav>` and each item button gained `flex-shrink-0` so the rail can no longer compress its own spacing as a fallback to scrolling, matching the fix's actual intent (scroll, don't shrink).
- **Cascaded to every consumer that passes a `CreateNew`-button-plus-cards `header`** — the "Agent Next Gen" `pinnedHeader`/`header` split was applied identically to `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, `agent-next-gen-v1/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, and `lyra-ux-templates/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`: `pinnedHeader={<CreateNew .../>}`, `header={<>{interactions.map(...)}</>}`. `AdminShell.stories.tsx`'s plain `<LeftNav items={NAV_ITEMS} .../>` (no `header`/`footer` at all) needed no changes — it just inherits the restructured scroll wrapper with nothing extra in it, unaffected visually.
- Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (both consuming apps).
- **Follow-up, from a screenshot**: "allow the vertical scroll but hide the scrollbar." Added a new `.lyra-scrollbar-hide` utility (`scrollbar-width: none`/`-ms-overflow-style: none` plus a `::-webkit-scrollbar { display: none }` rule — no single cross-browser property covers this) to both `lyra-tokens.css` and its Storybook fallback `storybook.css`, kept in sync per this repo's own convention. Applied to the new combined scroll wrapper in `left-nav.tsx` (both the inline and overlay branches) — scrolling itself is untouched (wheel/trackpad/keyboard all still work), only the browser's visible scrollbar track/thumb is hidden.
