No consumer changes needed: `agent-next-gen-v1`/`lyra-ux-templates` consume this CSS file live via the `@nicecxone/lyra-ui/styles` alias, so the fix took effect for both automatically.
- **Follow-up, on request**: "remove the directory from the agent left nav for now — it is covered in the CreateNew menu." Removed the "Directory" `NavItem` (`icon: <IdCard .../>`, label "Directory") from the item list, and the now-unused `IdCard` import, in all three places that hand-maintain this same nav item set: `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story, `agent-next-gen-v1/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, and `lyra-ux-templates/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`. Each app's own first/pinned nav item (`agent-next-gen-v1`'s "Desk", `lyra-ux-templates`' "Home") was left untouched, matching the earlier icon-reorder request's scoping. Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (both apps).
- **Follow-up bug, from a screenshot** of the collapsed/icon-only rail: a large empty gap sat between the pinned `CreateNew` "+" button and the first real nav icon (e.g. "Desk") whenever there were zero active interactions. **Root cause**: every consumer passes `header={<>{interactions.map(...)}</>}` — a `React.Fragment`, which is a real, non-null React element even when `interactions` is `[]` and the fragment renders no children. `left-nav.tsx`'s `{header && (<div>...)}` guard checks truthiness, not "did this render anything," so that wrapper `<div>` was always inserted as a flex sibling before `iconOnlyNav`/`TreeMenu` — and the scroll wrapper's `gap-2` (applied unconditionally to every sibling, not just ones with real content) turned that empty div into visible spacing regardless of whether `header` had any cards in it. **Fix, matching what was asked** ("remove the gap and add a margin to the InteractionNavItems"): removed the `gap-2`/`gap-1` classes from both the scroll wrapper and the `header`-wrapping div, in both the inline and overlay render branches; added `mb-2` directly on `InteractionNavItem`'s own rendered root (the compact avatar tile's outer div, and the expanded card's outer div — not `expandedCardClassName` itself, since that's shared with the compact tile's hover-popover preview, which floats and shouldn't carry a bottom margin). Spacing between cards, and between the last card and the nav list below, now comes entirely from each rendered card's own margin — with zero interactions, the header wrapper still renders (still truthy) but contributes zero children and zero margin, so no gap survives.
- Scoped to `left-nav.tsx` and `interaction-nav-item.tsx` only — no consumer changes needed, since every consumer already renders through this same shared component. Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (`agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`).

### AgentWelcomeMessage (`src/components/agent-welcome-message.tsx`)
- The once-per-session "welcome back" card `agent-next-gen-v1` showed on page load — icon + greeting + optional "Last login: {…}" line, a highlighted info-box slot (`children`) for whatever the app wants to surface there (current skills, teammates online, open items, etc.), then a `Divider` and a two-button footer for how to start the shift. Extracted from that app's hand-rolled welcome-modal markup, which was already composing exactly `LoginCard`'s own pattern (`Container variant="modal"` shell, `icon?: React.ReactNode` defaulting to this repo's own bundled `assets/app-icon.svg` via a `defaultAppIconSrc` import, same as `LoginCard`'s `appIcon` prop) — recognized as the same reusable "modal card" shape rather than something to keep re-deriving locally, per "if something this app needs doesn't exist yet in lyra-ui, add it there."
- **The original's third line, "Here's what's waiting for you today," was dropped entirely on request** — not replaced with a prop, just removed. `AgentWelcomeMessage` has no subhead slot between `lastLogin` and the info box as a result; a future consumer wanting that line back would need a real prop added, not a revival of hardcoded copy.
- Deliberately just the card, not the surrounding backdrop/dialog — same split as `LoginCard` (also backdrop-less). `agent-next-gen-v1` still wraps it in its own `Overlay`, since that app's backdrop has its own theme-aware `color-mix()` tint override (see the Overlay usage's own doc comment) that's specific to that app's dark-mode handling, not something to generalize into a shared component that other consumers might open differently.
- Props: `icon`, `title` (the full greeting line, e.g. "Good morning, John Smith" — greeting-period logic like `getGreetingPeriod()` stays app-side, this component just renders the resulting string), `lastLogin` (optional, hides its line when omitted), `children` (the info box, hides the whole box when omitted), `primaryLabel`/`onPrimaryClick` (default "Go Available"), `secondaryLabel`/`onSecondaryClick` (default "Start Offline") — the same "Go Available"/"Start Offline" pair `agent-next-gen-v1` already used, kept as overridable defaults rather than hardcoded strings since a future consumer's welcome flow may want different actions.
- Stories (`UI/AgentWelcomeMessage`, matching `LoginCard`'s category — a composed, page-level widget, not an atom): `Default` (full content, matching the reference screenshot minus the removed subhead), `MinimalContent` ("No Last Login / No Info Box" — proves both are genuinely optional, not just visually collapsed), `CustomActions` ("Custom Button Labels" — proves the footer isn't hardcoded to "Go Available"/"Start Offline" specifically).

### PageHeader (`src/components/page-header.tsx`) — added `min-h-[68px]` so the row height doesn't depend on whether `actions` is present
- **Bug, caught from a screenshot**: after removing the `Export`/`New Case` buttons from `agent-next-gen-v1`'s Desk `PageHeader` `actions`, the title ("Desk") shifted vertically relative to `LeftNav`'s own toggle chevron — the two used to line up.
- **Root cause**: `PageHeader`'s row had no explicit height — it sized to its tallest child, which was a default (`lg`, 36px-tall) `Button` whenever `actions` held any. With `actions` empty, the row's real height shrunk to just the title text's line height, and `LeftNav`'s toggle button (positioned via a hardcoded `top-[25px]`, explicitly documented as assuming a constant external `PageHeader` height) stayed put — so the two drifted out of alignment.
- **Fix**: `min-h-[68px]` on the root div — `py-4` (32px of padding) plus a default Button's 36px height, the same floor the row already had whenever `actions` was populated. Kept as `min-h`, not `h`, so a future taller `actions` element can still grow the row past that floor. This makes the row's height constant regardless of whether `actions`/`panelToggle` are present, which is what any external sibling assuming a fixed `PageHeader` height (like `LeftNav`'s toggle) actually needs.
- **Follow-up, from a screenshot**: "when a new assignment is added to the LeftNav, change the screen to a blank page with a new page header that includes the person's name + a User icon to the left, and their customer/agent id below the name." Added optional `icon`/`subtitle` props — when `icon` is passed, the title block switches from the plain `<h1>` to a "record header" layout: `icon` | vertical divider | stacked `title` (unchanged `lyra-heading-lg`) above `subtitle` (`lyra-body-sm text-lyra-fg-secondary`). Mutually exclusive with `breadcrumb` (icon wins if both are passed) — a record header and a parent-page breadcrumb are two different reasons to be on this page, never both at once in the reference screenshot. `chip` still renders next to the title in this mode. New story: `PageHeader.stories.tsx`'s `RecordHeader` ("Record Header (Icon + Subtitle)"), matching the screenshot's `User` icon + "Jamie Torres" + "CS-1239930" + "Ask AI" button exactly.
- **Cascaded** to every consumer that tracks its own `ActiveInteraction[]`/`activeInteractionId` (the same interaction-tracking block first built for `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story and hand-mirrored into every consumer since): `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, `agent-next-gen-v1/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `lyra-ux-templates/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`. Each now derives `activeInteraction` (`interactions.find((i) => i.id === activeInteractionId)`) and swaps its main content column: an active interaction renders the new record-header `PageHeader` (`icon`, `title` from `customerName`, `subtitle` from a new `recordId` field) over a blank body; otherwise it renders whatever that consumer's own "home" page was already showing (agent-next-gen-v1's full Desk dashboard + Tabs, the template's "Home" header, lyra-ux-templates' `AgentDashboard`). Switching back to the home page happens automatically once the interaction is dismissed, since `activeInteractionId` clears — no new dismiss logic needed.
- **`recordId` sourcing** (added to each consumer's own `ActiveInteraction` interface): `handleStartCall` uses `selection.contact.subtitle` — the contact's real id from `CreateNewOutboundContact` (customerId/agentId/`TEAM-…`/`SKL-…`/`ASN-…`), already surfaced there for exactly this kind of secondary-line display — falling back to a new `generateCaseId()` helper (`"CS-" + 7 random digits`, same shape as the reference screenshot's "CS-1239930") only when a record genuinely has none. `handleQuickDial` (no contact record at all) always uses `generateCaseId()`. agent-next-gen-v1's `handleRedial` reuses `entry.caseId` from Contact History instead, since that flow already has a real per-entry case id on hand.
- **Follow-up bug, from a screenshot**: "Desk" (agent-next-gen-v1) / "Home" (lyra-ux-templates) still showed as active in the LeftNav while an interaction's record page was on screen, with no way to click back to the dashboard — both had a hardcoded `active: true` and no `onClick`, unlike `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story, which already derived Desk's active state correctly (`active: activeId === ""`) but was also missing the `onClick` to actually navigate back. Fixed all three: agent-next-gen-v1 and lyra-ux-templates each gained a `buildNavItems(hasActiveInteraction, onClick)` function (replacing the static `NAV_ITEMS` array) so Desk/Home's `active` is `!hasActiveInteraction` and its `onClick` clears `activeInteractionId` back to `null`; the LeftNav story's existing derived `active` just gained the missing `onClick={() => setActiveId("")}`. `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` was left alone — its own `NAV_ITEMS`' "Home" was never marked `active` in the first place (by design, per that file's own doc comment), so this bug didn't apply there.
- **`subtitle` extended to the plain (no-`icon`, no-`breadcrumb`) title branch**: previously `subtitle` only rendered paired with `icon` (the record-header layout above). A reference screenshot (Outbound-Campaigns' "Monitor" dashboard) needed a plain section title ("4. Outbound Email") with a small uppercase category caption underneath it ("CALL CENTERS") — no leading icon, no breadcrumb trail, just title + caption. Rather than hand-roll that pairing again outside `PageHeader` (the same "check whether the real component already covers this" rule as `Label`/`readonly` and the `Field`-vs-`Input` entries above), the plain branch was extended to stack `subtitle` under `title` the same way the icon branch already does (`flex flex-col` wrapper, `lyra-body-sm text-lyra-fg-secondary` caption) — just without the icon/divider. `breadcrumb` branch intentionally left alone (no real usage for a caption under an inline breadcrumb trail yet).

### DashboardQueue (`src/components/dashboard-queue.tsx`) — "cards" header icon switched from a plain `Clock` to a per-type "hero" `Icon`
- Each queue widget's header icon was a hardcoded plain `Clock` glyph (no background chrome) regardless of the queue's actual type — `item.icon` (the real per-type icon: `MessageSquare` for Digital, `PhoneIncoming` for Inbound Voice, etc.) was already being used by the "accordion" variant, but "cards" ignored it entirely. Per a screenshot asking for "hero icons that match the component" with the same background treatment as the home tab's Activity widget, `headerIcon` now renders `<Icon icon={item.icon} size="md" background="active" shape="rounded" decorative />` — the exact `Icon` composition (and `background="active"`, a subtle blue square) `ActivityCard`'s own header icon already uses, so both widgets read consistently. `agent-next-gen-v1` needed no changes — `LATEST_CONTACTS.map(...)` was already passing each contact's own type icon through as `icon`, so this took effect automatically once the component itself started using it for "cards" too.

### FilterChip.stories.tsx — new "Custom Content (Popover)" story documenting when to reach for `Popover` instead of `Select`/`Menu`
- Raised from a screenshot of `agent-next-gen-v1`'s home tab "Date: Today" filter (Performance/Productivity cards), asking why it used a hand-rolled `Popover` instead of the `Menu` component the "real" `FilterChip` supposedly uses. Investigated both: `FilterChip` actually wraps `Select` (a checkbox list with search), not `Menu`; `Menu`'s own `items` API (`MenuItemDef[]`) is a fixed label/icon/onClick/selected list with no way to hold arbitrary root content like a live `RadioGroup` or a `DateRangePicker` — which is exactly why the date filter needed a hand-rolled `Popover` in the first place. `Menu` does have one relevant escape hatch, `submenuContent` (arbitrary content in a flyout off one item), but that's scoped to a single item's flyout, not a way to make the *whole* menu body arbitrary content — it wouldn't fit a flat "4 radio options, one of which reveals a date picker inline" shape without changing the interaction into something the reference screenshot didn't show.
- Per explicit instruction, **no component behavior changed** — `agent-next-gen-v1`'s `DateFilterChip` stays exactly as it was (`Popover` + `RadioGroup` + conditional `DateRangePicker`, trigger styled via `filterChipVariants({ variant: "default" })`). Instead, that same pattern was captured as a new story, `CustomContentPopover` ("Custom Content (Popover)"), on `FilterChip.stories.tsx` itself — so the "style a plain trigger with `filterChipVariants`, open arbitrary content via `Popover`" pattern is now documented as a supported, first-class way to build a filter-chip-styled control when `Select`/`Menu`'s fixed content shapes don't fit, rather than living only as unexplained one-off markup inside a consuming app.
- `variant="interior"` already had a `ResizeObserver`-driven `isNarrow` check (`parentWidth < 991`) that switches the panel from pushing/pinned inline layout to an absolute-positioned overlay (same look as an unpinned `side` panel — `absolute` + `shadow-lg`, floating over the content instead of shrinking it) once its parent container gets tight. Threshold changed to `1050` on request — same mechanism, just a wider trigger point, so the interior panel (e.g. `agent-next-gen-v1`'s Desk home tab "Work Item" detail panel) goes absolute a bit sooner as its container narrows.

### DashboardCard (`src/components/dashboard-card.tsx`) — body capped at 600px, scrolls past that instead of growing the card
- On request ("make the maximum height of all cards 600px before scrolling the body content"), wrapped the body (`children` for the "chart widget"/"data card" usages, `MetricRow` for the "metric card" usage) in a `max-h-[600px] overflow-y-auto` div — same `max-h-[…] overflow-y-auto` pattern `app-menu.tsx`'s dropdown list already uses, just with this card's own 600px cap instead of that menu's 480px. The header (rendered by `Container`, outside this wrapper) is unaffected — it never scrolls, only the body does once its content would otherwise push the card past 600px. Applies to every `DashboardCard` consumer automatically (queue cards, Performance/Productivity/Contact History widgets, etc.) since it's in the shared component, not something bolted on per-usage in `agent-next-gen-v1`.

### AgentDashboard (`src/components/agent-dashboard.tsx`) — the Agent Next Gen "Home" tab, promoted into a real shared component
- **Request**: "make a new template in the lyra-ui storybook for Dashboards and use the current agent-next-gen-v1 dashboard as the template base... use this template for the lyra-ux-templates Agent Next Gen page." Unlike `AgentNextGenTemplate` (the shell — app-menu/AI-panel/notifications wiring, kept story-only and hand-mirrored into each consumer, since that state is inherently bespoke per app) or `DataManagementTemplate` (also story-only), the Home tab's content — greeting, queue widgets, Contact History, Performance/Productivity — has no per-app state of its own and every consumer wants the exact same thing, the same situation `AdminShell` was extracted for. So this one is a **real exported component**, not a story-only reference to hand-copy.
- Exports `AgentDashboard` (the composition: greeting + `DashboardQueue` + Contact History + Performance/Productivity, all built from existing atoms — `DashboardCard`, `DashboardQueue`, `Tag`, `Icon`, `DonutChart`, `Divider`, `Popover`+`RadioGroup` for the shared date-range filter chip), `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown` (the per-queue skills/agent-status breakdown a consumer's own side/interior panel renders once a queue widget is clicked — kept separate since the panel itself, its placement, and whatever else shares that panel slot differ per app), and the demo data backing both (`AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_ITEMS`, `AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS`) so a consumer's own drill-down panel can look up the clicked queue's name/sub-items without hand-copying that data too.
- All of the dummy data (queue counts, Contact History rows, Performance/Productivity numbers per date range) is bundled in as-is from agent-next-gen-v1's own Home tab — none of it is real business data, so there's nothing for a consumer to configure to get a faithful result. The only true integration points are `onRedial` (Contact History's "Redial" action) and `selectedQueueId`/`onSelectQueueId` (controlled, same "report the selection, don't own what opens as a result" split `DashboardQueue`'s own `selectedId`/`onSelect` already follows).
- Exported from `index.ts` alongside `DashboardQueue`.

### AgentDashboard — content/functionality parity with agent-next-gen-v2's current Home tab (2026-08-27)
- **Request**: "update the agent dashboard component in lyra-ui to match the current agent-next-gen-v2 layout" — narrowed via a clarifying question to: skip a `PageHeader`-based greeting (keep the existing hand-rolled `<h1>`/`<p>`), but bring every widget's own content AND functionality up to date with that app's current Home tab, not just its layout.
- Queue widgets: added the 5th "Outbound Voice" queue (matching agent-next-gen-v2's `INITIAL_QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS["5"]`/`LATEST_CONTACTS_STATIC`), plus a self-contained live simulation (1s `clockTick` feeding `QUEUE_WAIT_BASE_SECONDS`, a 4s interval nudging one random queue's `inQueueCount` ±1, clamped 0-20) driving only `AgentDashboard`'s own queue row — `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown` (a consumer-owned panel) still reads the static `AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS` seed, unaffected.
- Contact History: replaced the smaller local `AgentDashboardContactHistoryEntry`/`ContactHistoryCard` with a port of agent-next-gen-v2's own richer `ContactHistoryEntry`/`ContactHistoryCard` (agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx) — 5-tier status color, a `skillName` third line, all 5 real channel types, a real search box, a 3-option cumulative date filter (Today/Last 48 Hours/Last 72 Hours), and real footer pagination over a 115-row stress batch (`CREATE_NEW_CUSTOMERS`-derived, same package fixture that app's own version sources via `@nicecxone/lyra-ui/customers-data`). Redial stayed inline on the row rather than moved to a summary panel's footer button (that app's newer behavior) since `AgentDashboard` has no owning interior panel to move it to. Added optional `onSelectEntry`/`selectedEntryId` (same "report the selection, don't own what opens" pattern as `selectedQueueId`/`onSelectQueueId`) so a consumer wanting full parity can still build its own panel from the reported entry.
- Performance card: single "Overall Performance" % row replaces Cases Resolved/CSAT, matching that app's current `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE`.
- Performance + Productivity cards: swapped the shared `DateRangeFilterChip` import for a new local `DateFilterChip` (Tooltip-wrapped, icon-collapsing below 480px via the existing `lyra-container-header-filter-*` CSS family + `headerActionsWrap`) matching that app's own `DateFilterChip` — kept local rather than changed on the shared `DateRangeFilterChip` component, which has other consumers (Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor) not asked to gain this behavior.
- Deliberately out of scope: `hideCustomerNames`, a `PageHeader`-based greeting, and porting the app's exact redial-to-panel wiring — all app-specific business rules, not this demo composition's own content/layout.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — clean, no new errors.

### AgentDashboard — PageHeader greeting + Personal Queue chip (2026-08-27)
- **Request**: "add a page header and populate the label & subhead with the Good {Time of Day}, {FirstName} and Date subhead and put the Personal Queue chip on the right" — a direct follow-up superseding the earlier "remove the page header for now" scope decision from this same day's first `AgentDashboard` parity pass.
- Replaced the hand-rolled `<h1>`/`<p>` greeting (and its "Below is your team's queue for the day:" caption, which agent-next-gen-v2's own current header no longer renders either) with lyra-ui's own `PageHeader` — `title={formatHeaderGreeting(agentFirstName)}` ("Good Morning/Afternoon/Evening, {name}", capitalized, hour<12/17 boundaries), `subtitle={formatHeaderDate()}` ("{Month Day, Year} · {H:MM AM/PM}"), `bordered={false}`, `titleSize="2xl"` — both helpers ported directly (not imported) from agent-next-gen-v2's own identically-named `formatHeaderGreeting`/`formatHeaderDate` (agent-next-gen-shared-utils.ts), since this package can't depend on that app.
- Added a `-mx-6` wrapper around `PageHeader` to cancel its own baked-in `px-6` so the title lines up flush with the cards below (`DashboardQueue` etc., which have no side padding of their own) — the exact same trick agent-next-gen-v2's own dashboard header call site uses, confirmed by reading that call site directly.
- Added a `PersonalQueueChip` (local, unexported) in the header's `actions` slot — same "Personal Queue: {N}" label, success-color pill, and trailing `ChevronRight` as that app's own header chip, simplified to a fixed "Empty" (success) state since `AgentDashboard` bundles no live personal-assignment data of its own (see the class doc comment's "all of the demo data ... is bundled in as sensible defaults" note) — the app's own richer three-tier success/warning/critical coloring and SLA-breach escalation depend on real interaction state this component doesn't model. Added an optional `onPersonalQueueClick` prop (same "report, don't own" shape as this file's other callbacks) so a consumer wanting the chip to actually open its own left nav/panel still can.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — clean, no new errors (unchanged pre-existing 3-error baseline in admin-shell.tsx/list-item.tsx, neither touched by this change).

### AgentDashboard Contact History — real right InteriorPanel, Redial/Re-open moved into it (2026-08-27)
- **Request**: "the contact history is not matching agent-next-gen-v2 - it should open a right interior panel (also the redial button should be in the panel)."
- `agent-dashboard.tsx`: removed the row's own inline "Redial" button (matching agent-next-gen-v2, which moved Redial/Re-open off the row entirely and onto a summary panel's footer). Added a new exported `AgentDashboardContactHistoryEntryDetail` component — a direct port of that app's own `ContactHistoryEntryDetail` (agent-next-gen-contact-history.tsx): a status · handle · time meta line, a bordered Duration/notes box, then a synthesized "Transcript" (voice, plain name+timestamp/line turns) or "Conversation" (chat/sms/whatsapp, real `ChatMessage` bubbles) section, or a synthesized "Body" paragraph for email — all deterministically hashed off `entry.id` so a row always renders the same content on reopen, same "no real backend" convention the rest of this file's fixtures already follow. `AgentDashboard`'s own now-unused `onRedial` prop was removed entirely (nothing inside this file called it anymore once Redial left the row) — a consumer's own panel footer button calls whatever handler it wants directly, using the entry `onSelectContactHistoryEntry` already reports.
- `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`: added an opt-in `interiorPanelOverride` prop to `AgentNextGenTemplate` (`{open, headerTitle, headerSubhead?, footer?, onClose, content}`) — when set, the Home tab's existing right `InteriorPanel` renders these instead of its hardcoded `interiorPanelOpen`/"Case Details" placeholder; every other story (which never passes it) is unaffected. The "Agent Home Dashboard" story was rebuilt from a bare `<AgentDashboard />` render into a small `AgentHomeDashboardDemo` wrapper holding `selectedQueueId`/`selectedContactHistoryEntry` state, wiring `AgentDashboard`'s `onSelectQueueId`/`onSelectContactHistoryEntry` callbacks to open a real right `InteriorPanel` — `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown` for a queue click, `AgentDashboardContactHistoryEntryDetail` for a Contact History row click (queue takes priority over Contact History, same "one docked slot, several jobs" order agent-next-gen-v2's own `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` uses for its identical panel) — with a mutually-exclusive Redial/Re-open footer `Button` gated on `entry.redial`, built at this story's own call site (not inside the exported detail component, which stays read-only content). Both footer buttons just close the panel on click, since this demo composition has no live interaction system to actually reopen a contact into.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` on both the main `tsconfig.json` (clean, same pre-existing 3-error baseline, none in agent-dashboard.tsx) and a temporary stories-inclusive tsconfig (clean vs. this file's own pre-existing 3-error baseline — an `app-icon.svg` module error, an unrelated `Expected 1 arguments` error, and a pre-existing `ReactNode`/`string` mismatch in the unrelated Customer Information Draggable-panel code — none introduced by or related to this change).

### AgentNextGenTemplate — controlled navOpen, Personal Queue chip toggles left nav (2026-08-27)
**Request**: "in the agent home dashboard story the personal queue: empty should toggle the left nav open and closed like in agent-next-gen-v2."
- Added a controlled/uncontrolled `navOpen`/`onNavOpenChange` prop pair to `AgentNextGenTemplate` — same "report, don't own" shape as `DashboardQueue`'s `selectedId`/`onSelect`. Internally, `useState` still owns the default (`!!initialInteractions?.length`, unchanged); when a consumer passes `navOpen`, that value wins and every internal `setNavOpen` call now also fires `onNavOpenChange` instead of writing local state. Every existing story (none of which pass either prop) is unaffected — same convention `homeBody`/`interiorPanelOverride` already established.
- `AgentHomeDashboardDemo` (the "Agent Home Dashboard" story's wrapper) now holds its own `navOpen` state and wires `AgentDashboard`'s `onPersonalQueueClick={() => setNavOpen((v) => !v)}` alongside `navOpen`/`onNavOpenChange={setNavOpen}` on `AgentNextGenTemplate` — the exact same toggle function agent-next-gen-v2's own identical header chip calls.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` via a temporary stories-inclusive tsconfig — clean vs. this file's own pre-existing 3-error baseline (unchanged from the prior entry), no new errors from the `navOpen` wiring.

### AgentDashboard.stories.tsx — fixed stale `onRedial` reference (regression fix, 2026-08-27)
- Found while re-verifying the `navOpen` change above: the standalone `UI/Agent Dashboard` story (`AgentDashboard.stories.tsx`, separate from `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Home Dashboard" story) still passed the now-removed `onRedial` prop to `<AgentDashboard>` — a real regression from the earlier "real right InteriorPanel, Redial/Re-open moved into it" entry above, missed at the time because that pass's verification only checked `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` and `agent-dashboard.tsx` by name, not every story file.
- Fixed by bringing this story's `InteriorPanel` wiring up to the same pattern as `AgentHomeDashboardDemo`: added `selectedEntry` state alongside the existing `selectedQueueId`, wired `AgentDashboard`'s `onSelectContactHistoryEntry`/`selectedContactHistoryEntryId`, and extended the panel's `open`/`headerTitle`/`headerSubhead`/`footer`/content to branch on whichever of queue/entry is selected (queue takes priority) — same mutually-exclusive Redial/Re-open footer `Button` gated on `entry.redial`, same "close on click" behavior.

### AgentDashboard Productivity card — dropped `tabular-nums` from time values (2026-08-27)
**Request**: screenshot of the Productivity card's duration numbers ("still appear to be the old font — they should match agent-next-gen-v2").
- Root cause: the `PerformanceBreakdownCard`'s per-status time spans (`row.time`/`row.teamTime`, e.g. "01:45:12") still had `tabular-nums` applied, unlike agent-next-gen-v2's identical card, which explicitly dropped it — Inter's tabular digit glyphs are drawn differently from its proportional ones used everywhere else in the UI, which reads as "a different font" at a glance (documented in that app's own BEHAVIOR.md §54, ported here as an inline comment). This was missed during the original content/functionality parity pass since it's a class-level styling detail, not a data/behavior difference.
- Fix: removed `tabular-nums` from both spans in `agent-dashboard.tsx`'s `PerformanceBreakdownCard` row. Left the Performance card's `tabular-nums` usages (`row.you`/`row.team`/`overallYou`/`overallTeam` — percentages, not durations) untouched, since those match agent-next-gen-v2's own Performance card exactly.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` — clean, unchanged pre-existing 3-error baseline.

### AgentDashboard/AgentNextGenTemplate — `header` toggle moves greeting to a real (non-scrolling) header slot (2026-08-27)
**Request**: "add a control in the agent home dashboard component that toggles header true/false and when set to true move the Good Morning, {First Name}, subhead and personal queue chip to the header and do not display it in the body of the dashboard" (screenshot of the existing inline greeting bar).
- Investigated agent-next-gen-v2's own `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` first: found it once had this exact behavior — a doc comment above its (now-empty) Home-tab header slot reads "the greeting `PageHeader` ... no longer renders in this top header slot — it moved into the dashboard body ... so it scrolls with the rest of the dashboard's content instead of pinning above it" — i.e. that app tried and reverted the very thing being asked for here. Ported the reverted (non-body) version rather than reinventing it.
- `agent-dashboard.tsx`: added `header?: boolean` to `AgentDashboardProps` (default `false`) — when true, suppresses `AgentDashboard`'s own inline greeting `PageHeader` block entirely (nothing renders in its place in the scrollable body). Added a new exported `AgentDashboardHeader({ agentFirstName, onPersonalQueueClick })` — the identical greeting/date/Personal Queue chip content, minus the `-mx-6` cancel-padding trick (that only exists to line up with a `px-6`-padded scroll body; a real header slot has no competing padding) — for a consumer to mount wherever its own actual header lives.
- `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`: added `homeHeader?: React.ReactNode` to `AgentNextGenTemplate` — rendered in the same slot the "Home" `PageHeader` (`showPageHeader` gate) already occupies above the Home tab's "Body row", a genuine non-scrolling row (not inside the `overflow-y-auto` `homeBody` wrapper). When set, it replaces that default "Home" `PageHeader` outright; every other story (which never passes it) is unaffected.
- `AgentHomeDashboardDemo` (the "Agent Home Dashboard" story's wrapper) now takes a `header` prop, forwarded straight to `AgentDashboard`'s own `header`, with `AgentDashboardHeader` (same `onPersonalQueueClick={() => setNavOpen((v) => !v)}` wiring as the inline chip) passed into `AgentNextGenTemplate`'s new `homeHeader` when true. Wired as a real Storybook boolean control (`args`/`argTypes` on `AgentHomeDashboardStory`, defaulting `false` — unchanged prior behavior) rather than a hardcoded prop, since this story's own `header` control has no equivalent prop on `AgentNextGenTemplate` directly — typed against `StoryObj<typeof AgentHomeDashboardDemo>` instead of the file's shared `Story` alias for this reason.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` on the main `tsconfig.json` (clean, unchanged 3-error baseline) and the temporary stories-inclusive tsconfig (`AgentDashboard.stories.tsx` clean, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` clean vs. its own pre-existing 3-error baseline, total story-tree error count unchanged at 157).

**Request**: "add a control to the Agent Home Dashboard for tabs True/false and display Dashboard/Customers/Tickets/Accounts when true" (follow-up after declining a different "force interior panels full width" change, scoped to agent-next-gen-v2 only).
- `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`: added a new `homeTabs?: React.ReactNode` prop, rendered as its own row directly below the Home tab's header (whichever of `homeHeader`/the default "Home" `PageHeader` is showing) and above the "Body row" — same placement the Customers record header's own `TabList` occupies below its `PageHeader`. `undefined` (every other story's default) renders nothing there, unaffected.

When on, it renders a plain `TabList`/`Tab` row of four static labels — "Dashboard" (active by default), "Customers", "Tickets", "Accounts" — into the new `homeTabs` slot, with its own local `homeTab` state so clicking updates which tab looks active. Purely cosmetic: this demo composition has no real Customers/Tickets/Accounts content to swap in behind the other three, only the tab row itself was requested.

- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` on the main `tsconfig.json` (clean, unchanged 3-error baseline) and the temporary stories-inclusive tsconfig (`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`'s own pre-existing 3-error baseline unchanged, total story-tree error count unchanged at 157).

Every existing caller that already passes a real `agentFirstName` (e.g. `AgentDashboard.stories.tsx`'s own `agentFirstName="John"`) is unaffected — this only changes the previously-unhandled "nothing passed" case.

- Net effect on the "Agent Home Dashboard" story specifically: since it never passed `agentFirstName`, its greeting now reads a plain "Good Morning" (previously "Good Morning, there") in both the inline body copy and the `header`-toggle's non-scrolling header slot.

### Agent Home Dashboard — "Connect Agent Leg" link now connects the real AgentProfile + shows the success toast (2026-08-27)
**Request**: "when you click connect agent leg from the header link it should connect the agent leg (in the profile) and display a toast success message - same behavior as if the agent connected from the profile."
- The local `AgentNextGenTemplate` (AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx — this template is defined in the stories file itself, not a separate shared component) gained: a `useToast` stack + `ToastContainer`/`Toast` render (new, this template had zero toast infrastructure before this); a `connectAgentLegSignal?: number` prop forwarded straight to its internal `AgentProfile`'s own identically-named prop (agent-profile.tsx); and `handleAgentLegStatusChange`, wired to `AgentProfile`'s `onAgentLegStatusChange`, which fires the same "Agent Leg Connected" / "Your agent leg is now connected." success toast agent-next-gen-v2's own `showAgentLegToast` uses on a real connect — regardless of whether that connect came from `AgentProfile`'s own "Agent Leg Disconnected" menu row or from `connectAgentLegSignal` being bumped externally, since both paths converge on `AgentProfile`'s own internal connect flow.
- "Agent Home Dashboard" story: added a `connectAgentLegSignal` counter alongside the existing `agentLegConnected` boolean (from the prior "Connect Agent Leg" header-link entry above) and a shared `handleConnectAgentLeg` click handler that bumps both — the header's own link/lag-time text flips immediately (unchanged from before), while `connectAgentLegSignal` separately triggers `AgentProfile`'s real ~2s connecting→connected transition and its new success toast. Deliberately two independent state updates from one click, not one derived from the other — see `connectAgentLegSignal`'s own doc comment for why.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` on the main `tsconfig.json` (clean, unchanged 3-error baseline) and the temporary stories-inclusive tsconfig (unchanged 157-error total).

### Agent Home Dashboard — hide Connected Apps, sync disconnect back to header, add "Connecting..." subhead (2026-08-27)
**Request**: "in the agent home dashboard story hide the connected apps and when the agent leg is disconnected, update the page header to say 'Connect Agent Leg' link again - also - when the agent leg is connecting, make the subhead say 'Connecting...' before lag time message is displayed."
- `AgentProfile` (agent-profile.tsx) gained `hideConnectedApps?: boolean`, hiding its "Connected Apps" menu row/flyout entirely — forwarded from the local `AgentNextGenTemplate` (AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx) via a new same-named prop, passed `hideConnectedApps` (unconditionally true) only from the "Agent Home Dashboard" story's `<AgentNextGenTemplate>` call — every other consumer/story is unaffected.
- `resolveGreetingContent`/`AgentDashboardHeaderProps`/`AgentDashboardProps` (agent-dashboard.tsx): the boolean `agentLegConnected` was replaced with a tri-state `agentLegStatus?: "disconnected" | "connecting" | "connected"`, deliberately mirroring `AgentProfile`'s own real state-machine type so a consumer tracking the real one can pass it straight through. Subhead now renders the `AgentLegConnectLink` only while `"disconnected"`, `"Connecting..."` while `"connecting"`, and `"Connection Lag Time: {connectionLagTime}"` only once `"connected"`.
- Closed the loop between the header's link and the real `AgentProfile`: `AgentNextGenTemplate` gained an outward `onAgentLegStatusChange?: (status: "disconnected" | "connected") => void` prop, and its existing `handleAgentLegStatusChange` (wired to `AgentProfile`'s own `onAgentLegStatusChange`) now relays both statuses outward (previously only fired the success toast on `"connected"` and reported nothing back). The "Agent Home Dashboard" story's `handleConnectAgentLeg` now sets local state to `"connecting"` immediately (instead of jumping straight to a connected-like state) and passes `onAgentLegStatusChange={setAgentLegStatus}` into `<AgentNextGenTemplate>` — so the header's copy is a true mirror of `AgentProfile`'s real ~2s connect transition (and its disconnect, triggered independently from `AgentProfile`'s own menu row, now correctly reverts the header back to showing the "Connect Agent Leg" link) rather than optimistic local-only state.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` on the main `tsconfig.json` (clean, unchanged 3-error baseline) and the temporary stories-inclusive tsconfig (unchanged 157-error total).

### EmptyState (`src/components/empty-state.tsx`) — new component
- A plain "no data" placeholder, extracted rather than re-hand-rolled a third time: `DraggablePanel`'s default body and `AiPanel`'s history view already had their own inline `<p className="lyra-body-md text-lyra-fg-disabled ...">"Nothing here yet."`/`"No conversation history."` — this component is the same look, generalized (`icon?`, `message` default `"No data available"`, `description?`), for any card/widget/chart body that needs the same treatment instead of a fourth copy.
- First real consumer: Outbound-Campaigns' "Monitor" dashboard's full-width "Outbound Email" card, whose chart area shows "No data available" until real send data exists.
- Story: `UI/EmptyState` — `Default`, `WithIcon`, `WithDescription`.
- Not retrofitted into `DraggablePanel`/`AiPanel`'s own inline copies in this pass — both are stable, low-risk one-liners; flagged here as a candidate cleanup if either needs to change again anyway, not urgent enough to touch on its own.

### AgentSearch / AgentChat / ScreenPop / SchedulePanel (4 new components) + AgentNextGenTemplate's top-right app area reworked to agent-next-gen-v2's model
2026-08-24: Four components ported from agent-next-gen-v2's shared AppHeader app panel, each exported from index.ts with a `UI/...` story: `AgentSearch` (agent-search.tsx — `SearchInput` header row + blank "Nothing here yet." body), `AgentChat` (agent-chat.tsx — the blank placeholder, faithful: v2's panel has no chat UI yet), `ScreenPop` (screen-pop.tsx — header `Select` of 8 apps, Salesforce/Zendesk rendering an internal `MockLoginCard` whose hex colors/raw inputs are deliberate third-party brand mimicry, see its doc comment), `SchedulePanel` (schedule-panel.tsx — Day/Week toolbar, "Shift" resource lane, 24h grid with live now-indicator; its date-jump `Popover` raised to `z-[9999]` so it clears a hosting float panel, same tier `Select`'s dropdown already uses).
- Each file also exports a `useXContent(): EmbeddablePanelContent` hook (same convention as `useAgentNotificationsContent`) for shared-`Draggable` shells; primary values (query/app/view/anchorDate) are controlled-when-provided, internal otherwise.
- `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`: the header's `NotificationsBell` + standalone Ask AI button (and its entire separate `AiPanel` wiring — v2's Desk header has no AI button) replaced with v2's model: a pinned app icon row (defaults pinned: Search, Agent Chat, Notifications; `ActionIconButton size="xl"` each), a trailing "View All Apps" `KebabMenuButton` (LayoutGrid, `PanelPinButton` per row toggling `pinnedKeys`; the unread badge moves onto the kebab while Notifications is unpinned), and ONE shared `Draggable` panel whose header/body swap by `activePanelKey` (closed → open docked; same key → close; different key → content swaps in place). Notifications' content now comes from `useAgentNotificationsContent` inside that shared shell — same handlers as before, no reimplementation.
- Simplifications vs. v2, also listed in the template's own header comment: no drag-to-reorder of the header icons/menu rows, no measured responsive icon hide/reveal (16px/84px hysteresis), no <768px combined-panel two-tab mode (the template keeps its pre-existing "close + undock below 1280px" behavior), no per-app fullscreen.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (pre-existing 3-error baseline unchanged), esbuild bundles of all 4 component files + all 5 touched story files, and prototype-kit build + smoke test (PASSED).

## Stories

### AppHeader (`src/components/__stories__/AppHeader.stories.tsx`)
- `Default`, `AppName Only`, and `With Background` previously rendered a static, non-interactive `AppName` — clicking it did nothing, unlike the `Agent Next Gen Header` story where clicking `AppName` opens a real `AppMenu` popover (Admin/Supervisor/Agent-active/Conginity AI, Workforce Management/Quality Management/Interaction Hub/My Zone, Dashboard/Analytics groups, `CXoneLogo` footer).
- Extracted that exact wiring into a shared `AppNameWithMenu({ name, alt })` helper (Radix `Popover.Root`/`Trigger`/`Portal`/`Content` around `AppName`, rendering `<AppMenu groups={APP_MENU_GROUPS} footer={<CXoneLogo />} />`) and the `APP_MENU_GROUPS` constant it needed, both moved above `Default` so all three static stories could reuse them — `AgentNextGen`'s own render function keeps its own separate copy of this wiring since it's bundled with unrelated AI-panel/notifications state specific to that story.

### Data Management (`src/components/__stories__/DataManagement.stories.tsx`)
- Template story under "Templates/Data Management" with two variants: Default and AutoFit
- Boolean controls: sortable, reorderable, groupable, showTabs, showToolbar, showAskAI, showQuickSearch, showRefresh, showEdit, showCopy, showDelete, showColumns, showFilters
- 50 sample records
- Filters use declarative `filterDefs`/`filterValues` API on TableToolbar
- Grouping uses filtered data (not raw sampleData)
- Layout: fullscreen with h-screen wrapper, component uses h-full
- Padding: px-6 on TabList, TableToolbar, table container, and TableFooter

### FilterChip Stories (`src/components/__stories__/FilterChip.stories.tsx`)
- Stories: Empty, Active, Error, Disabled, DisabledWithValues, Removable, AllStates
- 50 sample options per chip
- Removable story: dynamic add/remove filters with "+ Filter" button (uses Select with outline button trigger), overflow chips behind "+N" button with scrollable dropdown, Clear button clears all
- AllStates story: rows of empty, active, error, disabled chips with "+ Filter" and "Clear" buttons
- **"Basic" static value-chip demos, on request**: a separate `BasicValueChip` helper (built from the real `filterChipVariants`/`filterChipRemoveButtonVariants` CVA definitions, not the full dropdown-driven `FilterChip`) renders a plain value chip for `Basic` (32px) and `BasicSmall` (24px). `onRemove` is optional on `BasicValueChip` itself — when omitted the chip renders fully rounded/bordered on all sides instead of the split-border pairing used when a remove button is attached.
- **`Basic`/`BasicSmall` now carry three live Storybook boolean controls — `error`, `removable`, and `icon` — rather than separate dedicated stories per combination.** This followed the same "control, not a new story" correction applied twice more as the story evolved: an initial pass added dedicated `BasicError`/`BasicSmallError` exports, which per explicit feedback ("do not add new stories just have error state as a control for the stories") were removed in favor of an `error` control; a later pass added dedicated `BasicIconLeft`/`BasicSmallIconLeft` exports (a decorative leading icon, sized `icon-xs`/14px on the 24px chip and `icon-sm`/16px on the 32px chip, a box/cube placeholder on request), which were likewise folded into the same two stories as an `icon` control instead of staying separate exports. `removable` (defaults `true`) toggles whether the remove "x" renders at all. All three controls compose: e.g. `icon` + `error` together show the leading `Box` icon recolored to `status-critical` red, positioned before the value text, with the chip-level `ErrorIcon` further left signaling error state — the error icon never displaces/overtakes the decorative icon's own slot.
- **`error` also drives text/icon color correctly**: the value text has no hardcoded color of its own (inherits the outer div's `filterChipVariants({variant})` color, so it turns red only when `error` is set) and the decorative `icon` switches `color="status-critical"` in the same state — both were previously hardcoded to a default/neutral color regardless of `error`, fixed on request from a screenshot showing black text/icon next to a red error icon and border.

### LeftNav — "Outbound Engagement Left Nav" story (`src/components/__stories__/LeftNav.stories.tsx`)
- Three flat items, no children/expandable groups: Monitor (`Gauge` icon, `active: true`), Configure (`Settings` icon), Review (`BarChart3` icon) — mirrors the `AgentNextGen`/`CustomItems` stories' structure in the same file (local `open` state + `onToggle`, no header/footer content).

### LeftNav — "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story now launches real cards + auto-expands on a new one
- Previously the 3 `InteractionNavItem` cards in this story's header were permanently fixed — `CreateNew`'s "New Outbound" flow was passed `OUTBOUND_CONFIG` as-is, whose default `onStartCall`/`onQuickDial` just `console.log`, so starting a call from the story never actually did anything visible.
- On request, wired real `onStartCall`/`onQuickDial` handlers (same merge-by-contact-id pattern `agent-next-gen-v1`'s `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` and this repo's own `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` already use — starting a second channel with a contact who already has a card folds into that card instead of creating a second one) that add to a local `interactions` array, seeded with the original 3 demo cards.
- **New behavior**: launching or updating a card now also calls `setOpen(true)` — a collapsed nav would otherwise hide the card it just launched/updated from view entirely, so starting a call auto-expands the rail regardless of whether the nav happened to be collapsed at the time.
- **Cascaded on request** into the two consumers that were missing it: `agent-next-gen-v1`'s `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (`handleStartCall`/`handleQuickDial`/`handleRedial`, each now calling `setNavOpen(true)`) and this repo's own `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` (`handleStartCall`/`handleQuickDial`, calling `setNavOpen(true)` — this template has no Redial/Contact History feature, so no third handler here). All three "Agent Next Gen" consumers now auto-expand identically.

### InteractionNavItem/channel-row.tsx — "Unassign & Dismiss" now removes the card
- On request, clicking "Unassign & Dismiss" from any channel's kebab menu removes that `InteractionNavItem` card entirely — previously the item existed only as static, unwired `MenuEntry` data (`DIGITAL_MENU_ITEMS`/`VOICE_MENU_ITEMS` in `channel-row.tsx`, no `onClick`), so choosing it did nothing.
- `DIGITAL_MENU_ITEMS`/`VOICE_MENU_ITEMS` (flat consts) became `buildDigitalMenuItems(onDismiss?)`/`buildVoiceMenuItems(onDismiss?)` (functions) so the shared "Unassign & Dismiss" entry can carry a real `onClick` — the action ends the *whole* interaction, not just the channel it was clicked from, so it's threaded down from the card level (`InteractionNavItem`'s new `onDismiss?: () => void` prop → each channel row via `ChannelRowInstanceProps.onDismiss`), not decided by an individual channel row. A per-channel `menuItems` override still replaces the whole list as before — `onDismiss` only ever wires onto the *default* menu.
- Wired into all three "Agent Next Gen" consumers identically (`agent-next-gen-v1`'s `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, this repo's `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, and `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s `AgentNextGen` story): each adds a `handleDismissInteraction(id)` that filters the dismissed interaction out of its own `interactions` state and clears `activeInteractionId`/`activeId` if the dismissed card was the active one, passed as `onDismiss={() => handleDismissInteraction(interaction.id)}` on each `InteractionNavItem`.

### InteractionNavItem/channel-row.tsx — dismiss is per-channel, and only one channel highlights at a time
- **Follow-up fix, from a screenshot of a 2-channel card (SMS + WhatsApp) where both rows showed the blue "current" background at once.** Root cause: each consumer was setting a freshly-added channel's `current: true` directly in its own state without also flipping every sibling channel back to `false` in that same update — easy to get wrong (this repo's own `LeftNav.stories.tsx` demo had exactly this bug) since nothing enforced "only one `current: true` at a time" as an invariant.
- **Fix**: `InteractionNavItem` no longer trusts `InteractionChannel.current` verbatim. It resolves its own single `currentType` — the *last* channel actually flagged `current` (ignoring any earlier stale ones), or the last channel overall if none are flagged — and only that one ever renders `highlighted`. This makes the double-highlight bug structurally impossible regardless of what a consumer's state does.
- **New interaction, not just a bug fix**: each channel row is now clickable (`ChannelRow`'s new `onSelect`, threaded through `ChannelRowInstanceProps` and each per-type row) and overrides `currentType` to whichever channel was clicked — letting an agent toggle which open channel is "current" within one multi-channel card. The click still bubbles up to the card's own `onClick` too (selecting the whole card) since nothing calls `stopPropagation` — only the kebab button does that. The override self-heals back to the derived default once that channel is no longer present (e.g. dismissed).
- **"Unassign & Dismiss" is now per-channel, not per-card**, per a follow-up request: a card with more than one open channel only loses *that* channel when its "Unassign & Dismiss" is clicked; a card with exactly one channel loses the whole card (nothing would be left otherwise). This decision — which of the two applies — is made inside `InteractionNavItem` itself (`channels.length > 1` at click time), not left to each consumer to re-derive. `InteractionNavItemProps` gained `onDismissChannel?: (channelType: ChannelType) => void` alongside the existing `onDismiss?: () => void` (now only invoked in the single-channel case); `channel-row.tsx`'s per-row `onDismiss` callback (used to build each default menu's "Unassign & Dismiss" `onClick`) is unchanged in shape — `InteractionNavItem` just decides, per row, which of its own two callbacks to hand down as that row's `onDismiss`.
- All three consumers updated identically: each keeps its existing `handleDismissInteraction(id)` (whole-card removal, wired to `onDismiss`) and adds a new `handleDismissChannel(id, channelType)` (filters that one channel type out of the interaction's own channel list, wired to `onDismissChannel`).

### LeftNav.stories.tsx — "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story's launched-call preview now shows the real selected skill
- Bug: this story's `handleStartCall` set each new channel's body copy (`preview`) to `randomSkill()` — a random pick from a 3-name pool — completely ignoring `selection.skillId`, the skill the agent actually chose in `CreateNew`'s outbound flow. `agent-next-gen-v1`'s `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` and this repo's own `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` already did this correctly (`OUTBOUND_CONFIG.skillOptions.find((o) => o.value === selection.skillId)?.label`) — only this story had drifted, likely because `randomSkill()` was already sitting right there for the story's 3 *static* seed cards (which have no real launch selection to correlate to, so a random pick is legitimate for them) and got reused for the dynamic case too without noticing the mismatch.
- Fix: `handleStartCall` now resolves `skillLabel` the same way the other two files do and uses that as `preview`, so a launched call's card shows the skill the agent actually picked. `randomSkill()` is untouched and still correctly used for the 3 fixed demo cards.

### InteractionNavItem — opening a channel always makes it the selected one, even overriding a previous manual pick
- **Follow-up bug, from a screenshot**: a card with SMS already open, then WhatsApp opened on top of it, kept showing SMS's row as blank (no blue highlight) instead of highlighting the newly-opened WhatsApp row. Root cause: the "toggle between channels" click override (`manualCurrentType`, added in the previous per-channel-highlight fix) is sticky by design — once an agent clicks a row, it stays "current" until dismissed — but that stickiness was also blocking any *later-opened* channel from ever becoming current, since the click-derived override outranked the derived fallback unconditionally.
- **Fix**: `InteractionNavItem` now diffs this render's channel `type`s against the previous render's (tracked as state, adjusted directly during render per React's documented "adjusting state when a prop changes" pattern — this takes effect before paint, not one tick late the way a `useEffect` comparison would). Whenever a type appears that wasn't there before — i.e. a channel just got opened on this interaction — it becomes `manualCurrentType`, overriding whatever was clicked earlier. Dismissing a channel still self-heals back to the normal fallback as before (no type is "newly added" on a removal, so no override fires); only genuinely *new* channels take over "current".

### InteractionChannel/InteractionNavItem — a contact can now have two open channels of the same type (e.g. two SMS threads on different numbers)
- **Bug, reported directly**: starting a second SMS with a customer who already had one open (to a *different* phone number) silently replaced the first SMS row instead of adding a second one — "if a customer has an interaction launched (ie. an SMS) and then a new SMS to a different number is launched for that customer, both should appear." Root cause: every consumer's merge-into-existing-card logic (`handleStartCall`) matched an incoming channel against the interaction's existing channels by `type` alone (`channels.findIndex((c) => c.type === selection.channel)`) — a second SMS on a different number matched the first SMS's `type` and overwrote it, since there was no way to tell "restart this exact conversation" from "this is a different conversation on the same channel type" using `type` alone. The same `type`-as-identity assumption ran through `InteractionNavItem` itself: `manualCurrentType`/`currentType` (which row is blue-highlighted) and `onDismissChannel(channelType)` (which row "Unassign & Dismiss" removes) were both keyed on `type`, so even after fixing the merge, two same-type rows would have highlighted together and dismissed together.
- **Fix (structural, not a one-off patch)**: `InteractionChannel` (channel-row.tsx) gained an optional `id?: string` — the real identity for a channel instance, falling back to `type` when omitted (safe for any interaction that never opens two channels of the same type at once, e.g. every existing fixed demo card). `InteractionNavItem` was reworked to key everything off `channelKey(ch) = ch.id ?? ch.type` instead of `ch.type` directly: `manualCurrentType`→`manualCurrentKey`, `prevChannelTypes`→`prevChannelKeys`, `currentType`→`currentKey`, the row `key=` prop, and click-to-select. `onDismissChannel`'s signature changed from `(channelType: ChannelType) => void` to `(channel: InteractionChannel) => void` — the *whole* channel object, not just its type — so a consumer can match on `channel.id ?? channel.type` and drop exactly the one dismissed, not every row sharing that type.
- Each of the three "Agent Next Gen" consumers builds its own channel `id` as `` `${type}:${address}` `` (e.g. `"sms:+14565559981"`) at start-call time, so restarting the *same* address correctly refreshes that one row (merge-by-id still matches) while a *different* address on the same type opens as a new, additional row. `agent-next-gen-v1`'s `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` and this repo's `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` carry this on their local `TrackedChannel.id`; `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s `AgentNextGenDemoInteraction` sets `InteractionChannel.id` directly since it has no separate tracked-channel type of its own.
- **`CreateNewOutboundContact.openChannelAddresses` (added earlier this session to keep an in-use address out of CreateNew's detail screen) needed the same generalization**: it was `Partial<Record<ChannelType, string>>` (one address per channel type), which can only ever represent *one* open SMS number — insufficient once a contact can have two. Changed to `Partial<Record<ChannelType, string[]>>`.
- **Also corrected, on direct feedback, to filter rather than disable**: an in-progress version of this feature showed already-open addresses as disabled-but-visible options (grayed out, still in the list) — including, incorrectly, as the field's *pre-selected default value* (`defaultDetailValueFor` picked `phoneOptions[0]` unconditionally, so an already-open number could land there greyed-out-but-selected). Feedback: "you should not allow these to appear in the select and if non are available then nothing should display in the select." Fixed by having `create-new.tsx` filter open addresses out of `detailFieldMeta.options` entirely (not map them to `{ disabled: true }`), and by having `defaultDetailValueFor` itself skip any address already in `openChannelAddresses` when picking a default — falling through to the next unused `phoneOptions` entry, or `""` (rendering the field with no value and no placeholder — Select's own `{displayText || placeholder}` already renders empty in that case, no extra empty-state UI needed) once every option for that channel is already open.
- `--lyra-state-hover-critical-subtle`: light `#ffebeb`, dark `rgba(227,69,69,0.18)`
- `--lyra-state-pressed-critical-subtle`: light `#ffe0e0`, dark `rgba(227,69,69,0.24)`
- `--lyra-state-hover-active-subtle` updated: `#e4f2ff` → `#e8f1fc`
- All added to: lyra-tokens.css, storybook.css, tailwind-preset.ts, tailwind.config.js, Colors.stories.tsx

### CreateNew (`src/components/create-new.tsx`) — agent presence status chips in the Outbound "Select Agent" list
- **Request**: "add status chips to the right of the agent name in the new Outbound > Select Agent menu indicating whether they're available, busy, away, offline or in a call."
- Added an exported `AgentPresenceStatus = "available" | "busy" | "away" | "offline" | "in-call"` type, an internal `AGENT_PRESENCE_CONFIG` map to `{ label, variant }` (reusing `Tag`'s existing `success/critical/warning/neutral/info` variants), and an internal `AgentPresenceChip` that renders a `Tag` with `shape="pill"`. This is intentionally a separate type from `AgentProfile`'s existing `AgentStatus` (`available | busy | away | offline`, no `in-call`) — that one describes the *logged-in agent's own* status; `AgentPresenceStatus` describes *other agents'* observed presence in a picker list.
- `CreateNewContact` gained an optional `status?: AgentPresenceStatus`, set on agent and skill records; left unset for customers/teams, which have no individual presence concept. `ContactRow` renders the chip on its own line below the name, to the left of `subtitle` (e.g. above/left of "AGT-2004") — chip and subtitle share one flex row that only renders when at least one of them is present.
- Demo data: `create-new-agents-data.ts`'s `CreateNewAgentRecord` gained a required `status` field; `buildAgents()` assigns it deterministically via a weighted 10-entry `STATUS_CYCLE` (mostly `available`/`away`, `busy`/`in-call`/`offline` each appear once per 10) rather than an even split, so the roster looks realistic. `create-new-outbound-mock.tsx`'s shared `OUTBOUND_AGENTS` mapping (consumed by `CreateNew.stories.tsx`, `LeftNav.stories.tsx`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`) and `agent-next-gen-v1/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s parallel `OUTBOUND_AGENTS` mapping both pass `status: a.status` through.
- **Follow-up request**: "move the status chip below the agent name and to the left of the agent ID... also add status chips to the skills as well." The two hardcoded `OUTBOUND_SKILLS` entries ("Spanish Language"/"Technical Support") in both `create-new-outbound-mock.tsx` and `agent-next-gen-v1/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s parallel copy now set `status` too (`available`/`busy`) — no new type needed since `AgentPresenceStatus`/`AgentPresenceChip` were already generic to any `CreateNewContact`, not agent-specific.
- **Second follow-up, from a screenshot**: the text next to a skill's status chip should read "Queue: {N}   Wait Time: {M}m {S}s" instead of a subtitle/ID. Added `queueCount?: number` and `waitTimeSeconds?: number` to `CreateNewContact` (skill records only) plus a `formatQueueText()` helper; `ContactRow` now computes its secondary-line text as the formatted queue string when both fields are set, falling back to `subtitle` otherwise (agents/teams/customers, which don't set these fields, are unaffected). Both `OUTBOUND_SKILLS` copies set `queueCount`/`waitTimeSeconds` (Spanish Language: 4 / 200s → "3m 20s"; Technical Support: 7 / 95s → "1m 35s").
- **Third follow-up, from a screenshot showing the queue text truncating (`"Queue: 4 Wait Time: ..."`)**: on the chip's own row there wasn't enough width left for the full string. Fixed by giving skill rows a different, taller layout than agent/team rows: `ContactRow` now branches on `hasQueueText` — when true, the status chip and the queue/wait text stack in a `flex-col` (chip on top, text on its own line below); when false (agents/teams/customers), they stay on one `items-center` row as before, next to `subtitle`. Also brought `DashboardQueue`'s "cards" header label in line with this new "Wait Time" (both words capitalized) wording — it previously read "Wait time: {wait}" (lowercase "time"); now "Wait Time: {wait}", matching the capitalization convention below. ("Wait: {wait}" in `DashboardQueue`'s "accordion" variant is a single word and was already correctly capitalized — left as is.)
- **Fourth follow-up**: "I'm seeing the overflow-y and overflow-x flash when the CreateNew menu opens." Root cause: the `Popover` call's `maxHeight` prop (which activates `popover.tsx`'s `display:flex; overflow:hidden` height-clamping — see that file's own comments) was only ever passed for `"detail"` screens and the outbound flow's contacts-group screen. Every other screen — drill-down's root/category/channels, and the outbound flow's dialpad/favorites/empty groups — had no height ceiling at all, so a long enough list could grow the popover taller than Radix Popper had room for, and the excess could render past the viewport edge (or force page scroll) for a frame before Radix's positioning settled — visible as a flash of stray overflow on open. Separately, the drill-down category screen's search field lived inside `content` (scrolling with the list, in an ad hoc `max-h-80 overflow-y-auto` div) instead of in `header` (pinned outside the scroll area) the way the outbound flow's group/contacts search already correctly did — an inconsistency that was also *why* that screen never got a `maxHeight` in the first place (a fixed `max-h-80`/320px stood in for it instead, which doesn't shrink to fit whatever room is actually available near the bottom of a viewport, unlike `var(--radix-popper-available-height)`).
  - **Fix**: moved the category screen's search field into `popoverHeader` (mirroring the outbound flow's pattern exactly — see the new `isDrillDown && screen.kind === "category"` block there), reduced its `content` to just the plain contact list (dropped the ad hoc `max-h-80 overflow-y-auto`), and made `maxHeight="var(--radix-popper-available-height, 480px)"` unconditional on the `Popover` call instead of screen-dependent. Every screen is now uniformly height-clamped to whatever space Radix actually measured, so the popover can never grow past available viewport room in the first place — its own internal scroll region (already built into `popover.tsx`) takes over instead of the page ever needing to.
  - **Why agent-next-gen-v1 didn't show the flash while lyra-ui's own "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story did**: `Popover`'s content portals to `document.body` (Radix's default), so it was never actually being clipped by either app's own layout wrappers — ruling out an ancestor-`overflow-hidden` explanation. The real difference is viewport height: agent-next-gen-v1 runs in a normal full browser window tall enough to fit the unclamped content without ever spilling, while `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story renders `layout: "fullscreen"` with no Storybook canvas chrome — a shorter effective viewport where the same missing height-clamp actually spilled. Same underlying bug in both; only one had enough headroom to hide it. The fix above makes both consumers behave identically regardless of viewport size.

### CreateNew (`src/components/create-new.tsx`) / InteractionNavItem — "Add Outbound" button on each live interaction card
- **Request** (from a screenshot showing a "+" in an `InteractionNavItem` card's header, opening a Call/SMS/WhatsApp flyout): "add a '+' button to the header of each interactionNavItem card and when hovered add a tooltip that says 'Add Outbound' and when clicked display the list of channels available to the selected Agent/Customer - keep the same flow for launching the outbound as if it was clicked from the CreateNew button."
- **`InteractionNavItem` gained a generic `headerAction?: React.ReactNode` prop** (`interaction-nav-item.tsx`), rendered at the end of the expanded card's header row next to the customer name (not rendered in compact/icon-rail mode, which has no header row). Kept as a plain slot rather than a dedicated `onAddOutbound` prop specifically so this component has **no direct dependency on `create-new.tsx`'s outbound-picker types** — the consumer composes whatever trigger it needs, same reasoning as `Accordion`'s own `endSlot`.
- **New exported `OutboundAddButton` (`create-new.tsx`)** — a small standalone "+" icon button wrapped in `Tooltip` ("Add Outbound") + a click-triggered `Popover` listing `channelOptions` via `Menu`, calling `onSelect(channel)` and closing itself. Factored out rather than duplicated because it's *almost* `OutboundContactRow`'s existing per-row flyout, with one real difference: `OutboundContactRow`'s flyout opens on **hover** (sensible for a big clickable row); this one opens on **click** (sensible for a bare icon button) — so it's its own component sharing the same `Menu`-based flyout shape, not a copy-pasted fork. Not nested inside another `Popover` (`InteractionNavItem` cards live in the nav rail, not inside `CreateNew`'s own panel), so it uses the standard top-level overlay tier (`z-[9999]`), not `OutboundContactRow`'s nested `z-[10003]` — see CONTRIBUTING.md §5. Its trigger `<button>` stops click propagation (same pattern as `FavoriteButton`/`KebabMenuButton`) so picking a channel doesn't also select the whole card underneath it.
- **The harder half — "keep the same flow for launching the outbound as if it was clicked from the CreateNew button" — is `CreateNewOutboundConfig.launchRequest`/`onLaunchRequestHandled`**, a new deep-link pair: setting `launchRequest: { contactId, channel }` makes `CreateNew` look the contact up across every group's `contacts` (same lookup `allOutboundContacts`/"Favorites" already uses), reset its internal screen stack straight to `[{ kind: "group", groupId }, { kind: "detail", ...contactId, channel }]` (bypassing the group/contact-list screens entirely, unlike `goToDetail`'s normal *push*), seed the detail screen's fields exactly like a normal row click would (`defaultDetailValueFor`, etc.), and open the popover — landing the agent on the exact same "Select Channel / Select Phone / Outbound Skill → Start Interaction" screen a real `OutboundContactRow` click would, just entered from a different starting point. `onLaunchRequestHandled` exists because `CreateNew` can't clear its own prop — the consumer resets its `launchRequest` state back to `null` there so an unrelated re-render doesn't replay the same request.
- **Wired into all three "Agent Next Gen" consumers via one shared hook, not three hand-copied blocks** — see the `useOutboundAddButton` entry further down for why this changed from the original hand-mirrored approach and what actually forced the change.
- **Follow-up request, from a screenshot**: "add the '+' to the headers of all interactionNavItem components" — the "no button at all for cards with no backing contact record" scoping decision (previously documented here) was explicitly overturned. Every `InteractionNavItem` (including quick-dialed/redialed cards and, in `LeftNav.stories.tsx`, the three fixed demo cards with no `outboundContactsById` entry) now always renders `headerAction`; the per-card `addOutboundChannelOptions` falls back to the full unfiltered `OUTBOUND_CONFIG.channelOptions` when there's no matching `contactRecord` to filter against, rather than omitting the button. Selecting a channel from one of these still ends in a no-op inside `CreateNew`'s `launchRequest` effect (there's genuinely no contact to look up), but the button itself is now visually consistent across every card, matching the request.
- **Follow-up bug, reported directly, twice**: "when I click the add from the interactionNavItem header and the channel menu opens then I select a channel the next menu flashes open then closes quickly - it should stay open" — then, after an initial fix attempt, "it's still flashing open then closing." The first fix attempt (an `openedViaLaunchRequestRef` flag paired with deferring the `launchRequest` effect's state changes by one `setTimeout(0)` macrotask) treated the symptom as an auto-focus-*on-mount* problem and did not resolve it, because the real mechanism fires later, on **unmount**.
  - **Actual root cause** (found by reading `@radix-ui/react-focus-scope`'s source directly): every Radix `Popover.Content` mounts a `FocusScope`. On mount it records `previouslyFocusedElement = document.activeElement` (here, the "+" button itself, since `OutboundAddButton`'s own popover already cancels its mount auto-focus via `onOpenAutoFocus`). On unmount, regardless of `trapped`/modal settings, `FocusScope` unconditionally schedules `setTimeout(() => focus(previouslyFocusedElement ?? document.body), 0)` — restoring focus to whatever had it before the popover opened. `OutboundAddButton`'s `Popover.Content` doesn't unmount immediately on `setOpen(false)`; Radix's `Presence` keeps it mounted through its exit animation (`popover.tsx`'s `data-[state=closed]:animate-out duration-100`, ~100ms) before actually unmounting and running that cleanup. By the time the delayed `focus()` call fires, `CreateNew`'s own popover has already opened (via `launchRequest`) and mounted its own `DismissableLayer`, which keeps a **persistent, document-level `focusin` listener** for as long as it's mounted (not just a one-time mount-time check). The delayed `focus()` call fires a real `focusin` on the "+" button — an element outside `CreateNew`'s newly-mounted layer — which that layer's `useFocusOutside` hook reads as an outside focus and immediately dismisses on. That's the "flashes open then closes quickly": `CreateNew` opens, then ~100ms later (`OutboundAddButton`'s exit animation plus its own `setTimeout(0)` cleanup) gets involuntarily closed by a focus event indistinguishable, from `CreateNew`'s own layer's perspective, from a genuine outside click.
  - **Real fix**: add `onCloseAutoFocus={(e) => e.preventDefault()}` to `OutboundAddButton`'s own `Popover` (`create-new.tsx`). This suppresses exactly the delayed refocus-on-unmount behavior described above — `OutboundAddButton`'s flow always hands off to a different surface (`CreateNew`) on selection, so returning focus to the "+" button on close serves no purpose. One line, at the actual source of the problem, rather than working around Radix's mount-time auto-focus (which was never the culprit). The earlier `openedViaLaunchRequestRef`/deferred-`setTimeout` changes in the `launchRequest` effect and the main `Popover`'s `onOpenAutoFocus` were left in place (harmless, and they still correctly distinguish a `launchRequest`-driven open from a normal user click), but they were not what fixed this bug — **no headless browser was available in this sandbox to verify interactively (Playwright's Chromium can be downloaded but not launched, since `--with-deps`/`sudo` are blocked here), so this fix is verified by source-level reasoning plus `tsc`/Storybook/`vite build`, not a live click-through.**
- **New story added**: `InteractionNavItem.stories.tsx` gained `NavItemHeader` ("Header — Add Outbound Button"), composing the real `OutboundAddButton` (imported from `create-new.tsx`) into `headerAction` on a stack of expanded cards — the first story in this file to demonstrate the header/`OutboundAddButton` combination, since every prior story only exercised `InteractionNavItem` in isolation.
- **Drift incident, then `useOutboundAddButton` extraction**: the "wire it into all three consumers identically" step above originally meant three separate, hand-copied blocks of `outboundLaunchRequest` state + `outboundContactsById` lookup + `headerAction` JSX — one in each of `agent-next-gen-v1/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, and `LeftNav.stories.tsx`. That drifted almost immediately: when the "add the '+' to every card" follow-up landed, `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s copy of the surrounding *comment* kept describing the old "no button for cards without a contact record" behavior after the code itself had already been updated to always render the button — caught when the user asked why a specific story wasn't behaving like the other two ("how is this possible since they should all be based on [it]?"), which also surfaced a broader misunderstanding worth correcting here: these three files are **not** derived from one another — they're independently maintained, hand-mirrored copies of the same pattern, so nothing structurally prevents them from actually diverging in behavior, not just in comments.
  - **Fix**: extracted the shared logic into one exported hook, `useOutboundAddButton(outboundConfig)` (`create-new.tsx`, exported from `index.ts` alongside `OutboundAddButton`). It owns the `launchRequest`/`onLaunchRequestHandled` state pair and the `outboundConfig.groups`-keyed contact lookup internally, and exposes `getHeaderAction(interactionId)` — call it once per `InteractionNavItem` card and pass the result straight to `headerAction`; it looks up that interaction's contact, scopes the flyout to whatever channels it actually supports (or falls back to the full unfiltered list when there's no match), and wires `onSelect` into the same `launchRequest` state the hook already returns for `CreateNew`'s `outbound` prop. All three consumers now call this one hook instead of retyping the block — `onStartCall`/`onQuickDial`/`handleDismiss*` (the truly consumer-specific parts, since each app tracks its own `interactions` state differently) still live in each file, but the "+" button wiring itself can no longer drift, because there's only one implementation of it to drift from.
- **Follow-up, from screenshots of `InteractionNavItem.stories.tsx`'s `NavItemHeader` story and `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story side by side**: "the Header — Add Outbound Button does not load the secondary menu when a channel is selected - this is causing the placeholder cards you put in the Agent Next Gen Left Nav story to not display the secondary menu." Two related but distinct problems:
  1. **`NavItemHeader` never actually wired the second screen.** The version added just above (see the "New story added" entry) deliberately only logged the picked channel to the console instead of opening `CreateNew`'s real call-setup screen, on the theory that a standalone component story had "no outbound flow to hand off to." That made the second screen look broken when it simply didn't exist. **Fix**: the story now builds its own small, self-contained `CreateNewOutboundConfig` (two contacts, "Sofia Martinez"/"Ray Torres", matching the cards' own display names) and calls the real `useOutboundAddButton` hook against it — the same hook every production consumer uses — with an actual `<CreateNew>` rendered in the story (trigger button included, same as any real consumer) so there's somewhere for the launch-requested popover to mount. Picking a channel now opens the real second screen. The third card intentionally keeps no matching contact, to keep demonstrating the "no backing contact → fall back to the unfiltered channel list" case the hook also handles.
