  2. **The 3 fixed "Sofia Martinez"/"Ray Torres"/"Customer" placeholder cards in `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story were never real contacts.** They were seeded directly into `interactions` state on mount with ids `"sofia"`/`"ray"`/`"call"` that don't exist anywhere in `OUTBOUND_CONFIG`'s groups — so `useOutboundAddButton`'s `getHeaderAction` could never find a matching contact for them, and any channel picked from their "+" button was always going to be a no-op inside `CreateNew`'s `launchRequest` effect (silently, since there's genuinely no contact to look up). This is exactly the same root confusion as (1): a demo card that looks real but has no real contact behind it, so its own "+" button can never fully work. **Fix**: this story's `interactions` state now starts as `[]`, matching how `agent-next-gen-v1` and `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` already behaved (neither ever seeded fixed demo cards) — cards only appear once the agent actually starts one via `CreateNew`'s "New Outbound" trigger (`handleStartCall`) or a quick dial, at which point the card's id is a real contact id and its "+" button works end-to-end like every other card. The now-unused fixture data (`AGENT_NEXT_GEN_SOFIA_CHANNELS`/`_RAY_CHANNELS`/`_CALL_CHANNELS`, `INITIAL_AGENT_NEXT_GEN_INTERACTIONS`, `SKILL_NAMES`/`randomSkill`) was deleted rather than left dead in the file.
- **Follow-up, from a screenshot**: `NavItemHeader`'s own `<CreateNew>` trigger was rendering as its default collapsed, icon-only square button, even though every `InteractionNavItem` card in that same story renders in expanded mode — a visible mismatch, since `CreateNew`'s `expanded` prop (`create-new.tsx`) defaults to `false` and the story never set it. `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`/`agent-next-gen-v1`/`LeftNav.stories.tsx` all already tie it to their own nav rail's real open/collapsed state (`expanded={navOpen}`/`expanded={open}`), so this was specific to the one story that added `<CreateNew>` without an equivalent rail to read the state from. **Fix**: pass `expanded` (unconditionally true, since this story has no collapsible rail of its own — every card in it is always shown expanded) on `NavItemHeader`'s `<CreateNew>` call, so its trigger renders as the full-width labeled button matching the rest of the story instead of the collapsed icon square.

### InteractionNavItem — compact mode gets a hover popover previewing the expanded card
- **Request** (from a screenshot of the live app: hovering the collapsed rail's "JT" avatar tile showed a floating card with the full "Jamie Torres" detail — name, "+", the open SMS chip): "when the interactionNavItem compact is hovered, display a popover with the expanded chip that a user can interact with."
- **Refactor first**: the compact and expanded branches previously had zero shared render logic — the expanded branch inlined its own header row + `channels.map(...)` loop directly in the `return`. Pulled that into two local values computed once per render, above both branches: `cardBody` (the header row + channel list JSX) and `expandedCardClassName` (the outer card's border/shadow/background classes, which vary with `active`/`awaitingResponse` exactly as before). The real expanded return is now just `<div className={cn(expandedCardClassName, className)}>{cardBody}</div>` — same output as before, just no longer a copy any new compact-mode usage would have to keep in sync by hand.
- **The compact branch** now wraps its existing tile (unchanged markup) as the trigger of a `Popover` (`popover.tsx`), whose `content` is `expandedCardClassName` + `cardBody` again — i.e. the literal same expanded-card markup, previewed in a floating panel instead of inline. Hover-intent state (`hoverCardOpen`, `openHoverCard`, `scheduleCloseHoverCard`) mirrors `OutboundContactRow`'s existing flyout in create-new.tsx exactly: open immediately on `onMouseEnter`, close on a 150ms-delayed `setTimeout` (not instantly) on `onMouseLeave`, with the delay's timer cleared if the pointer re-enters (either the tile or the popover's own content re-arms it) — so there's time to move the pointer from the tile into the preview to actually read or click something in it. `onOpenAutoFocus={(e) => e.preventDefault()}` since a hover shouldn't steal focus the way a real click-driven open should. The popover's own default panel chrome (rounded-lg border/bg/shadow) is stripped to a bare frame (`border-0 bg-transparent p-0 shadow-none`) since the inner preview already supplies its own complete chrome via `expandedCardClassName` — same "let the real content supply its own chrome, don't double it with the Popover's" convention `OutboundAddButton`'s Menu-in-Popover already uses.
- **This makes `headerAction` reachable from compact mode for the first time** — previously true only in the expanded card, since `cardBody` (which renders `headerAction`) is now shared by both. `InteractionNavItemProps.headerAction`'s own doc comment (previously "Not rendered in compact (icon-rail) mode, which has no header row to put it in") was corrected to describe this.
- **z-index fix this exposed**: `OutboundAddButton` (the most common real `headerAction`) opens its own `Popover` for its channel flyout, previously at the top-level `z-[9999]` tier — correct as long as it was only ever used inside a plain, non-popover expanded card (its only context before this change). Now that it can also render inside `InteractionNavItem`'s own new hover popover, it's sometimes a **popover nested inside another popover** — the tier CONTRIBUTING.md §5 reserves `z-[10003]` for (same tier `OutboundContactRow`'s flyout already uses, for the same reason). A fixed z-index can't detect which context it's in at render time, and nothing else in this codebase needs to sit between the top-level and nested tiers, so `OutboundAddButton`'s Popover now unconditionally uses `z-[10003]` — correct in the (new) nested case, harmlessly conservative in the (existing, still far more common) top-level case.
  - **Why this doesn't reopen the "flash open then closes" bug**: clicking a channel inside `OutboundAddButton`'s flyout, or inside a channel row's kebab menu, happens inside content that's a *React* descendant of the hover popover's own `Popover.Content` (passed via `content`/`headerAction` props), even though each is *DOM*-portaled elsewhere. Radix's outside-click/focus detection keys off React-tree bubbling specifically to handle exactly this "portaled but still a logical descendant" case (see `popover.tsx`'s own comment on this, and CONTRIBUTING.md §17) — so those clicks correctly register as "inside" the hover popover's layer, not as an outside interaction that would dismiss it.
  - **Verification caveat**: same as every other fix in this session that touches Radix's focus/dismiss internals — no headless browser is available in this sandbox (Playwright's Chromium can be downloaded but not launched here), so this is verified by source-level reasoning about Radix's actual implementation plus `tsc`/Storybook/`vite build`, not a live hover-and-click-through.
- **New story**: `InteractionNavItem.stories.tsx` gained "Compact — Hover Popover", reusing the same `NAV_ITEM_HEADER_OUTBOUND_CONFIG`/`useOutboundAddButton` wiring as "Header — Add Outbound Button," but with `expanded={false}` cards, to exercise the full nested scenario (hover popover → `headerAction`'s own flyout → `CreateNew`'s call-setup screen) end to end in one place.

### Dashboards (`src/components/__stories__/Dashboards.stories.tsx`) — new Templates story wrapping `AgentDashboard`
- New story under `Templates/Dashboards` — a `PageHeader` ("Desk") + `TabList` (Dashboard/Customers/Accounts/Tickets/Tasks, only "Dashboard" wired — matches agent-next-gen-v1's own Desk page, where the other four are decorative tab highlights with no distinct content behind them yet either) + `AgentDashboard` in the scrollable body + a right-side interior `Panel` rendering `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown` when a queue widget is selected. See the `AgentDashboard` entry above (Key Components) for why the underlying content is a real shared component rather than a story-only reference.
- **Placement required an explicit `storySort` order**: plain alphabetical sort (the existing `method: "alphabetical"` in `.storybook/preview.ts`) would put "Dashboards" *before* "Data Management" ("Das" < "Dat" lexicographically) — the opposite of the requested position (directly below Data Management). Added an explicit nested order for the `Templates` group (`["Admin UIs", "Agent Next Gen", "Data Management", "Dashboards"]`) to `preview.ts`'s `storySort.order` so it lands in the right spot; any future `Templates/*` story not listed there still falls back to alphabetical, same as before.

### lyra-ux-templates — Agent Next Gen page brought up to parity with the other two consumers
- **Request**: "update the lyra-ux-template for the Agent Next Gen page to have the left nav and home screen as the agent-next-gen-v1... use this template for the lyra-ux-templates Agent Next Gen page." Previously `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx` was the least complete of the three "Agent Next Gen" consumers — its `LeftNav` header rendered only a static `CreateNew` trigger with no `InteractionNavItem` cards at all (starting a call from `CreateNew` had nowhere to surface), and its main content area was an empty `<div className="flex-1" />` placeholder.
- **Left nav**: brought in the same interaction-tracking wiring `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` (this repo's own template) already establishes — `TrackedChannel`/`ActiveInteraction` types, `handleStartCall`/`handleQuickDial`/`handleDismissInteraction`/`handleDismissChannel`, the `outboundConfig` `useMemo` (open-address filtering), and `useOutboundAddButton` for each card's "+" flyout — so starting or quick-dialing a call now surfaces a real `InteractionNavItem` card, identically to the other two consumers, instead of doing nothing.
- **Home screen**: the empty placeholder now renders `<AgentDashboard>` (see above), with `selectedQueueId` wired to the same right-side interior `Panel` slot the page already had for "Case Details" — reusing that one slot for both, the same dual-purpose pattern agent-next-gen-v1 uses, via `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown`.
- Verified with `vite build` (this repo's `tsc`, like agent-next-gen-v1's, can't resolve the `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` alias on its own since it's a bundler-only Vite alias, not a `tsconfig.paths` entry — `vite build` is the meaningful check here, and it exercises the same resolution the dev server uses).

### ChannelTab (`src/components/channel-row.tsx`) + Tab's new `menuItems` kebab slot + InteractionNavItem's controllable current-channel — per-channel tabs in the record-header area, synced with the card
- **Request, from a screenshot** of the "Jamie Torres" record page (two SMS channels open, the InteractionNavItem card showing both rows): "when channels are added, add corresponding tabs to the active conversation area (below the customer name in the page header) — clicking between tabs should update the card's active channel." Read literally off the request's own template ("{SMS icon} SMS | {phone number} kebab") for what each tab shows.
- **New shared component, `ChannelTab`** (`channel-row.tsx`, exported from `index.ts`): one open channel rendered as a `Tab` — icon + type label (from a new exported `CHANNEL_TYPE_META` per-type icon/label lookup, `h-4 w-4`-sized versions of the same choices each `*ChannelRow` already bakes in) + optional "| address" (only when an address is on hand — a redialed voice call with no stored number just shows icon + label) + a kebab reusing the exact same per-type default menu (`buildDigitalMenuItems`/`buildVoiceMenuItems`, now exported) `ChannelRow`'s own rows already use, wired to `onDismiss` the same way. Not a full "tab list" wrapper — the consumer still renders its own `<TabList>` around one `ChannelTab` per channel.
- **New `Tab` prop, `menuItems`** (`tabs.tsx`) — the actual trailing-kebab mechanism `ChannelTab` composes. `Tab` is a real `<button>`, and HTML forbids nesting a real `<button>` inside one, so this couldn't just drop `KebabMenuButton` in directly (same problem `InteractionNavItem`'s own `role="button"` outer `<div>` and `Tab`'s pre-existing `onRemove` span already solved). Fixed at the root: `KebabMenuButton` gained an `as?: "button" | "span"` prop (default `"button"`, unchanged for every existing caller) that renders its trigger as a `role="button" tabIndex={0}` `<span>` instead — same portal/positioning/keyboard/outside-click behavior either way. `Tab`'s `menuItems` slot uses `as="span"`, and takes priority over `onRemove`/`rightIcon` (a tab needs at most one trailing action). Demonstrated on its own (not just via `ChannelTab`) in `Tabs.stories.tsx`'s new `WithMenuItems` ("With Kebab Menu (functional dropdown)") story — a genuine working dropdown, unlike the pre-existing `WithRightIcon` story's purely decorative `MoreVertical` icon.
- **`InteractionNavItem` becomes controllable for which channel is "current"** — previously entirely internal state (`manualCurrentKey`), invisible to any consumer. Added `currentChannelKey?: string` (wins over the card's own computation whenever passed) and `onCurrentChannelChange?: (key: string) => void` (fired from both write sites — a row click, and the existing "a newly-opened channel always takes over as current" auto-select effect) — a controlled-with-uncontrolled-fallback pattern: omit both and every existing consumer/story keeps working exactly as before; pass both and a parent-owned piece of state (e.g. `ActiveInteraction.currentChannelId`) becomes the single source of truth for both the card and a synced `ChannelTab` bar elsewhere on the page.
- **Cascaded** to every consumer that already tracks `ActiveInteraction`/`TrackedChannel` (agent-next-gen-v1, lyra-ux-templates, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` — *not* `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story, which has no record-header/`PageHeader` content to put a tab bar under, same scoping precedent as the earlier Desk-active-state fix): each gained `ActiveInteraction.currentChannelId` (set to the new/refreshed channel's id by `handleStartCall`/`handleQuickDial`/(`handleRedial` in agent-next-gen-v1)/re-pointed to the new last channel by `handleDismissChannel` if the dismissed one was current) and a `handleChannelSelect(interactionId, channelKey)` setter, wired as `currentChannelKey`/`onCurrentChannelChange` on the card *and* as each `ChannelTab`'s `active`/`onClick` in the `activeInteraction` block's new `<TabList>` (rendered under the record-header `PageHeader`).
- **Follow-up, from a screenshot**: the bar was initially gated on `channels.length > 1` (reasoning: a single channel has nothing to switch *to*). Corrected on request — "the first tab should be visible if only one channel is open" — to `channels.length > 0`, since the tab isn't just a switcher; it's also the only place that channel's kebab (Unassign & Dismiss/Consult/Transfer/etc.) is reachable from this page. The dismiss-decision logic inside each tab's `onDismiss` (whole-card vs. single-channel, still keyed on `channels.length > 1`) was intentionally left as-is — that check is about which handler to call, not whether the bar renders. `handleDismissChannel`'s parameter type loosened from the full `InteractionChannel` to `Pick<InteractionChannel, "id" | "type">` in all three, since a `ChannelTab`'s dismiss only has the raw `TrackedChannel` on hand, not a fully-formed `InteractionChannel`.
- **Address label sourcing**: each `TrackedChannel` gained `addressLabel?: string` — the human-readable version of `value` (e.g. "(456) 383-3329" vs. `value`'s raw "+14563833329") shown on the tab, kept separate from `value` since that field has to stay raw for the existing `openChannelAddresses` dedup match. `handleStartCall` looks it up from `OUTBOUND_CONFIG.phoneOptions` (same pattern as the pre-existing `skillLabel` lookup); `handleQuickDial` just uses the dialed number itself (no options list to look it up in); `handleRedial` (agent-next-gen-v1 only) leaves it undefined — `ContactHistoryEntry` has no stored phone number, so that tab shows icon + "Voice" with no address, same as any other channel with nothing to show.
- New stories: `ChannelRow.stories.tsx` gained `ChannelTabBar` (two clickable SMS tabs with different addresses, matching the reference screenshot) and `ChannelTabNoAddress` (a single Voice tab with no address).
- Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (both consuming apps).
- **Follow-up request**: "show the email address and whatsapp handle in the tabs - add a tooltip to the interaction tabs that display the channel and the number/address/handle and hide the number/address/handle in the tabs when the container drops below the first container query point, if the container goes to the smallest container query point just show the icons in the tabs." Three parts:
  1. **Email/WhatsApp addresses weren't reaching the tab at all** — root cause, not a `ChannelTab` bug: `handleStartCall`'s `addressLabel` lookup (`OUTBOUND_CONFIG.phoneOptions.find(...)?.label`) only ever matches phone numbers. For email/WhatsApp, `create-new.tsx`'s `defaultDetailValueFor` already derives a synthesized address (`name.toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g,".")+"@example.com"`, `"@"+name`) where `value` *is* the display string (no separate raw/formatted pair like phone has) — so the phone-only lookup just silently produced `undefined` for those two types. Fixed with a fallback: `?? selection.phone` — correct for email/WhatsApp (their `value` is already human-readable) and a no-op for phone (which always resolves through the lookup instead). Fixed in all three copies (`agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`).
  2. **Tooltip**: `ChannelTab` now wraps its `Tab` in a `Tooltip` (`placement="bottom"`) showing `"{label} | {address}"` (or just `{label}` with no address) — the same text the tab shows visibly, so it stays available once the container-query stages below hide part or all of it.
  3. **Responsive collapse**: new two-stage CSS container query, `.lyra-channel-tab-list-wrap` / `.lyra-channel-tab-label` / `.lyra-channel-tab-address` (added to both `lyra-tokens.css` and `storybook.css`, see the "Channel tab bar" section there for the full writeup) — same `container-type: inline-size` pattern as `.lyra-container-grid-wrap`/`.lyra-metric-row-wrap`. Stage 1 (`≤480px`) hides the "| address" span, keeping icon + type label; Stage 2 (`≤320px`, narrower so both queries fire together) additionally hides the label span, leaving icon (+ kebab) only. The wrap class goes on the consumer's `<TabList>` (added at all three `ChannelTab`-bar call sites), not inside `ChannelTab` itself, matching the existing wrap-on-ancestor/query-on-descendant convention.
  - New `ChannelRow.stories.tsx` story, `ChannelTabResponsive` — a `resize-x` dashed box around a `lyra-channel-tab-list-wrap`'d `TabList`, so the collapse can be seen live by dragging its edge, rather than only inferred from the CSS. (No such demo existed for the container-query behavior itself when it first shipped — added retroactively alongside this follow-up.)
  - Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (both consuming apps).
- **Follow-up request**, from a screenshot of a "16 Messages  Private | #707535188548" chip: "add the number of messages and interaction ID to the tooltips below the channel and address/number/handle." (The "Private" segment in the screenshot wasn't part of the ask — only the message count and the id.) `ChannelTab` gained two new optional props, `messageCount?: number` and `interactionId?: string`, rendered as a second `Tooltip` line below the existing "Label | address" line (e.g. "16 Messages | #707535188548"), omitted entirely when neither is on hand rather than leaving a half-blank line. Neither ever appears on the tab face itself — tooltip-only, same reasoning as the address text once the bar has collapsed past Stage 1.
  - **Data**: there's no real message store or per-channel session id in this demo, so both are synthesized once at channel-creation time (`handleStartCall`/`handleQuickDial`/`handleRedial` in all three consumer files), via a new helper alongside the existing `generateCaseId`: `generateInteractionId()` (a 12-digit numeric string, matching the reference screenshot's `#707535188548` shape — deliberately *not* `generateCaseId`'s `"CS-"` shape, since that's the customer/case-level id shown in the page header, a different concept from a per-channel conversation id). `TrackedChannel` gained matching `messageCount?`/`interactionId?` fields in all three files, passed straight through to each `<ChannelTab>`.
  - Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (both consuming apps).
- **Follow-up request**, from a screenshot of a WhatsApp tab + its Tooltip ("WhatsApp | @Jamie Torres" / "29 Messages | #294889396221"): "Remove the divider lines between the channel name and the address/number/etc. for both the tab and the tooltip - make the font-sm for the # of messages and interactions #. The # of messages should be 0 for new outbounds and there should be no message field in the tooltip for voice calls." Four distinct changes:
  1. **Dividers removed** between the type label and the address, on both the tab face (`{meta.label} {address}` — no more `| {address}`) and the Tooltip's first line (`${meta.label} ${address}`, no `|`). The `|` between `messageCount` and `interactionId` on the Tooltip's *second* line was intentionally left alone — that's two distinct facts side by side, not a label-and-its-own-value pair like the first line, and the request specifically scoped the removal to "between the channel name and the address."
  2. **Smaller font for the second line**: added `lyra-body-sm` to the `messageCount`/`interactionId` line's `<span>` — one step below the Tooltip's own default `lyra-body-md`, marking it as secondary/reference info rather than the tab's primary identity.
  3. **`messageCount` is `0`, not random, for a freshly started outbound conversation**: the earlier `generateMessageCount()` helper (`1`–`40` at random) is gone from all three consumer files — a just-opened conversation hasn't exchanged any messages, so `handleStartCall` now sets `messageCount: 0` directly for any non-voice channel (`0` renders "0 Messages", not a blank segment).
  4. **No message field in the tooltip for voice**: voice has no message concept at all, so `messageCount` is left `undefined` for every voice channel — `handleStartCall`'s voice branch, and `handleQuickDial`/`handleRedial` (which only ever create voice channels) all omit the field entirely. `ChannelTab`'s existing `metaLine` logic already dropped the message segment cleanly whenever `messageCount === undefined` (added in the prior follow-up), so this needed no `channel-row.tsx` change — just correct data going in.
  - `ChannelRow.stories.tsx` gained `ChannelTabNewOutbound` (0 messages) and updated `ChannelTabNoAddress` to also demonstrate the no-address-and-no-message-field voice case together.
  - Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (both consuming apps).

### agent-next-gen-v1-only: Settings moved from AppHeader into the LeftNav rail, Help restored to the AppHeader
- **Request, scoped explicitly to agent-next-gen-v1** ("in agent-next-gen-v1"): "Add settings leftNav item below Desk and remove it from the top right in the appHeader, then move Help '?' back to the left of the notifications icon button in the top right of the appHeader." No lyra-ui component changed — this was purely a composition/data fix inside `agent-next-gen-v1/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, using components that already existed (`NavItem`, `ActionIconButton`, lucide's `CircleHelp`).
- **Settings**: removed the `<ActionIconButton title="Settings">` from `AppHeader`'s `actions`; added a plain `{ icon: <Settings .../>, label: "Settings" }` entry to `buildNavItems`'s returned array, directly below the existing "Desk" item. This matches the standing convention already used by `lyra-ux-templates`' own `buildNavItems` and the lyra-ui template story's `NAV_ITEMS` — both already end their LeftNav rail with a Settings item rather than a standalone header icon; agent-next-gen-v1 was the one outlier still carrying it in the header.
- **Help**: agent-next-gen-v1's `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` never actually had a Help icon (confirmed via `git log -p`) — but every other `AppHeader` usage in this same codebase that has one (`agent-next-gen-v1/src/components/Header.tsx`, used by the separate Agent Workspace Premium/Outbound Engagement/Login pages; every `AppHeader.stories.tsx` story with a Help icon) places it as an `ActionIconButton` with lucide's `CircleHelp`, `size="xl"`, immediately before `NotificationsBell`. Added it there for consistency, rather than trying to locate a "previous" Help icon that never existed on this specific page.
- Verified with `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1 only — no other repo touched).
- **Immediate follow-up request**: "remove Help from the status menu since we moved it to the top right" — from a screenshot of `AgentProfile`'s own status/profile menu (Connected Apps / Agent Leg Disconnected / **Help** / Log Out), which still had a "Help" row even after agent-next-gen-v1 gained its own standalone Help icon in the `AppHeader` above. This one *did* require a `lyra-ui` core change: `AgentProfile`'s `onHelpClick` prop already had a doc comment reading "Shows a 'Help' row ... when provided," but the actual `items` array added that row unconditionally regardless of whether `onHelpClick` was passed — a pre-existing bug where the code didn't match its own documented contract. Fixed by wrapping the row in the same `...(condition ? [...] : [])` conditional-spread pattern this same `items` array already uses for its Favorites/no-results sections, gated on `onHelpClick` being truthy. Net effect: the row now actually disappears wherever `onHelpClick` isn't passed — which is every consumer app today (agent-next-gen-v1, lyra-ux-templates) — while remaining available to any consumer that does wire it up (only `AgentProfile.stories.tsx` currently does, to demonstrate the row exists). Not scoped to agent-next-gen-v1 alone since the bug lived in the shared component, not the app.
- Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (all three consuming apps/templates).

### DashboardCard gains a hover lift
- **Request, explicitly "in lyra-ui"**: "add a hover state to Dashboard cards." Added `hover:shadow-md` (with `transition-shadow`) to `DashboardCard`'s own root className — one step up the same elevation scale the card already sits on (`default`'s `shadow-sm`, from `container.tsx`'s `containerVariants`), the identical "elevate on hover" idiom `InteractionNavItem`'s expanded card already uses for the same reason.
- Applies regardless of whether a given card happens to be clickable — none of agent-next-gen-v1's current `DashboardCard` usages (Productivity, Contact History, the metric-row widgets) pass an `onClick` today, and gating the hover behind one would have made this change invisible everywhere it was just requested. A plain "this is a distinct card" hover affordance is common for read-only dashboard widgets too, not just interactive ones.
- Skipped when `showContainer` is `false` — a card with no surface/border/shadow of its own (`border-0 bg-transparent shadow-none`) shouldn't grow a shadow only on hover; that would look like the hover state contradicts the card's own "no chrome" configuration.
- Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1, lyra-ux-templates).

### agent-next-gen-v1-only: Designer side panel had no reachable trigger — wired one to the interaction record icon
- **Request**: "re-add the left Side panel to the agent-next-gen-v1," refined across follow-ups to "when in an interaction" / "not to the dashboard." Investigation (there's no dev server reachable from this sandbox to the person's actual browser, so this was traced statically) found the "Designer" `Panel` (`variant="side" side="left"`, both the pinned-inline and unpinned-overlay render branches) was never actually unreachable *specifically* in the interaction view — it's a shared sibling outside the `activeInteraction` ternary, rendered identically either way. The real bug: `sidePanelOpen`/`sidePanelPinned` both default `false`, and `Panel`'s own unpinned/closed render is `width: 0` with `pointer-events-none` — no visible sliver or handle to hover or click. Nothing anywhere in the page ever called `setSidePanelOpen`/`setSidePanelPinned` except the pin button *inside* the panel itself (unreachable while the panel is invisible) — a dead end regardless of which view was showing.
- Asked the user how they wanted it triggered (auto-pinned, a header toggle button, or a hover-reveal edge handle) since nothing in the code hinted at an intended mechanism; answer: "display on hover of the user icon next to the customer name."
- Fixed by wrapping the interaction `PageHeader`'s `icon` prop (the `<User>` record icon) in a `<span>` with `onMouseEnter={onSidePanelHoverStart}`/`onMouseLeave={... onSidePanelHoverEnd}` — the exact same hover-start/hover-end handler pair the unpinned `Panel` already accepted (`onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave`) for exactly this purpose, just never wired to any trigger before now. Scoped entirely to the interaction `PageHeader` (which only renders when `activeInteraction` is set) — the Desk dashboard's own `PageHeader` (no `icon` prop) is untouched, matching "not to the dashboard."
- `lyra-ui`'s `PageHeader` already ships a purpose-built `panelToggle="left"` prop (a separate toggle button, with its own hover/click wiring) for this exact job — not used here because the person specifically wanted the hover trigger on the existing record icon itself, not a new adjacent button.
- Verified with `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1 only — no other repo touched).
- **Immediate follow-up request**: "allow the Designer panel to be pinned and close when the user icon is toggled." The hover-reveal above only ever opened the panel (and its own internal pin button, `handleSidePanelPinToggle`, always leaves it open — it just switches between inline-pinned and overlay modes). Added a new `handleSidePanelIconToggle` handler, distinct from that pin button: reads the current `sidePanelPinned` state and sets both `sidePanelPinned` and `sidePanelOpen` to its inverse in one shot, so the first click pins the panel open and the next click both unpins *and* closes it — a real on/off switch, not just a pin/unpin toggle. Wired to the same record-icon `<span>` via `onClick`/`onKeyDown` (Enter/Space), plus `role="button"`, `tabIndex={0}`, `aria-pressed={sidePanelPinned}`, and an `aria-label` that reflects the pending action ("Pin"/"Unpin Designer panel"), alongside the existing hover handlers from the previous fix.
- Verified with `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1 only — no other repo touched).
- **Immediate follow-up request**: "when the designer panel is pinned, the user icon should animate it closed and open like the side panel component in lyra-ui - in fact, don't reproduce this component locally, use the existing side-panel component and just change the icon to the user icon." The previous fix's hand-rolled `<span role="button" onClick=... onKeyDown=...>` was a parallel reimplementation of a control `Panel` already ships internally — its own pin/unpin button (Tooltip + button + a `Pin` icon that rotates 45° when pinned), rendered in `PanelHeader` whenever `onPinToggle` is passed. Fixed at the root, per CONTRIBUTING.md's "Composition over reimplementation" rule, by extracting that button into its own shared atom:
  - **New `PanelPinButton`** (`panel-pin-button.tsx`, exported from `index.ts`) — the exact same Tooltip/button/focus-ring/rotate-45°-on-pin JSX that used to live inline in `panel.tsx`, now taking `pinned`, `onToggle`, and an optional `icon` (defaults to `Pin`) plus `pinnedLabel`/`unpinnedLabel` for the Tooltip/aria-label text. `panel.tsx`'s own `pinButton` now just renders `<PanelPinButton pinned={pinned} onToggle={onPinToggle} />` — behavior is unchanged, just de-duplicated. New story, `PanelPinButton.stories.tsx` (`Default` + `CustomIcon`, the latter demonstrating exactly the `User`-icon case below).
  - **agent-next-gen-v1's Designer-panel trigger now renders that same `PanelPinButton`** (`icon={<User .../>}`, `pinnedLabel`/`unpinnedLabel` set to "(Un)pin Designer panel") instead of the hand-rolled span — same click/keyboard/aria/animation behavior as `Panel`'s own pin button, just relocated to the interaction record header. The hover-preview behavior from the prior fix is preserved by wrapping the button in a plain (non-interactive) `<span onMouseEnter=.../onMouseLeave=...>` — the hover zone and the click target are now cleanly separated instead of one element doing both jobs manually.
  - **`PageHeader` gained `iconAriaHidden?: boolean`** (default `true`, matching prior behavior for every existing consumer) — its `icon` slot wrapper was unconditionally `aria-hidden="true"`, correct for a purely decorative icon but wrong now that the slot can hold a real, labeled `PanelPinButton`: `aria-hidden` on an ancestor hides the whole subtree from assistive tech regardless of the button's own `aria-label`. agent-next-gen-v1 passes `iconAriaHidden={false}` alongside the new icon.
  - **Bugfix surfaced along the way**: `onSidePanelHoverEnd`'s 300ms auto-close timer fired unconditionally, including while `sidePanelPinned` was `true` — so pinning the panel via a click, then moving the mouse off the icon, would silently un-render it a moment later, defeating the pin. Guarded with an early return when `sidePanelPinned` is true; hover-driven closing is now scoped to the unpinned/preview case only, matching how `Panel` itself never listens for hover once `effectivePinned` is true.
- Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1). Confirmed `lyra-ux-templates` and `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` never adopted the record-icon trigger in the first place (they only have `Panel`'s own generic `handleSidePanelPinToggle`), so neither needed a mirrored change.
- **Immediate follow-up request**: "when the panel is pinned then closed it should require a click to open again, also, don't tilt the user icon that looks stupid, just add a selected state when it's open." Two distinct fixes:
  1. **`PanelPinButton`'s rotate-45°-on-pin animation is now `Pin`-only.** It's a pin-specific metaphor (rotating to look like it's "stuck in") that doesn't read correctly on an arbitrary glyph — tilting a `User` icon 45° just looks broken. When a custom `icon` is passed, the button instead gets a selected/pressed background (`bg-lyra-bg-active-moderate text-lyra-fg-active-strong`) while `pinned`, the same "currently active" idiom `LeftNav`'s own active nav item already uses — communicating "this is currently open" without borrowing an animation that only makes sense for the default `Pin` glyph. `panel.tsx`'s own header button (still the default, no custom icon) is unaffected — it keeps the original rotate.
  2. **agent-next-gen-v1: closing the Designer panel via the icon now requires an explicit click to reopen — hover no longer does it.** Added `sidePanelHoverEnabled` (default `true`, so the very first hover-to-preview interaction from the earlier fix still works). `handleSidePanelIconToggle` sets it to `false` the moment a click results in unpinning/closing; `onSidePanelHoverStart` early-returns while it's `false`. Rationale: once the icon has been used to deliberately pin-then-close the panel, that's an explicit "I'm done with this" decision — hovering back over the icon while moving the mouse elsewhere shouldn't silently override it. Once disabled it stays disabled for the rest of the session (only a click reopens/re-pins from then on); there was no request to ever re-enable hover, so no additional state machine was added for that.
- Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1).
- **Immediate follow-up question**: "why isn't it animating open and closed when it's pinned? it should be the same component as Side Panel story in the panel component." Root cause, found by comparing against `Panel.stories.tsx`'s `Side` story: that story renders exactly **one** `<Panel>` element and toggles `pinned`/`open` as props on it — React keeps the same component instance (and the same underlying `<div>`, since both of `Panel`'s side-variant branches return a plain `<div style={{ width, transition }}>`), so the `width` CSS transition animates smoothly across every prop change. agent-next-gen-v1, by contrast, rendered **two** separately-gated `<Panel>` elements at two different points in the JSX tree — `{effectivePinned && <Panel pinned .../>}` as a flex sibling before the content column, and `{!effectivePinned && <Panel pinned={false} .../>}` after it. Flipping `effectivePinned` (which happens on *every* click, pinned or not, via `handleSidePanelIconToggle`/`handleSidePanelPinToggle`) unmounted whichever one was showing and mounted the other from scratch — a fresh mount has no prior width to transition *from*, so the panel just snapped straight to its resting width/0 instead of animating, on every single toggle.
  - **Fix**: merged the two into a single `<Panel pinned={effectivePinned} .../>` (positioned once, where the old "pinned" branch used to sit — first child of the same `relative`-positioned `Container`, so `Panel`'s internal `absolute` overlay branch still overlays correctly when unpinned). `onPinToggle`/`onMouseEnter`/`onMouseLeave`/`onResizeStateChange` — previously split across the two branches with slightly different props each — are now attached unconditionally on the one element; this is safe because the pinned-only no-ops were already guarded inside the handlers themselves (`onSidePanelHoverEnd` already returns early while `sidePanelPinned`), not because the branch omitted the prop.
  - Same root cause, so same fix, also silently restores the animation for the pin/unpin toggle that lives *inside* the panel's own header (`Panel`'s internal `PanelPinButton`, via `handleSidePanelPinToggle`) — that path also used to force a remount every time, even though nothing about this session's other fixes had touched it directly.
- Verified with `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1 only — `panel.tsx` itself is unchanged, this was purely a consumer-side JSX structure fix).
- **Immediate follow-up, from a screenshot** of the Designer panel sitting open as a full-height overlay in a narrow layout: "when it goes responsive it should close and only open on hover - now it doesn't close at all." The panel-unification fix above was necessary but not sufficient — merging the two `<Panel>` elements fixed the *animation*, but nothing had ever force-closed the panel on the width-based narrow transition itself; `effectivePinned` only re-skins it (inline → overlay), it doesn't touch `sidePanelOpen`, so a panel left pinned+open at a wide width stayed open (just visually reflowed to an overlay) the instant `isNarrowContainer` flipped `true`. Fixed with a new `useEffect` keyed on `isNarrowContainer`, mirroring the existing `isNavNarrow` auto-collapse effects a few lines above it: entering narrow now resets `sidePanelOpen` and `sidePanelPinned` to `false` and `sidePanelHoverEnabled` back to `true`. That last reset matters on its own — narrow mode's *only* way to open the panel is hover (there's no pin button once `effectivePinned` is forced `false`), so if a prior wide-mode pin-then-close had already latched `sidePanelHoverEnabled` to `false` (see the "click to reopen" fix above), narrowing the window would otherwise leave the panel with no reachable trigger at all.
- Verified with `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1 only).
- **Immediate follow-up, from a screenshot** of the Desk dashboard (Dashboard/Customers/Accounts/Tickets/Interactions/WEM tabs, "Good evening, John") with the Designer panel still pinned open on the left: "also it should be native to the customer it's on since the user icon is opening the panel - right now it's displaying on the Desk page - this isn't correct." The panel's only trigger — the record icon — lives on the interaction `PageHeader`, which only renders inside `activeInteraction`; but the `<Panel>` itself was gated solely on `showPanelToggle`, a prop that's `true` for the whole page regardless of which tab/view is showing. So pinning it open on one customer, then clicking "Desk" in the left nav (clearing `activeInteraction`, not dismissing the interaction itself — its card stayed in the nav), left the panel mounted and pinned on a page with no way to close it. Two-part fix, same shape as the narrow-container one above:
  1. Added a `useEffect` keyed on `activeInteractionId` that closes/unpins/re-arms hover the moment there's no active interaction — mirrors the narrow-container effect exactly, just triggered by a different condition.
  2. Changed the `<Panel>`'s render guard from `showPanelToggle &&` to `showPanelToggle && activeInteraction &&`, so it doesn't exist in the tree at all outside an interaction — consistent with the very first version of this feature's own scoping ("when in an interaction" / "not to the dashboard"), which had drifted since the panel-unification fix reworked this condition without re-checking it. Unmounting here doesn't reintroduce the animation bug the unification fix solved, since effect (1) has already reset the open/pinned state before this transition happens — there's nothing mid-animation to lose.
- Verified with `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1 only).

### Cross-repo drift audit: lyra-ux-templates + AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx catch up to agent-next-gen-v1's Designer panel
- **Request**: "let's make sure lyra-ux-templates is up to date and no drift between components... make sure no other components have drifted in the entire repo," from two reported symptoms in lyra-ux-templates — the active interaction not showing in the LeftNav card, and the record icon not toggling the side panel.
- **LeftNav card symptom**: read `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s entire interaction-tracking pipeline (`handleStartCall`/`handleQuickDial`/`outboundConfig`/the `<LeftNav header={...}>` block) line-by-line against agent-next-gen-v1's copy and against this file (the canonical reference both apps port from) — they're identical. `CreateNew`'s `onStartCall`/`onQuickDial` overrides land correctly after the `...outboundConfig` spread, contact ids don't collide with the local team/skill/assignment ids, and `LeftNav`'s `header` rendering (`left-nav.tsx`) isn't gated on `open`/collapsed state. `npx vite build` in lyra-ux-templates also compiles clean. No code-level bug found — the interaction-tracking port (from an earlier, previously-uncommitted session) is already correct and complete; if the card still doesn't appear, it's most likely a stale dev-server/HMR state rather than a logic bug, since this exact code was sitting as an uncommitted working-tree diff this whole time.
- **Record icon symptom, confirmed**: lyra-ux-templates' interaction `PageHeader` still had a bare `icon={<User .../>}` with zero handlers — it simply never received any of agent-next-gen-v1's Designer-panel work from this session (the hover-reveal, click-to-pin/close, `PanelPinButton`, the narrow/activeInteraction-scoped close effects, or the single-`<Panel>`-element unification). Ported the entire feature verbatim into `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`: `sidePanelHoverEnabled` state, the narrow-container and `activeInteractionId` reset effects, `handleSidePanelIconToggle`, the `onSidePanelHoverEnd` pinned-guard, the merged single `<Panel pinned={effectivePinned} .../>` gated on `activeInteraction`, and the `PanelPinButton`-based `icon` (with `iconAriaHidden={false}`) on the interaction `PageHeader`.
- **Also ported the same fix into `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`** (this repo's own canonical "Templates/Agent Next Gen" story) — it had the exact same gap (bare `icon={<User .../>}`, two separately-gated `<Panel>` elements) since it predates this session's Designer-panel work entirely. Left `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story alone — same precedent as the earlier per-channel-tabs fix, it has no record-header/`PageHeader` content for this to attach to.
- **Other drift found and fixed**: lyra-ux-templates' `OUTBOUND_AGENTS` mapping was missing `status: a.status` (present in agent-next-gen-v1 and the canonical mock), which silently dropped the presence chip from the Outbound picker's agent rows — added it back. lyra-ux-templates' extra local `"assignments"` picker group was confirmed intentional (its own comment says so) and doesn't collide with any id space, so it was left as-is. No hand-rolled reimplementations of `Menu`/`KebabMenuButton`/`PanelPinButton`/etc. were found in either consuming app — both compose the shared components correctly.
- Updated both `agent-next-gen-v1/.lyra-ui-sync` and `lyra-ux-templates/.lyra-ui-sync` to lyra-ui's current HEAD, so this session's already-reviewed lyra-ui changes aren't re-flagged as "new" by the sync check next session.
- Verified with `tsc`/Storybook build (lyra-ui) and `vite build` (agent-next-gen-v1, lyra-ux-templates).

### lyra-ux-templates — channel-row "current interaction" highlight silently missing (real root cause: hand-copied Tailwind color map, not a component bug)
- **Report**: the Voice channel row inside an active `InteractionNavItem` card rendered with a plain white background in lyra-ux-templates, instead of the blue `bg-lyra-status-info-subtle` highlight lyra-ui's own reference shows for the same state.
- Traced `channel-row.tsx`'s `highlighted` prop and `interaction-nav-item.tsx`'s `effectiveCurrentKey`/`active` computation first — both matched lyra-ui's reference exactly, and lyra-ux-templates' `<InteractionNavItem active=.../>` call site wired `active`/`current` the same way agent-next-gen-v1 does. The highlight logic itself was never the bug.
- **Actual root cause**: `lyra-ux-templates/tailwind.config.js` still hand-copied its `theme.extend.colors` object instead of `require`-ing the shared `lyra-ui/tailwind-tokens.cjs` map — the exact anti-pattern that `tailwind-tokens.cjs`'s own doc comment and `agent-next-gen-v1/tailwind.config.js`'s comment both already document as a past incident ("Tailwind just generates *no CSS at all* for a `bg-`/`text-`/`border-` class using an unrecognized color name... found only by someone noticing a color that should be there wasn't"). The hand-copied list was missing `lyra-status-info-subtle` outright, plus `lyra-status-info-medium`, most of `lyra-accent-*` (customer-avatar colors), several `lyra-bg-*`/`lyra-fg-*`/`lyra-border-*`/`lyra-state-*` tokens added to the shared map since the hand-copy was last updated — 94 tokens in the shared source vs. ~44 hand-copied. Every one of those classes was sitting inert on the DOM with zero visual effect and no build error.
- **Fix**: swapped `lyra-ux-templates/tailwind.config.js`'s literal `colors` object for `...require("../lyra-ui/tailwind-tokens.cjs")`, matching `agent-next-gen-v1/tailwind.config.js`'s existing pattern exactly (`cxone-navy` kept as the one app-specific literal alongside the spread).
- Verified live: drove `localhost:5174` through New Outbound → Voice call, confirmed the "Voice" row now renders with the blue highlight background matching lyra-ui's reference.
- **Takeaway for future drift audits in this app**: a visual/CSS discrepancy that survives an exhaustive component-logic diff is a signal to check the consuming app's `tailwind.config.js` against `tailwind-tokens.cjs` next, not to re-re-check the component source — this class of bug produces zero errors/warnings anywhere.

### CustomerInformationPanel (`src/components/customer-information-panel.tsx`) — new atom replacing the bare "Designer" `Panel` on the interaction record header
- **Request**: replace the record-header side panel's content — previously a plain `<Panel headerTitle="Designer" .../>` with no body — with a "Customer Information" panel whose header shows the interaction's customer/agent name and record id as a subhead, matching a provided screenshot ("Customer Information" title, "Sarah Miller · CST-10591" subhead below it).

- Built `CustomerInformationPanel` as a thin composition over `Panel`, not a fork: fixes `variant="side"` and `headerTitle="Customer Information"`, defaults `side="left"`, and computes `headerSubhead` as `` `${person.name} · ${person.id}` `` from a new `person: { name, id }` prop. Every other `Panel` prop (`open`, `pinned`, `onPinToggle`, `width`, resize/hover handlers, `children`) passes straight through unchanged, so it's a drop-in replacement everywhere the "Designer" panel was used for this purpose.

- **Wired into all three consumers**, replacing the old `<Panel variant="side" side="left" headerTitle="Designer" .../>` block with `<CustomerInformationPanel side="left" person={{ name: activeInteraction.customerName ?? "Customer", id: activeInteraction.recordId }} .../>` — same `person` derivation the interaction `PageHeader`'s own `title`/`subtitle` already use, so the panel and the record header always agree: `agent-next-gen-v1/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, and `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` (the canonical reference). Left the unrelated "Designer" `SidePanel` in `DataManagement.stories.tsx`/`PageHeader.stories.tsx` alone — that one's a generic "Desktop Designs" tool panel with placeholder content, not tied to a customer/agent record, so it isn't the same feature.

Confirmed live on `localhost:5174`: clicking the record header's user icon now opens a panel titled "Customer Information" with subhead "Alex Kowalski · CST-10000", pin/hover/resize/animation behavior unchanged.

### Follow-up: default tooltip changed to "Toggle Overview" in both apps; `sidePanelHoverEnabled` gating removed everywhere to match `Panel.stories.tsx`'s plain "Side Panel" model
- **Request**: a screenshot of a real app (not Storybook) still showing "Unpin Designer panel" on hover — asked to update that default tooltip in both `lyra-ux-templates` and `agent-next-gen-v1` to match "the newest storybook." Also reported what looked like a bug: after pinning then unpinning the panel via the icon, hovering the icon again doesn't reopen it as a preview.
- **Tooltip**: changed the hardcoded fallback strings in both apps' `PanelPinButton` call (`pinnedLabel={sidePanelToggleLabel ?? "..."}`/`unpinnedLabel={...}`) from `"Unpin Designer panel"`/`"Pin Designer panel"` to `"Toggle Overview"` for both states, and updated the `sidePanelToggleLabel` prop's doc comment to match. Note: lyra-ui's own `Default`/`WithPageHeader` stories in `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` were **not** touched and still show "Unpin/Pin Designer panel" — only `ActiveInteractionStory` and both real apps say "Toggle Overview."
- **"Bug" investigation, corrected**: an initial pass found the `sidePanelHoverEnabled`/`onSidePanelHoverStart`/`handleSidePanelIconToggle` block line-for-line identical across all three files (including the canonical lyra-ui story) and concluded this was intentional, matching an earlier request in this session ("when the panel is pinned then closed it should require a click to open again" — see "Require click-to-reopen after pin+close cycle" above) — reported back without changing anything. The user corrected this twice: first confirming the code comparison was right but that the shared behavior was itself wrong, then definitively — "they should match the behavior of the Side panel story in the panel component," i.e. `Panel.stories.tsx`'s "Side Panel" story, where `pinned`/`open` are two plain independent booleans with no hidden gating flag.
- **Actual fix**: removed the `sidePanelHoverEnabled` state, its two reset-effect lines (narrow-container and leave-interaction), the `if (!sidePanelHoverEnabled) return;` guard in `onSidePanelHoverStart`, and the `if (!nextPinned) setSidePanelHoverEnabled(false);` line in `handleSidePanelIconToggle` — from all three files: `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` (lyra-ui), `agent-next-gen-v1/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, and `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`. Hovering the record icon now always previews the panel while unpinned, including immediately after an icon-click unpin — `pinned` and `open` behave as plain independent state everywhere, matching the plain `Panel` model. This reverses part of the earlier "Require click-to-reopen after pin+close cycle" entry; that stricter behavior is no longer in place anywhere.
- Verified with `tsc --noEmit`/Storybook build (lyra-ui — pre-existing, unrelated `tsc` errors in `admin-shell.tsx`/`list-item.tsx`/`panel.tsx` untouched by this fix) and `npx vite build` in both apps (clean).

### Follow-up #2: `handleSidePanelIconToggle` still unpinned the panel on "close" — the actual remaining bug
- **Report**: pin the Customer Information panel, then toggle it closed via the record icon — it comes back *unpinned*, not just closed. Reopening it later (e.g. by hovering) no longer stays pinned, contradicting `Panel.stories.tsx`'s "Side Panel" reference behavior (`pinned`/`open` are independent; only the panel's own internal pin button should ever unpin).
- **Root cause**: the previous fix above (Follow-up #1) removed the `sidePanelHoverEnabled` gating flag, but left `handleSidePanelIconToggle`'s core logic untouched — it still computed `nextPinned = !sidePanelPinned` and called *both* `setSidePanelPinned(nextPinned)` and `setSidePanelOpen(nextPinned)`. So clicking the icon to close a pinned panel (`nextPinned = false`) unpinned it in the same click — the exact bug the "Side Panel" reference model forbids. `admin-shell.tsx`'s own `handleLeftToggle`/`handleRightToggle` (the original canonical reference for this whole feature) never had this bug: `if (effectiveLeftPinned) setLeftPanelOpen((v) => !v);` — a no-op while unpinned, and never touches the pinned flag at all. Also independently confirmed already-correct in `Agent Nav Testing/src/components/Option0{1,2,4,5}Page.tsx` (a different, earlier-diverged copy of this same feature) — useful as a second working reference.
- **Fix**: `handleSidePanelIconToggle` in all three files (`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, `agent-next-gen-v1/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`) simplified to `if (effectivePinned) setSidePanelOpen((v) => !v);` — matching `admin-shell.tsx` exactly. `handleSidePanelPinToggle` (the panel's own internal pin button) is untouched and remains the only path that changes `sidePanelPinned`.
- Verified with `tsc --noEmit` (lyra-ui clean aside from the same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors; the two consumer apps' raw `tsc` still can't resolve the `@nicecxone/lyra-ui`/Storybook aliases outside Vite, a pre-existing condition unrelated to this change — no new errors attributable to the edited lines in either app).

### Follow-up #3: hover could still reopen a pinned-but-closed panel — `onSidePanelHoverStart` needed the same pinned guard `onSidePanelHoverEnd` already had
- **Report**: pin the panel, close it via the click toggle — hovering the record icon afterward reopens it, when pinned mode should be click-only (open **and** close) with hover doing nothing at all, matching `SidePanel`'s other real usage (`admin-shell.tsx`).
- **Root cause**: `onSidePanelHoverStart` had no pinned guard at all — `clearTimeout(sidePanelTimer.current); setSidePanelOpen(true);` ran unconditionally on every `onMouseEnter`, pinned or not. Only `onSidePanelHoverEnd` was guarded (`if (sidePanelPinned) return;`), so hover could still *open* a pinned panel, just not auto-*close* one. `admin-shell.tsx`'s `handleLeftHoverStart`/`handleLeftHoverEnd` (the canonical reference) guard both directions identically: `if (!effectiveLeftPinned) { ... }`.
- **Fix**: added the matching `if (sidePanelPinned) return;` guard to `onSidePanelHoverStart` in all three files (`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, `agent-next-gen-v1/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`, `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`) — hover now does nothing in either direction while pinned; `handleSidePanelIconToggle`'s click toggle (Follow-up #2, above) is the only way to open or close it in that state.
- Verified with `tsc --noEmit` (lyra-ui clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors only).

### `agent-next-gen-v1` — re-added the icon-only "Ask AI" trigger to the AppHeader
- **Request**: "let's re-add the Ask AI icon button to the top right appHeader in agent-next-gen-v1." A code comment on the Desk `PageHeader`'s `askAiButton` documented that this exact icon-only AppHeader trigger had been deliberately removed in an earlier session ("the earlier icon-only AppHeader trigger was removed since this labeled PageHeader button covers the same `aiPanelOpen`/`AiPanel` instance") — the user has now decided they want both entry points back, not just the one.
- Checked lyra-ui's own `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` and `lyra-ux-templates`' `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` first: both still have this trigger in their AppHeader (`NotificationsBell` → Ask AI → `AgentProfile`) — only `agent-next-gen-v1` had ever removed it, so this brings it back in line with the other two rather than introducing something new.
- **Implementation choice, not a straight copy of the other two files**: both lyra-ui's story and `lyra-ux-templates` implement this trigger as a hand-rolled `<button>` around a locally-duplicated `AiSparkleIcon` SVG function (predating some later additions to lyra-ui). `agent-next-gen-v1` already has both of the real shared pieces those two are missing — `ActionIconButton` (already used one slot to the left, for "Help") and lyra-ui's own exported `AiIcon` (already imported and used by `askAiButton` on the same page) — so the re-added trigger composes those instead of re-deriving the hand-rolled version: `<ActionIconButton size="xl" title="Ask AI" onClick={...} aria-expanded={aiPanelOpen} className={aiPanelOpen ? "bg-lyra-state-hover" : undefined}><AiIcon className="h-5 w-5" /></ActionIconButton>`, placed between `NotificationsBell` and `AgentProfile` (matching the other two files' ordering). `ActionIconButton` already wraps its `title` in a real `Tooltip` internally, so this also incidentally picks up the tooltip `lyra-ux-templates`' version has (via its own separate `Tooltip` wrapper) that lyra-ui's story is missing entirely.
- Reuses the existing `aiPanelOpen` state and toggle handler verbatim — this is a second entry point into the same panel `askAiButton` already opens, not a new panel instance.
- **Flagging, not fixing**: lyra-ui's story and `lyra-ux-templates` still use the older hand-rolled-button + local-`AiSparkleIcon` pattern. Out of scope for this request (which was specifically about `agent-next-gen-v1`), but worth revisiting in a future pass so all three converge on `ActionIconButton`/`AiIcon`.
- Verified with `npx vite build` in `agent-next-gen-v1` (clean).

### Follow-up: Ask AI trigger switched from lyra-ui's gradient `AiIcon` to a solid-color icon
- **Request**: "make the icon button a solid color for Ask AI in the appheader in agent-next-gen-v1." Asked which solid color via `AskUserQuestion` (primary blue / the AI gradient's blue anchor / the AI gradient's purple anchor); the user's actual answer was "the same color as the notifications icon" — a different axis than any of the three options offered, clarifying that the ask was about the *icon's* color (solid vs. lyra-ui's `AiIcon` multi-hue gradient fill), not picking a new background fill color.
- **Root cause of the mismatch**: `AiIcon` (`icons/ai-icon.tsx`) hardcodes `fill="url(#ai-icon-gradient)"` on its path — a fixed blue-to-purple gradient that ignores `currentColor`/text-color classes entirely, so it always renders multi-hued no matter what color utility is applied to it or its wrapper. Sitting between two plain single-color icons (`CircleHelp` for Help, `Bell` for Notifications, both inheriting a normal text color), it visually stood out as inconsistent — which is what "make it a solid color" was asking to fix.
- **Fix**: swapped `AiIcon` for lucide's `Sparkles` (same icon set as every other icon in this row) and forced `text-lyra-fg-default` via `className` on the `ActionIconButton` (`cn("text-lyra-fg-default", aiPanelOpen && "bg-lyra-state-hover")`) — overriding `ActionIconButton`'s own default `text-lyra-fg-action`, since `NotificationsBell` is a separate hand-rolled component that uses `text-lyra-fg-default` on its own button, not the `ActionIconButton` default. `cn()`'s `twMerge` resolves this deterministically at the class-string level (last conflicting text-color utility wins), unlike the CSS-cascade-order issue from the `TabList` entry above — no bundler-order risk here.
- Scoped to this one AppHeader trigger only — `askAiButton` on the Desk `PageHeader` (same page, opens the same panel) still uses the gradient `AiIcon` unchanged, since it wasn't part of this request and sits in a different visual context (a labeled button, not an icon row next to Help/Notifications).
- Verified with `npx vite build` in `agent-next-gen-v1` (clean).

### Follow-up: Help + Ask AI AppHeader triggers resized from `size="xl"` to `size="lg"` to match `NotificationsBell`
- **Request**: "make the size of the AI and ? icon buttons the same size as the notifications button (make them smaller) - make a note that when adding additional siblings to an existing pattern to use similar sized components in lyra-ui."
- **Root cause**: both `ActionIconButton`s in this row (Help, Ask AI) had been added at `size="xl"` (44px, `h-11 w-11`) at the time each was written, with no check against their row-mate `NotificationsBell` — a separate hand-rolled component whose trigger `<button>` is hardcoded `h-10 w-10` (40px), i.e. exactly `ActionIconButton`'s `size="lg"`. Neither `xl` value was a deliberate match to anything; it just went unnoticed since `ActionIconButton` and `NotificationsBell` don't share a prop that would have surfaced the mismatch automatically.
- **Fix**: changed both `<ActionIconButton>`s (Help, Ask AI) in `agent-next-gen-v1/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx` from `size="xl"` to `size="lg"`, with inline comments citing `NotificationsBell`'s `h-10 w-10` as the source of truth. `askAiButton` on the Desk `PageHeader` (unrelated row, different context) was left untouched.
- **Standing note added** under Important Patterns above: when adding a new icon button as a sibling to an existing icon-button row, measure the row's actual existing size (including non-`ActionIconButton` siblings like `NotificationsBell` — check for a hardcoded height/width class) and pick the matching `size` explicitly, rather than leaving it at whatever `size` was last typed elsewhere.
- Verified with `npx vite build --outDir /tmp/anv1-size --emptyOutDir=false` in `agent-next-gen-v1` (clean, `✓ built in 7.93s`).

### Follow-up: extracted the shared `AiSparkleIcon` and swapped `agent-next-gen-v1`'s Ask AI trigger back to lyra-ui's real markup
- **Request**: "check with Lyra-ui on the AI-Assist icon button. it looks different in lyra-ui then in agent-next-gen-v1 - use the one in lyra-ui." The user compared the two directly and caught a real visual mismatch the previous two follow-ups (solid-color swap, size fix) hadn't touched.
- **Root cause**: three separate files — `AppHeader.stories.tsx`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` (lyra-ui's own canonical template for this exact page), and `lyra-ux-templates`' `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` — all hand-roll an identical local `AiSparkleIcon` function (a solid `currentColor`-filled sparkle shape, same path data as `AiIcon` but without its hardcoded gradient) inside a plain `<button className="h-10 w-10 rounded-lyra-lg text-lyra-fg-default ...">`, matching `NotificationsBell` exactly. `agent-next-gen-v1`, when it was made solid-color in an earlier follow-up, reached for lucide's `Sparkles` icon inside an `ActionIconButton` instead — a different icon shape *and* a different border radius (`ActionIconButton` uses `rounded-lyra-sm`, not `rounded-lyra-lg`), so it never actually matched lyra-ui's real component even though it was solid-colored and now correctly sized.
- **Fix, extraction not just a swap**: rather than copy the hand-rolled SVG into a fourth place (which is exactly the kind of local reimplementation `CLAUDE.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s composition-over-reimplementation rule warn against), extracted it into a real shared icon: `lyra-ui/src/components/icons/ai-sparkle-icon.tsx` (`AiSparkleIcon`, same `forwardRef`/`cn(...)`/`className` pattern as `DashboardIcon`/`AiIcon`, default `h-5 w-5`, `fill="currentColor"`), exported from `index.ts`. Removed the local duplicate function from all three existing consumers and pointed them at the shared export. `agent-next-gen-v1`'s Ask AI trigger was rewritten from `<ActionIconButton size="lg" ...><Sparkles .../></ActionIconButton>` to the same hand-rolled `<button>` + `Tooltip` markup the other three files use, rendering `<AiSparkleIcon />`.
- This also retires the "Flagging, not fixing" note from the original "re-added Ask AI trigger" entry above (which predicted these three would eventually need to converge) — they now share one icon definition instead of three duplicates plus a fourth divergent one.
- `askAiButton` on the Desk `PageHeader` (gradient `AiIcon`, unchanged) is unaffected — still out of scope, different visual context (labeled button, not an icon row).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (same 3 pre-existing unrelated errors in `admin-shell.tsx`/`list-item.tsx`/`panel.tsx`, none touching this change), `storybook build` (clean, `✓ built in 19.98s`), `vite build` in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` (clean).
