- Updated a stale comment inside `agent-profile.tsx`'s `onHelpClick` conditional that referenced `agent-next-gen-v1` as the example of a consumer using its own standalone AppHeader icon instead of this prop — no longer true, so the comment was corrected rather than left to mislead the next pass.
- **Flagging, not fixing**: neither `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` nor `lyra-ux-templates`' `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` currently pass `onHelpClick` — neither has a Help entry point at all right now. Out of scope for this request (`agent-next-gen-v1` only), but worth a look in a future pass.
- Verified with `npx vite build --outDir /tmp/anv1-help-move --emptyOutDir=false` in `agent-next-gen-v1` (clean, `✓ built in 8.38s`).

### Follow-up: Ask AI panel defaulted to a floating popover instead of an immediately-docked side panel; PageHeader "Ask AI" button removed
- **Request**: "you made a custom change to the behavior of the Ask AI panel in agent-next-gen-v1: open the panel on first click instead of a dropdown. I want you to revert that back to how it is in Lyra-UI and have it open the popover first - then remove the Ask AI button from the page header."
- **Root cause**: `agent-next-gen-v1`'s AI panel is built on the same `Draggable`/`DraggableVariant` ("docked" vs "float") pattern as its Notifications panel — `"docked"` pushes into the page layout as a fixed-width column; `"float"` is a transient, portal-positioned panel anchored under the trigger button via `getBoundingClientRect()` (the same pattern lyra-ui's own `AppHeader.stories.tsx`/`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` use for this exact button — plain `ReactDOM.createPortal` + fixed position, no docked variant exists in either story at all). `notifVariant` was initialized `useState<DraggableVariant>("float")`, but `aiVariant` was initialized `useState<DraggableVariant>("docked")` — an unintentional asymmetry between two panels built from the identical pattern, which meant the very first click on Ask AI skipped the floating/popover step lyra-ui shows and went straight to the full docked panel.
- **Fix**: changed `aiVariant`'s initial state to `"float"`, matching `notifVariant` and lyra-ui's own behavior. Docking is still reachable afterward (same drag-to-dock interaction Notifications already supports) — only the *default* on first open changed.
- **Also removed**: the labeled "Ask AI" `PageHeader` action button (`askAiButton`, `<Button variant="outline"><AiIcon/>Ask AI</Button>`) from both places it appeared — the Desk dashboard `PageHeader` and the active-interaction record `PageHeader` — per the second half of the request. The AppHeader icon-only trigger (added/fixed in the two follow-ups above) is now the sole "Ask AI" entry point in this app. Removed the now-unused `AiIcon` import and updated stale comments that referenced `askAiButton` as "the other" entry point.
- Verified with `npx vite build --outDir /tmp/anv1-ai-fix --emptyOutDir=false` in `agent-next-gen-v1` (clean, `✓ built in 7.12s`); `tsc --noEmit` shows only the same pre-existing unrelated errors (`@nicecxone/lyra-ui` alias/`@storybook/react` resolution, implicit-`any` params elsewhere in the file) that predate this change — nothing new introduced.

### New: `DraggablePanel` (lyra-ui) + "Messages"/"Schedule" AppHeader triggers in `agent-next-gen-v1`
- **Request**: "add 2 more icon buttons to the left of the notifications icon button for: 1) Messages 2) Schedule - when each are clicked open a blank draggable dropdown (same component as Notifications/AI Assist) that can be docked/dragged - each should have a header that matches the label of the icon button."
- **New lyra-ui component, not another hand-rolled panel**: `AiPanel` and `AgentNotifications` each independently wrap `Draggable` + `ContainerHeader` (grip icon in float mode, dock/undock button, close button) around their own specific body content. Adding two more one-off panels the same way would make it four near-identical `Draggable`/`ContainerHeader` compositions in the app. Extracted the shared shape into `src/components/draggable-panel.tsx` (`DraggablePanel` — `title` prop for the header, `children` for body content, defaults to a blank "Nothing here yet." empty state when omitted, same `draggableVariant`/`onVariantChange`/`onWidthChange`/`onResizeStateChange`/`onInteract`/`onClose` contract as the other two). Exported from `index.ts`; story added (`UI/DraggablePanel` — Blank, With content, Interactive float↔docked).
- **AppHeader**: added two triggers left of `NotificationsBell` — `MessageSquare` "Messages" and `CalendarDays` "Schedule" (the same icon lyra-ui's own `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` app-menu already uses for a "Schedule" entry). Both use the identical hand-rolled `<button>` shape as the Ask AI trigger (`h-10 w-10 rounded-lyra-lg text-lyra-fg-default`, `Tooltip`-wrapped) — not `ActionIconButton`, consistent with the "match the row's real markup" fix from the AiSparkleIcon entry above.
- **Single-dock rule generalized from 2 panels to 4**: the pre-existing pairwise checks ("if docking Notifications and AI is already docked, force AI to float") don't scale — a third and fourth panel would need three checks each, 12 total. Replaced with `dockPanelExclusively(dockingKey)`, which looks up all four panels' variant/width/position refs in a small lookup object and floats whichever *other* one is currently docked, regardless of how many panels exist. Also generalized the open/close/mount animation effect (previously copy-pasted per panel) into a `usePanelOpenEffect(...)` helper called once per panel, and the float-position-style calculation into one `getFloatStyle(floatLeft, floatTop, width, key)` used by all four. `topPanel` (z-index "bring to front" tracking) widened from `"ai" | "notif" | null` to include `"messages" | "schedule"`.
- Each new panel gets its own full state set (open/mounted/state/variant/width/height/isResizing/float-position refs/anim timer), matching AI/Notifications exactly — same docked (flex-sibling column) and float (portal, fixed-position, fade/slide) render blocks, added to `agent-next-gen-v1/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s existing JSX pattern.
- **Flagging, not fixing**: this is `agent-next-gen-v1`-only per the request — lyra-ui's own `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` and `lyra-ux-templates`' `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` don't have Messages/Schedule triggers and weren't asked for. `DraggablePanel` itself is available to them whenever that's wanted.
- Verified: `storybook build` in lyra-ui (clean), `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build --outDir /tmp/anv1-msg-sched2 --emptyOutDir=false` in `agent-next-gen-v1` (clean — one real type error caught and fixed along the way: `captureFloatPosition`'s `panelRef` param needed `React.RefObject<HTMLDivElement | null>`, not `React.RefObject<HTMLDivElement>`, to match what `useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null)` actually returns), `vite build` in `lyra-ux-templates` (clean, confirms the new `index.ts` export didn't break anything there).
- **Follow-up, same session**: "rename Messages to Conversations." Renamed the trigger's `Tooltip`/`aria-label` and the `DraggablePanel`'s `title` from "Messages" to "Conversations" — label only, `MessageSquare` icon unchanged. Also renamed every internal identifier that had been named after the old label (`msgOpen`/`msgVariant`/`msgPanelRef`/etc. → `convOpen`/`convVariant`/`convPanelRef`/etc., `messagesPanel` → `conversationsPanel`, the `"messages"` string key in `topPanel`/`dockPanelExclusively`/`getFloatStyle` → `"conversations"`) rather than leaving the label and the code out of sync — matching this doc's own repeated point about not letting a display change drift away from the names describing it. Verified with `vite build` in `agent-next-gen-v1` (clean) and a targeted `tsc --noEmit` diff confirming no new errors.

### New: Settings `LeftNav` item wired up in `agent-next-gen-v1` (blank page + active state)
- **Request**: "when the settings icon is clicked in agent-next-gen-v1 display a blank page with a page header that says "Settings" and make the settings left nav icon active."
- **Root cause**: the Settings `NavItem` in `buildNavItems` had no `onClick` and no `active` — a fully dead rail item, unlike Desk's item which already wired both.
- **Fix**: added `showSettings` state (with a `useEffect` that clears it whenever `activeInteractionId` becomes truthy, so starting/selecting an interaction always takes over the content column the same way it already does relative to Desk — one effect instead of touching all 5 `setActiveInteractionId` call sites). `buildNavItems` now takes `showSettings`/`onSettingsClick`, sets Settings' `active`/`onClick` accordingly, and adds `!showSettings` to Desk's own `active` check so the two rail items stay mutually exclusive. The content column gained a new top-priority branch — `showSettings ? <PageHeader title="Settings" /> + blank body : activeInteraction ? … : …` — reusing the exact "just a header, blank `<div className="flex-1 overflow-y-auto" />` below it" placeholder pattern the interaction-record view already uses, rather than inventing a new empty-state shape.
- **Flagging, not fixing**: `agent-next-gen-v1`-only per the request; lyra-ui's own `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` and `lyra-ux-templates` weren't asked for a Settings view and don't have one.
- Verified: targeted `tsc --noEmit -p .` diff on `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` showed only the pre-existing known error categories (module resolution, implicit-`any`, `Expected 1 arguments, but got 0`) — no new errors. `vite build --outDir /tmp/anv1-settings --emptyOutDir=false` succeeded (only a pre-existing unrelated Tailwind ambiguous-class warning).
- **Follow-up, same session**: "when the user navigates to settings from an active interaction that interaction should no longer be active." The mutual-exclusion effect only went one direction — starting/selecting an interaction cleared `showSettings`, but clicking Settings while an interaction card was selected left it selected (visible in a screenshot: "Jamie Torres" still showed its blue selected-card border with Settings open). Fixed by having the `LeftNav` `onSettingsClick` handler clear `activeInteractionId` directly (`() => { setShowSettings(true); setActiveInteractionId(null); }`) rather than adding a second effect — there's only the one call site for this handler, unlike `setActiveInteractionId`, which has several and is why the *other* direction uses an effect. Verified with a targeted `tsc --noEmit` diff (only pre-existing errors, shifted line numbers from the added comment) and a clean `vite build --outDir /tmp/anv1-settings-fix --emptyOutDir=false`.

### `Draggable` panels now stay within the viewport
- **Request**: "can you control the logic of the draggability in lyra-ui to be contained within the visible screenspace?" — prompted by a screenshot of the Ask AI float panel dragged partially off the left edge of the browser window (text clipped, square un-rounded left edge visible at `x = 0`).
- **Root cause**: `draggable.tsx`'s drag (`onDragMouseDown`) and resize (`onCornerResizeDown`, `onLeftEdgeResizeDown`) handlers only clamped against `minWidth`/`maxWidth`/`minHeight` — never against the actual browser viewport, so a float panel could be dragged or resized fully or partially off-screen in any direction.
- **Fix, contained entirely to `draggable.tsx`** (no changes needed in any consuming app — the component measures its own `getBoundingClientRect()` rather than depending on how a consumer positions the float wrapper):
  - Added a local `rootRef` merged with the forwarded `ref` (`setRootRef`) so the component can read its own on-screen position.
  - Float drag: captures the panel's un-offset base position at drag start, then clamps every subsequent offset so `base + offset` stays within `[0, viewport − panelSize]` on both axes (prioritizing keeping the top-left corner on screen if the panel is larger than the viewport).
  - Float corner resize: captures the fixed top-left corner at resize start, caps new width/height so growth never pushes the right/bottom edge past the viewport.
  - Docked left-edge resize: the right edge is already pinned by the consumer's flex layout, so only caps width so the left edge can't cross `x = 0`.
  - Documented as a new "Viewport Containment" section in the file's existing rules comment block, alongside Panel Resize Direction Rules / Animation Rules / Multi-Panel Z-Index & Docking Rules.
- **Also fixed while verifying**: `draggable-panel.tsx` (added earlier this session) had `DraggablePanelProps` exported twice — once via `export interface`, once via a redundant `export type { DraggablePanelProps }` two lines later — causing a real `tsc` error (`TS2484: Export declaration conflicts...`) that hadn't been caught yet. Removed the redundant re-export line; `index.ts`'s own `export type { DraggablePanelProps } from "./components/draggable-panel"` is a separate, valid cross-module re-export and didn't need to change.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` in lyra-ui (no errors from either file; remaining output is the same pre-existing errors in unrelated files — `admin-shell.tsx`, `list-item.tsx`, `panel.tsx`), `storybook build` (clean), `vite build` in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` (clean).
- **Follow-up, same session**: "if the screensize is shrunk you can make the draggable panel go off screen - can you make it so it sizes stays within the viewable area on resize?" — screenshot showed a docked AI Assistant panel with its right portion clipped past the actual browser window edge after the window was shrunk.
  - **Root cause**: none of the drag/resize handlers above fire from a passive browser window resize — they only run during an active mouse gesture. The docked panel's width is a plain React state value, mirrored 1:1 into the consumer's (`agent-next-gen-v1`'s) flex-sibling wrapper via `onWidthChange`, and that wrapper is `flexShrink: 0` with a fixed pixel width. So when the window shrinks, the nav rail and content column (both flexible/`min-w-0`) get squeezed down to their floor, but the panel's fixed-width wrapper doesn't move — once there's no room left, the ancestor's `overflow-hidden` (added earlier specifically "so docked panels never push layout past the viewport") just clips the excess instead of the panel shrinking.
  - **Fix, still fully contained to `draggable.tsx`** — added a `window` `resize` listener (mounted once, reading a `latestRef` snapshot of `variant`/`offset`/`width`/`height`/`minWidth`/`maxWidth`/`minHeight`/`onWidthChange` kept fresh every render, so the listener never needs to re-subscribe on every drag-driven state change):
    - Docked: by the time this listener runs, the browser's own flex layout has already squeezed the nav rail/content column to their floor — so `rootRef.current.getBoundingClientRect().right` at that moment already reflects the panel's true current right edge. If that exceeds `window.innerWidth`, shrink `width` by exactly the overflow (floored at `minWidth`) and call the *same* `onWidthChange` callback already used for manual resize — which the consumer already has wired to its mirrored width state, so the flex-sibling wrapper shrinks in lockstep with zero consumer-side changes.
    - Float: re-clamps `offset` the same way `onDragMouseDown` does, and shrinks `width`/`height` too if the panel itself is now larger than the shrunk viewport.
    - Documented as a new bullet under "Viewport Containment" in the file's rules comment block.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` (no new errors — same three pre-existing unrelated errors as above), `storybook build` (clean), `vite build` in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` (clean).

### `LeftNav`'s nav item list (Desk/Settings) sticks to the bottom once pushed there, instead of always being pinned
- **Request**: "when there are enough interactions open that it pushes the static leftNav elements below the viewable screen - make them sticky to the bottom and scroll the interactions behind them." Screenshot showed three `InteractionNavItem` cards with "Desk" and (cut off) "Settings" crowded at the very bottom of the crop — with a fourth+ card open, they'd scroll out of view entirely.
- **Root cause**: `header` (the `InteractionNavItem` card list) and the nav item list (`TreeMenu`/icon-only rail) shared one single scrollable box (`flex flex-1 flex-col overflow-y-auto ...`). Since both lived in the same scroll region, a long enough card list just pushed the nav items further down inside that box — visible only by scrolling the whole rail down, exactly like a footer that isn't actually pinned.
- **First attempt (wrong) and the follow-up correction**: initially split `header` into its own `flex-1` scroll box with the nav list as a `flex-shrink-0` sibling *always* pinned right above `footer` — this does stop the overflow case, but the user caught (via a follow-up screenshot) that it also changed the common case: with only one or two cards, the nav list now sat unconditionally at the very bottom of the rail with a large empty gap above it, instead of staying right below the cards like before. The actual ask was for the nav list to stay in its normal position and *become* sticky only once pushed to the edge — not to be permanently anchored regardless of content.
- **Real fix, in `left-nav.tsx`, both the default (inline) and overlay render branches**: `header` and the nav list (`TreeMenu` or the icon-only rail) go back to sharing one `overflow-y-auto` scroll box, with the nav list in its normal flow position right after `header`'s content — but given `position: sticky; bottom: 0` plus an opaque `bg-lyra-bg-surface-shell` (so header cards scrolling underneath don't show through the nav items' icons/labels). This is exactly CSS sticky's actual purpose: the nav list stays in place after the cards (no gap when there are only a few), and only "sticks" to the bottom of the scroll viewport once continued scrolling would otherwise push it past that edge — matching "sticky to the bottom" literally rather than "always pinned to the bottom."
- **Flagging, not fixing**: this is a `lyra-ui` component fix, so it benefits all three consumers automatically — no consumer-side changes needed.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` (no `left-nav.tsx` errors), `storybook build` (clean — exercises `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story with its dynamic interaction list), `vite build` in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` (clean).

### Screen Pop panel gets real content: an app `Select`
- **Request**: "in the screenpop dropdown - add a select field that lists multiple external apps like salesforce, zendesk, etc."
- **Fix**: `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s `screenPopPanel` now passes a `<Select label="App" placeholder="Select an app..." options={SCREEN_POP_APPS} value={screenPopApp} onValueChange={setScreenPopApp} />` as `DraggablePanel`'s `children`, using lyra-ui's real `Select` component and its documented controlled-single-select contract (`value`/`onValueChange`), not a hand-rolled dropdown. `SCREEN_POP_APPS` is a small dummy `SelectOption[]` (Salesforce, Zendesk, ServiceNow, HubSpot, Freshdesk) — wiring an actual per-app screen-pop integration is out of scope, this is just the field the request asked for.
- **`DraggablePanel` (lyra-ui) tweak needed to support real content**: its body wrapper centered everything on both axes (`items-center justify-center`), sized for the short "Nothing here yet." placeholder — reasonable for an empty state, but it meant a real form field would render floating in the vertical middle of an ~860px-tall panel instead of at the top like a normal form. Changed the wrapper to only center-both-axes when there's no `children` (still true for the still-blank Conversations/Schedule panels); real `children` now top/left-align and stretch to the panel's width instead.
- **Flagging, not fixing**: the `Select` itself is `agent-next-gen-v1`-only per the request; the `DraggablePanel` body-alignment fix is in lyra-ui so it's available to Conversations/Schedule (still blank) and any future panel content automatically.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` in lyra-ui (no `draggable-panel.tsx` errors), `storybook build` (clean), targeted `tsc --noEmit` diff on `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (only the same pre-existing known error categories), and clean `vite build`s in `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates`.
- **Follow-up, same session**: "move the select into the header (above the divider - so it's sticky to the top) and remove the 'App' label." The app `Select` had been placed in the scrollable body, so it would scroll away with the panel's content instead of staying fixed like a real header control.
  - **New `DraggablePanel` prop: `headerContent`** — content rendered directly below the title row, still above the header's bottom divider (`ContainerHeader`'s own `bordered` is set to `false` when `headerContent` is present, and the divider moves onto a new wrapper below it instead). Since this sits outside the scrollable `children` wrapper entirely (a sibling of it, both inside `Draggable`'s non-scrolling `renderHeaderControls` region), it's fixed at the top the same way the title row already is — "sticky to the top" by construction, no `position: sticky` needed here (unlike the `LeftNav` fix above, which genuinely needed CSS sticky because its scroll region and pinned content share one box; here the header is already a separate, non-scrolling region).
  - `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s Screen Pop `Select` moved from `children` to `headerContent`, and its `label="App"` prop removed — a label reads as redundant once the field is presented directly in the header next to "Screen Pop" rather than as a body form field.
  - **Flagging, not fixing**: the `Select`/`headerContent` usage is `agent-next-gen-v1`-only per the request; `headerContent` itself is available to any other `DraggablePanel` consumer (Conversations, Schedule, future panels) whenever they need the same fixed-header-field pattern.
  - Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` in lyra-ui (no new errors), `storybook build` (clean), targeted `tsc --noEmit` diff on `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (only pre-existing known error categories), clean `vite build`s in `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates`.
  - **Follow-up, same session**: "remove the padding-top on the select container." The `headerContent` wrapper's own `py-3` stacked on top of `ContainerHeader`'s existing `py-5`, doubling up the vertical gap above the `Select`. Changed to `pb-3` only — `ContainerHeader`'s own bottom padding now supplies the gap above `headerContent`, so there's exactly one gap, not two. Verified with a clean `agent-next-gen-v1` `vite build`.

### Docked/floating panel max-width raised 600px → 1024px, moved from a per-instance override to a `Draggable` component default
- **Request**: "can you update this in lyra-ui at the component level so the max-width is 1024px?" — following a question confirming every docked panel (AI, Notifications, Conversations, Schedule, Screen Pop) was capped at 600px.
- **Root cause of why "component level" mattered**: `Draggable`'s own default was `maxWidth = Infinity` — genuinely uncapped unless a consumer passed a value. But every single consumer (`agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, and lyra-ui's own `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`/`AdminShell.stories.tsx`) independently hard-coded `maxWidth={600}` / `maxDraggableWidth={600}` on every panel instance. Bumping the value at each call site would have "worked" but left the same hard-coded literal duplicated across 3 repos and ~9 call sites — exactly the kind of drift this project's `CLAUDE.md` (§1, "use components as designed — never hard-code") flags as a bug waiting to happen the next time someone adds a 10th panel and forgets the literal.
- **Fix**: in `draggable.tsx`, changed the default parameter from `maxWidth = Infinity` to `maxWidth = 1024` (doc comment on the prop updated to note the default and that it's still overridable per-instance for a panel that genuinely needs to go wider/narrower). Then removed every now-redundant `maxWidth={600}`/`maxDraggableWidth={600}` prop that would otherwise have shadowed the new default: `agent-next-gen-v1`'s `App.tsx` and `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (AI, Notifications, Conversations, Schedule, Screen Pop — 6 sites), `lyra-ux-templates`'s `App.tsx` and `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (AI, Notifications — 2 sites), and lyra-ui's own `AdminShell.stories.tsx` and `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` (AI, Notifications — 2 sites).
- **Flagging, not fixing**: this is a `lyra-ui` component-level default, so any future `DraggablePanel`/`AiPanel`/`AgentNotifications` consumer gets the 1024px cap automatically with no prop needed — no consumer-side plumbing left to maintain.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` in lyra-ui (only pre-existing known error categories — `admin-shell.tsx`/`panel.tsx`'s `useRef` calls, `list-item.tsx`'s `title` type — nothing new), `storybook build` (clean), `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` (only pre-existing known error categories, clean builds).

### Responsive max-width for `Draggable` panels: 800px once the viewport drops below 1440px
- **Request**: "can you add a media query that if the screen size drops below 1440px the max-width of the draggable panels is 800px?"
- **Why not a literal CSS media query**: `maxWidth` isn't just a CSS property here — it's a JS value consulted inside `Draggable`'s own mousemove resize math (`Math.min(maxWidth, ...)` in both the float corner-resize and docked left-edge-resize handlers) to cap how far a drag can grow `width`. A `@media` rule could cap the rendered box visually but wouldn't stop the resize handlers from computing and storing a `width` past 800px, so the panel would immediately snap wider again the moment anything re-triggered a re-render. The equivalent had to live in JS, reading `window.innerWidth` at the moment each handler runs.
- **Fix, `draggable.tsx`**: added a small `getResponsiveMaxWidth(maxWidth)` helper (module-level, two named constants `RESPONSIVE_MAX_WIDTH_BREAKPOINT = 1440` and `RESPONSIVE_MAX_WIDTH_CAP = 800`) that returns `Math.min(maxWidth, 800)` once `window.innerWidth < 1440`, otherwise returns `maxWidth` unchanged. Wired into every place `maxWidth` was already being consulted: the float corner-resize handler, the docked left-edge-resize handler, and the existing window-`resize` listener (see "Viewport Containment" further up this doc) — which now also proactively shrinks an already-wider docked/float panel down to the tightened cap even when there's no viewport-overflow at all (previously that listener only reacted to actual edge-overflow, so a 900px-wide panel sitting comfortably inside a 1600px window wouldn't have shrunk on its own as the window narrowed past 1440px without this addition).
- **Shrink-only, matching existing viewport-containment behavior**: like the rest of the viewport-containment logic already in this file, the cap only ever tightens — resizing the window back up past 1440px does not grow an already-shrunk panel back to its previous width. Consistent with "browser resize never restores a size the user didn't explicitly set."
- **Flagging, not fixing**: this is entirely internal to `Draggable`, with no new prop and no consumer-side change needed — every panel built on `DraggablePanel`/`AiPanel`/`AgentNotifications` in all three repos gets the 1440px→800px behavior automatically.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` in lyra-ui (only pre-existing known error categories, nothing new), `storybook build` (clean), and `vite build` in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` (clean — no API surface changed, so this was a smoke check).

### Queue widget cards redesigned: Contacts/Agents metrics + live-ticking, simulated numbers (`agent-next-gen-v1`)
- **Request**: "update the queue dashboard cards to display the content depicted in the screenshot - have the Queue wait time & contacts update in real time but simulate the numbers." The screenshot showed each of the four queue cards (Digital/Inbound Voice/Voicemail/Work Item) with an HH:MM:SS "Wait Time" subhead and two metric boxes reading "Contacts" and "Agents" — not the "Skills"/"Contacts" pair the cards showed before.
- **`DashboardQueue` (lyra-ui, `dashboard-queue.tsx`)**: added a required `agentsCount: number` to `DashboardQueueItem`; the "cards" variant's `metrics` now reads `[{ value: contactsCount, label: "Contacts" }, { value: agentsCount, label: "Agents" }]` instead of Skills/Contacts. The "accordion" variant is untouched (still Skills/Contacts via `skillsCount`/`contactsCount`) — the screenshot only concerned the card layout, and `skillsCount` stays on the type for that variant's sake. `wait`'s doc comment now notes it can be any pre-formatted string (e.g. `"00:02:34"`, not just `"1m"`) — `DashboardQueue` just renders whatever string it's given, so the HH:MM:SS format below is a consumer choice, not something this component needed to change to support.
- **Type-contract ripple, kept to two other spots**: `agentsCount` being required meant every other `DashboardQueueItem[]` construction needed the field too, or the whole design system would fail to typecheck: lyra-ui's own `DashboardQueue.stories.tsx` `QUEUE_ITEMS` (updated to match the new screenshot exactly: Digital 8/3, Inbound Voice 0/2, Voicemail 3/3, Work Item 5/11 contacts/agents, wait now HH:MM:SS) and `agent-dashboard.tsx`'s `AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_ITEMS` (same static values — `contactsCount` here is still derived via `sumInQueue` from `AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS`, and happened to already sum to the exact new screenshot figures with no data changes needed, confirming the underlying sub-item counts hadn't drifted). No live-ticking behavior was added to `agent-dashboard.tsx` — that's the promoted `AgentDashboard` template used by `lyra-ux-templates` and lyra-ui's own `Templates/Dashboards` story, and the user's request was specifically about `agent-next-gen-v1`'s own Home tab; adding a ticking-clock effect to a shared, currently-static template wasn't asked for, so it was left as plain (but now correctly-shaped) static data.
- **Live simulation, `agent-next-gen-v1`-only (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`)**: this is where "wait time & contacts update in real time but simulate the numbers" is actually implemented.
  - `QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS` (module constant) renamed to `INITIAL_QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS` (seed) and moved into `useState` (`queueSubItems`) inside the component, so it can be mutated over time.
  - A `setInterval` every 4s nudges one random sub-item's `inQueueCount` by -1/0/+1 (clamped to [0, 20]) — the *only* thing randomized. `sumInQueue` (now `(items: QueueSubItem[]) => number`, no longer keyed by a module-level id lookup) still derives the widget's Contacts number and the side panel's "In Queue" figures from this one shared list, so — same fix this pattern was originally built for (see the `sumInQueue` doc comment) — the two numbers can never disagree, even while ticking.
  - Added `formatWaitTime` (HH:MM:SS, mirrors `formatElapsedTime`'s MM:SS) and a `QUEUE_WAIT_BASE_SECONDS` baseline per queue (154/0/120/24, matching the screenshot's 00:02:34/00:00:00/00:02:00/00:00:24). Wait time = `QUEUE_WAIT_BASE_SECONDS[id] + clockTick` — reusing the same shared 1-second `clockTick` counter that already drives every open channel's live elapsed-time display, rather than a second independent timer.
  - Wait is pinned to `00:00:00` whenever that queue's `contactsCount` is 0 (rather than ticking upward regardless), matching the screenshot's own Inbound Voice row (0 contacts, 00:00:00 wait) — a small realism touch: nothing waits if nothing's queued.
  - `LATEST_CONTACTS` (module constant) removed; its unchanging fields (id/name/icon/status/channel/caseId/interactions) moved to `LATEST_CONTACTS_STATIC`, and a new `latestContacts` `useMemo` (deps: `queueSubItems`, `clockTick`) merges those with the derived `contactsCount`/`skillsCount`/`wait` and the static `agentsCount` (`AGENTS_COUNT_BY_QUEUE`, seeded 3/2/3/11 — no underlying per-queue "agents" list exists to derive it from, unlike Contacts/Skills) every render. Every render-body reference to the old module constants (`DashboardQueue`'s `items`, the interior panel's `headerTitle` lookup, the side panel's sub-item list) now reads `latestContacts`/`queueSubItems` instead.
- **Flagging, not fixing**: the live/simulated behavior is `agent-next-gen-v1`-only per the request's own scope; `lyra-ux-templates` and lyra-ui's `Templates/Dashboards` story keep static (but now correctly Contacts/Agents-shaped) queue data.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` in lyra-ui (only pre-existing known error categories, nothing new), `storybook build` (clean, including the updated `QueueCards` story), `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` (only pre-existing known error categories, clean builds).

### "CSAT Score" → "Overall Performance" (percentage) on the Performance card (`agent-next-gen-v1`)
- **Request**: "in the performance card replace CSAT score with Overall Performance and make the value a percentage."
- **Fix, in `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s own `PerformanceSummaryCard`** (this app's hand-rolled version — not lyra-ui's promoted `AgentDashboard`/`PerformanceSummaryCard`, which has its own identical-looking "CSAT Score" row left untouched since the request wasn't about that shared template): `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE`'s `csat` field renamed to `overallPerformance`, values changed from a 0–5 rating to a pre-formatted percentage (today "96%", yesterday "92%", last7 "94%", custom "—" — same relative ordering the old 4.8/4.6/4.7 ratings had). The row's label changed from "CSAT Score" to "Overall Performance"; the value binding changed from `{data.csat}` to `{data.overallPerformance}`.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` in `agent-next-gen-v1` (only pre-existing known error categories, nothing new), clean `vite build`.
- **Follow-up, same session**: "Rename cases resolved to Assignments Resolved in the Performance card." Label-only change (`casesResolved` field name and its data left as-is, since only the display text was asked for) — "Cases Resolved" → "Assignments Resolved". Verified with a clean `vite build`.

### Queue drill-down panels: "{n} Skills" header subhead, "Longest Wait Time:" label, agent-badge tooltips
- **Request** (with a screenshot of the "Digital" queue's drill-down panel): "for all Queue panels - add a subhead to the panel header that says how many skills are in the Queue (in the case of the screenshot it would be '4 Skills') - then update the wait: to say Longest Wait Time: and add hover states to the agent 3 icons that say: for the green check: available agents, for the yellow: Working Agents and for the red: Unavailable Agents." "All Queue panels" meant every place this drill-down exists — `agent-next-gen-v1`'s own hand-rolled version and lyra-ui's shared `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown` (used by `lyra-ux-templates` and lyra-ui's own `Templates/Dashboards` story) both needed the identical three changes.
- **`agent-next-gen-v1` (`AgentNextGenPage.tsx`)**: the `InteriorPanel` rendering the queue drill-down already supported `headerSubhead` (an existing lyra-ui prop, no component change needed) but wasn't passing one — added `headerSubhead={selectedQueueId ? \`${queueSubItems[selectedQueueId].length} Skills\` : undefined}`, the same count already backing the queue widget's own Skills metric, just surfaced in the panel header too. Changed each sub-item row's "Wait: {item.wait}" to "Longest Wait Time: {item.wait}". Wrapped each of the three Available/Working/Unavailable `Icon` badges in its own `<Tooltip>` ("Available Agents" / "Working Agents" / "Unavailable Agents").
- **lyra-ui's shared `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown` (`agent-dashboard.tsx`)**: same "Wait:" → "Longest Wait Time:" and per-badge `Tooltip` changes — this component owns the sub-item rows for both its consumers, so this one edit covers `lyra-ux-templates` and the `Templates/Dashboards` story automatically. The "{n} Skills" subhead couldn't go here, though — the drill-down component only renders the panel's *body* (the consumer's own `InteriorPanel` owns the header) — so each `InteriorPanel` call site needed its own `headerSubhead`: added to `lyra-ux-templates`'s `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (using the newly-imported `AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS`) and lyra-ui's own `Dashboards.stories.tsx`, both computing the same `${AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS[id].length} Skills` string agent-next-gen-v1 does.
- **Flagging, not fixing**: `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` and `LeftNav.stories.tsx` don't render any queue drill-down at all (confirmed via search), so nothing there needed touching.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p .` in lyra-ui (only pre-existing known error categories, nothing new), `storybook build` (clean, including the updated `Dashboards` story), `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` in both `agent-next-gen-v1` and `lyra-ux-templates` (only pre-existing known error categories, clean builds).

## Typography Presets Added This Session
- `.lyra-body-xs` / `.lyra-body-xs-emphasis`: `font-size: 10px; line-height: 14px;` (weight 400 / 500), `letter-spacing: 0.01rem` — one step below `lyra-body-sm` (12px/16px), added for small numeric labels (e.g. `InteractionNavItem`'s compact-mode elapsed-time text) that need to be smaller than any existing preset. Added to both lyra-tokens.css and storybook.css (kept in sync, per convention), and registered in Typography.stories.tsx's `presets` table so it shows up in the "Presets" story alongside every other size.

## Important Patterns
- **Text capitalization — use this for any new copy unless the user specifies otherwise:**
  1. Labels (button text, input labels, input placeholders, chip/tag labels, etc.) — **Title Case: every word capitalized**, e.g. "Wait Time", "Select Agent". (Previously worded here as "first-letter capitalized (each word capitalized)" — that phrase reads, on its own, as "capitalize only the string's first letter," the opposite of what it actually meant, and genuinely caused a misapplied fix in Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard: card titles/subheads and the page header were flipped to sentence case, reverted back to Title Case once the mixup was caught. Reworded to remove the ambiguity — say "Title Case," not "first-letter capitalized," everywhere this convention is referenced.)
  2. Body copy — sentence case (only the first word/proper nouns capitalized, e.g. "This channel is already open with this contact.").
  3. Tooltip copy — sentence case, same as body copy.
  4. Header text — Title Case, same convention as labels.
  Caught in practice on `DashboardQueue`'s "cards" header, which read "Wait time: {wait}" (lowercase "time") while `CreateNew`'s skill-row queue text used the correct "Wait Time" — fixed by capitalizing `DashboardQueue`'s label to match (see the `CreateNew` entry above).
- **Before reaching for a vanilla Tailwind utility (spacing, sizing, radius, etc.) in any lyra-ui component or story, check other lyra-ui stories/components first for the established pattern/token.** Case in point: `InteractionNavItem`'s status badge was sized with raw Tailwind `h-3.5 w-3.5` (intended as 12×12px) — `h-3.5`/`w-3.5` is actually 14px (0.875rem); `h-3`/`w-3` is the one that's 12px (0.75rem). Skipping this check produced a badge that measured 14×14 in devtools when 12×12 was intended, and wasn't caught until the user inspected it directly. Grepping sibling components (e.g. other badge-dot implementations like `ActionIconButton`/`NotificationsBell`) or checking the `lyra-radius-*`/spacing scale before picking a raw utility value would have caught the size mismatch immediately.
- Always reuse existing components (Select, SearchInput, Checkbox, Button) — never rebuild custom versions
- **This applies inside `__stories__` files too, even for a purely illustrative/non-functional example.** The `Menu.stories.tsx` "Width Scale" story's `md` example was first built with a hand-rolled fake search bar (`<div><Search icon /><span>Search…</span></div>`) instead of the real `Input` component — despite the exact same file (`agent-profile.tsx`) it was citing as the real-world reference already using `<Input startIcon={<Search .../>} placeholder="Search statuses" />` right there to copy from. Being non-functional/decorative is not a reason to skip using the real component — fixed by rendering the actual `Input` with the same `startIcon` prop. This is exactly the "composition over reimplementation" rule from `CONTRIBUTING.md` §3 (see also §1, "Use Lyra components as designed — never hard-code"), and it was violated in a story added specifically to document/enforce a different convention (the width scale) in the same sitting — check every element of a story against `src/index.ts`, not just the component the story is nominally about.
- **lyra-ux-templates must import UI atoms from `@nicecxone/lyra-ui`, never from local `src/components/ui/*` reimplementations.** A Login template (Phone Setup card with radio group + phone number field) was first built with ad hoc local copies of Radio/Input/Button/Checkbox in the templates repo. They visually diverged from the real Storybook components (radio dot vs. filled circle indicator, plain text input vs. the real `PhoneInput` with country selector/mask/validation). Fix: always check `lyra-ui/src/index.ts` and that component's `__stories__` file *before* building template UI — use `RadioGroup`/`RadioGroupItem` (`radio.tsx`) and `PhoneInput` (`phone-input.tsx`) directly, both of which already support built-in `label` props (no need to hand-roll `<label>` wrappers). If a lyra-ui component doesn't exist yet for something a template needs, that's a signal to add it to lyra-ui (per Scope Rules below) rather than approximate it locally in the template.
- **When adding a new icon button as a sibling to an existing row of icon buttons (AppHeader trigger rows, toolbar rows, etc.), match the row's existing `ActionIconButton` `size` — don't default to whatever `size` was last used elsewhere or leave the prop unset.** Concrete incident: `agent-next-gen-v1`'s AppHeader "Help" and "Ask AI" triggers were added as `size="xl"` (44px), sitting next to `NotificationsBell`, which is a separate hand-rolled component whose own button is hardcoded `h-10 w-10` — exactly `ActionIconButton`'s `size="lg"` (40px). The mismatch was never a deliberate choice, just each button's implicit default at the time it was written, and it went unnoticed until the user caught it visually ("make the size of the AI and ? icon buttons the same size as the notifications button"). Fix: measure the actual sibling(s) already in the row (check for a hardcoded height/width class if it's not `ActionIconButton`-based, like `NotificationsBell`) and pick the matching `size` variant explicitly — treat "what size are the components next to this one" as a required check before adding any new sibling to an existing icon-button pattern, the same way color/spacing tokens are checked against existing usage elsewhere in this doc.
- **Any new `<TabList>`, in lyra-ui or either consuming app, should default to `overflowMenu` unless it has its own bespoke narrower collapse strategy** (the only current exception is `ChannelTab`'s record-header conversation bar, which sheds text at 480px/320px instead of moving tabs into a menu — turning `overflowMenu` on there too would fire at 991px and pre-empt that behavior first). See the `TabList` "Overflow Menu" entry and its follow-up below, and `CONTRIBUTING.md` §11's "CSS container-query pattern" subsection.
- New Tailwind color utilities must be added to BOTH `tailwind-preset.ts` AND `tailwind.config.js`
- New CSS variables must be added to BOTH `lyra-tokens.css` AND `storybook.css`
- **`.lyra-heading-lg` had drifted out of sync between the two files** (`lyra-tokens.css` had `font-weight: 600`, `storybook.css` and `Typography.stories.tsx`'s docs table both said `500`) — caught by comparing `PageHeader`'s title weight in a real consuming app against lyra-ui's own Storybook side by side. User confirmed `500` (the Storybook/docs value) as correct; fixed by changing `lyra-tokens.css` to `500` to match. This is exactly the kind of two-file drift the "kept in sync" convention above exists to prevent — when comparing a rendered value against "what lyra-ui says," check both files (and the Typography story's docs table) before assuming which one is stale.
- New color tokens should be added to `Colors.stories.tsx` (swatches + alias table)
- **Using the "correct" component isn't enough — the exact markup shape shown in its `__stories__` file matters too.** On the Login template, `Checkbox` was used with its `label` prop (`<Checkbox label="Save Preferences" ... />`), which is valid API but internally wraps the input in an extra `div.flex.items-center.h-5`. Every plain "checkbox + text label" story (`Default`, `Checked`, `Interactive`, `WithSecondaryText`, `StateMatrix`, etc.) instead renders a bare `<Checkbox />` beside a manual `<label className="flex items-center gap-2">…</label>` — the `label` prop path is only ever demonstrated for `Required`/`Readonly` states. Using the prop form produced DOM that didn't match Storybook and looked like a bug. Fix: for a plain labeled checkbox, always use `<label className="flex items-center gap-2 cursor-pointer"><Checkbox .../><span className="lyra-body-md text-lyra-fg-default">…</span></label>`, matching the `Default` story exactly.
- **Every modal's outer card must use `Container variant="modal"`, never a hand-rolled div with its own background class.** The home-screen welcome modal in `lyra-ux-templates` was first built with a plain `<div className="... bg-lyra-bg-surface-base ...">`, while `LoginCard` (also a modal) uses `Container variant="modal"` (`bg-lyra-bg-surface-overlay`). In light mode both tokens are `#ffffff` so the mismatch was invisible; in dark mode `bg-surface-base` is `#1f1f1e` and `bg-surface-overlay` is `#2e2e2e` — visibly different shades of "black," so the two modals looked inconsistent side by side. Fixed by switching the welcome modal to `Container variant="modal"` too. Any new modal should do the same from the start rather than approximating the look with a raw div.

### Post-layout QA checklist (do this before calling any template "done")
1. **Re-scan every field/element just built against `lyra-ui/src/index.ts`, one at a time, as a dedicated final pass** — not a one-time check done earlier for a different sub-task. Checking the export list once at the *start* of a build (e.g. while researching an unrelated component) doesn't cover what gets written later in the same session — it's easy to fall back on a plausible-looking generic component from memory instead of re-verifying each field. Concrete incident: `lyra-form-generator`'s `FormDetailPage.tsx` used a plain `Input` for both `Phone` and `Email` fields even though `PhoneInput` (country/dial-code selector, mask formatting) and `EmailInput` (mail icon, built-in validation) already existed and had been seen in `index.ts` minutes earlier for a different lookup — the generic `Input` compiled and rendered fine, so nothing forced a second look. For every label-shaped/data-typed field (phone, email, currency, date, etc.), confirm there isn't a more specific component before settling for `Input`/`Textarea`.
2. Open each lyra-ui component's `__stories__/*.stories.tsx` file for every component used in the new layout.
3. For each one, find the story that matches the case being built (plain vs. required vs. readonly vs. grouped, etc.) and copy its exact prop/markup shape — don't just satisfy the TS prop types.
4. Inspect the rendered DOM (e.g. browser devtools) and diff it mentally against the story's structure — watch for extra wrapper `div`s, different class lists, or different parent/child nesting than the canonical story produces.
5. If a prop *could* produce two different DOM shapes for the same visual result (e.g. `Checkbox`'s `label` prop vs. a manual `<label>`), default to whichever shape the majority of stories for that component use, not just whichever is more convenient to type.

### Every data table built from a screenshot/prompt includes baseline Column Options + Refresh
**Always include a Column Options (`ColumnToggle`) control and a Refresh action on any `Table`/`TableToolbar` generated from a screenshot or prompt — even if the reference screenshot doesn't show them.** These are treated as baseline table functionality, not screenshot-specific features: `ColumnToggle` (`actions` slot, wired to a `visibleCols` Set + `allColumnDefs` derived from the column config) and a `refresh` entry in `actionDefs` (icon `RefreshCw`, `onClick` handler), exactly as `DesktopDesignsPage.tsx` demonstrates. Concrete incident: `Outbound-Campaigns`' `OutboundCampaignsPage.tsx` was rebuilt to match a "Campaigns" data-table screenshot that showed neither control, and both were initially omitted to match the screenshot exactly — user corrected this, since matching a screenshot's *content* (columns, data, filters) shouldn't mean dropping standard table chrome the screenshot simply didn't happen to capture. Fixed by adding both back in.

### Never drop a component's transitions or responsive behavior when adding it to a prototype
When copying a reference story's usage of a component into a prototype, copy its **entire** styling contract — not just enough of it to satisfy the prop types and look right in a static screenshot. Two concrete incidents in the same `Outbound-Campaigns` `CampaignDetailsModal.tsx` build:
- **Responsiveness**: `TabList`'s 7-tab bar (General/Campaign Records/Interactions/State Breakdown/Segmentation/Results/Change History) was added without its `overflowMenu` prop. `TabList` already has a native responsive-collapse behavior for exactly this case (see "TabList gains a native `overflowMenu` responsive-collapse behavior" above) — without it, narrow tab labels just wrap onto two lines instead of collapsing to an active-tab + "N More" dropdown. User caught this from a screenshot of wrapped tab text. Fixed by adding `overflowMenu` to the `TabList`.
- **Transitions**: the same modal's fullscreen toggle copied `Modal.stories.tsx`'s "Fullscreen (toggleable)" story's width/height-swap logic but dropped its `transition-all duration-200` (and `rounded-lyra-lg` for the non-fullscreen state) — so the reference story animates smoothly between sizes and the copy snapped instantly. User caught this by testing the toggle interactively, not from a static screenshot, since a screenshot can't show a missing transition.
- **Standing rule**: before shipping any component copied from a `__stories__` reference, diff the new usage against the story's `className`/props line by line — animation/transition classes and responsive props (`overflowMenu`, `transition-*`, `duration-*`, `animate-in`/`animate-out`, etc.) are exactly the kind of thing that's invisible in a single static screenshot and easy to silently drop while focused on getting the visible structure right. A static screenshot is necessary but not sufficient for verifying a component was ported faithfully — also check it interactively (toggle it, resize it) before calling it done.

### Every flex sibling around a `Table` needs its own horizontal padding — `px-*` on `TabList` doesn't cascade to `TableToolbar`/the table wrapper/`TableFooter`
A page built from `TabList` + `TableToolbar` + `Table` + `TableFooter` (the shape `DataManagement.stories.tsx`'s Designer page demo uses) has no shared padded ancestor around the toolbar/table/footer — each is its own flex child stacked in a `flex flex-col` column, so padding added to only one of them doesn't reach the others. Concrete incident: a Segmentation prototype was built with `<TabList overflowMenu className="px-4">` but no padding on `<TableToolbar>`, the `<div>` wrapping `<Table>`, or `<TableFooter>` — the tabs sat with a left inset while the search bar, table rows, and pagination footer ran edge-to-edge, flush against the container. Caught from a screenshot. Fixed by matching `DataManagement.stories.tsx`'s real reference exactly: `px-6` on all four — `TabList`, `TableToolbar`, the `Table`-wrapping `div` (alongside its `flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-auto`), and `TableFooter` — since `TableToolbar`/`TableFooter` already carry their own vertical `py-3` internally (table.tsx) and only need horizontal padding from the consumer. General rule: when composing this tabs+toolbar+table+footer shape from scratch, check `DataManagement.stories.tsx` for the real padding pattern rather than padding only the first element added — every sibling in the column needs its own `px-*`.

### When a screenshot/prompt doesn't clearly match an existing component
If a prototype's design is being changed from a screenshot or a prompt and either (a) it's unclear which existing lyra-ui component a description in the prompt is referring to, or (b) nothing in lyra-ui visually matches what the screenshot shows, ask the user which component to use, or whether to build something new — don't guess. If "build something new" is chosen, keep the new UI local to the consuming app/prototype; do not add it to lyra-ui's own component library until the user explicitly asks for it to be promoted there. (Mirrors the existing AppMenu-in-new-apps guidance under Known Debt: don't default into a guessed mismatch, and don't default into an unrequested library addition either.)

### Every `lyra-ux-templates` page fades in on load
Every top-level page component in `lyra-ux-templates` (the components rendered directly by `App.tsx`'s router — currently `AgentNextGenPage`, `LoginPage`, `OutboundEngagementPage`, `DesktopDesignsPage`, `ShellPage`, and the shared Header/Sidebar shell in `App.tsx` itself) fades its entire root element in on mount, for a bit of polish rather than content just snapping into view.
- Implementation: add `animate-in fade-in-0 duration-500` to the page's single outermost returned element's `className` (the `tailwindcss-animate` plugin utilities already used elsewhere in both repos for Popover/Overlay/etc. — no extra JS or state needed, it's a plain CSS animation that plays once on mount).
- **When creating any new page/route in `lyra-ux-templates`, add these three classes to its outermost element as part of building it** — don't treat it as optional polish to add later.
- Since React only replays a mount animation when the element/component actually (re)mounts, this only self-triggers correctly if each distinct page is its own component swapped in by the router (as they are here). If a new page is ever nested *inside* another already-mounted page's JSX instead of being a router-level swap, the fade won't retrigger on that inner navigation — put the classes on whichever element actually mounts fresh for that transition.

### After promoting a template block into a real lyra-ui component
When markup that used to live inline in a `lyra-ux-templates` page gets promoted into a proper `lyra-ui` component (e.g. `Divider`, `LoginCard`), that promotion isn't done until every consumer is switched over:
1. Search the templates repo for the old inline markup/text that the new component replaces (e.g. `grep` for a distinctive string or class combo from the old JSX) and confirm zero matches remain — a leftover duplicate is exactly the kind of drift this whole doc exists to prevent.
2. Replace the template's inline implementation with an import of the new component from `@nicecxone/lyra-ui`, passing only the props needed to fit that page (callbacks for routing/navigation, branding overrides, etc.) — the template file should shrink, not stay the same size with an import added on top.
3. Rebuild both repos (`tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui, `vite build` in lyra-ux-templates) to confirm the swap didn't silently drop behavior.
4. Record the new component in this file's Key Components list (props, defaults, gotchas) so the next pass doesn't have to rediscover them by reading the source.

### Left nav rail / AdminShell side panel must preserve open-vs-closed state across page navigation
Navigating between pages that share a nav rail or `AdminShell` side panel (e.g. `lyra-form-generator`'s `FormsPage.tsx` list view swapping for its `FormDetailPage.tsx` breadcrumb detail view) must never reset either panel's open/closed state to a hardcoded default — whichever state it was in immediately before the navigation, open or closed, is the state it should be in immediately after, in both directions.
- **Fixed incident**: `AdminShell`'s side panel used to only persist the *pinned* flag via a `${storageKeyPrefix}_panel_pinned` cookie (see `admin-shell.tsx`). `leftPanelOpen` was separate local state — it read its *initial* value from that same pin cookie on mount, but got toggled independently of it afterward via `handleLeftToggle` while pinned (collapsing/expanding without changing the pin). If the panel was pinned open and then collapsed via that toggle (still pinned, just closed), that closed state lived only in that mount's component state. Remounting `AdminShell` on a page swap (a fresh `<AdminShell>` element for `FormDetailPage` vs. `FormsPage`) re-initialized `leftPanelOpen` from the pin cookie alone and snapped it back open, discarding the toggled-closed state. Fixed by adding a second `${storageKeyPrefix}_panel_open` cookie that's written every time `handleLeftToggle`/`handleLeftPinToggle` change `leftPanelOpen`, and read (falling back to the pin cookie/`defaultLeftPinned`) on initial mount — so whichever open/closed state the panel was left in survives a remount.
- The app-level `LeftNav`/`Sidebar` rail in `lyra-form-generator`'s `App.tsx` is less exposed to this same remount reset today, since its `sidebarOpen` state is already lifted up to `App.tsx` and persisted via its own cookie (`lyra_sidebar_open`) rather than living inside a page component — but any future refactor that moves that state down into a per-page component would reintroduce the same bug.
- When building or reviewing this kind of breadcrumb-style navigation, verify explicitly in both directions: close the panel, navigate via breadcrumb, confirm it's still closed; open the panel, navigate, confirm it's still open — for both the side panel and the left nav rail.

### A `container-type: inline-size` root needs an explicit `w-full`, not just flex-stretch
Any element carrying a container-query wrap class (`container-type: inline-size` — `.lyra-container-grid-wrap`, `.lyra-form-grid-wrap`, etc.) needs an explicit, non-content-based width — `w-full` (or a definite width) — even when it looks like it should already get one from its parent's flex/grid stretch behavior. `AgentDashboard`'s root has `w-full max-w-[1200px] mx-auto lyra-container-grid-wrap`; when `FormTemplate` (form-template.tsx) was promoted out of `lyra-form-generator`'s `FormDetailPage.tsx`, the `w-full` was dropped, leaving just `max-w-[1000px] mx-auto flex flex-col gap-8 lyra-container-grid-wrap` (that class was later split into its own `.lyra-form-grid`/`.lyra-form-grid-wrap` — see the next entry — but this bug predates that split and applied the same either way). In `FormDetailPage.tsx` this div sits inside a plain block container, so ordinary `width: auto` block sizing masked the problem. But the new `Templates/Forms` Storybook story (`Forms.stories.tsx`) wraps it in a `flex flex-col` shell (matching `Templates/Dashboards`' own shell) — and relying on `align-items: stretch` alone to size an element that also has `container-type: inline-size` collapsed it to a near-zero width instead: every field inside rendered as a tiny square, confirmed at full desktop width in Storybook (so not a narrow-viewport issue). Root cause: an element with size containment can't use its own content to determine its size (avoids a circular dependency with the container queries reading that same size), and in at least this flex-stretch context the browser fell back to a content-based/collapsed size instead of honoring stretch — so the container-query root needs a width that's assigned some other, definite way. Fix: always pair any container-query wrap class with `w-full` (or another definite width) on the same element, regardless of what the surrounding layout looks like it should already provide.

### `FormTemplate` uses its own `.lyra-form-grid` thresholds, not `.lyra-container-grid`'s
`FormTemplate` (form-template.tsx) originally reused `.lyra-container-grid`/`.lyra-container-grid-wrap` (the same classes `AgentDashboard` uses, ≤991px 2-up / ≤767px 1-up). Per user request, its field rows now collapse at different, narrower thresholds (≤768px 2-up / ≤550px 1-up) tailored to a form's 2-4-field rows rather than a card grid — so this was split into its own `.lyra-form-grid`/`.lyra-form-grid-wrap` pair (see the "Form grid" block in lyra-tokens.css, kept in sync in storybook.css) rather than changing `.lyra-container-grid`'s thresholds directly, which would have also shifted `AgentDashboard`'s card rows. When a template-specific responsive tweak is requested, prefer adding a new dedicated class over editing a shared one used by other templates.

### `TabList` / `overflowMenu` — current state (consolidated)
Dated 2026-07-27. Current truth of the `TabList` responsive-collapse work; the full incident-by-incident history is archived.
- `overflowMenu` (tabs.tsx, opt-in, default `false`) collapses a `TabList` to "active tab + N More" (a real `Menu`-backed dropdown whose entries `.click()` the hidden real tabs; `overflowMoreLabel` overridable; the trigger is styled as a plain tab). Standing rule: every new `TabList` turns it on — no exception remains (`ChannelTab`'s bespoke text-shedding collapse and its `.lyra-channel-tab-list-wrap` CSS were removed; ChannelTab bars now use `overflowMenu` like every other tab bar).
- `overflowBreakpoint`: `"wide"` (default) collapses at ≤400px of the row's own container width (lowered from the original 991px); `"compact"` is content-aware — a hidden measurement clone (its wrapper needs `overflow-x-hidden`) + `ResizeObserver`, collapsing only when the tabs genuinely don't fit — for narrow/resizable hosts like a docked or interior panel.
- `"wide"`'s full row above 400px is horizontally scrollable (`overflow-x-auto lyra-scrollbar-hide`) with left/right chevrons built on the shared `useScrollChevrons`/`ScrollChevronButton` (scroll-chevron.tsx, `orientation: "horizontal"`) — continuous hover-driven scrolling, each chevron hidden when there's nothing further that way; native scroll still works. To keep that state reachable, `Tab` caps at `max-w-[22ch]` and `"wide"`'s full row disables per-tab shrink (`[&>[role='tab']]:flex-shrink-0`); `fullWidth` mode and the collapsed row's active-tab slot both reset `max-w-none` — all load-bearing, don't remove.
- CSS: `.lyra-tab-overflow-full`/`.lyra-tab-overflow-collapsed` are ancestor-scoped under `.lyra-tab-overflow-wrap` (two-class specificity, so Tailwind's `.flex` can never win a cascade-order tie in any bundler), and `.lyra-tab-overflow-wrap` carries an unconditional `w-full` (a `container-type: inline-size` element can't size itself from its content). Rules live in both lyra-tokens.css and storybook.css.
- Consumer `className` reaches both the full row and `collapsedRowEl`. A horizontal inset (`px-4` etc.) goes directly on `TabList` itself — its own `border-b` stays full-bleed — never on a wrapping div; layout/sizing classes (`flex-1 min-w-0`) go on a consumer-owned wrapping `<div>` instead of patching tabs.tsx.
- A tab strip is pinned to the top of its scrolling container: `shrink-0` on the strip, kept outside the wrapper carrying `overflow-y-auto`.
Supersedes 14 archived entries — see PROJECT_SUMMARY_ARCHIVE.md, "TabList / overflowMenu saga (archived)".

### `InteriorPanel` opens at `maxWidth` by default, remembering any manual resize
Per request, `InteriorPanel` (interior-panel.tsx) now opens fully expanded (`maxWidth`, 425 by default) rather than its old fixed `width` default (350) — unless it's been drag-resized before, in which case it reopens at that remembered width instead. Resizing while mounted already worked via existing local state (`usePanelDragResize`'s `dragWidth`, which survives the panel merely toggling `open` false/true since `AdminShell` never unmounts it just for that) — the gap was surviving an actual remount, e.g. navigating from `FormDetailPage.tsx` back to `FormsPage.tsx`'s list and back again, which unmounts and remounts the whole component tree. Fixed with a new `storageKey?: string` prop: when set, the resized width is written to a cookie on every `onWidthChange` and read back as the initial width on mount (falling back to `maxWidth` if nothing's stored yet, or to an explicit `width` prop if one was passed). `AdminShell` wires this automatically for its own interior panel, keyed by *both* `storageKeyPrefix` and `interiorPanelTitle` (not the prefix alone) — `FormsPage.tsx` and `FormDetailPage.tsx` intentionally share one `storageKeyPrefix` (so the left panel's pin state stays consistent across both), but their interior panels are different panels ("Details" vs. "Settings") that need independently remembered widths, not one shared value bleeding between them.

### An `Accordion` inside a form needs its own `max-w-[1000px] mx-auto` — capping only its content misaligns the header
`Accordion`'s root (accordion.tsx) is plain `w-full`; it has no opinion about a form's max-width and will stretch to fill whatever it's placed in. Inside `FormTemplate` itself this is a non-issue — the accordion sits directly under the template's own `max-w-[1000px] mx-auto lyra-form-grid-wrap` root, same as every other section, so it's automatically capped/centered (see the "Placement Information" accordion added to form-template.tsx as the reference example, with real input fields rather than display-only content). Outbound-Campaigns' Campaign Details modal (`CampaignDetailsModal.tsx`) doesn't have that outer root — its "General" tab is just a wide `TabPanel` — so the `Accordion`'s width had to be capped explicitly. The first pass only put `max-w-[1000px] mx-auto` on the `<div>` inside the accordion's `content`, which centered the fields but left the accordion's own header/trigger row spanning the full tab width — header and content visibly misaligned. Fixed by moving `max-w-[1000px] mx-auto` onto the `Accordion` component's own `className` prop instead, capping the whole thing (header row included) as one unit. General lesson: when a component with its own "chrome" (a header/trigger row, a border, etc.) needs to match a form's width, the max-width has to go on that component's own root — capping only the content inside it will cap the content but not the chrome around it.

### `Label`'s `readonly` prop is for a label paired with a real (locked) input, not for label/value display pairs with no input at all
When `CampaignDetailsModal.tsx`'s General Information fields were converted from a hand-rolled `Field` (label span + value div) to real `Label` + `supportingText` (label.tsx), the conversion also passed `readonly`, reasoning that these are "read-only" in the sense of not being editable. That was the wrong read of the prop: `readonly` exists to style a label attached to an actual `<input>`/`<Select>`-type control that's locked but still presented as an input (label.tsx dims it to `text-lyra-fg-secondary` to signal that). These fields have no input at all — they're a plain label/value pair — so the caption should render exactly like any other field label (`text-lyra-fg-default`, matching every `Input`/`Select` label in `FormTemplate`), not the muted "this input is locked" color. Fixed by dropping `readonly` from `LabelField`; the value line underneath was already correctly secondary via `supportingText`'s own built-in styling, that part didn't need changing.

### Prefer an existing real component over a hand-rolled one — check `Input`/`Label`/etc. before building a custom display row
`CampaignDetailsModal.tsx`'s Uploaded Date / Last Modified On row used the local hand-rolled `Field` component (a caption `<span>` + value `<div>`) even though these are exactly what `Input`'s existing `readonly` state (input.tsx) already renders: a bordered, muted input box holding a locked value, label included. `Field` only ever should have existed for content `Input`/`Label` genuinely can't express (an icon-plus-text value, or an inline action button — see the `Field` doc comment in this file) — a plain timestamp string had no reason not to just be `<Input label="Uploaded Date" value={timestamp} readonly />`. General rule going forward: before writing a new local display component for a label/value row (or any similar bit of UI), check whether `Input`, `Label`, or another existing lyra-ui component already covers it in some prop/variant (a `readonly` state, a `disabled` state, etc.) — reach for that first, and only hand-roll something new for the leftover cases an existing component's API genuinely can't cover.

### A screenshot of an older/differently-styled interface still means "use the matching real component," not "reproduce this exact shape"
When a screenshot (or a description of one) shows a component that looks visually different from any current lyra-ui component — a different corner radius, size, shape, or color — the right first read is "what existing component is this actually asking for, functionally, and what prop/className gets its *current* look to match," not "hand-roll new markup that reproduces this picture." Concrete incident: asked to add "an icon button" to the bottom-left of Outbound-Campaigns' left nav, from a screenshot of an older interface showing a filled blue circular "Online Help" icon button. First pass built an entirely new, hand-rolled `<button>` — its own duplicated background/hover/active/focus classes, its own `Tooltip` wrap — reasoning that a filled circle needed a brand-new "launcher" component, since no current lyra-ui component is literally circular. That reasoning was backwards: every one of those classes is exactly `Button`'s existing "default" (primary) variant (button.tsx) — hover/active/focus/tooltip behavior didn't need reimplementing by hand at all (`Button`'s own `isIconVariant && title` branch already turns a `title` prop into both the accessible name and a real `Tooltip` for free). Fixed by rebuilding it as a real `Button` (see the sizing follow-up entry directly below for how its *size* went through two more wrong guesses before landing on the right one, and the shape follow-up entry after that for how the corner radius went through its own wrong guess too). General rule: an older/external screenshot's shape (rounder corners, a different size, a color) is a styling detail to reach via an existing component's props/className, not evidence the design system needs a whole new component to match it — treat "this doesn't look like anything current" as a prompt to ask *how close* an existing component gets with minimal overrides before treating it as a from-scratch build, per CONTRIBUTING.md §1 and the "Prefer an existing real component" entry above.

### Picking a real size token isn't enough — it has to be the one that matches this button's actual neighbors
Reusing an existing component fixes hand-rolled markup; it doesn't by itself fix a wrong size guess, and a size pulled from thin air is a different, easy-to-miss failure mode from using the wrong component entirely. Concrete incident, the same `OnlineHelpButton` build as the entry above: the first draft's hand-rolled `<button>` was sized `h-12 w-12` (48px) — not one of `Button`'s actual icon sizes at all (`icon-sm`/`icon-md`/`icon-lg`/`icon-xl`/`icon-2xl`, button.tsx), just an arbitrary number that looked about right. Rebuilding it as a real `Button` fixed the hand-rolled-markup problem, but the size chosen for it — `icon-2xl` (44px) — was picked for sounding like the biggest/most "launcher"-appropriate tier, not by checking anything. `icon-2xl` is specifically the AppHeader-only size (button.tsx's own doc comment: `ActionIconButton`/`NotificationsBell`'s row), a different context — this button actually sits in the *same rail* as Monitor/Configure/Review, whose own buttons are `h-9 w-9` (`icon-lg`, 36px). The already-standing rule for exactly this ("When adding a new icon button as a sibling to an existing row of icon buttons... match the row's existing size" — this same section, above) predates this incident and still got missed, twice in a row on the same component. General rule: after confirming *which component* to reuse, treat picking its size as its own separate check — find the actual sibling elements this new button will sit beside, read their real rendered/class-based size, and match that specific one, rather than picking whichever named size sounds right for the button's general "importance" or role.

### A component's real shape (corner radius, etc.) isn't a free-form override either — check what the component actually supports
Same `OnlineHelpButton` build, a third wrong guess in the same incident: once rebuilt as a real `Button` (see the entry above) and sized correctly (see the entry above), it still carried a leftover `rounded-full` — reasoning that the original screenshot's rounder/circular corners were a legitimate one-off shape override, the same way a `className` can override a component's color or size. That reasoning doesn't hold for radius the way it does for other overrides: `buttonVariants` (button.tsx) hardcodes `rounded-lyra-sm` as the *only* corner treatment across every variant and size it defines — there's no circular/pill option anywhere in `Button`'s real API to opt into. Adding `rounded-full` wasn't selecting a documented alternative, it was inventing a value outside the component's actual scale — the same failure as the earlier fabricated `h-12` size (the entry above this one), just applied to shape instead of size. A `shadow-md` added alongside it was dropped for the same reason: not part of `Button`'s real treatment either. Fixed by removing both and reverting to plain `rounded-lyra-sm`. General rule: before adding *any* className override to a reused component — shape, shadow, spacing, whatever — check that component's own source for whether that property is actually meant to vary at all; a prop/variant that's hardcoded identically across every branch is a signal that no override belongs there, not an invitation to add one anyway.

### Card/grid responsiveness must be container-based, not viewport media queries — new `.lyra-metric-card-grid`/`.lyra-card-split` classes
Same screenshot as above also showed the Monitor dashboard's metric-card grid and its full-width summary card not responding to a narrower render width — a card's title and its "Last 30 days" filter chip were overlapping instead of the grid dropping to fewer columns. Root cause: the grid (`grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 xl:grid-cols-3`) and the summary card's stat/chart split (`sm:flex-row`, `sm:w-48`, `hidden sm:block`) all used Tailwind's default responsive prefixes — plain `@media` viewport breakpoints, which only react to the *browser window* resizing, not to the actual rendered width of the container these cards sit in (a left nav opening, a docked panel taking space, or anything else that narrows the content area without touching the window). This is exactly the class of bug `.lyra-container-grid`/`.lyra-form-grid`/`.lyra-metric-row` (all container-query-driven) already exist to prevent — the Monitor dashboard just didn't reach for one of them.
**Fix**: two new container-query classes added to lyra-tokens.css (and mirrored in storybook.css, per the standing "keep both files in sync" rule):
- `.lyra-metric-card-grid-wrap`/`.lyra-metric-card-grid` — a 3-up card grid (unlike `.lyra-container-grid`, which starts as one unconstrained row of however many cards there are; this one is meant for a grid that should wrap into rows of 3 from the start), collapsing to 2-up at ≤900px of its own width and 1-up at ≤500px. Kept as its own new class rather than repurposing `.lyra-container-grid`, same reasoning `.lyra-form-grid` was split out on its own: different templates need different thresholds/starting layouts, and changing a shared class out from under existing consumers (`AgentDashboard`'s card rows) isn't an option.
- `.lyra-card-split-wrap`/`.lyra-card-split` (+ `.lyra-card-split-divider`, `.lyra-card-split-fixed`) — a `DashboardCard`'s two-region `children` body (e.g. stat blocks beside a chart), row by default, stacking to a column at ≤480px of its own width. `.lyra-card-split-divider` hides the vertical rule between the two regions once stacked (a divider mid-column doesn't read as anything); `.lyra-card-split-fixed` is the optional fixed-width-in-row/full-width-when-stacked treatment for whichever region needs it (here, the stat-block column).
**General rule, now in both CLAUDE.md files**: card/grid layout responsiveness is always container-query-based in this design system, never a Tailwind `sm:`/`md:`/`lg:`/`xl:` viewport prefix — check for an existing container-query class first (`.lyra-container-grid`, `.lyra-form-grid`, `.lyra-metric-row`, `.lyra-metric-card-grid`, `.lyra-card-split`), and add a new dedicated one (mirrored in both lyra-tokens.css and storybook.css) if none of the existing ones fit, rather than reaching for a viewport breakpoint as a shortcut.

### `Tab` truncates its label and shows a tooltip, but only once genuinely truncated
Request: make `TabList`/`Tab` truncate a tab's label instead of wrapping/overflowing when the tab is narrower than its label needs (`fullWidth`'s equal-width columns being the clearest case, but also just a squeezed row before `TabList`'s own overflow handling — chevrons or the collapsed row — engages), and show the full label in a tooltip on hover.
**Implementation**: `Tab`'s label (`children`) now renders inside its own `<span ref={labelRef} className="min-w-0 truncate">` — `truncate` (Tailwind's `overflow-hidden text-ellipsis whitespace-nowrap`) for the ellipsis, `min-w-0` on both that span and the button itself so they can actually shrink below the label's natural content width in the first place (a flex item's default automatic minimum width would otherwise refuse to, the same family of gotcha the table-resize and `TabList`-wrapper entries elsewhere in this file already ran into). A `ResizeObserver` on `labelRef` compares `scrollWidth` to `clientWidth` (`isTruncated`) — real, measured truncation, not inferred from a specific pixel width, so it stays correct regardless of the label text or the tab's actual rendered width.
**Tooltip, only when truncated — via `disabled`, not conditional wrapping**: the whole button is always wrapped in `<Tooltip content={children} disabled={!isTruncated}>`, following the exact pattern `Button`'s own icon-variant tooltip already uses (build the interactive element once, conditionally wrap it) — except `Tooltip`'s own `disabled` prop is used here instead of Button's conditional-return style, because `isTruncated` changes at runtime (a resize can flip it either way) and `Tooltip`'s own doc comment is explicit about why: toggling `disabled` keeps the wrapper's shape constant, where conditionally mounting/unmounting the `<Tooltip>` itself would remount the button underneath it every time truncation state changes, breaking focus/interaction state for no reason.
**Cross-repo check**: `Tab`'s rendered output changed for every existing consumer (an extra wrapping `<Tooltip>`/`<span>`), including `ChannelTab` (`channel-row.tsx`, the `menuItems` consumer). `agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, and `lyra-form-generator` all still build clean after this change.
