### Whenever building a form or any form-like set of fields, consult `Input`/`Select`'s own Default playground controls first
`Input.stories.tsx` and `Select.stories.tsx` each have a "Default" story built as an interactive playground, not just a single fixed prop set — its controls enumerate every documented field variant these two components support: Required, With buttons, Buttons position (left/right/both), Button type (every real `Button` variant), Icon buttons (icon-only vs. text), Button size, Button count (1-3), Help, Error, and Max width (`Input` additionally has Label only and Horizontal, which don't apply to `Select`). Before building any form field from a screenshot or prompt — not just a single `Input`/`Select` in isolation — check this control list against what the design actually shows (a trailing edit-pencil icon next to a field, a primary-colored icon button, a help tooltip, an inline error, a capped width, etc.) and compose the field from that matching combination of real props, the same way the "Post-layout QA checklist" above already requires re-checking `src/index.ts` for the right component. Treat a form field that doesn't obviously match one of these documented states as a signal to ask (per "When a screenshot/prompt doesn't clearly match an existing component" above), not to hand-roll a one-off variant these controls don't yet cover — if the design genuinely needs something new, that's a signal to extend the playground/component, not approximate it inline.

### Resizable table columns — added via a shared `Table`-level width context, not local `TableHead` state
`Table` (table.tsx) is a flex-based "fake table," not a native `<table>`/`<colgroup>` — `TableRow` is just `display: flex`, and a column's width is a Tailwind flex-basis className (`flex-[2] min-w-[160px]`) repeated independently on that column's `TableHead` *and* every row's matching `TableCell`. Nothing syncs those automatically today; every consumer just has to keep them matching by hand (some, like Outbound-Campaigns' Campaign table, centralize this into one `columnConfig` object read by both header and body rows off the same `columnOrder`; others hardcode the className inline on each pair). That architecture is what resize had to work around: a resized width can't live inside `TableHead` alone, or the body cells for that column would never find out about it.

**Fix**: `Table` now owns a `{ columnKey: width }` map (`useColumnResize`, new hook at the top of table.tsx) and provides it via a new internal `ColumnResizeContext`. `TableHead`, `SortableTableHead`, and `TableCell` each gained an optional `columnKey` prop; when a `TableHead`/`SortableTableHead` also sets `resizable`, it renders a thin drag/keyboard handle (`ColumnResizeHandle`) on its right edge that writes into that shared map, and any cell (header or body) with a matching `columnKey` reads its width back out and applies `flex: 0 0 {width}px` as an inline style, overriding its own className-based flex-basis. This means a resize applies to the whole column — header and every row — the moment `columnKey` is set consistently, with no per-row wiring beyond that one prop. Existing `min-w-[Npx]` classNames keep working unchanged as a CSS floor even under the inline override.

Fully backward compatible: `resizable`/`columnKey`/`minWidth`/`maxWidth` are all new optional props, so every existing `<TableHead>`/`<TableCell>` in every consuming app keeps rendering exactly as before, off its own className, having touched none of this. `Table` also gained optional `columnWidths`/`onColumnWidthsChange` props (controlled/uncontrolled, same duality as `InteriorPanel`'s `storageKey` pattern) for a consumer that wants to persist resized widths — plain usage needs neither, the map is just internal state.

**The one real interaction risk**: `SortableTableHead` already uses native HTML5 drag-and-drop for column reorder on the whole `<th>` (`draggable`, `onDragStart`, etc.). A resize handle living inside that same cell needs its own gesture space, or grabbing it to resize would also kick off a column-reorder drag. Fixed by giving the handle `draggable={false}` (a child can override an ancestor's `draggable="true"` for drags starting on that child specifically — this is what actually stops the native drag, not event bubbling) plus `stopPropagation` on its own `mousedown`/`click` (blocks the cell's `onClick`-based sort toggle from also firing when the user's really grabbing the handle).

**Reference example wired end-to-end**: Outbound-Campaigns' Campaign table (`OutboundCampaignsPage.tsx`) already centralizes column width in one `columnConfig` object read by both header and body via `columnOrder`, making it the natural first real usage — `resizable`/`columnKey` added to both the sortable and plain `TableHead` branches, `columnKey` added to every `TableCell` branch, and each column's existing `min-w-[Npx]` (already in `col.flex`) reused as the resize's own min-width floor via a small regex helper (`minWidthFor`) rather than picking a second, possibly-inconsistent number. The `controls` column (fixed-width icon buttons — Refresh/Pause/Stop, `w-[120px] shrink-0`, no `min-w-[Npx]` to even clamp against) was deliberately left out of resize rather than letting it shrink to where the three buttons no longer fit. A consumer whose table *doesn't* already have a `columnConfig`-style single source of truth (e.g. `lyra-form-generator`'s `FormsPage.tsx`) would need to refactor to one before wiring resize the same way — that's consumer-side work, not something this change forces on tables that don't opt in.

New `Custom Primitives/Table` → `Resizable` Storybook story (Table.stories.tsx) demonstrates the drag handle + arrow-key resize on a plain (non-reorderable) table.

**Follow-up bug, reported via screenshot**: after resizing columns in Outbound-Campaigns' Campaign table, row separators appeared to vanish under whichever columns had scrolled into view — the report was "when columns are moved off the screen the separators disappear." Root cause: `TableRow` (and `TableGroupRow`) set `className="flex w-full border-b ..."` — `w-full` is a hard `width: 100%`, capping the row's own box at the table's un-overflowed width. Cells inside aren't clipped (`overflow: visible` is the default), so once resize pushes their total width past 100%, they keep rendering further right same as any overflowing flex content — but `border-bottom` paints at the edges of the row's own *box*, which stayed capped at the old 100%. The separator wasn't disappearing; it was accurately marking where the row's box used to end, while the (now-wider) content kept going past it. Same root issue on `Table`'s own `<table className="w-full ...">`. This was technically always latent (enough columns' `min-w-[Npx]` floors summing past the container could trigger it even pre-resize), but resize makes it trivial to trigger on purpose, which is what surfaced it.

**Fix**: changed `w-full` → `min-w-full` on `<table>` (table.tsx's `Table`), `TableRow`, and `TableGroupRow`. `min-w-full` means "at least 100%, but grow to fit content" instead of a hard cap — so a row whose resized cells need more than 100% now actually expands its own box to match, and its border tracks the real content width instead of stopping at the old boundary.

**Second follow-up bug, also reported via screenshot**: the row separator still looked broken after the fix above — this time a wide *blank gap* between two columns, not a border stopping short. Different root cause: resizing only pins the *dragged* column to a fixed pixel width; every other column keeps its original `flex-1`/`flex-[n]` proportional className, which still does exactly what flex-grow means — expand to absorb whatever space is freed up. Shrinking a couple of columns doesn't shrink the table; it force-feeds that freed space into whichever column is still flexible, ballooning it out with blank space (short content like "0%" sitting at the left of a now much-wider cell). Not a rendering bug — correct CSS flexbox behavior given the mixed fixed/flexible columns — but not what a "resize a column" gesture should do to its neighbors.

**Fix**: `useColumnResize` now has a `registryRef` (a `Map<columnKey, () => number>`) that every `TableHead`/`SortableTableHead` with a `columnKey` registers a natural-width getter into on mount (a new `registerColumn` context method; the getter isn't called until needed). The *first* resize in a table (`widthsRef.current` still empty) now freezes every registered column at its current natural width, not just the one being dragged (`freezeIfFirstResize`, called from both `startResize` and `nudge`) — so from that point on every column has an explicit pixel width, none of them still "growing to fill leftover space" behind the scenes, matching how spreadsheet/data-grid column resize is expected to behave. Subsequent resizes just update the single dragged column on top of that already-frozen map. Confined entirely to table.tsx (the registration effect, the registry, and the freeze check) — no consumer changes needed beyond what resizable columns already required.

**Third report, same screenshot symptom recurring**: after both fixes above, the row separator still appeared to stop partway across the row per a follow-up screenshot. Went looking for a way to verify visually instead of continuing to reason blind — got further than expected: downloaded a working Playwright Chromium headless-shell binary (`playwright install --only-shell chromium`, using `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0` to route around a `SELF_SIGNED_CERT_IN_CHAIN` error from the default download mirror), but it's missing system shared libraries (`libXdamage.so.1` etc.) that `apt-get install`/`apt-get download` can't fetch in this sandbox (package mirror returns 403, and there's no root to install even if it could). Tried `@sparticuz/chromium` (a self-contained Chromium build meant to avoid exactly this system-dependency problem) as a fallback — it downloaded fine over npm, but its bundled binary is x86_64 and this sandbox is aarch64, so it won't execute either. Live visual verification of this specific bug is not achievable in this environment.

Absent that, hardened one more real gap found by re-reading the CSS carefully: `TableHeader` (`<thead>`) and `TableBody` (`<tbody>`) never had an explicit `display: flex` — only flex-*item* properties (`flex-shrink-0`/`flex-1`) governing their own sizing as children of `<table>`, while relying on their native `table-header-group`/`table-row-group` UA default to arrange their own `<tr>` children. Since `<table>` itself is overridden away from `display: table`, a `table-row-group` box with no real table ancestor to belong to is a well-known area of cross-browser inconsistency rather than a reliably-specified fallback. Added explicit `flex flex-col` to both (at this point, still combined with `min-w-full`).

**User confirmed the bug persisted even after that** (restarted `npm run dev` to rule out stale HMR, same result) — meaning the actual root cause was still unaddressed. Re-derived the CSS from scratch rather than guessing again, and found the real mistake: **an explicit `min-width` does not add a content-aware floor on top of anything — it *replaces* the browser's automatic minimum size (flexbox's `min-width: auto` behavior, which *does* look at a flex item's own content, including a non-shrinking/overflowing descendant) with a fixed value.** `min-w-full` (`min-width: 100%`) was doing exactly what `w-full` (`width: 100%`) did — pinning the row's box to precisely 100%, just via a different CSS property — so it never actually let the row grow to fit a resized-wide cell; both previous "fixes" were cosmetically different but functionally identical mistakes. This is the same family of gotcha as `min-w-0` (needing to explicitly override the automatic minimum to let a flex item shrink *below* its content size) — just the mirror-image case of needing to *not* set an explicit minimum at all so a flex item can grow *past* 100% when its content needs to.

**Actual fix**: removed the `min-w-full`/`w-full` classes entirely from `Table`'s `<table>`, `TableHeader`, `TableBody`, `TableRow`, and `TableGroupRow` — left with no explicit `width`/`min-width` at all. `align-items: stretch` (the default on their flex-column ancestors) still sizes each of these to 100% as a baseline in the normal case, and flexbox's own automatic minimum size — content-aware by default, once nothing overrides it — grows them further when a resized column needs more room. No manual width class needed, and re-adding one (of any kind) would reintroduce the same class of bug.

Verified with `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors) and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (clean transform). Could not visually confirm in this sandbox — a working Playwright Chromium headless-shell was downloaded but is missing system shared libraries unreachable without root/blocked package mirrors, and a self-contained fallback (`@sparticuz/chromium`) turned out to be built for the wrong CPU architecture (x86_64 vs. this sandbox's aarch64) — but this fix is grounded in a specific, well-documented flexbox behavior (the same one behind why `min-w-0` is needed elsewhere to let a flex item shrink), not another structural guess.

**Fourth report: user confirmed via DevTools that the "no explicit width" fix above still didn't work.** Two screenshots settled it. First, a DOM inspector tree of the affected `<tr>` showed the code was live (no `w-full`/`min-w-full` anywhere) and every cell already had its own frozen `flex: 0 0 Wpx` — the `freezeIfFirstResize` fix from the second report was working correctly, ruling that out. Second, a plain page screenshot (after ruling out a false alarm — an initial screenshot's blue diagonal-hash overlay turned out to just be Chrome DevTools' own "selected flex item" highlight, not a rendering artifact) showed the actual bug directly: the row's **hover background** — its real painted box, not merely wherever a border happens to be drawn — stopped at the same short boundary as the missing separator. That's conclusive: the `<tr>` element's own box is still capped short in real Chrome rendering, which falsifies the automatic-minimum-size theory in practice even though it's textbook spec-correct — Chrome is apparently not reliably propagating a content-aware automatic minimum through this many nested flex levels (`table` → `tbody` → `tr` → `td`) for this case.

**Actual fix**: stopped relying on CSS to size the row at all. `useColumnResize` already tracks every resized column's exact pixel width in its `widths` map, so its return value now also includes a `totalWidth` — the sum of every currently-registered column's width, computed directly in JS (`undefined` until the first resize, since nothing's tracked yet at that point). `Table`'s `<table>` element applies this as a concrete inline `style={{ minWidth: resize.totalWidth }}` rather than any CSS class. This sidesteps the unreliable automatic-sizing mechanism entirely: a plain pixel `min-width` is a basic, universally-supported CSS clamp, not dependent on any browser's content-aware sizing heuristics, and the normal `align-items: stretch` behavior on the ancestor flex-column wrappers still reliably carries that width down through `<thead>`/`<tbody>`/`<tr>` without any of them needing an explicit width of their own. One acknowledged, harmless gap: `totalWidth` only sums *registered* (`columnKey`-bearing) columns, so it slightly under-counts by the width of any small fixed-width column deliberately left out of resize (e.g. Outbound-Campaigns' `controls` column) — not the source of the original overflow, so not worth tracking separately.

Verified with `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui — clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors (`admin-shell.tsx`, `list-item.tsx`), neither touching table.tsx. Full `vite build`/`tsup` in this sandbox is currently blocked by two environment-only issues unrelated to this change: a missing `@rollup/rollup-linux-arm64-gnu` optional dependency (resolved by installing it directly) and, after that, an `EPERM` on unlinking tsup's own bundled temp config file on this particular mounted folder — a filesystem permission quirk of the sandbox's mount for this repo, not something introduced by this fix. Live visual confirmation remains unavailable here for the same reasons documented in the third report above; please pull this, restart `npm run dev`, and check whether the separator/hover now tracks the full resized row width.

### Admin apps' AI-panel float block had a redundant `pointerEvents: "auto"` wrapper that Agent Next Gen doesn't
**Reported bug**: in `lyra-form-generator` (an "Admin" app, `AdminShell`-based), buttons inside the Ask AI panel stop responding to hover/click after the very first undock: open a form → open Ask AI → click undock → drag the floated panel left → its buttons lose hoverability. Redocking fixes it. Agent Next Gen apps don't have this problem with the same drag interaction.
- **Comparison**: `lyra-ux-templates`' admin `App.tsx` (the template `lyra-form-generator` copied its own `App.tsx` from) and its `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` both render the AI panel's "float" state via the same shape — a `position: fixed` wrapper carrying `getAiFloatStyle()` + `pointerEvents: "none"` (so the wrapper itself doesn't block clicks to whatever's behind it while animating/positioned) around `aiPanel`. The one concrete structural difference: the admin `App.tsx` wrapped `aiPanel` in an *extra* inner `<div style={{ pointerEvents: "auto" }}>` before rendering it; `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` renders `{aiPanel}` directly, with no such wrapper.
- **Why the extra div is redundant regardless**: `Draggable`'s own float-mode root (draggable.tsx) already sets `pointerEvents: "auto"` on itself inline (`style={{ transform: ..., width, height, pointerEvents: "auto" }}`) — that's what actually re-enables clicks through the outer `pointerEvents: "none"` wrapper (CSS `pointer-events` is inheritable but a descendant can always explicitly opt back in to `"auto"`, regardless of an ancestor's `"none"`). The extra wrapper added nothing `Draggable` wasn't already doing itself.
- **Fix**: removed the extra `<div style={{ pointerEvents: "auto" }}>` wrapper from both `lyra-ux-templates`' admin `App.tsx` and `lyra-form-generator`'s own `App.tsx`, so `{aiPanel}` renders directly inside the float wrapper — now byte-for-byte matching `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`'s working reference for this block.
- **Caveat**: this environment can't drive a real browser to reproduce the drag interaction directly (no working Chromium available in this sandbox), so the fix is based on making the code structurally identical to the known-working reference rather than an observed before/after in a live repro. `tsc --noEmit` and `vite build` are clean in `lyra-ui`, `lyra-ux-templates`, and `lyra-form-generator` after the change — please confirm the actual drag interaction is fixed, and if it recurs, that rules out this specific structural difference as the cause.

### `ContainerHeader`'s title never actually truncated — `items-start` on a flex-column silently broke the width constraint the `truncate`/`min-w-0` chain depended on
**Report**: a screenshot of Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard metric cards, narrowed to a 3-up grid (`DashboardCard`'s "Metric Card" mode, `.lyra-metric-card-grid` at its default 3-column width), showing each card's `showFilterChip` pill ("Last 30 days") overlapping straight through the header title text ("Email Delivered Rate") with no ellipsis at all — not a squeeze, a literal overlap, as if truncation wasn't happening.
- **Why the existing code looked correct but wasn't**: `container-header.tsx`'s own doc comment (right above this fix) already documented a `flex-1`/`min-w-0` chain "at every level down to the title/subhead text" as what makes `truncate` work — and every level below the outer wrapper does have it. The outer wrapper itself (the `<div>` holding `topSlot` + the icon/title row, directly under `ContainerHeader`'s top-level flex row) is a flex COLUMN with `flex-1 min-w-0` (correctly shrinkable as a flex ITEM of the row above it) — but it also had `items-start`. In a flex column, `align-items` governs the CROSS axis, i.e. each child's *width* — `items-start` sizes every child to its own fit-content width instead of stretching it to match the column's own (correctly, narrowly, `min-w-0`-computed) width. So the icon+title row nested inside never actually received a constrained width to truncate against at all — it just grew to its title's natural, full content width and got visually painted over by the header's `shrink-0` actions on the right, instead of clipping with an ellipsis. The `min-w-0`/`truncate` classes deeper in the tree were all real and correctly written; they had nothing to measure against.
- **Fix**: dropped `items-start` from that wrapper (`container-header.tsx`), leaving flexbox's own default `items-stretch` — every child (the top slot, and the icon+title row) now stretches to the column's real available width, restoring the width constraint the `truncate` chain already assumed was there. Left-aligned appearance is unaffected: nothing inside that column was ever centered or right-aligned on its own; stretching the invisible wrapping box just gives the truncation machinery a real width to clip against once space runs out, and looks identical whenever everything already fits.
- **Scope**: `ContainerHeader` is shared by every `Container`/`DashboardCard` consumer in every repo (not something added just for Outbound-Campaigns), so this was a real, previously-invisible bug anywhere a header title + trailing actions (filter chip, kebab menu) could end up narrower than the title's natural width — not only the Monitor dashboard's 3-up metric grid, just the first place it was noticed.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors) and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns, `agent-next-gen-v1`, and `lyra-ux-templates` (all clean transforms).

### `SidePanel`'s container-width pin guard isn't an `AdminShell`-only extra — every installation needs it
**Report**: Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard side panel (`CallCentersSideMenu.tsx`/`MonitorDashboardPage.tsx`) didn't switch to an absolute-positioned hover overlay at a narrow width the way `AdminShell`'s own left `SidePanel` and the "Configure" page's panel do. Looking back at how that page was originally built, its own doc comment said so explicitly: "Mirrors AdminShell's left `SidePanel` wiring... at a simpler scale: no cookie persistence, no narrow-container pin guard... (not needed at this page's scale)" — that reasoning was wrong. `SidePanel` itself (`side-panel.tsx`) has no built-in breakpoint of its own; the "becomes unpinned/overlay below a width" behavior everyone sees in `AdminShell` isn't part of `SidePanel` at all — it's `AdminShell`'s own `isNarrowContainer` (`containerWidth < 1024`, measured via `ResizeObserver` against the shell's own rendered width, not `window.innerWidth`) forcing `effectiveLeftPinned = isNarrowContainer ? false : leftPanelPinned` and hiding the pin button (`onPinToggle={isNarrowContainer ? undefined : handleLeftPinToggle}`) while narrow. Since every other real `SidePanel` consumer in the design system happens to be `AdminShell`-based, this pattern read as "part of the component" when it's actually a wiring convention every consumer has to bring themselves — and the Monitor dashboard, being the first non-`AdminShell` `SidePanel` installation, was the first place that got skipped.
**Fix**: `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` now measures its own root width the same way (`rootRef`/`ResizeObserver`/`containerWidth < 1024` → `isNarrowContainer`), derives `effectivePanelPinned = isNarrowContainer ? false : panelPinned` (the underlying `panelPinned` state is preserved so the previous pin preference restores once the container widens back out), and passes `effectivePanelPinned` to both `CallCentersSideMenu`'s `pinned` and `PageHeader`'s `panelPinned`, with `onPinToggle` set to `undefined` while narrow (hiding the pin button, same as `AdminShell`). `CallCentersSideMenu`'s `onPinToggle` prop was widened from required to optional to allow this.
**Standing takeaway, now in CLAUDE.md (both repos)**: any new `SidePanel` usage — not just ones built on `AdminShell` — needs this same container-width pin guard from the start; it's core to how the component is meant to be installed, not an `AdminShell`-specific nicety to skip at a "simpler scale."
- Verified: `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (clean transform, only the known harmless sandbox `EPERM` dist-cleanup error).

### Agent Next Gen rail reduced to Home + Settings (both lyra-ui navs)
2026-08-03. Per explicit request, the Agent Next Gen left rail is now just Home + Settings (plus the pinned "New Outbound" CreateNew) in BOTH lyra-ui definitions: `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`'s `NAV_ITEMS` (dropped Contacts/Directory/Schedule) and `LeftNav.stories.tsx`'s "Agent Next Gen Left Nav" story (dropped Search/WEM/Help; its Home-iconed "Desk" row keeps its derived-active behavior). The two had drifted apart — they now genuinely mirror each other again, restoring the template's own "mirrors LeftNav.stories exactly" claim. Removed the now-unused icon imports (Users/BookUser/CalendarDays; Search/NotebookPen — HelpCircle kept, still used elsewhere). Those surfaces live in the header app-panel icons in consumer apps (see agent-next-gen-v2), not the rail. Verified: esbuild bundle of both stories, tsc clean on touched files, prototype-kit agent build + smoke test still pass.

### Five rules from the Agent-next-gen-v2 review (hover previews, slots, z-index, 2-tab TabList)
2026-08-03. Consolidated review of every correction the user made in the Agent-next-gen-v2 session; each became a standing rule (now in CLAUDE.md / CONTRIBUTING §4):
- Previews/popovers of existing UI render the REAL content (never a paraphrase) and copy an existing analogous affordance's mechanics (collapsed LeftNav / InteractionNavItem hover previews), including the onOpenAutoFocus/onCloseAutoFocus guards — without them Radix's focus return re-fires the trigger's onFocus and the popover flash-loops on mouse-off.
- Named slots have semantics: check a slot's stories/consumers before filling it; "component doesn't fit my content" is a wrong-slot signal, not a patch-the-component signal. Incident: Add Channel button in ContainerHeader's headerTitleBadge clipped the subhead and skewed spacing across two patches; the fix was moving it to headerActions.
- Consumer apps follow CONTRIBUTING §4's z-index scale — an invented z-[5] interior panel painted over interaction content in responsive mode.
- TabList with ≤2 tabs never collapses to "N More" and stretches both tabs evenly in the narrow state (built into tabs.tsx; collapse engages at 3+).
- Process: before coding any responsive/visual behavior change, state the expected end visual state in one sentence — the 2-tab fix shipped the mechanic without the visual intent and needed a second round.

## Scope Rules
- **Do NOT modify `lyra-ui` core components** (anything under `lyra-ui/src/components/`) unless the request explicitly specifies making changes to lyra-ui. Template and story work in `lyra-ux-templates` should be solved at the template/consumer level.
- When a fix is needed that would normally require a lyra-ui component change, first look for a prop, className, or wrapper approach in the consuming template before touching the library.
- **Always use the shared `app-icon.svg` mark for the "Agent Next Gen" smiley/logo** — it exists identically in both `lyra-ui/src/assets/app-icon.svg` and `lyra-ux-templates/src/assets/app-icon.svg`; import it directly (`import appIcon from ".../assets/app-icon.svg"`) and render it as `<img src={appIcon} ... />`, the same way `AppName`/`AppHeader` and `LoginCard` already do. This is the two-dot-eyes + single blue gradient swoosh mark. Do **not** use `CXoneSmiley` (a visually different, unrelated multicolor rainbow-smile component) or hand-roll a local one-off smiley SVG for this — this exact "used the wrong smiley" mistake has now happened twice (see the `LoginCard` and `CXoneSmiley` entries above); check for the real asset/component and a reference screenshot before assuming which mark is meant.

### `AdminShell` gains `navHeaderBadge`/`navExactSelection`/`navKey`/`pageSubtitle` — every new page belongs on `AdminShell`, not a hand-rolled root layout
**Request**: "why doesn't the monitor page fade in like the other pages?" led to finding that Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard hand-rolled its own root `<div>` + a `CallCentersSideMenu` component that rendered a real `SidePanel` directly + its own `PageHeader`, instead of `AdminShell` (which `OutboundCampaignsPage`/Configure already used) — that's why Monitor never got the `animate-in fade-in-0 duration-500` entrance baked onto `AdminShell`'s own `<main>` root. Once that was fixed (added the same classes directly to Monitor's root), the user pushed further: **"all new pages should be in the AdminShell — why would you hand roll your own root layout??"** — a standing directive, not a one-off fix.
**Why Monitor wasn't on `AdminShell` in the first place**: `AdminShell`'s left panel only ever showed a single, static `navTitle` + one fixed `navItems` tree with default (cascading-parent-active) selection — Monitor's tree needed a per-page view switcher (`headerTitleBadge` for "Call Centers"/"Service Groups"), `exactSelection` (single non-cascading selected row at any depth), and a `PageHeader` `subtitle` — none of which `AdminShellProps` exposed. Rather than accept that gap as a reason to hand-roll a parallel layout, `AdminShell` itself was extended (admin-shell.tsx):
- **`navHeaderBadge?: React.ReactNode`** — forwarded to the left `SidePanel`'s own `headerTitleBadge`, rendered inline right after `navTitle`.
- **`navExactSelection?: boolean`** (default `false`) — forwarded to the internal `<TreeMenu exactSelection>`. Off by default so every other `AdminShell` page's nav tree keeps its normal cascading-parent-active behavior; only Monitor turns it on.
- **`navKey?: React.Key`** — forwarded as the internal `TreeMenu`'s own `key`. Needed because `TreeMenu` rows track open/closed state via `useState(item.defaultOpen ?? false)`, initialized once per component instance — if `navItems` swaps to an entirely different dataset (Monitor's view switcher) without `TreeMenu` itself remounting, React's index-based reconciliation would hand a stale, already-open row's component instance to whatever new item now lands at that index, ignoring its own `defaultOpen`. Every other `AdminShell` page has one fixed `navItems` set and can leave this `undefined`.
- **`pageSubtitle?: string`** — a plain passthrough to `PageHeader`'s existing `subtitle`, for a page whose header needs a caption under the title (Monitor's "Call Centers"/"Service Groups").
All four are optional and off/undefined by default — confirmed via `vite build` in every `AdminShell` consumer (Outbound-Campaigns, `agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, `lyra-form-generator`) that nothing changed for pages that don't pass them.
**Outbound-Campaigns side**: `CallCentersSideMenu.tsx` stopped rendering a `SidePanel` itself — it now only exports the tree's plain data model, `buildCallCentersNavItems(view, selectedId, onSelect): TreeMenuItem[]` (renamed/retyped from the former `withSelection`, since `TreeMenuItem`/`TreeMenuChild` are structurally identical), `VIEW_HEADER_TITLE`, `SideMenuView`, and a small `CallCentersViewSwitcher` component (the bare-chevron `Select`, unchanged internals). `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` was rewritten to render `<AdminShell>` (mirroring `OutboundCampaignsPage`'s own usage) instead of its hand-rolled root — every bit of pin/open/hover state, the container-width pin guard, and cookie persistence this page had manually re-implemented earlier this session was deleted entirely, now owned by `AdminShell` itself. The workspace tab strip and card grid became `children`, unchanged otherwise.
**Standing rule, now in both repos' `CLAUDE.md`**: any new page in a consuming app renders `AdminShell` — check whether an existing `AdminShellProps` field (or a small, well-scoped addition to it, like the four above) already covers what the new page needs before reaching for a one-off root layout. A design requirement `AdminShell` doesn't yet support is a reason to extend `AdminShell`, not a reason to bypass it.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors) and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns, `agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, and `lyra-form-generator` (all clean transforms).

### `AgentDashboard` shouldn't be the template — extracted `DashboardTemplate` as the actual generic dashboard shell
**Request**: after the "why didn't you use the dashboard template" question above was answered (see the `Planned Future Work` note below), the user drew the real conclusion: **"so agent dashboard shouldn't be the template - the template should be the dashboard container, content width, break points and cards - update lyra-ui to have that as the template so it can be re-used with new content added based on the design."** `AgentDashboard` had been standing in for "the dashboard template" only because its own outer wrapper (`w-full max-w-[1200px] mx-auto lyra-container-grid-wrap`) was inlined directly into that one component — nothing reusable existed separately from its specific agent-persona content.
**Fix**: extracted that wrapper into a new, genuinely generic `DashboardTemplate` component (dashboard-template.tsx) — owns *only* the shell: a `maxWidth`-capped (default `1200`, matching `AgentDashboard`'s long-established width), centered content column, plus the `lyra-container-grid-wrap` container-query boundary its card rows react against. No content, no demo data, no bundled cards — a consumer composes it with whatever grid class fits its own layout (`.lyra-container-grid`, `.lyra-metric-card-grid`, `.lyra-card-split`, or a plain `DashboardCard` with no grid class at all) and its own `children`. `AgentDashboard` itself was updated to render `<DashboardTemplate>` instead of its own inline wrapper `<div>` — its actual content (greeting, queue widgets, Contact History + Redial, Performance/Productivity) is completely unchanged, it's just now built *on* the shared shell instead of *being* the shell.
**Storybook split**: `Templates/Dashboards` (Dashboards.stories.tsx) now demonstrates `DashboardTemplate` itself with placeholder cards (generic Activity/Volume/Response-Time metric cards, no persona attached) — the generic template, as asked for. `AgentDashboard`'s own story moved out of `Templates` entirely into `UI/Agent Dashboard` (AgentDashboard.stories.tsx, new file, per a follow-up "move Agent Dashboard to UI section" request) — unchanged otherwise, still a real, fully-supported template for an individual agent's Home tab, just no longer standing in for the generic one and no longer filed under `Templates`. `.storybook/preview.ts`'s explicit `storySort` order no longer lists `"Agent Dashboard"` (it briefly did, right after `"Agent Next Gen"`, before the move to `UI`) — `UI` has no explicit sub-order, so it falls back to alphabetical there.
**Exports**: `index.ts` gained `DashboardTemplate`/`DashboardTemplateProps`; the `AgentDashboard` export section's comment now clarifies it's "the Agent Next Gen 'Home' tab's specific content, built on `DashboardTemplate`."
**Follow-up — Container, 1200px max-width, 4 rows, click-to-open panel, toolbar**: a further round of requests fleshed out `Templates/Dashboards`' demo into a fuller worked example: (1) wrapped in the same `Container` + right `InteriorPanel` shell `AgentDashboard`'s own story uses, instead of a bare `<div>`; (2) `DashboardTemplate maxWidth={1200}` passed explicitly; (3) 10 placeholder `DashboardCard`s arranged into 4 rows of 1/2/3/4 cards, showing the shell holds an arbitrary row composition rather than one fixed grid shape; (4) every card is clickable (`onClick`/`role="button"`/`tabIndex`/`onKeyDown`, all plain `DashboardCardProps`/`ContainerProps` passthrough — no new prop added to `DashboardCard` itself) and opens the right `InteriorPanel` with that card's placeholder detail; (5) a `TableToolbar` (table.tsx) — the exact same toolbar `Templates/Data Management` already uses, not a hand-built search/filter bar — sits at the top of the template's content: `searchQuery` really filters the visible cards by title, and the `filterDefs` (Description/Created By/Published, identical data to `Templates/Data Management`'s own demo) are decorative — there's no matching field on a placeholder card, they're only there to show the toolbar's filter-chip layout, same reasoning as their source story.
**Follow-up — fullscreen toggle replaces the columns toggle**: the toolbar's `ColumnToggle` (originally repurposed as a per-card visibility toggle) was removed per "remove the show/hide columns and make it a full screen toggle icon button" — there's no table/columns concept in a card dashboard, so that repurposing didn't actually make sense here. Replaced with a `Maximize2`/`Minimize2` icon-button toggle in the same `actions` slot (same icon pair + toggle pattern as `ContainerHeader.stories.tsx`'s own "WithActions" fullscreen button), wired to real behavior instead of a no-op: toggling `isFullScreen` drops the outer shell's `p-3` gutter so `Container` fills the story canvas edge-to-edge.
**Bug caught mid-session — a second premature `*/` broke `Dashboards.stories.tsx`, and `tsc --noEmit` didn't catch it this time**: the same mistake as the earlier `agent-dashboard.tsx` incident (a doc-comment edit left a duplicate/early `*/`, leaving the original comment's remaining prose as bare unparseable code) happened again in this file, but unlike that first incident, `tsc --noEmit` reported clean at the time — the user hit it live as Storybook's "Failed to fetch dynamically imported module" error. Root-caused by bundling the file directly with `esbuild` (the same bundler Storybook's Vite dev server uses), which failed immediately with a precise `Expected ";" but found "directly"` at the broken line — `tsc`'s type-checker and a real bundler's parser don't always agree on what's a hard error. **New verification step for any doc-comment edit in this repo**: after fixing, run both `tsc --noEmit` AND a direct `esbuild` bundle of the touched file(s) (`platform: "browser"`, `external` for react/lucide/`@storybook/*`) — `tsc` alone is not sufficient to catch this class of bug.
**`DashboardTemplate.maxWidth` widened to `number | string`, and its own doc comment was wrong**: adding a "Full Width" story (duplicating "Dashboards" with no width cap) surfaced that the original `maxWidth` doc comment's suggested escape hatch — `className="max-w-none"` — can't actually work: `maxWidth` is applied via inline `style={{ maxWidth, ...style }}`, and an inline style always wins over a Tailwind class in the browser, regardless of `cn`/`twMerge` source order or specificity. Fixed for real: `maxWidth?: number | string` (a bare number still means px, same as before; a string is used as-is), so a consumer passes `maxWidth="none"` to genuinely remove the cap — that's what `Templates/Dashboards` → "Full Width" now does. `Dashboards.stories.tsx`'s `DashboardsTemplate` gained a `fullWidth` prop (passes `maxWidth={fullWidth ? "none" : 1200}`) rather than being duplicated wholesale, so the toolbar/rows/click-to-open-panel logic has one copy, not two to keep in sync.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors), a direct `esbuild` bundle of both changed files (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5021 modules transformed`, clean).
**Applied to a real consumer — Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor page**: "apply the full width dashboard component story to the outbound campaigns monitor page and use the current outbound campaigns cards" — `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx`'s card region (the metric-card grid + full-width summary card, previously a bare wrapper `<div>`) now renders `<DashboardTemplate maxWidth="none">`, the same "Full Width" mode as the story, wrapping Monitor's own real cards/data rather than the story's placeholders. Since Monitor already rendered its cards edge-to-edge with no width cap, this is a like-for-like swap (real shared shell component instead of a page-owned equivalent), not a visual change. Mid-task, the user clarified via screenshot that the toolbar slot at the top of `DashboardTemplate` should be Monitor's own existing "Showing data for: {label}" + Duplicate/Settings row — not the generic Quick Search/filter-chip/action-icon `TableToolbar` the story demonstrates, since that toolbar's contents are invented demo content with nothing real to wire up in Monitor. See Outbound-Campaigns' `CLAUDE.md` and `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx`'s own doc comment.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean), a direct `esbuild` bundle of `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5021 modules transformed`, clean).
**Follow-up — Edit icon + fullscreen toggle added to Monitor's toolbar**: "make the settings button in outbound campaigns the edit icon button from the dashboard template and add the full screen button to the outbound campaigns" — the old filled "Settings" text button became an icon-only Edit (`Pencil`, `Button variant="icon" size="icon"`, same as the demo toolbar's own "Edit" action), and a `Maximize2`/`Minimize2` fullscreen toggle was added after a divider (same icon pair/pattern as `ContainerHeader.stories.tsx`'s own fullscreen toggle and the Dashboards story's). Unlike the story's decorative/padding-only version, Monitor's toggle has a real, page-scoped effect: it hides the workspace tab strip so the card region gets the full scrolling height; the toolbar row (with the same button, now reading "Exit Fullscreen") stays visible either way.
**Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor cards reuse AgentDashboard's date filter**: "the filter chips in the outbound campaign cards should be radio toggles like the agent dashboard" — Monitor's metric cards and summary card used `showFilterChip`/`filterChipProps` (`FilterChip`'s default checkbox multiselect, "Last 30 days" pill), which was the wrong control: only one date range ever applies at a time. `AgentDashboard` already had the correct control — an unexported local `DateFilterChip` (single-select `RadioGroup` popover, "Date: {label}" trigger, Today/Yesterday/Last 7 days/Custom, revealing a `DateRangePicker` for "Custom") — duplicated identically three times within `agent-dashboard.tsx` itself (Contact History/Performance/Productivity), with nothing generic about its own name or file location. Extracted to a new, standalone, exported component: `DateRangeFilterChip` (date-range-filter-chip.tsx), with `DateRangeFilterValue`/`DateRangeFilterOption`/`DATE_RANGE_FILTER_OPTIONS` also exported. Made genuinely reusable, not just moved: `value`/`onValueChange` are now optionally controlled (uncontrolled by default, matching every existing `AgentDashboard` call site exactly), `options` is a prop (defaults to the original four), and `label` (default `"Date"`) lets a consumer relabel the trigger. `agent-dashboard.tsx` now imports it and keeps `AgentDashboardDateRange` only as a type alias (`= DateRangeFilterValue`) for backward compatibility — zero behavior change for any existing `AgentDashboard` consumer. `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx`'s three cards now pass `headerActions={<DateRangeFilterChip />}` instead of `showFilterChip`/`filterChipProps`.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean), direct `esbuild` bundles of `date-range-filter-chip.tsx`/`agent-dashboard.tsx`/`index.ts` (clean, `index.ts` needed an explicit `.svg` loader in the standalone check — that's a check-harness detail, not a real issue), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns, `agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, and `lyra-form-generator` (all clean transforms).

### `BarChart` — the first axis-labeled chart-type component
**Request**: Monitor's "Outbound Email" summary card had an `EmptyState` ("No data available") in its chart area — "make the no data available area in the outbound email card a bar chart with x-axis amount of emails and y-axis day - display 2 columns - one the emails launched and two the emails remaining." No bar/axis chart existed in lyra-ui yet — `DonutChart`/`Sparkline` (both built on the shared `Chart` ECharts wrapper) are deliberately bare, no axes/grid/legend, meant to be glanced at as a shape, not read/compared against. Per "if lyra-ui doesn't have something a consuming app needs, add it there, don't approximate it locally," added a new `BarChart` (bar-chart.tsx), following the exact same architecture (`Chart` + `resolveCssColor`/`useThemeVersion` for the canvas color-resolution gotcha both existing chart components already document).
**API**: `categories: string[]`, `series: { label, data, colorVar }[]` (grouped bars per category, not stacked — no stacked-bar consumer exists yet), `orientation?: "vertical" | "horizontal"` (default `"vertical"`, upright columns; `"horizontal"` swaps category/value axes — Monitor's own "x-axis amount of emails, y-axis day" ask), `showTooltip`/`showLegend` (legend defaults to on whenever there's more than one series)/`valueAxisLabel`.
**Storybook**: added to `Custom Primitives/Chart` (Chart.stories.tsx) — grouped alongside `DonutChart`'s own section there rather than a new top-level file, matching that file's existing "one file per Chart-type-component family" convention. Shows `"vertical"` and `"horizontal"` side by side with the same 2-series (Emails Launched/Remaining) data Monitor itself uses.
**Real colors, not fabricated ones**: the story/Monitor's second series first reached for a plausible-sounding `--lyra-color-border-subtle-strong` token that doesn't actually exist in lyra-tokens.css — caught before shipping and replaced with the real `--lyra-color-fg-secondary` token. **Superseded** — see "chart series colors must be accent (data-viz) colors, not status colors" below: `fg-secondary` (along with the series' other color, `status-info-strong`) turned out to still be the wrong category of token, not just a typo-checked one; both were later replaced with real accent colors.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean), direct `esbuild` bundles of `bar-chart.tsx` and `Chart.stories.tsx` (clean).

### Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor "Outbound Email" card gets a real `BarChart`
Replaced the card's `EmptyState` with `<BarChart categories={EMAIL_CHART_DAYS} series={EMAIL_CHART_SERIES} orientation="horizontal" valueAxisLabel="Emails" />` — two series (Emails Launched/Emails Remaining), static/dummy day-by-day data (non-zero, unlike the "0.00%" stat cards elsewhere on this page, specifically so the chart has something to actually draw). The chart's wrapping div got an explicit `h-[220px]` rather than relying on `.lyra-card-split`'s `align-items: stretch` alone — the two "contained" metric-card boxes beside it are short enough that stretch-to-match would otherwise squeeze the chart (legend + axis labels + bars) into an illegibly small area.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean), a direct `esbuild` bundle of `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns, `agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, and `lyra-form-generator` (all clean transforms).

### `.lyra-card-split-chart` — a chart's explicit height must itself respect `.lyra-card-split`'s row/stacked breakpoint
**Bug**: "how are you calculating the height of the dashboard cards?" surfaced that the `BarChart` in Monitor's "Outbound Email" card had an arbitrarily small `h-[220px]` — fixed to a generous `h-[420px]` in response, deliberately picked against `DashboardCard`'s own `max-h-[600px]` body cap (dashboard-card.tsx). That fix was itself incomplete: `h-[420px]` was hardcoded unconditionally, in both `.lyra-card-split`'s row state AND its stacked state (`@container (max-width: 480px)`, where the two stat boxes go full-width *above* the chart instead of beside it). Fine in the row state; once stacked, the stat boxes' own height plus a flat 420px chart comfortably exceeded the 600px cap, forcing the whole card into a scrollbar — reported as "there is an overflow-x and -y on the smaller card, it should respect the max-height and stack the email metric cards on top of the chart."
**Fix**: a new shared class, `.lyra-card-split-chart` (lyra-tokens.css + storybook.css, mirrored per the established cross-file convention) — `height: 420px` in the row state, `height: 260px` once `.lyra-card-split` stacks, using the exact same `@container (max-width: 480px)` breakpoint `.lyra-card-split-fixed`/`.lyra-card-split-divider` already key off. Any region needing an explicit chart height inside this split pattern should reach for this class instead of a one-off `h-[…px]` that can't respond to the stacking breakpoint at all. Monitor's stat column also dropped a `justify-center` that was only ever compensating for the column being stretched to match the old flat-420px sibling — no longer needed now that the chart's own height is itself breakpoint-aware, so the column stays plain top-aligned (`flex flex-col gap-3`).
**Also checked and ruled out**: whether `echarts-for-react`'s chart canvas was failing to re-measure/resize when its container's size changes from a CSS container-query breakpoint (as opposed to a `window resize` event) — confirmed via `node_modules/echarts-for-react/lib/core.js` that it already binds the `size-sensor` package to its own container element by default (`autoResize` prop, default `true`), which handles exactly this case already. No change was needed in `chart.tsx` for this — the actual bug was purely the unconditional fixed height described above.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean), a direct `esbuild` bundle of `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), a CSS brace-balance check on both token files (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (clean transform).

**Follow-up — the real bug wasn't the height number, it was `flex-basis` fighting `height`**: "can you make the height of the chart bigger when the card is stacked?" (with a screenshot showing the chart still squished/garbled at the just-added 260px) surfaced that the actual root cause was never the specific pixel value — it was that Monitor's chart wrapper div carried `className="lyra-card-split-chart flex min-w-0 flex-1"`, and Tailwind's `flex-1` sets `flex-basis: 0%`. `flex-basis` governs a flex item's size on the *main axis*, which is width in `.lyra-card-split`'s row state but becomes height once it stacks to a column — so `flex-basis: 0%` was silently overriding `lyra-card-split-chart`'s own explicit `height` back down toward zero the entire time (the stacked column has no defined height of its own to `flex-grow` into), regardless of whatever height number the class specified. That's why bumping 220px → 260px didn't visibly fix anything: both were being collapsed the same way. Fixed at the source: `.lyra-card-split-chart` now owns its own `flex-grow`/`flex-basis` internally (`flex: 1 1 0%` in the row state, `flex: none` once stacked) instead of a consumer applying a `flex-1` utility alongside it — the class's own explicit height is only ever honored once nothing else is fighting it for the main axis. Stacked height bumped to 340px (up from 260px) now that there's no more collapse bug masking the real number, and 340px was re-checked against `DashboardCard`'s `max-h-[600px]` cap together with the stat column's actual height (recomputed more carefully this pass: ~84px per "contained" metric box × 2 + 12px gap + 24px `.lyra-card-split` gap + 16px bottom padding ≈ 220px fixed overhead, leaving ~380px of real headroom under 600px — 340px fits comfortably). Monitor's `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` dropped the now-redundant `flex`/`min-w-0`/`flex-1` classes from the chart wrapper, relying entirely on `lyra-card-split-chart` itself.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean), a direct `esbuild` bundle of `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), a CSS brace-balance check on both token files (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns, `agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, and `lyra-form-generator` (all clean transforms).

### Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor cards click (or kebab "Edit") to open a right `InteriorPanel`
**Request**: "when any of the cards are clicked or the kebab menu 'edit' is clicked, open a right interior panel (it should open beneath the tabs)." All 7 `DashboardCard`s in Monitor (6 metric cards + the "Outbound Email" summary card) gained `onClick`/`role="button"`/`tabIndex`/`onKeyDown` (Enter/Space) plus a consumer-supplied `kebabMenuItems` array (Edit/Refresh/Remove, matching `DashboardCard`'s own `DEFAULT_KEBAB_MENU_ITEMS` icons) — required since the default kebab items are decorative with no `onClick`. Both paths call one `openInteriorPanel(title)` helper, so a click on the card body and a click on its own kebab "Edit" open the identical panel with the identical title.
**Layout — "beneath the tabs"**: the workspace tab strip stays a `shrink-0` sibling *before* a new `<div className="flex flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-hidden">` row; that row holds the existing scrolling `DashboardTemplate` column and the new `<InteriorPanel side="right">` as flex siblings — `InteriorPanel`'s own contract (interior-panel.tsx) requires sitting beside the main content column in a flex row for its "push the content over" behavior; it self-manages an absolute-overlay fallback below ~1050px of that row's width via its own internal `ResizeObserver`.
**A real bug this surfaced in `DateRangeFilterChip`**: making the card itself clickable exposed that `DateRangeFilterChip`'s trigger button (sitting in every card's `headerActions`) never called `stopPropagation()` — clicking it to open the date popover would also bubble up and fire the card's own `onClick`. Fixed at the source (date-range-filter-chip.tsx), not patched around in Monitor: the trigger button now calls `e.stopPropagation()`, the same treatment `KebabMenuButton`'s own trigger already has (kebab-menu-button.tsx) and for the identical reason — any control meant to live inside a `DashboardCard`'s header should not also trigger the card's own click.
**Checked and accepted as a non-issue**: `MenuRadix`'s actual menu-item `onSelect` (menu-radix.tsx) does not itself call `stopPropagation()` (and `MenuEntry.onClick` has signature `() => void`, no event param, so an item can't stop it from the inside) — meaning a kebab "Edit" click could in principle also bubble to the card's own `onClick` via the Radix portal. Not fixed, because both handlers converge on the exact same idempotent action (open the same panel, same title) — there's no observable bug even if both fire.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing unrelated errors), direct `esbuild` bundles of `date-range-filter-chip.tsx` and `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5023 modules transformed`, clean).

**Follow-up — the toolbar's own Edit button opens the same panel, scoped to the whole dashboard**: "when the edit icon button is clicked open an interior side panel that is edit for the entire dashboard" — the toolbar row's Edit icon button (`Pencil`, next to Duplicate/Fullscreen) now calls the same `openInteriorPanel("Edit Dashboard")` helper every card and kebab "Edit" already call, rather than a separate panel/state pair. One `InteriorPanel`, one open/title state, three trigger points; the panel body branches on `interiorPanelTitle === "Edit Dashboard"` to show dashboard-scoped placeholder copy instead of the per-card one.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing errors), a direct `esbuild` bundle of `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5023 modules transformed`, clean).

**Follow-up — the triggering card shows a selected state while its panel is open**: "when the dashboard cards are clicked and their associated interior panel is open they should be in a selected state... built into the functionality of the dashboard when interior panels are triggered" (a reference screenshot showed a "Digital / Wait Time: 00:02:34 / 8 Contacts / 3 Agents" card with a blue border and light-blue background). That exact treatment already exists — it's `DashboardQueue`'s own click-to-select "cards" variant (dashboard-queue.tsx), which flips a queue card's `variant` between `"neutral-subtle"` (unselected) and `"info-strong"` (selected, blue) — no new prop was needed on `DashboardCard`, `variant` is already plain `ContainerProps` passthrough. Applied the identical mechanism in two places: (1) lyra-ui's own `Templates/Dashboards` demo (Dashboards.stories.tsx) — the reference "click a card, open its InteriorPanel" pattern — where `DemoDashboardCard` now takes an `isSelected` prop and renders `variant={isSelected ? "info-strong" : "neutral-subtle"}` plus `aria-pressed`; (2) Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor page, where every card's `variant` is conditional on `interiorPanelOpen && interiorPanelTitle === {that card's title}`. The toolbar's "Edit Dashboard" trigger (see above) deliberately never matches a card's own title, so it never falsely selects one.
**Bug caught mid-edit — a third premature `*/`**: adding this note to `Dashboards.stories.tsx`'s top doc comment repeated the exact mistake documented twice already (a new paragraph's closing `*/` landed mid-comment, leaving several more paragraphs of real prose as bare unparseable code) — caught immediately on a re-read of the surrounding text before running any verification, not by `tsc`/`esbuild` this time. Reinforces the standing rule: always re-read the *entire* comment after editing it, don't just check the paragraph you touched.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing errors), direct `esbuild` bundles of `Dashboards.stories.tsx` and `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5023 modules transformed`, clean).

### New standing rule: chart series colors must be accent (data-viz) colors, not status colors
**Request**: "never use primary main, light or dark colors for charts or icons in dashboards - only use subtle colors for icons in dashboards and only use data visualization colors for charts." Auditing existing dashboard icon usage (every `headerIcon` across `DashboardCard`/`DashboardQueue`/`AgentDashboard`/the Storybook demos) turned up no violations — none use `background="primary"` (the solid brand-fill option), all already use one of the subtle-toned options (`"active"`/`"info"`/`"success"`/`"warning"`/`"critical"`/`"neutral"`/`"surface"`/`"shell"`) — so the icon half of this rule locks in existing practice rather than fixing anything. Auditing chart series colors found two real violations: the Email `BarChart`'s two series (both `Chart.stories.tsx`'s demo and Outbound-Campaigns' real `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` usage) used `--lyra-color-status-info-strong` and `--lyra-color-fg-secondary` — neither an accent/data-viz color, and neither tied to any real status (they're arbitrary categorical data, "Emails Launched" vs. "Emails Remaining"). Fixed to `--lyra-color-accent-teal-strong`/`--lyra-color-accent-purple-strong`.
**"By data visualization I mean accent — but don't use the blue strong"**: the accent palette (lyra-tokens.css: `slate`/`red`/`orange`/`yellow`/`lime`/`green`/`teal`/`blue`/`purple`/`pink`, each with a `-soft`/`-strong` pair) is the intended data-viz palette, with `accent-blue-strong` specifically excluded — it reads too easily as the primary/brand color rather than a neutral category.
**Exception, clarified when asked about `DonutChart`'s Available/Working/Unavailable activity breakdown (status colors on purpose)**: "if the colors match to a specific element that has a color assigned to it (i.e. status, success, error, etc.) then you can use the associated color." Left unchanged — its green/amber/red *is* the status, not an arbitrary categorical choice, so it's the correct exception case, not a violation. The rule targets series with no inherent meaning of their own.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing errors), direct `esbuild` bundles of `Chart.stories.tsx` and `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5023 modules transformed`, clean).

### `BarChart`'s axis/legend text now resolves real lyra tokens instead of ECharts' hardcoded default gray
**Request**: "can you apply the lyra-ui colors to the chart text so they switch in dark mode?" (with a screenshot of the Email `BarChart`, its bar colors already correctly theme-aware per the accent-color fix above, but the axis tick labels/axis name/legend text were never touched — left on whatever gray ECharts defaults to, same value regardless of `[data-theme]`). The exact same **canvas color gotcha** `bar-chart.tsx`'s own doc comment already covers for series colors applies equally to any canvas-rendered text: axis labels, the axis name, and the legend are all painted onto `<canvas>`, so a lyra CSS variable has to be resolved to a concrete value via `resolveCssColor` (chart.tsx) before ECharts ever sees it, or it's stuck unresolved (canvas silently ignores an unresolvable `var(...)` and falls back to its own default). Fixed by adding `axisLabel`/`nameTextStyle` (`--lyra-color-fg-secondary`, the standard muted/secondary text token) and `axisLine`/`splitLine` (`--lyra-color-border-subtle`, the standard light-separator token) to both axes, and `textStyle` to the legend — all resolved through the same `resolveCssColor`/`useThemeVersion` pair already used for each series' own `colorVar`, so a `[data-theme]` flip now re-resolves the text/line colors too, not just the bars.
**Scope**: `DonutChart`/`Sparkline` don't need this — both are deliberately bare (no axes/legend of their own) — but this is now the pattern any *future* axis-labeled or legend-bearing chart in lyra-ui should follow, documented directly in `bar-chart.tsx`'s own doc comment so it isn't re-derived (or skipped) next time.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing errors), direct `esbuild` bundles of `bar-chart.tsx` and `Chart.stories.tsx` (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5023 modules transformed`, clean).

### Monitor's per-card date filters consolidated into one toolbar-level filter
**Request**: "remove the individual filters in the dashboard cards in outbound campaigns and add a filter to the toolbar that allows the widgets to filter by date: Today, Yesterday, Last 7 days, Last 30 Days, Last 90 Days) - default to Today and persist the filter if users click between call centers - change the data appropriately in the cards." Every card on Monitor (6 metric cards + the "Outbound Email" summary card) had its own independent `DateRangeFilterChip` in `headerActions` — seven separate, uncoordinated filters instead of one real page-level control. Removed all seven; added a single `DateRangeFilterChip` to the toolbar row (`MonitorDashboardPage.tsx`), controlled by one `dateFilter` state (`useState<MonitorDateRange>("today")`) that now drives every card and the chart.
**`DateRangeFilterValue` widened in lyra-ui**: the requested option set (Today/Yesterday/Last 7 days/Last 30 Days/Last 90 Days, no "Custom") needed two values the shared type didn't have yet — `DateRangeFilterValue` gained `"last30"`/`"last90"` (additive; `DATE_RANGE_FILTER_OPTIONS`, the default 4-option list every other consumer uses, is untouched). Monitor passes its own `options` list (`MONITOR_DATE_FILTER_OPTIONS`, the 5 requested values) rather than the shared default.
**Fallout — `AgentDashboardDateRange` needed to explicitly exclude the two new values**: `agent-dashboard.tsx`'s own per-range dummy-data lookups (`PRODUCTIVITY_DATA_BY_RANGE`, `CHANNEL_TYPE_DATA_BY_RANGE`, `PERFORMANCE_DATA_BY_RANGE`, `CONTACT_HISTORY_BY_RANGE`) are all `Record<AgentDashboardDateRange, ...>`, exhaustively keyed on the original 4 values — since `AgentDashboardDateRange` used to just alias `DateRangeFilterValue` directly, widening the base type broke all four (TypeScript demanded `"last30"`/`"last90"` entries in lookups that will never actually receive those keys, since `AgentDashboard`'s cards never offer them as options). Fixed by narrowing the alias itself: `export type AgentDashboardDateRange = Exclude<DateRangeFilterValue, "last30" | "last90">` — decouples `AgentDashboard`'s own type from the shared type's future growth, the same `Exclude<...>` pattern Monitor's own `MonitorDateRange` now uses for the inverse case (excluding `"custom"`, which Monitor never offers). Also needed a small cast at each of `AgentDashboard`'s 3 `onValueChange={setDateFilter}` call sites (→ `onValueChange={(v) => setDateFilter(v as AgentDashboardDateRange)}`) since `DateRangeFilterChip`'s callback type is still the full, wider `DateRangeFilterValue` — safe in practice since `AgentDashboard` never passes a wider `options` list, so `"last30"`/`"last90"` can never actually reach that callback.
**"Change the data appropriately in the cards"**: `METRIC_CARDS_BY_RANGE`/`EMAIL_CHART_BY_RANGE` (`MonitorDashboardPage.tsx`) replace the old single fixed `METRIC_CARDS`/`EMAIL_CHART_DAYS`/`EMAIL_CHART_SERIES` consts — still static/dummy data (no real query, per this page's standing convention), but genuinely different, plausible numbers per range rather than one dataset behind 5 identical-looking option labels. The email chart's categories change shape per range too, not just the numbers — "Today"/"Yesterday" are a single bar each, "Last 7 days" breaks down by weekday (the original baseline data, unchanged), "Last 30 Days" by week, "Last 90 Days" by month — real category semantics per range, not one fixed axis with scaled-up values. The "Emails Launched"/"Emails Remaining" stat boxes beside the chart are each range's series total (`.reduce`), not separately-authored numbers, so they can't drift out of sync with the chart itself.
**"Persist the filter if users click between call centers"**: `dateFilter` lives in `MonitorDashboardPage` itself, entirely independent of `selectedId`/`view` (the tree-selection state) — nothing in the tree-selection handler touches it, so it naturally persists across call-center clicks without any dedicated persistence code.
**Bug caught mid-edit — a fourth premature `*/`**: expanding the page's own doc comment with the new toolbar-filter paragraph repeated the exact mistake documented three times already this session — caught immediately on review before running any verification, same as the third time. The standing rule (re-read the *entire* comment after any edit, not just the touched paragraph) keeps proving necessary.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` in lyra-ui (clean, same 2 pre-existing errors after the `AgentDashboardDateRange` fix), direct `esbuild` bundles of `agent-dashboard.tsx`, `date-range-filter-chip.tsx`, and `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` (clean), and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns, `agent-next-gen-v1`, `lyra-ux-templates`, and `lyra-form-generator` (all clean transforms).

### Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor page header gets the default Secondary/Primary/Ask AI buttons
**Request**: "add the default top right buttons to the page header secondary, primary | Ask AI (with panel functionality)." No lyra-ui component changes needed — `AdminShell`'s `pageActions` slot, `Button`, `AiIcon`, and `AiPanel` all already existed and are already exported; this was purely a consumption task. Composed the exact same `pageActions` shape `AdminShell.stories.tsx`'s "With Page Header" story (`WithPageHeaderStory`) already demonstrates: `<Button variant="outline">Secondary</Button>`, a default-variant `<Button>Primary</Button>`, a divider, then `<Button variant="outline"><AiIcon/>Ask AI</Button>`.
**"With panel functionality"**: Outbound-Campaigns' own `App.tsx` already had a full docked/float/resize `Draggable`-backed state machine for an "Online Help" panel (`DraggablePanel`, no AI content) — added an identical second one for "Ask AI" (`AiPanel` instead of `DraggablePanel`), one shared instance for the whole app, with an `onAskAiToggle` callback threaded down into `MonitorDashboardPage` the same way lyra-form-generator's own `App.tsx` threads `onAiPanelToggle` into its pages. This is now the second real (non-Storybook) production consumer of this exact `AiPanel` docked/float/resize pattern.
- Verified: `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5023 modules transformed`, clean — same count as before, since no new external deps were added, only already-exported lyra-ui components) and direct `esbuild` bundles of `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx`/`App.tsx` (clean). A raw `tsc --noEmit` run directly in Outbound-Campaigns was tried once as an extra check and found to be unusable for this repo — `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` is a live Vite alias, not an installed package, so bare `tsc` can't resolve it for ANY file in the app (confirmed via `node_modules/@nicecxone` not existing and no matching `paths` entry in `tsconfig.json`) — `vite build` remains the one reliable check for this app, as it has been all session.

### Outbound-Campaigns' "Online Help" and "Ask AI" panels coordinated into one docked + one float slot
**Request**: "the panel behavior appears to contradict the lyra-ui because I am suggesting the ability to open 2 side panels - it should behave: 1) if Ask AI or Online Help is already open, the content is replaced with the latest selected item 2) if the panel is undocked and another panel is opened, the latest panel opens in the side and the undocked remains where it is 3) if 2 panels are open: 1 undocked 1 docked, and the undocked is docked, the currently docked panel then switches and becomes the undocked panel (see the behavior of the agent desktop) - do not modify any lyra-ui behavior - just match it to the outbound campaigns repo." Before this, Outbound-Campaigns' `App.tsx` had two fully INDEPENDENT `Draggable`-backed panel state machines (Online Help via `DraggablePanel`, Ask AI via `AiPanel`, added earlier this session) — nothing stopped both from being simultaneously docked (stacking two docked columns) or both floating on top of each other.
**No lyra-ui change** — explicitly requested, and unnecessary: `draggable.tsx` already documents the "single-dock" contract as a comment for consumers to implement themselves (its own "Multi-Panel Z-Index & Docking Rules" section), and agent-next-gen-v1's `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` already has a generalized `dockPanelExclusively` helper implementing exactly the eviction half of this (rule 3 below) for its own larger (5-panel) set — confirmed via reading both files that this coordination has always been consumer-owned, never something `Draggable`/`AiPanel`/`DraggablePanel` themselves manage.
**What was reused vs. newly designed**: rule 3 (re-docking a floating panel evicts whichever OTHER panel is currently docked, swapping it to float) is the *exact* `dockPanelExclusively` pattern from agent-next-gen-v1, generalized down to 2 panels via a small `PanelKey`("help"|"ai")/`PanelHandle` abstraction rather than hand-duplicated per-pair checks. Rules 1 and 2 (what happens on the OPEN trigger itself — replace-if-docked vs. leave-alone-and-dock-beside-it-if-floating) have **no existing reference anywhere** in agent-next-gen-v1, lyra-ux-templates, or lyra-ui — every other app's panel set is large enough (5 panels) that letting several float simultaneously is fine; Outbound-Campaigns only ever has these two, so a stricter "at most one docked + one floating, full stop" rule was designed fresh for `openPanel(key)`.
- Verified: direct `esbuild` bundle of `App.tsx` (clean) and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5023 modules transformed`, clean, no lyra-ui files touched at all).

**Follow-up — z-index "bring clicked panel to front" was still missing**: "you aren't respecting the z-index rule in agent templates when multiple drag panels are active - the one clicked on should move to the front." Right — the coordination above only handles docked-vs-floating placement, not z-index ordering between two SIMULTANEOUSLY floating panels (reachable by manually undocking one panel while the other is already floating — the dock/float rules don't prevent this, they only govern what happens on the OPEN trigger and on re-docking). `draggable.tsx` already documents the fix as a consumer contract ("Multi-Panel Z-Index & Docking Rules": most-recently-opened-or-clicked panel gets z-index 10000, others get 9999, tracked via a `topPanel` state set from `onMouseDown` on each float wrapper) — the same pattern agent-next-gen-v1's `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` already uses for its own panel set. Added `topPanel` state (`PanelKey | null`) to Outbound-Campaigns' `App.tsx`, set from `openPanel` (newly-opened panel comes to front) and from `onMouseDown` on each float wrapper's inner (pointer-events-enabled) div; each float wrapper's `zIndex` now reads `topPanel === key ? 10000 : 9999` instead of a hardcoded `9999` for both. No lyra-ui change, same as the dock/float coordination above.
- Verified: direct `esbuild` bundle of `App.tsx` (clean) and `vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns (`5023 modules transformed`, clean).

### New `MenuItem` component (`src/components/menu-item.tsx`) — a standalone "list item within a menu"
**Request, from a screenshot of a design-file "Menu Item" component (a rounded row with a persistent left accent bar and a single label)**: "is there a component in lyra-ui for list item within a menu? - if not, create one and add it to the list item component stories." There wasn't one — the exact visual/behavior already existed, but only baked into `Menu`'s internal, unexported `MenuItemRow` (menu.tsx) and `MenuRadix`'s internal `MenuRadixItem` (menu-radix.tsx), both only reachable by building a `MenuItemDef[]` and handing it to the full `Menu`/`MenuRadix` container — there was no standalone component a consumer could drop into a bespoke dropdown, a virtualized list, or any other composed surface that just wants one menu-styled row.
- `MenuItem` extracts that single-row visual as a real, directly-usable component: `label`, optional `description`/`icon`/`shortcut`/`rightElement`, and `active`/`destructive`/`disabled` states — the same left accent bar (persistent for `active`, otherwise opacity-0 → opacity-100 on hover/press via a `group/item` class), the same `rounded-lyra-sm px-3 py-2.5` row shape, and the same color rules `MenuItemRow` uses for each state. It intentionally does NOT reimplement `Menu`'s submenu flyout, arrow-key/Home/End keyboard navigation, or section labels — those stay `Menu`-only features; `MenuItem` is the single-row primitive, not a replacement for `Menu`'s full data-driven list.
- Demoed in `ListItem.stories.tsx` (per the request to add it to the "list item" stories) rather than a new dedicated stories file, since `ListItem` and `MenuItem` are both single-row primitives — three new stories: `MenuItem — Basic` (the plain "Menu Item" row from the design-file screenshot), `MenuItem — States` (default/active/destructive/disabled), and `MenuItem — Icon, description, shortcut` (a small nav-style list demonstrating every optional prop together).
- **`Menu` is hand-rolled, not built on Radix — only `MenuRadix` (menu-radix.tsx) is, and it's explicitly experimental ("not wired into `index.ts` or either consuming app — exists purely as a side-by-side Storybook comparison," per its own header comment), not used anywhere real. Worth knowing before assuming any menu-family component in this repo is Radix-backed.**
- **Follow-up, on request ("are the new list item components being used inside that menu component currently? if not, can they be?")**: initially `MenuItem` was built as a fully independent component — `Menu`'s own internal `MenuItemRow` kept its own separate, duplicated copy of the same row markup, to keep the original change scoped and avoid risking regressions in `Menu`'s submenu positioning/keyboard-nav logic. After confirming the user wanted them unified, `MenuItemRow` was refactored to render `MenuItem` directly for the row (button, accent bar, icon, label/description, shortcut/rightElement) — `Menu` itself still owns everything specific to being a *list* (the wrapping `relative` row div, submenu open/hover/timeout state, the portal-rendered flyout, and the outer `Menu` container's own arrow-key/Home/End keyboard navigation), none of which moved. Two new `MenuItem` props exist specifically to support this: `highlighted` (forces the hover look — background + accent bar — for a row whose submenu is open via mouse hover, since that isn't real CSS `:hover`) and `trailingIcon` (renders after `rightElement`/`shortcut` rather than replacing them — `Menu`'s submenu chevron). `MenuRadix`'s own separate `MenuRadixItem` (built on Radix's `DropdownMenu.Item`, which only works nested in a `DropdownMenu.Root`) was left as its own thing — merging that one in would require `MenuItem` to become Radix-dependent, which would stop it from being usable standalone outside a menu container at all.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unrelated to this change, both before and after the `Menu`/`MenuItem` unification) and `esbuild` bundles of every story file that consumes `Menu` (`KebabMenuButton.stories.tsx`, `Popover.stories.tsx`, `ProfileMenu.stories.tsx`, `AppMenu.stories.tsx`) plus `ListItem.stories.tsx` itself — all clean.
- **`MenuItem — Basic` gained six boolean controls on request, all composed from `MenuItem`'s existing props**: "Icon left" (`icon`), "With description" (`description`), "With badge" and "With right slot" (both feed the same `rightElement` slot together — `MenuItem` only exposes one generic right-side slot, so badge renders first and the right-slot icon (a `Star` placeholder) after it, in a small flex row), and "With submenu" (`trailingIcon`, a `ChevronRight` — always rendered furthest right regardless of what's in `rightElement`, which is exactly the requested "right slot icon sits left of the chevron" ordering for free).
- **"Header" needed a real new `MenuItem` prop, not just a story-level wrapper — a genuine `header?: React.ReactNode`** (menu-item.tsx), rendered as a `lyra-body-md-emphasis text-lyra-fg-default` line above the label, INSIDE the button. First attempt rendered "New Case" in a separate `<div>` positioned above the row in the story's own demo container (matching `ListItem`'s title styling, per a screenshot correcting an earlier, even smaller/secondary-colored attempt copied from `Menu`'s section-label treatment) — but a follow-up screenshot showed the header sitting outside the row's own hover/pressed background, visually attached but not actually sharing the row's interactive surface. Moving `header` inside `MenuItem`'s own button (same "Label + optional description" text stack, header first) means hovering/clicking anywhere across header+label+description now highlights as one unit, matching the screenshot's intent exactly.

### `AgentNotifications` (agent-notifications.tsx) migrated from `ListItem` to `MenuItem`
**Request, from a screenshot of the real "Notifications" panel**: "update the notifications menu with the new menu list item and implement the controls to match the current content (don't change the content)."
