Each notification row swapped from `ListItem` (`leading`/`title`/`subtitle`/`meta`) to `MenuItem` (`icon`/`header`/`label`/`description`) — a 1:1 field mapping, no data/content change: `header` = `title` (bold, e.g. "New Case") when a `subtitle` exists, `label` = `subtitle` (the name, e.g. "Noah Patel"), falling back to `title` itself for the rare notification with no `subtitle` (since `MenuItem.label` is required), `description` = `timestamp`, `icon` = the existing `NotificationIcon`.
- **The unread indicator changed from a hand-rolled `border-l-2` + manual `bg-lyra-bg-active-subtle` override to `MenuItem`'s own `active` prop** (`active={!n.read}`) — this is the "implement the controls" half of the request: reach for the component's real built-in state instead of re-deriving the same visual by hand. This does shift the accent bar from a full-row-height 2px border to `MenuItem`'s short, centered, rounded 3px pill — an inherent difference from adopting the component's own accent-bar treatment, not a data change.
- **One deliberate deviation from `MenuItem`'s own `active` styling**: `active` normally recolors a row's entire text `text-lyra-fg-active-strong` (blue), matching `Menu`'s "current nav item" semantics — but the screenshot shows unread notification text staying dark/neutral, with only the background and accent bar tinted blue. Fixed by wrapping the `label` value in its own `<span className="text-lyra-fg-default">` — an explicit color on the child always wins over the inherited value from the button's own active-state text color class, without needing a new `MenuItem` prop for it. `header`'s own span already hardcodes `text-lyra-fg-default` unconditionally (added for the earlier "Header" fix above), so it needed no additional change.
- The dismiss ("Clear") button, its hover-reveal (`group/notif` + `group-hover/notif:opacity-100`), and the divider between rows (moved onto the wrapping div as `border-b border-lyra-border-subtle last:border-0`, matching `ListItem`'s own `divider` convention) are all unchanged in behavior.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `AgentNotifications.stories.tsx` and `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` (the two real consumers) — both clean.

### Follow-up polish, on request: notifications panel padding, `MenuItem` icon alignment/spacing, and a `MenuItem — Basic` separator control
- **`AgentNotifications`' scrollable list gained `p-2`** on its outer container — rows previously sat flush against the panel's own edges, with no breathing room beyond each row's own internal `MenuItem` padding. Matches `Menu`'s own `p-1` convention of never letting a container's outer edge and its rows' edges land on the same line.
- **`MenuItem`'s leading icon is now always vertically centered, and given its own padding, in `menu-item.tsx`** — this was a real component fix, not just a story tweak, since the bug affected every `MenuItem` consumer. Previously the icon wrapper was a fixed `h-5 w-5` box that switched to `self-start mt-0.5` (top-aligned) whenever a `description` was present; combined with `AgentNotifications`' 36px circular `NotificationIcon` avatars (which don't fit inside a 20px box at all — they simply overflowed it, uncentered and cramped against the label text), this produced exactly the misaligned, tight icon layout the request was about. Fixed by dropping the fixed `h-5 w-5` size and the `description`-conditional `self-start` in favor of a content-hugging `flex items-center justify-center p-0.5` wrapper: small Lucide icons (the common case, `h-4 w-4`) end up with almost the same effective footprint as before (16px icon + 2px padding per side ≈ the old 20px box), so existing `Menu`/`MenuRadix` consumers (`ProfileMenu`, `AppMenu`, `CreateNew`, `KebabMenuButton`) are visually unaffected, while a large custom icon like `NotificationIcon` now gets properly centered and padded instead of overflowing a too-small fixed box.
- **`MenuItem — Basic` gained a seventh control, "Separator"** (`separator`, boolean) — renders `Menu`'s own separator treatment (`border-b border-lyra-border-subtle my-1.5`, matching `menu.tsx`'s `"separator"` entry) directly below the row in the same demo container, so a menu item followed by a divider can be previewed without a real `Menu`.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `ListItem.stories.tsx`, `AgentNotifications.stories.tsx`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, `KebabMenuButton.stories.tsx`, `Popover.stories.tsx`, and `ProfileMenu.stories.tsx` — all clean.

### `AgentNotifications`' row divider replaced with real spacing, from a screenshot question
**"if there's a separator wouldn't there be spacing between the items?"** — right call: an unread row's `MenuItem` has its own rounded corners (`rounded-lyra-sm`) plus a tinted `active` background painted only onto that rounded button shape, not onto the row's own wrapper div. Stacked back to back, each row's rounded corners reveal a small notch of the wrapper's plain background behind them — which reads as a real gap between separate rounded cards, even though the rows were actually flush and the `border-b` hairline divider was still rendering underneath, just partially hidden by that same rounding on read rows too (which don't visibly show the notch, since they have no fill to contrast against). A flush hairline divider assumes adjacent, touching rows; the rounded/tinted unread rows already read as separated cards, so the two conflicted.
- Fix (user's choice between "real spacing, no separator" vs. "flush rows, keep separator"): removed the `border-b border-lyra-border-subtle last:border-0` from each row wrapper entirely, and added `gap-1` to the list's own `flex flex-col` container instead — embracing the "stack of rounded cards" look the unread rows already had, rather than fighting it with a hairline that only worked on flat read rows.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `AgentNotifications.stories.tsx`/`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` — clean.

**Follow-up, from a screenshot of `ProfileMenu`'s real dropdown ("Contact Us" / "Sign Out" / "Dark Mode")**: "what if I still want the border but I also want the spacing? ... you already have this paradigm." Right — `ProfileMenu` (via `MenuRadix`) already renders a real divider WITH spacing between its menu groups, using `Menu`/`MenuRadix`'s actual `"separator"` entry (`border-b border-lyra-border-subtle my-1.5`, its own margin providing the spacing) as a dedicated sibling between rows — not a border living on the row itself, which was the root issue in both the original (flush, no spacing) and the just-previous fix (spacing, no divider). Replaced the plain `gap-1` in `AgentNotifications` with this exact convention: rows now render via `notifications.map((n, i) => ...)` wrapped in a `React.Fragment`, with a `<div role="separator" className="border-b border-lyra-border-subtle my-1.5" />` inserted between consecutive rows (skipped after the last one) — the same divider styling already used by `MenuItemBasic`'s own "Separator" control and by `Menu`'s/`MenuRadix`'s `"separator"` MenuEntry, reused rather than re-derived.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `AgentNotifications.stories.tsx`/`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` — clean.

### `MenuItem` gains a real `comfortable` density prop
**Request**: "add a control for comfortable (current padding: 12px top/bottom) true and if false it is 6px top/bottom for list items." Added `comfortable?: boolean` (default `true`) directly to `MenuItem` (menu-item.tsx) — a real component prop, not a story-only affordance, since row padding is core visual behavior every consumer inherits. `comfortable={true}` is `py-3` (12px top/bottom, the existing default); `comfortable={false}` is `py-1.5` (6px), a denser row for contexts that need to fit more rows without shrinking type. `MenuItemBasic` gained an eighth control, "Comfortable" (`comfortable`, default `true`), mapping 1:1 to this real prop. `Menu`'s own `MenuItemRow` and `AgentNotifications` don't pass `comfortable` explicitly, so they keep the default (unchanged, comfortable) row height.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `ListItem.stories.tsx`, `AgentNotifications.stories.tsx`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, `KebabMenuButton.stories.tsx`, `Popover.stories.tsx`, and `ProfileMenu.stories.tsx` — all clean.

### `Menu` gains the shared hover-chevron scroll affordance — no more native scrollbars in scrollable dropdowns
**Request, from a screenshot of `Autocomplete`'s country dropdown showing a plain OS scrollbar**: "for auto complete, phone number input there is a scroll bar in the menu - dropdown menus should all be the same with the chevron up/down unless specified to have a scroll bar." `Select`'s multi-select listbox and `MenuRadix` already use the shared hover-driven chevron affordance (`scroll-chevron.tsx`) instead of a native scrollbar; the hand-rolled `Menu` (menu.tsx) — which `Autocomplete` and `PhoneInput` both compose for their scrollable option/country lists — never had this, so any consumer capping `Menu`'s height with a `max-h-*`/`overflow-y-auto` className (only `Autocomplete` and `PhoneInput` actually did) got a plain browser scrollbar instead.
- Fixed at the source in `Menu` itself, not by patching each consumer independently: `Menu`'s single root div split into an outer surface wrapper (border/shadow/rounded/min-width/`p-1`, whatever a consumer's `className` still controls — unchanged for `create-new.tsx`'s override, `agent-profile.tsx`'s override, etc.) and an inner scrollable div (`flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-y-auto lyra-scrollbar-hide`, holding the actual mapped items) with `ScrollChevronButton`s as siblings above/below it — the exact structure `MenuRadix`'s own `Content` already uses. The forwarded `ref` now points at the outer surface (matching ordinary expectations of "the whole menu's DOM node," and confirmed compatible with `table.tsx`'s existing outside-click `.contains()` check); the keyboard-nav `:scope`-relative query and the new scroll tracking both operate on the inner scrollable div instead, since that's still the direct parent of the mapped items (unchanged from before).
- `Autocomplete`'s and `PhoneInput`'s own `<Menu>` `className`s dropped their now-redundant `overflow-y-auto` (`max-h-60` and `p-2` respectively are all that remain) — the actual scrolling/chevrons now live inside `Menu` itself. Every other `Menu` consumer (`ProfileMenu` [via `MenuRadix`, already fine], `create-new.tsx`, `agent-profile.tsx`, `table.tsx`'s column menu, `TabList`'s overflow menu) renders short, non-scrolling lists today and is visually unaffected — they'd only ever see the new chevrons if a future list actually grows past whatever height cap they apply.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of every real `Menu`/`MenuRadix` consumer's story file: `Autocomplete.stories.tsx`, `PhoneInput.stories.tsx`, `Table.stories.tsx`, `CreateNew.stories.tsx`, `AgentProfile.stories.tsx`, `Tabs.stories.tsx`, `Popover.stories.tsx`, `KebabMenuButton.stories.tsx`, `ProfileMenu.stories.tsx`, `MenuRadix.stories.tsx` — all clean.

### New `Foundations/Breakpoints` Storybook page — every media query/breakpoint in lyra-ui, live
**Request, following a research-only ask ("identify and outline all the media queries and breakpoints in lyra-ui")**: "can you add stories to the foundations section of lyra-ui that visually depicts each media query in the ui currently?" New file `src/components/__stories__/Breakpoints.stories.tsx` (`title: "Foundations/Breakpoints"`, matching `Spacing.stories.tsx`'s existing Foundations convention: `autodocs` tag, `layout: "padded"`).
- **The research turned up that lyra-ui's responsive system is almost entirely CSS `@container` queries, not `@media`** — `@media` only reacts to the browser window resizing, so it can't catch a side panel/nav opening and shrinking a component's real available width while the window itself stays put; a container query measures the nearest ancestor container element instead, so it's correct either way. This distinction is the page's own opening explanation (`Overview` story) before any of the live demos.
- Reused the established "drag the edge to see it collapse live" convention already present in `Tabs.stories.tsx`'s `OverflowMenuDemo` and `ChannelRow.stories.tsx`'s `ChannelTabResponsive` — a `resize-x overflow-auto` dashed-border box — as a shared `ResizeBox` helper, rather than introducing a new demo mechanism. Every demo wraps the REAL component/class, never a reimplementation, so nothing on this page can drift from what the actual components do.
- **Nine stories**, one per catalogued family: `Overview` (a summary table of every family/threshold/consumer), `ContainerGrid` (`.lyra-container-grid`, 991px/768px, placeholder cards — real usage is `AgentDashboard`/`DashboardQueue`), `FormGrid` (`.lyra-form-grid`, 768px/550px, placeholder fields — `FormTemplate`), `MetricCardGrid` (`.lyra-metric-card-grid`, 900px/500px), `CardSplit` (`.lyra-card-split` + its `-divider`/`-fixed`/`-chart` companions, 480px), `MetricRow` (the real `DashboardCard metrics={...} metricVariant="divided"` — the most elaborate family, three ordered collapse stages at 760px/560px/360px, applied automatically by `DashboardCard` itself), `ChannelTabList` (the real `TabList`+`ChannelTab`, modeled directly on `ChannelTabResponsive`, 480px/320px), `TabOverflow` (the real `TabList overflowMenu`, modeled on `OverflowMenuDemo`, 400px), and `BreadcrumbCollapse` (the real `PageHeader breadcrumb={[...]}`, a new *live* resize-x version — `PageHeader.stories.tsx`'s existing `WithBreadcrumbsNarrow` is a fixed, non-draggable 420px demo of the same collapse).
- **Two additional stories cover the cases that AREN'T container queries and can't be demonstrated the same way**: `ViewportMediaQuery` documents `TransferBox`'s real `md:` Tailwind breakpoint (the one true viewport `@media` in the whole library) — explicitly explains why a resize-x box can't demo it (it only reacts to the actual browser/canvas viewport, not a wrapping div) and points at shrinking the Storybook preview panel or using the Viewport toolbar addon instead. `JsMeasuredThresholds` documents the two `ResizeObserver`-based "container-width pin guards" (`AdminShell` at 1024px, the "Agent Next Gen" template's own separate copy of the same pattern at 768px) with a small self-contained `PinGuardDemo` stand-in (measures its own width via `ResizeObserver`, flips a "pin available"/"pin disabled" label) rather than embedding the real, context-dependent `AdminShell`/template components, which need a full shell to render — the story's own text is explicit that this is an illustrative simplification, not the real component, and points to `AdminShell.stories.tsx`/`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` for those.
- **Confirmed no `prefers-color-scheme`/`prefers-reduced-motion`/`prefers-contrast` queries exist anywhere in the library** — theming is entirely `[data-theme]`-attribute-driven (manual toggle), never OS-level detection — not included as its own story since there's nothing to demo, but worth knowing if this page is extended later.
- **Found, not yet fixed**: `Tabs.stories.tsx`'s existing `OverflowMenuDemo` comment says its threshold is "991px" — the actual, current CSS threshold for `.lyra-tab-overflow-wrap` is 400px (confirmed via `lyra-tokens.css` and `tabs.tsx`'s own doc comments, which say it was deliberately lowered from 991px per an earlier explicit request and never fully reconciled in that one comment). The new `TabOverflow` story here uses the correct 400px figure; the stale comment in `Tabs.stories.tsx` itself hasn't been touched — flag to the user before editing, since it's a different file than what was asked for here.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected — story files aren't type-checked by `tsc` regardless) and a direct `esbuild` bundle of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` itself (externalizing `react`/`react-dom`/`@storybook/*`) — clean.

### `.lyra-metric-card-grid` redesign explored, then reverted — stays a fixed 3-up grid
A multi-step exploration: a demo screenshot ("the sparkline card overflowing instead of shrinking") surfaced that `DashboardCard`'s `metrics` mode is a separate family (`.lyra-metric-row`) from `.lyra-metric-card-grid`; separately, extending the `Foundations/Breakpoints` page's `MetricCardGrid` demo to 4 cards (previous entry) exposed that `.lyra-metric-card-grid`'s fixed 3-column template wraps a 4th card even at full width, unlike `.lyra-container-grid`'s "stay inline, evenly split among however many there are" mechanism. Several redesign directions were considered — unifying the two families' mechanism outright, adding a 1024px/1200px/1280px breakpoint tier so a fixed count (Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard's real 6 metric cards) could wrap into "2 rows of 3," and a locally-scoped Storybook prototype (6 rows, one per card count 1–6, previewing a 1200px/991px/768px three-breakpoint version) built specifically to preview the idea before touching real CSS, per the user's own "depict it in the storybook before moving to the css" sequencing.
- **This surfaced a real conflict worth keeping in mind if this comes up again**: `DashboardQueue` (dashboard-queue.tsx, lines 22-28/150-158) explicitly chose `.lyra-container-grid`'s "any number of equal-width flex children" mechanism specifically to avoid "a fixed N-column grid that squeezes its cards ... into an unreadably narrow space" — its own doc comment says so. Any future redesign that gives `.lyra-container-grid` itself a fixed-column stage (to solve a *different* family's fixed-count case) would undo that guarantee for `DashboardQueue`'s genuinely arbitrary, unbounded queue-count usage. Keep the two use cases (arbitrary count vs. a fixed real-world count like Monitor's 6) in separate classes rather than merging them into one.
- **Final decision: don't change any of this.** `.lyra-metric-card-grid` stays exactly as it already was — a fixed 3-column grid, collapsing to 2-up at ≤991px and 1-up at ≤480px — and the real-world card count to design/demo around is capped at 4, not 6. The Storybook prototype (locally-scoped `mcg-proto-*` class, never touched `lyra-tokens.css`/`storybook.css`) was removed; `MetricCardGrid`'s story is back to the plain 4-card demo against the real class. No CSS, doc-comment, or production behavior changed as a net result of this whole exploration — `lyra-tokens.css`'s own comment on this family now just notes that this alternative was considered and not adopted, for future reference.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` and `Dashboards.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Fixed a real bug: `.lyra-container-grid`/`.lyra-form-grid` cards shrank once stacked to a single column
**Found from a screenshot question**: "why do the height of the cards stay the same when the metric card grid goes to one column but the container grid cards shrink?" Root cause: `.lyra-container-grid > *`'s `flex: 1 1 0%` (flex-basis: 0%) exists so cards evenly split the ROW's *width* in the wide flex-row stage — but that rule was unconditional, so it stayed in effect at the ≤768px stage too, where `flex-direction` flips to `column`. Once the main axis is vertical, `flex-basis: 0%` governs each card's *height* instead, overriding its natural/intended height and shrinking it toward its minimum content size — exactly the visible symptom in the `Foundations/Breakpoints` page's `ContainerGrid` demo. `.lyra-metric-card-grid` never had this problem since it's pure CSS Grid throughout (no flex-basis to hijack a child's height).
- **Real production impact, not just the demo**: this affects `.lyra-container-grid`'s actual consumers (`AgentDashboard`'s Performance/Productivity row, `DashboardQueue`) any time their row stacks to a column on a narrow screen — their cards would have been shrinking the same way.
- **Fix**: added `.lyra-container-grid > * { flex: none; }` inside the existing `@container (max-width: 768px)` stage (both `lyra-tokens.css` and `storybook.css`), resetting grow/shrink/basis back to their initial values once stacked, so cards use their own natural height again instead of dividing available vertical space.
- **Same fix applied to `.lyra-form-grid`** (`FormTemplate`'s own consumer) at its own ≤480px single-column stage — identical mechanism, identical bug, not explicitly asked about but caught proactively since it's the exact same pattern documented as sharing `.lyra-container-grid`'s mechanism.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx`, `Dashboards.stories.tsx`, and `Forms.stories.tsx` (the real `FormTemplate` consumer) — all clean.

### Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor dashboard switched from `.lyra-metric-card-grid` to three `.lyra-container-grid` rows
**Request**: "update outbound campaigns to use the container grid and instead of a single row of 6 do 3 rows: 1 with 3-up, 1 with 3-up and then 1 with 1-up (the Outbound Email card)." `MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` previously rendered all 6 `metricCards` in one `.lyra-metric-card-grid` grid, with the "Outbound Email" summary card as a separate full-width section below it. Restructured into three `.lyra-container-grid` rows sharing one `flex flex-col gap-4 p-4` wrapper (replacing each section's own one-off padding): `metricCards.slice(0, 3)`, then `metricCards.slice(3, 6)`, then the "Outbound Email" `DashboardCard` alone as its own single-child row — `.lyra-container-grid`'s "evenly split among however many children" mechanism naturally renders 3 cards as 3-up and 1 card as a full-width 1-up with no fixed column count needed. All three rows now collapse at the same 991px/768px thresholds (rather than metric-card-grid's 991px/480px), matching `AgentDashboard`'s own row. The "Outbound Email" card's internal `.lyra-card-split-wrap`/`.lyra-card-split` (its own stat-blocks-vs-chart split) is unchanged, just nested one level deeper inside its new `.lyra-container-grid` row instead of being a bare wrapped div.
- Updated this page's own doc comments (the "Responsiveness note" and the row-by-row comment) to describe the new three-row structure instead of the old single grid.
- Verified: `npx vite build` in Outbound-Campaigns — `5024 modules transformed`, clean (an unrelated sandbox permission error on clearing the old `dist/` folder was bypassed by building to a fresh `--outDir`, which completed with no errors).

### `.lyra-container-grid`'s 2-up stage: a lone trailing card now spans the full row
**Found from the `Foundations/Breakpoints` page's "5 cards" row**: at the ≤991px 2-up grid stage, an odd card count (e.g. 5) left the last card alone in its own row, sitting half-width with an empty gap beside it — plain CSS Grid doesn't know to stretch a lone trailing item across empty tracks on its own. **Request**: "update the container grid to function so that the cards fill the space when they go to a second line - ie. in the screenshot Card 5 would stretch full width." Added `.lyra-container-grid > *:last-child:nth-child(odd) { grid-column: 1 / -1; }` inside the existing `@container (max-width: 991px)` stage (both `lyra-tokens.css` and `storybook.css`) — `:last-child:nth-child(odd)` is only ever true for exactly the "one card alone in the final row of a 2-column grid" case, regardless of the total card count, so this works generically without needing to know how many cards there are. This is a real fix to `.lyra-container-grid` itself, so it applies to any real consumer that ends up with an odd count at this stage (`AgentDashboard`'s row is a fixed 2 cards so unaffected; `DashboardQueue`'s arbitrary queue count could plausibly hit this).
- **Not yet applied to `.lyra-form-grid`/`.lyra-metric-card-grid`**, which have the identical 2-up-grid-with-odd-trailing-card gap potential at their own 2-up stages — flagged for later, not fixed here since only `.lyra-container-grid` was asked about this time.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` and `Dashboards.stories.tsx` — clean.

### FormGrid "Static Width" (Breakpoints story) — current state (consolidated)
Dated 2026-07-27. Current truth of `Foundations/Breakpoints`' Form Grid "Static Width" Storybook control (`Breakpoints.stories.tsx`); the rework-and-revert history is archived.
- "Static Width" is a demo-only boolean control on the `FormGrid` story, not a `.lyra-form-grid` CSS variant. When on, each row keeps the real shared `.lyra-form-grid` class but pins `display: "flex", flexDirection: "row", flexWrap: "wrap"` via inline `style` (inline styles are the only reliable override of the class's own ≤768px grid / ≤480px column stages), plus `items-start`; fields reflow purely by flex-wrap, dropping to the next line only once there's genuinely no room.
- Field sizing lives on a real class, `.form-grid-static-field` (`FORM_GRID_STATIC_FIELD_CSS`, a shared `<style>` tag — a class rather than an inline style specifically so `@container` rules can override it): a stepped/sawtooth function with four stages layered via `@container (max-width: ...)`, one per "N fields fit side by side" (4/3/2/1). Each stage's width is `(100cqi - (N-1)×16px) / N`, clamped to a 240px floor / 320px ceiling — fields grow to max, shrink to min, and snap back to max on every wrap. Exact thresholds: stage 4 active ≥1008px (ceiling reached at 1328px), stage 3 at 752–1007px, stage 2 at 496–751px, stage 1 at ≤495px, where fields go `width: 100%` (full width once alone on their line).
- Sizing keys off the shared `.lyra-form-grid-wrap` container's own `cqi` only, never a row's own field count, so every field in every row (the demo shows 4/3/2/1-field rows) is pixel-identical at any given width.
- The 240px/320px range is the same standard `Input.stories.tsx`/`Select.stories.tsx`'s "Max width" control uses (see the separate standardization entry, still in place below).
Supersedes 12 archived entries — see PROJECT_SUMMARY_ARCHIVE.md, "FormGrid \"Static Width\" saga (archived)".

### `FormGrid`'s fields are now real, swappable lyra-ui components ("slots") with a "Field Type" control
**Request**: "treat the fields in form grid as slots and add a controls dropdown to be able to choose an input component - use: input field, select, text area, switch, checkbox, radio group, and any others you can think would be appropriate." `PlaceholderField` previously rendered a fake styled div (a label span + an empty bordered box) — replaced it with a `switch` over a new `fieldType` prop that renders one of eight real, already-existing lyra-ui components, all of which already accept a `label` prop, so the existing "First Name"/"Last Name"/"Email"/"Phone" slot labels carry over unchanged regardless of type: `Input` (default, matches the old placeholder's look), `Select` (with a small 3-option sample list), `Textarea` (2 rows), `Switch`, `Checkbox`, `RadioButtonGroup` (2-option, horizontal — "Radio Group" from the request), plus `DatePicker` and `PhoneInput` as the two additional "appropriate" components judged worth including (both are real, common form-field types already in the library, both already accept a plain `label` prop with no other required config).
- Added one shared `argTypes.fieldType` select control on the `FormGrid` story (options: Input Field, Select, Text Area, Switch, Checkbox, Radio Group, Date Picker, Phone Input) — a single control re-renders every field slot across all four rows (4/3/2/1) as the chosen component type, consistent with the existing single shared `staticWidth` boolean control already on this story rather than one dropdown per slot (10 total slots across the four rows).
- The slot wrapper div (`.form-grid-static-field` vs. `flex-1`) is unchanged — it only ever controlled width/flex behavior, and every real component still renders inside it exactly as the placeholder box did.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` — clean.

### `Popover` border color updated + arrow enlarged to 24×12, switching to Radix's real Arrow primitive
**Request**: "update the popover component to use the border-soft 0,0,0,.16 and increase the size of popover arrows to be 24 x 12." No token is literally named "border-soft" in `lyra-tokens.css`, but `--lyra-color-border-default` resolves to exactly `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.16)` in light mode — the precise value given — so the Content panel's border moved from `border-lyra-border-subtle` (0.10 alpha) to `border-lyra-border-default` (0.16 alpha).
- The arrow itself couldn't just be resized in place: it was a hand-rolled CSS rotated 10×10 square (a diamond, via `rotate-45` + border on two adjoining sides), and rotating a *non-square* 24×12 rectangle 45° produces a lopsided rhombus, not a symmetric triangle — that trick fundamentally only works for a square.
- Fixed by switching to Radix's real `PopoverPrimitive.Arrow` (built on `@radix-ui/react-popper`'s `Arrow`), the exact same fix already applied to `tooltip.tsx`'s arrow earlier in this project for an unrelated bug (a hand-rolled arrow drifting off-center once `avoidCollisions` shifts the content box). Sized via `width={24} height={12}` — Radix's Arrow always renders a fixed `points="0,0 30,0 15,10"` polygon in a `viewBox="0 0 30 10"` with `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, so the `width`/`height` props just stretch it non-uniformly to fit; confirmed against `@radix-ui/react-popper`'s installed source. Reused Tooltip's `stroke-dasharray: "0 30 40"` mask unchanged (0 drawn, skip the 30-unit flat base that overlaps the content box, draw the next 40 units to cover both slanted "wing" edges) — this works at any `width`/`height` since the dasharray is specified in the fixed viewBox's own coordinate units, not final pixel size.
- Also confirmed (reading `@radix-ui/react-popper`'s source) that Radix already computes the floating content's offset as `sideOffset + arrowHeight`, so the taller 12px arrow doesn't need a manual `sideOffset` bump — it's automatic.
- Removed the now-dead `group` class from Content's className (only existed to scope the old arrow's `group-data-[side=*]` selectors) and the `border-lyra-border-subtle`/rotated-square implementation entirely.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `Popover.stories.tsx`, `MenuRadix.stories.tsx`, `FilterChip.stories.tsx`, and `select.tsx` (all real `Popover` consumers) — clean.

### `Popover`'s header and body content given a consistent 20px left/right padding
**Request**: "remove the padding-bottom of popover headers and make sure the body padding left and right is 16px on all popover content" — followed immediately by a correction: "the padding left and right on the main container in the popover should stay at 20px — i made a mistake — just make the padding-left and right in the header 20px as well to match." Final behavior implemented (the 20px figure, not the initially-requested 16px):
- **Header**: the built-in `title`-based header (rendered via `PanelHeader`/`ContainerHeader`) previously used `ContainerHeader`'s own default `px-4 py-2.5`. Popover now overrides this to `px-5 pb-0` — `px-5` (20px) so the header's left/right edge lines up with the body's own inset, `pb-0` so `py-2.5`'s bottom half (dead space between the title and whatever sits directly below it) is dropped. `cn`'s twMerge resolves the combination down to effectively `px-5 pt-2.5 pb-0`.
- **Body**: added a new `bodyPadding` prop (default `true`) that applies `px-5` (20px) to the `content` wrapper div — previously that div had zero built-in horizontal padding at all, fully left to each consumer to add their own (inconsistently — some used `px-4`, some `px-5`, some none).
- Real consumers whose `content` is a full-bleed `Menu`/listbox (own small `p-1`/`p-2` inset for the hover background) or content that already supplies its own complete padding/chrome end to end now pass `bodyPadding={false}` to keep their exact previous look: `select.tsx`'s multi-select listbox, `create-new.tsx` (all three Popovers — two channel/action menus, one multi-screen panel where every section already owns its own `p-4`/`p-2`), `interaction-nav-item.tsx`'s hover-preview card (deliberately zero framing from Popover itself), `agent-profile.tsx`'s status menu, `table.tsx`'s Advanced Search (`advancedSearchContent` is an arbitrary consumer-supplied slot that already wraps itself in its own `p-4`, per `Table.stories.tsx`'s `AdvancedSearchContent`), and both `Popover`+`Menu` demos in `MenuRadix.stories.tsx`.
- Plain/body-style consumers had their own redundant horizontal padding removed so they rely on the new default instead of doubling up: `date-range-filter-chip.tsx` and `FilterChip.stories.tsx`'s `PopoverContentDemo` (`p-3` → `py-3`), and every plain-content example in `Popover.stories.tsx` (`px-4 pb-4` → `pb-4`, `px-5 pb-5` → `pb-5`, `px-5 py-4` → `py-4`) — `MenuPopover`'s own `Menu`-based example got `bodyPadding={false}` instead, matching the other Menu-in-Popover cases.
- This scoping decision (apply the default broadly, but let full-bleed/self-chromed content opt out) was confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before implementation, given the real risk of silently insetting `Select`'s widely-used multi-select dropdown and several menu-style popovers if the default applied unconditionally.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of every touched file (`popover.tsx`, `date-range-filter-chip.tsx`, `select.tsx`, `create-new.tsx`, `interaction-nav-item.tsx`, `agent-profile.tsx`, `table.tsx`, `Popover.stories.tsx`, `FilterChip.stories.tsx`, `MenuRadix.stories.tsx`) — clean.

### `Sparkline` no longer double-wraps itself in an identical `h-full w-full` div
**Report**: "why are you wrapping the sparkle lines in two of the same divs" (from a DevTools screenshot showing two nested `<div class="h-full w-full">` elements around one `<canvas>`). Root cause: `sparkline.tsx` rendered its own `<div className="h-full w-full">` around `<Chart option={option} />`, but `Chart` (`chart.tsx`) already renders that exact same `h-full w-full` div around the real `ReactECharts` instance and forwards `ref`/`className`/rest props straight onto it (`ChartProps extends React.HTMLAttributes<HTMLDivElement>`) — so Sparkline's own wrapper was a redundant, do-nothing extra layer.
- Fixed by having `Sparkline` forward its `ref`/`className`/rest props directly onto `<Chart>` instead of wrapping it in a second div — `Chart ref={ref} option={option} className={className} {...props}` replaces the old two-div structure with one. Removed the now-unused `cn` import.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `sparkline.tsx` — clean.

### Metric Row's horizontal overflow bug fixed (missing `min-width: 0`) + thresholds adjusted to 768px/550px/360px
**Investigation**: "instead of having a separate query for metric row dashboard card can these just resize like a 4-up row in container grid?" → "or actually can they resize like the other metric cards?" — prompted by a screenshot of a 4-metric "divided" row overflowing its card with a horizontal scrollbar, the exact unresolved bug flagged earlier this session ("why is the sparkline card overflowing instead of shrinking?").
- **Root cause, unrelated to which breakpoint family is used**: each metric's per-item wrapper (`dashboard-card.tsx`'s `<div className="flex flex-1 lyra-metric-row-item">` for "divided," `<Container className="flex flex-1 p-4">` for "contained") is `flex: 1` but had no `min-width: 0` — a flex item defaults to `min-width: auto`, refusing to shrink below its own content's natural width (the value/trend text plus a fixed-80px sparkline sitting beside it), so the row overflowed instead of shrinking. Same class of bug already fixed for `.lyra-container-grid`/`.lyra-form-grid`'s children earlier this session, just never applied here. Fixed by adding `min-w-0` directly to both wrappers' JSX (no dedicated CSS class exists for the "contained" variant to hang a shared fix on, so both got the Tailwind utility for consistency).
- **Design question, decided separately**: whether to unify Metric Row's column-collapse mechanism with `.lyra-container-grid`'s (991px/768px) instead of its own 3-stage system was raised and discussed via `AskUserQuestion` — Metric Row has one extra stage (sparkline moves from beside the value/trend text to below it) that Container Grid has no equivalent for, since a single metric shares its row with a fixed-width sparkline in a way a whole card never does. Decision: **keep Metric Row's own 3 stages**, but retune the two upper thresholds — stage 1 (sparkline-below-text) moved from 760px to 768px (now matching `.lyra-container-grid`'s own single-column threshold exactly, rather than an independently-rounded value) and stage 2 (2-up grid) moved from 560px to 550px; stage 3 (1-up) stays at 360px.
- Updated in lockstep: `lyra-tokens.css` and `storybook.css` (both `@container` rules + the doc comment's threshold list and "reuses 761px, rounded" note, now replaced with a note that 768px matches Container Grid exactly), the `Overview` story's `REGISTRY` row ("768px, 550px, 360px"), and the `Metric Row (Dashboard Card)` story's own descriptive copy.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `dashboard-card.tsx`, `Breakpoints.stories.tsx`, and `Dashboards.stories.tsx` — clean.

### `Metric Row (Dashboard Card)` demo rebuilt: "contained" variant, generic data, 3-up/2-up/1-up rows
**Request**: "for the metric row story just use card containers with generic numbers and subheads (no sparkle lines), and show me rows 3-up, 2-up and 1-up." The previous demo was a single row of 4 "divided" metrics (flat, separator-based) with real trend/sparkline data — replaced with `metricVariant="contained"` (each metric in its own bordered box — "card containers" per the request) and a small shared placeholder dataset (`{ value, label: "Subhead" }` × 3, no `trend`/`sparkline`), sliced to 3/2/1 items across three stacked rows in one `ResizeBox`, matching the established per-row-count demo pattern already used by `ContainerGridRow`/`FormGridRow`/etc. elsewhere in this file.
- Descriptive copy updated to note the sparkline-relocation stage (≤768px) isn't visually demonstrated here since this data has no sparkline — pointed to the real `DashboardCard` "Divided"/"Contained" stories for that in context — while still calling out the 2-up (≤550px) and 1-up (≤360px) stages this demo does show.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` — clean.

### `Metric Row (Dashboard Card)` demo: added a 4-up row
**Request**: "also show 4-up at the top." Added a 4th generic placeholder metric (`{ value: 2, label: "Subhead" }`) to `METRIC_ROW_DEMO_DATA` and a 4-up row at the top of the stack (`[4, 3, 2, 1]`), matching the existing per-row-count pattern.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Metric Row's 1-up state fixed: a single metric now always fills the full row width
**Report, with a screenshot showing a 1-up "contained" metric card at roughly half width with a truncated "Subh..." subhead**: "1-up should always stretch full width." Root cause: Stage 2's `@container (max-width: 550px) { .lyra-metric-row { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 1fr)); } }` applies unconditionally, regardless of how many metrics are in the row. With only one metric, default CSS Grid auto-placement fills column 1 and simply leaves column 2 empty rather than stretching the lone item across both — so a single-metric row rendered at roughly half width well above the 360px Stage 3 threshold `.lyra-metric-row--compact` covers (that override only kicks in at ≤360px, and only changes column *count*, not spanning).
- Fixed with a new `isSingle = metrics.length === 1` flag in `MetricRow` (`dashboard-card.tsx`, both "divided" and "contained" branches) setting a new `.lyra-metric-row--single` class, paired with `.lyra-metric-row--single > * { grid-column: 1 / -1; }` in `lyra-tokens.css`/`storybook.css` — makes the lone item span every column the row currently has, at whichever stage. No `@container` guard needed: `grid-column` is a no-op outside grid mode, so the rule is harmless in the wide flex-row stage (where a lone item already stretches via its own `flex: 1`) and only takes effect once Stage 2 or 3 actually switches the row to `display: grid`. Coexists fine with `--compact` (also true for a 1-item row, since `isCompact` is `metrics.length <= 2`) — whichever column count `--compact` leaves the row at, `1 / -1` still spans all of it.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `dashboard-card.tsx`, `Breakpoints.stories.tsx`, and `Dashboards.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Container Grid story gained live controls: swap placeholders for real metric-row cards, pick metric count
**Request**: "add a container grid control that displays metric rows in the card slots on true and allow the user to select how many metrics are displayed in the metric rows." The `Container Grid` story (`Breakpoints.stories.tsx`) previously had no Storybook args/controls at all — just a fixed `render: () => (...)` showing plain `PlaceholderCard`s in every slot. Gave it a typed `ContainerGridArgs` (`showMetricRows: boolean`, `metricCount: number`) and per-story `argTypes`/`args`, even though the shared file `meta` has no `component` — CSF3 supports per-story controls regardless.
- `showMetricRows` ("Show metric rows in card slots") — boolean control, default `false`. When on, `ContainerGridRow` renders a real `DashboardCard` (`metricVariant="contained"`) in each slot instead of `PlaceholderCard`, reusing the same generic `METRIC_ROW_DEMO_DATA` placeholder values already defined for the Metric Row story further down the file (referencing a `const` declared later in the module is safe here — by the time a story's `render` actually runs, the whole module has already finished evaluating top to bottom).
- `metricCount` ("Metrics per row") — range control, 1-4, default 3, sliced onto `METRIC_ROW_DEMO_DATA` via `.slice(0, metricCount)`. Only shown as a control when `showMetricRows` is on, via `argTypes.metricCount.if: { arg: "showMetricRows", truthy: true }`.
- No CSS/class changes needed — `.lyra-container-grid > *` already applies `flex: 1 1 0%; min-width: 0` to any direct child regardless of what it is, so a real `DashboardCard` slots in exactly like a `PlaceholderCard` did, proving the "any card type, including metric cards" design principle live rather than just in a static screenshot-matched demo.
- Descriptive copy above the demo updated to mention both new controls and what they demonstrate (the outer grid's own collapse vs. each metric row's independent, narrower collapse inside its own card).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Every text-entry/select-style component gained a `size` prop ("sm" 32px / "md" 36px, default "md")
**Request** (following a question about whether a 32px input exists for the table toolbar — answer: no, `Input`/`SearchInput`/`Select` were all hardcoded to `h-9`): "add a size prop to the input and select primitives and add a control to the Search Input to make it small 32px - also add this control to any other inputs and selects." Clarified scope via `AskUserQuestion` — all 11 text-field-style components in the library, not just the three named, since 8 more (`EmailInput`, `PasswordInput`, `PhoneInput`, `NumberField`, `Autocomplete`, `DatePicker`/`DateRangePicker`, `TimePicker`/`TimeRangePicker`, `DateTimePicker`/`DateRangeTimePicker`) are built the same way (custom markup, hardcoded `h-9`, no shared base component to patch once).
- Every component gets `size?: "sm" | "md"` (default `"md"`, so no consumer needed a change — purely additive/backward-compatible). `"sm"` renders `h-8` (32px) instead of `h-9` (36px); nothing else (padding, icon size/position, popover/dropdown content) changes — only the field's own height.
- Where a props interface extends `React.InputHTMLAttributes<HTMLInputElement>` (`Input`, `SearchInput`, `EmailInput`), added `"size"` to the existing `Omit<...>` list — the native HTML `size` attribute is a `number` (character width), which would otherwise conflict with our own string-union prop of the same name.
- For components with only one internal field (`Input`, `SearchInput`, `EmailInput`, `PasswordInput`, `PhoneInput`, `NumberField`, `Autocomplete`), the change is a plain ternary in the existing `cn(...)` call.
- `Select`'s single shared `triggerClassName` (already reused between the Radix single-select trigger and the multi-select `Popover` trigger button) got the same ternary — one change covers both modes. Only the closed trigger shrinks; the open dropdown's search field/rows/footer stay full-size, same design choice made for `Autocomplete`, `PhoneInput`'s country panel, `DatePicker`'s calendar panel, and `TimePicker`/`DateTimePicker`'s time selector — a compact trigger doesn't imply a compact popover.
- `TimePicker`/`TimeRangePicker`'s shared `inputShell` was already a factory function taking `(disabled, readonly)` — added `size` as a third parameter rather than inlining the ternary at each of the two call sites.
- `DatePicker`/`DateRangePicker` share a single **module-level** `inputClass` const (not a per-render function) that already encodes disabled/readonly via `data-[disabled=true]`/`data-[readonly=true]` attribute selectors instead of JS-computed classes — extended the same pattern with `data-[size=sm]:h-8` + a `data-size={size}` attribute on each wrapping div, so the const could stay static rather than being converted into a function both components would need to call.
- `DateTimePicker`/`DateRangeTimePicker` each compute their own `inputClass` inline per render (already closure-scoped over `disabled`/`readonly`), so `size` just joined that closure directly — no structural change needed.
- Added a "Size" Storybook control (`select`, options `["sm", "md"]`) to all 11 story files (`Input`, `Select`, `SearchInput`, `EmailInput`, `PasswordInput`, `PhoneInput`, `NumberField`, `Autocomplete`, `DatePicker.stories.tsx` covering both `DatePicker`+`DateRangePicker`, `TimePicker.stories.tsx` covering both `TimePicker`+`TimeRangePicker`, `DateTimePicker.stories.tsx` covering both `DateTimePicker`+`DateRangeTimePicker`) — meta-level `argTypes` where `meta.component` is set and the story already spread `args`/`rest` onto the component (`Input`, `SearchInput`, `EmailInput`, `PhoneInput`, `NumberField`, `Autocomplete`, `DatePicker`'s `Default`, `DateTimePicker`'s `Default`), explicit `size={size}` prop-passing where the story enumerates props one-by-one instead of spreading (`Select`), and per-story typed `StoryObj<{ size: "sm" | "md" }>` overrides for the second component sharing a file whose `meta.component` points at the first (`Select` didn't need this since it's the sole `meta.component`; `DatePicker.stories.tsx`'s `DateRange` story, `TimePicker.stories.tsx`'s `Default`+`Range` stories, `DateTimePicker.stories.tsx`'s `RangeWithTime` story all did).
- Did not change any real consumer's actual usage (e.g. `table.tsx`'s toolbar `SearchInput` stays at the "md" default) — this task was scoped to adding the capability, not applying it anywhere yet.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` across the whole repo (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of all 11 component files, all 11 story files, plus three real consumers (`table.tsx`, `filter-chip.tsx`, `create-new.tsx`) to confirm the additive/optional prop didn't need any consumer changes — all clean.

### Table toolbar: search field is now "sm" (32px), placeholder default changed to "Search", and a "Clear" ghost button was moved to the right of the whole filters group
**Request**: "update the toolbar in the table component to be a sm input and change the text to say 'Search' instead of 'Quick Search' - add a clear ghost button to the right of the filters" — the first real consumer of the `size` prop just added.
- Both `TableToolbar` `SearchInput` instances (title-mode's row 2, no-title-mode's row 1) now pass `size="sm"`.
- `searchPlaceholder`'s default changed from `"Quick Search"` to `"Search"` (`table.tsx`'s prop doc comment updated to match). Real consumers that explicitly pass `searchPlaceholder="Quick Search"` as a literal override (`Outbound-Campaigns/OutboundCampaignsPage.tsx`, `lyra-form-generator/FormsPage.tsx`, `agent-next-gen-v1`/`lyra-ux-templates`'s `DesktopDesignsPage.tsx`) are unaffected by a default-value change and were intentionally left alone — out of scope for this request, flagged to the user in case they want it propagated. lyra-ui's own three stories that had the same explicit override (`Table.stories.tsx`, `DataManagement.stories.tsx`, `Dashboards.stories.tsx`) had it removed so they actually show the new default rather than silently keeping the old text.
- **"Clear" button repositioning**: previously baked into the `filterChips` variable, right after `filterDefs`' own mapped `FilterChip`s — meaning it rendered in the *middle* of the filters group whenever a consumer also passed a custom `filters` node or Advanced Search, not at the true right edge. Extracted into its own `clearFiltersButton` variable (same `variant="ghost"` `Button`, same `hasActiveFilters`/`onFilterClear` wiring) and moved to the end of all three `{filterChips}{filters}{advancedSearchNode}` render groups (title-mode's row 2, no-title-mode's two `isWide` branches) — now always the rightmost element of the whole filters section. The narrow/collapsed filter dropdown's own "Clear all" text link (a different, already-existing affordance inside that popover) was left untouched.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `table.tsx`, `Table.stories.tsx`, `DataManagement.stories.tsx`, and `Dashboards.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Follow-up: the "Clear" button was invisible in both toolbar demos — `hasActiveFilters` never covered the Query Builder, and the normal toolbar's default state had nothing active to clear
**Report**: "you didn't add the clear ghost button to the toolbar - add to both the normal table toolbar and the query builder toolbar." The button's code was correct and in the right position (per the entry above), but it's gated behind `hasActiveFilters`, which had two separate reasons to never be true in either demo:
- **"Toolbar — Query Builder" story** (`Table.stories.tsx`'s `AdvancedSearch` export): this toolbar doesn't pass `filterDefs`/`filterValues` at all — it's driven entirely by `showAdvancedSearch`/`advancedSearchApplied`. `hasActiveFilters` only ever checked `filterDefs`, so it was structurally impossible for the Clear button to appear here no matter what the user did in the query builder panel. Fixed at the source in `table.tsx`: `hasActiveFilters` now also considers `advancedSearchApplied`, so the "Clear" button shows once a query is actually applied, regardless of which of the two filtering mechanisms (`filterDefs` chips vs. Advanced Search) is in play. Wired the story's `onFilterClear` (previously unset in this story, since there was nothing driving it) to reset `appliedRoot`, `root`, and `savedSearchName` back to empty, so clicking "Clear" fully resets the query builder rather than just hiding the applied-state label.
- **"Toolbar" story** (`ToolbarDemo`): `hasActiveFilters` here was already correctly wired to real `filterDefs` state, but the demo's own default `filterValues` was `{ description: [], createdBy: [] }` — nothing selected, so nothing to clear, so the button correctly didn't render on load. Changed the default to `{ description: [], createdBy: ["Jim Smith"] }` so the Clear button is visible immediately without first having to click a filter chip — otherwise the demo looked identical to before the button existed.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `table.tsx` and `Table.stories.tsx` — clean.

### `Input.stories.tsx`'s "With buttons" layout ignored the "Max width" control entirely
**Report, with a screenshot**: "when i select the control 'With buttons' and the max width control is set to false, the input component is adding a max-width - it should be full width." The "With buttons" branch of `Default`'s `render` had a leftover hardcoded `w-72` wrapper and `w-48` `Input` className from before the `maxWidth` control existed — every other layout in this story (the plain field, "Label only", the horizontal row) already read the `maxWidth` arg, but this one branch never did, so toggling "Max width" off still left the field capped at 192px instead of going full width.
- Fixed to match the same pattern the plain branch (and `Select.stories.tsx`'s already-correct "With buttons" branch) use: the wrapper is `cn("flex flex-col gap-1.5", maxWidth ? "max-w-[320px]" : "w-full")`, and `Input` itself gets `className="flex-1 min-w-0"` so it grows to fill whatever room the buttons leave inside that row instead of sitting at a fixed 192px.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Input.stories.tsx` — clean.

### "Max width" fields (Input/Select) and Form Grid's "Static Width" fields standardized on a 240px min-width / 320px max-width range
**Request**: "can we make all input / select fields that are set to max-width to have a max-width of 320px and a min-width of 240px and when they get to 240px? - apply this to the form Grid after applying to the input / select fields." Previously Input.stories.tsx's and Select.stories.tsx's "Max width" control only ever applied a bare `max-w-[320px]` — a ceiling with no floor, so the field could shrink arbitrarily narrow with no lower bound at all.
- `Input.stories.tsx`: both the plain field and the "With buttons" branch now use `min-w-[240px] max-w-[320px]` (was `max-w-[320px]` on the plain branch; the "With buttons" branch's own hardcoded-width bug was already fixed in the prior entry, now carries the same 240/320 range instead of the interim `max-w-[320px]`-only fix).
- `Select.stories.tsx`: both the plain field and "With buttons" branch's wrapper divs updated the same way.
- Applied to Form Grid second, per the request's explicit ordering: `Breakpoints.stories.tsx`'s `FORM_GRID_STATIC_FIELD_CSS` — `clamp(260px, calc(20cqi + 120px), 320px)` → `clamp(240px, calc(20cqi + 120px), 320px)`. Only the clamp's floor argument changed; the linear formula itself (`20cqi + 120`) was left untouched since it already produces the identical curve, just now unclamped 60px lower — the ceiling is still reached at the same ≥1000px container width as before, and the floor point simply moved from ≤700px to ≤600px. Wrapping mechanism (flex-wrap once a field can't fit at its current shared width) is unchanged.
- Updated every doc comment/story description referencing the old "260px"/"260-320px" language across both files to say 240px/240-320px, and cross-referenced the three call sites (Input, Select, Form Grid) in each other's comments so the shared standard is discoverable from any of them.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `Input.stories.tsx`, `Select.stories.tsx`, and `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Table toolbar's SearchInput adopts the 240px min-width/320px max-width standard
**Request**: "now apply the min-max width sizing to the search input in the toolbar of the table."
- `table.tsx`'s `TableToolbar` had both `SearchInput` instances (title-mode row 2, no-title-mode row 1) hardcoded to a static `w-[260px]` — never responsive to available toolbar width. Changed both to `flex-1 min-w-[240px] max-w-[320px]`, matching the same 240/320 standard already used by Input.stories.tsx/Select.stories.tsx's "Max width" control and Form Grid's "Static Width" fields.
- Title-mode's row 2 (`{SearchInput}{filters}`) is already a full-width flex-col child (default `align-items: stretch`), so `flex-1` on the search field alone was enough to let it actually grow/shrink against the filters' natural width.
- No-title-mode's row needed one more change: the search field's immediate flex parent (the `flex items-center gap-2` div holding the left-panel toggle, search, and filters) previously had no `flex-grow` of its own, so any extra row width (from `justify-between` against the action buttons) was never handed down for the search field to grow into. Added `flex-1 min-w-0` to that wrapping div so it claims the row's available space, which the search field (the only `flex-1` child inside it) then fills up to its own 320px cap — toggle button and filters keep their natural widths either way, so this doesn't change their positioning.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Table.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Table toolbar's title-mode filters now go responsive too
**Request**: "in the toolbar, when the show title control is active the filters do not go responsive - they should always respond to the query regardless of if they are inline with the action button or not."
- `table.tsx`'s `TableToolbar` title-mode branch (row 1 = title + actions, row 2 = search + filters) always rendered `filterChips`/`filters`/`advancedSearchNode`/`clearFiltersButton` inline, with no `isWide` check at all — unlike the no-title layout, which already collapses to `collapsedFilterChip`/`advancedSearchNode` once the toolbar's own measured width (`containerWidth`, via the existing `ResizeObserver` on `stableRef`) drops below the 991px `isWide` threshold.
- Root cause: the title-mode branch was written before the no-title layout's responsive collapse existed and was never updated to match it.
- Fix: row 2 now branches on the same `isWide` exactly like the no-title layout does — inline chips/filters/Query Builder/Clear button when wide, `collapsedFilterChip` + `advancedSearchNode` when narrow. Also added the same "custom `filters` node gets its own row once narrow" fallback the no-title layout uses, so a consumer-supplied `filters` node (distinct from the `filterDefs`-driven chips) isn't dropped when the chips collapse.
- `isWide` is measured off the toolbar's own root regardless of title mode (the `ResizeObserver` target is `stableRef`, attached to the outermost toolbar `div` in both branches), so this Just Works without any new measurement plumbing.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Table.stories.tsx` — clean.

Now `!isNarrow && actionButtons` on that row, plus a new row below (after the existing narrow-mode `filters` row) rendering `isNarrow && actionButtons` inside a `flex items-center justify-end` wrapper.

### InteriorPanel stories: fixed panel escaping its container below the 1050px narrow breakpoint
**Request**: "what is the pixel width of the container breakpoint for interior panels? - when this hits the panel should still be contained within its parent container (underneath the page header and other fixed elements) not outside like shown in the screenshot" (screenshot showed the Left/"Filters" panel rendering full-page-height, over the PageHeader instead of confined below it).
- The breakpoint is 1050px — `interior-panel.tsx`'s `isNarrow = parentWidth < 1050`, measured via `ResizeObserver` against `InteriorPanel`'s own DOM parent element (not a `@container` CSS query). Below that width, it switches from a normal in-flow flex child to `position: absolute; top: 0; height: 100%`, overlaying the content column instead of squeezing it further.
- Root cause of the screenshot's bug: `position: absolute` anchors to the nearest ANCESTOR with `position: relative` (or any non-static position) — `InteriorPanel.stories.tsx`'s row wrapping the content column + panel (`<div className="flex flex-1 overflow-hidden">`) had no such positioning, so once narrow, the panel escaped to whatever positioned ancestor was next up the tree (or the viewport, absent one), rendering above/over the `PageHeader` instead of confined beneath it.
- This is a known, already-documented issue elsewhere: `admin-shell.tsx`'s own "Interior panels row" comment describes and guards against the exact same failure mode with `relative` on that row. Applied the identical fix here — both `Right` and `Left` stories' row is now `className="relative flex flex-1 overflow-hidden"`.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `InteriorPanel.stories.tsx` — clean.

### AdminShell's side-panel pin guard fixed to match Agent Next Gen's behavior
**Request**: "the side panel components for admin and agent should functionally be the same - there may be differences in content and icons but from a responsiveness perspective they should not be different." Investigated by comparing `admin-shell.tsx`'s real "container-width pin guard" against `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`'s own copy of the same pattern (already used as the two entries in the "JS-Measured Thresholds" Breakpoints story).
- Found a real discrepancy: both compute `effectivePinned = isNarrowContainer ? false : pinned` to force the rendered `pinned` prop false below their threshold, but Agent Next Gen additionally has a `useEffect` that force-closes the panel (`setSidePanelOpen(false)`) AND resets the underlying pin state (`setSidePanelPinned(false)`) the moment `isNarrowContainer` becomes true. `admin-shell.tsx` was missing this effect entirely — `leftPanelOpen`/`rightSidePanelOpen` were left untouched by the narrow-container transition.
- Consequence of the missing effect: a panel that was open-and-pinned when the container crossed 1024px didn't close — it instantly became an open-and-FLOATING overlay sitting on top of the content (since `pinned` flips to `false`, switching `SidePanel` to its absolute-overlay branch, but `open` was still `true`), rather than collapsing away and requiring a deliberate hover to reveal it again, same as Agent Next Gen already does correctly.
- Fix: added the equivalent `useEffect` to `admin-shell.tsx`, resetting both `leftPanelPinned`/`leftPanelOpen` and `rightSidePanelPinned`/`rightSidePanelOpen` to `false` when `isNarrowContainer` becomes true — local React state only, not the pin/open cookies, so a fresh page load at a wide width still honors the user's last real saved preference; widening back within the same session requires re-pinning manually, matching Agent Next Gen's behavior exactly.
- The Breakpoints story's `SidePanelPinGuardDemo` (added in the previous change) didn't need updating — its simplified `open = effectivePinned || hoverOpen` was never stateful/sticky in the first place, so it already matched the corrected (not the buggy) behavior.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `admin-shell.tsx` and `AdminShell.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Agent Next Gen's pin-guard threshold unified to 1024px (was 768px)

- Updated the surrounding comments in that file (both mentions of "768px") to say 1024px and cross-reference `admin-shell.tsx`.
- Updated the Breakpoints "JS-Measured Thresholds" story: both `SidePanelPinGuardDemo` calls now pass `threshold={1024}`, the Agent Next Gen entry's label changed from "768px" to "1024px", its `ResizeBox` widened from 900 to 1200 to match the AdminShell entry, and the intro paragraph/demo doc comment rewritten to describe one shared threshold instead of "two different values, not a single shared constant."

### Breakpoints story's SidePanel demo no longer auto-restores pinned state
**Request**: "i noticed in the storybook story the side panel knows it was pinned and returns to the pinned state when the threshold goes above 1024 yet in Lyra UX templates and Outbound Campaigns (and probably others) it does not go back to pinned if the threshold goes above 1024 after being unpinned when it went below the threshold."
- Root cause: a mistake in the previous change's own reasoning. `SidePanelPinGuardDemo`'s `effectivePinned = isNarrow ? false : pinned` only overrides the RENDERED value while narrow — the underlying `pinned` state itself was never reset, so it stayed `true` (if it started `true`) the whole time the demo was narrow, and widening back out simply reverted to that still-`true` value, auto-restoring the pinned state. The real, now-fixed components (`admin-shell.tsx`'s pin guard, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`'s copy) both reset the UNDERLYING pin state itself (not just its rendered effect) the moment they go narrow, so re-pinning after widening back out requires a deliberate click — the demo had silently drifted from that.
- Fix: added a `useEffect` to `SidePanelPinGuardDemo` that resets `pinned` (and `hoverOpen`) to `false` whenever `isNarrow` becomes `true`, mirroring the real components' reset effects exactly (previously the demo only had this derived-value override with no underlying reset at all).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Reversed: pin guard now auto-restores pinned state instead of permanently clearing it
**Request**: "actually I wanted the reverse - I wanted it to go behave like the storybook but you made storybook like the apps - reverse it." Follows directly from the previous two entries: those made `admin-shell.tsx`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, and the Breakpoints demo all agree on "permanently clear the pin state when narrow, requiring a manual re-pin after widening back out" — consistent with each other, but the wrong shared behavior. The desired one (matching what the Breakpoints demo originally did before any of today's changes) is: auto-restore to pinned once widened back out.
- `Breakpoints.stories.tsx`'s `SidePanelPinGuardDemo`: removed the `useEffect` that reset `pinned` to `false` on narrow. `pinned` is untouched by `isNarrow` again — only the derived `effectivePinned` is overridden for rendering, so widening back out naturally restores it.
- `admin-shell.tsx`: replaced the "reset `leftPanelPinned`/`rightSidePanelPinned` AND `leftPanelOpen`/`rightSidePanelOpen`" effect with one that only manages `leftPanelOpen`/`rightSidePanelOpen` — closes them when going narrow (still avoids the open-and-pinned-panel-instantly-becomes-a-floating-overlay bug that motivated the original fix), and on widening back out, sets them to match whatever `leftPanelPinned`/`rightSidePanelPinned` already are (untouched throughout) — restoring open-and-pinned automatically. A `skipFirstRun` ref guards the very first effect run so a fresh page load doesn't clobber a legitimately cookie-saved "collapsed while still pinned" state.
- `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`: same corrected pattern — `sidePanelPinned` no longer reset; only `sidePanelOpen` is managed, restored to `sidePanelPinned`'s value when widening back out, with the same first-run guard.
- Cross-repo note surfaced to the user (not yet acted on): the REAL `AgentNextGenPage.tsx` in `lyra-ux-templates` (aliased to this same `agent-next-gen-v1` pattern, referenced in its own comments) has its own independent copy of the OLD "permanently clear pin" behavior, predating this session — `OutboundEngagementPage.tsx`/`OutboundCampaignsPage.tsx`/`MonitorDashboardPage.tsx` all consume `AdminShell` directly (via the `@nicecxone/lyra-ui` → `../lyra-ui/src/index.ts` alias in `lyra-ux-templates/vite.config.ts`), so they're already fixed by the `admin-shell.tsx` change above with no separate edit needed.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `admin-shell.tsx`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`, and `Breakpoints.stories.tsx` — clean.
