### Fixed Draggable's built-in header overlay drifting out of vertical alignment
**Request**: [screenshots] "I'm noticing the Draggable header is not center-aligned horizontally center with the drag handle, yet it is in actual prototype repo panels (screenshot 2) - fix this so the storybook is center-aligned like the prototype repo."
- Root cause: `draggable.tsx`'s `BuiltInHeaderControls` (the grip + dock-toggle overlay used whenever a consumer doesn't supply `renderHeaderControls`) is `position: absolute`, fixed at `h-16` (64px), and vertically centers its own contents within that fixed band — a constant dating back to when `container-header.tsx` used `py-5` padding (64px total header height, noted in the old comment). `container-header.tsx`'s padding was since trimmed twice (py-5 → py-3 → py-2.5, its own comment explains why), bringing its real rendered height down to 60px (py-2.5×2 + its h-10 title box) — but `draggable.tsx`'s `h-16` constant was never updated to track it, so the overlay's vertical center drifted ~4px away from the real header's center underneath it. `Draggable.stories.tsx`'s "Float" story renders a bare `ContainerHeader` with no `renderHeaderControls`, so it relies entirely on this now-mismatched overlay, which is why the drift was visible there.
- `AiPanel` (the real prototype's usage) never hits this bug at all: it supplies its own `renderHeaderControls`, rendering the grip icon INLINE as `ContainerHeader`'s own `icon` slot — a literal sibling of the title in the same flex row, centered together via that row's own `items-center`, immune to any drift between two separately-hardcoded heights.
- Fix: changed `BuiltInHeaderControls`'s `h-16` to `h-[60px]`, matching `ContainerHeader`'s actual current height exactly. Updated the comment to explain the drift, cross-reference `AiPanel`'s alternate (immune) approach, and flag keeping this in sync if `ContainerHeader`'s padding/title-box height changes again.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `draggable.tsx`, `Draggable.stories.tsx`, and `ai-panel.tsx` — clean.

### Draggable's built-in header overlay now measures the real header instead of hardcoding its height
**Request**: "is there a reason youre hard coding the container header instead of using the containerheader component?" — a fair challenge to the previous fix (which just corrected the hardcoded `h-16` to `h-[60px]`, still a hardcoded assumption, still capable of drifting again).
- Honest answer: no good one. `Draggable` doesn't own or render the header itself (`children` is generic `React.ReactNode` — convention, not enforcement, decides the first child acts as the header), so `BuiltInHeaderControls` assuming a fixed pixel height was always fragile — exactly what caused the previous bug, and it could just as easily happen again (or with any consumer using a different header component entirely, not `ContainerHeader` at all).
- Fix: replaced the hardcoded height with a real measurement. Added `headerContentRef` (attached to the wrapping div that renders `children` in both the docked and float branches) plus a `ResizeObserver`-backed `useLayoutEffect` that reads `headerContentRef.current?.firstElementChild`'s actual `getBoundingClientRect().height` into `headerHeight` state, which `BuiltInHeaderControls` now uses via inline `style={{ height: headerHeight }}` instead of a Tailwind height class. Skipped entirely when `renderHeaderControls` is supplied (that path never renders `BuiltInHeaderControls` at all, so there's nothing for it to drive). `headerHeight` starts at a `60` fallback (matching `ContainerHeader`'s current real height) until the first real measurement lands.
- Confirmed no regression risk: every other real consumer of `Draggable` (`draggable-panel.tsx`, `agent-notifications.tsx`, `ai-panel.tsx`) already uses `renderHeaderControls` (the inline-grip approach, immune to this class of bug) — `Draggable.stories.tsx`'s "Float" story (bare `ContainerHeader`, no `renderHeaderControls`) was the only place actually exercising `BuiltInHeaderControls`, which is exactly where the original misalignment was reported.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `draggable.tsx`, `Draggable.stories.tsx`, and `ai-panel.tsx` — clean.

### Fixed ghost hit-area bug across all Draggable stories
**Request**: "in the With main container story I'm noticing a bug that you are always needing to fix in other prototypes. To reproduce: click undock -> move panel and resize to full width -> try to click Toggle Right Panel Button (it cannot be clicked) - this is the same bug you have been fixing in other prototypes - let's fix it here in Storybook so it never happens again - make sure whatever you do applies to all draggable stories."
- Root cause: an ancestor wrapper positioning a float-mode `Draggable` panel (`position:fixed`/`absolute`) had `pointerEvents:"auto"` set on it, but that wrapper's own layout box does NOT follow `Draggable`'s CSS-`transform`-based drag movement — after the panel is dragged away, the wrapper's stale, invisible, pre-drag-position rectangle stays hit-testable and silently swallows clicks meant for real UI underneath it (here, the `PageHeader`'s "Toggle right panel" button). `Draggable`'s own root already sets `pointer-events:auto` on itself and correctly moves with the transform, so no ancestor wrapper ever needs to also be interactive.
- Confirmed via grep that `AdminShell.stories.tsx`'s `AdminShellWithAiDemo` and `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`'s AI/notifications float wrappers already use the correct unconditional `pointerEvents:"none"` pattern — `Draggable.stories.tsx` was the only place in the repo still buggy, and the only story-level exhibit of the pattern that recurs across consumer apps (per `AdminShell.stories.tsx`'s own comment: "recurred at least 3 times across consumer apps").
- Fixed in all three float-mode stories in `Draggable.stories.tsx` (the `Docked` story never enters float mode, so it was never at risk):
  - `WithMainContainer`: changed `pointerEvents: panelState === "closed" ? "none" : "auto"` to unconditional `pointerEvents: "none"`, with a comment quoting the user's repro steps and cross-referencing the already-correct patterns elsewhere.
  - `Float`: added `pointer-events-none` to the outer positioning wrapper (previously had no explicit pointer-events at all, defaulting to inherited `auto`) — not user-visibly broken in this demo (nothing else clickable nearby), but the same latent risk, fixed proactively per "make sure whatever you do applies to all draggable stories."
  - `Interactive`: same `pointer-events-none` fix applied to its float-mode wrapper, with a short cross-reference comment to `Float`'s fuller explanation instead of repeating it verbatim.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Fixed float panel landing off-screen after resize-then-redock-then-undock
**Request**: "in the With main container story I'm noticing a bug ... To reproduce: click undock -> move panel and resize to full width -> try to click Toggle Right Panel Button (it cannot be clicked)" was the previous (ghost hit-area) bug; this is a follow-up report against the same story: "click undock -> move panel and resize to full width -> redock -> undock again immediately -> the draggable container is off the screen ... the user cannot drag it back to the page - it should stay within the screen's viewable area."
- Root cause: `WithMainContainer`'s own float-position anchor math (`floatLeft.current = r.left + containerRef.current.offsetWidth - width - 16`, run on every undock) measures `containerRef`'s remaining width at the moment the dock button is clicked — but that click still fires synchronously against the CURRENT (pre-update) DOM, where the previously-docked panel (left at whatever large `width` it was resized to before redocking) is still occupying its full layout space. The panel's own (large) `width` therefore gets subtracted out of an already-squeezed remaining width, producing a deeply negative `left` and stranding the panel off-screen — with no way to drag it back, since the drag handle itself was off-screen too. The identical anchor-math pattern (`aiFloatLeft.current = r.left + containerRef.current.offsetWidth - aiWidth - 16`) also exists in `AdminShell.stories.tsx`'s AI panel demo, confirming this is a general risk of the documented "consumer anchors floatLeft" pattern (see `draggable.tsx`'s own "Panel Resize Direction Rules" comment) rather than a one-off mistake in this one story — and per the user's own suspicion ("this may be occurring in prototypes, I'm not sure"), any real consumer following that same documented pattern is equally exposed.
- `draggable.tsx` already documents that "the panel's box must always stay fully inside the browser viewport ... enforced entirely inside this component ... does NOT depend on how a consumer positions the float wrapper" — but that containment previously only ran reactively, in the `window` `resize` listener. It never ran on mount or on a dock→float transition, so a bad *starting* position from a consumer's own anchor math was never self-corrected on an otherwise-static viewport.
- Fix (in `draggable.tsx`, not the story): extracted the resize handler's float clamp math into a shared `clampOffsetIntoViewport(offset, width, height)` helper, and added a `useLayoutEffect` keyed on `[variant]` that calls it every time the panel transitions into (or mounts already in) float mode — so Draggable now self-corrects its on-screen position immediately, before the browser paints, regardless of what math any consumer's float wrapper uses to position it. This fixes the bug at its source for every current and future consumer (the story itself needed no changes) and is a no-op when the panel is already on-screen.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `draggable.tsx`, `Draggable.stories.tsx`, `AdminShell.stories.tsx`, and `ai-panel.tsx` — clean.

### Added a two-panel single-dock rule story for Draggable
**Request**: "we need a story for multiple dragged panels with one dock at a time: add another panel (call them panels A and B) and when one is docked the other is in drag mode - same behavior as Lyra UX templates prototype)."
- New `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` story in `Draggable.stories.tsx` ("With main container — two panels (single-dock rule)"), modeled directly on the real prototype's implementation in `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx` (its AI panel + Notifications panel, `handleAiVariantChange`/`handleNotifVariantChange`): two independent `Draggable` panels, each with its own open/close state machine and float anchor (duplicated per-panel — `a*`/`b*` — rather than shared via a hook, matching how the real prototype keeps `ai*`/`notif*` state as separate named blocks).
- Single-dock rule: `handleAVariantChange`/`handleBVariantChange` check whether the OTHER panel is already `"docked"` when this one docks; if so, force the other back to `"float"` (recomputing its float anchor from the container), so at most one of the two panels is ever docked simultaneously — exact same cross logic as the real prototype's pair of handlers.
- One shared docked slot (sibling of the content `containerRef`, same collapsing-width wrapper as `WithMainContainer`) renders whichever of the two panels is currently docked, since only one ever needs it at a time. Panel A starts docked, Panel B starts floating (staggered position, offset left and down from A's anchor) so the demo opens already showing the scenario to test: dock B, watch A auto-float.
- Both panels' float wrappers use the unconditional `pointerEvents:"none"` pattern (task #78) and rely on `draggable.tsx`'s own mount/variant-change viewport clamp (task #79) for containment — no per-story workarounds needed for either previously-fixed bug class.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Fixed Draggable resetting its size on dock/undock (remount bug)
**Request**: "there is a bug. To reproduce: resize panel B to max width -> click dock -> the panel area is the correct size but the panel itself is not (see screenshot)" — reported against the new `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` story, but the same structural bug already existed in `WithMainContainer` too (just never exercised by that story's simpler repro path).
- Root cause: both stories rendered a panel's `<Draggable>` element inside TWO SEPARATE, mutually-exclusive conditionally-rendered JSX blocks — one for float mode (`{variant === "float" && (...)}`, nested inside `containerRef`) and one for docked mode (`{variant === "docked" && (...)}`, a sibling after it). Even though both blocks referenced the exact same `panel` variable, React reconciles by tree position, not by object identity — moving an element from one conditional branch to a structurally different one is an unmount of the old instance plus a mount of a brand new one. So every dock/undock toggle fully remounted `Draggable`, and its internal `useState(defaultWidth)`/`useState(defaultHeight)` re-initialized from the (now stale) `defaultWidth`/`defaultHeight` props rather than keeping whatever size the panel had actually been resized to. The docked-slot wrapper's own collapsing-width `div` is owned by the STORY (mirrored via `onWidthChange`), so it stayed correctly sized — only the remounted `Draggable` inside it snapped back, exactly matching "the panel area is the correct size but the panel itself is not."
- Fix (both `WithMainContainer` and `MultiplePanelsSingleDock`): each panel now renders inside exactly ONE persistent wrapper (present continuously whenever the panel is `mounted`), moved outside `containerRef` (safe — `position:fixed` isn't affected by a non-transformed ancestor). Only that wrapper's own inline `style`/`className` switches between "docked, normal flex-flow box" and "float, fixed-position overlay" based on `variant`; the panel element's position in the JSX tree never changes, so `Draggable` is never unmounted and its internal size state survives every dock/undock. `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` additionally used to funnel BOTH panels through one shared "docked slot" whose CONTENT switched between panel A and B — replaced with two independent per-panel wrappers (each collapsing to `width:0, overflow:visible` whenever that panel isn't the currently-docked one), relying on the already-enforced single-dock rule to guarantee at most one has real width at a time.
- Cross-repo note surfaced to the user (not acted on, per the standing "not yet I need to validate" hold on `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`): the real prototype in `lyra-ux-templates/src/components/AgentNextGenPage.tsx` has the identical structural pattern (`aiPanel`/`notifPanel` each rendered across two separate float/docked JSX blocks), so it likely has this exact same remount-resets-size bug live today. Left untouched.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Fixed Draggable's own header controls shifting position on dock/undock
**Request**: "agent next gen and lyra-ux-templates work correctly - but the issue now is when I swap panel docks the containerheader buttons are weirdly positioned (see screenshot). They should not change positions." — a direct side effect of the previous fix: keeping `Draggable` mounted continuously across dock/undock (instead of remounting) exposed a second, previously-masked bug inside `draggable.tsx` itself.
- Root cause: `draggable.tsx`'s docked and float branches return DIFFERENT numbers/orders of children — docked: `[edgeResizeHandle, headerControls?, content]`; float: `[headerControls?, content, cornerResizeHandle]`. With no explicit `key` on any of them, React reconciles a component's children by POSITION, not identity. Previously this never mattered: every consumer's own float/docked wrapper structure caused the whole `Draggable` to fully remount on every toggle anyway (the bug just fixed), so there was never a live diff between the two branches to get wrong. Now that `Draggable` survives a dock/undock toggle, switching branches makes React see, at the same index, an element of a different type than before (e.g. index 0 is `edgeResizeHandle` in docked but `headerControls` in float) — it tears down whatever was at that index and mounts something new instead of recognizing `headerControls` and `content` (which contains `headerContentRef`) as the same logical elements that just moved position. Visible as the built-in grip/dock overlay and header content very briefly misaligning immediately after a dock/undock.
- Fix: extracted `headerControlsNode` and `contentNode` as shared JSX (built once, referenced from both branches) and gave each an explicit `key` (`"header-controls"`, `"content"`), plus keys on the two resize-handle divs (`"edge-resize"`, `"corner-resize"`) for the same reason. React's children reconciliation matches by `key` across a reordering/reshaping of the children array, so `headerContentRef`'s div (and the built-in overlay) are now recognized as the same persistent elements across a dock/undock toggle instead of being torn down and recreated.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `draggable.tsx`, `Draggable.stories.tsx`, `ai-panel.tsx`, `agent-notifications.tsx`, and `draggable-panel.tsx` — clean.

### MultiplePanelsSingleDock: Panel B now starts closed, opens straight into the docked slot
**Request**: "for the with main container - two panels: have them both start docked (do not display Panel B until it is clicked then have it open in the docked position replacing panel A)."
- Panel B's initial state changed from open+floating (`bOpen=true`, `bVariant="float"`) to closed (`bOpen=false`, `bMounted=false`, `bState="closed"`, `bVariant="docked"`) — its dock preference is "docked" from the start, same as Panel A, it's just not displayed until toggled open.
- The `bOpen` open/close effect now also runs the single-dock cross-check (previously only present in `handleBVariantChange`, triggered by an explicit dock-button click): if B is opening directly into `"docked"` and A is currently the docked one, force A to `"float"` first. This covers the new path where B claims the docked slot by being OPENED (its variant was already `"docked"`) rather than by the user manually clicking its dock toggle.
- Net effect: on load, only Panel A is visible (docked). Clicking "Toggle panel B" opens B straight into the docked slot, displacing A to float — no manual dock click needed on B first.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Added close buttons to WithMainContainer and MultiplePanelsSingleDock's panels
**Request**: "we are missing close buttons. Add them to the top right of the container headers."
- Naively adding `ContainerHeader`'s own `onClose` prop to these panels' headers would have visually collided with `Draggable`'s default `BuiltInHeaderControls` — an absolutely-positioned overlay (used whenever a consumer doesn't supply `renderHeaderControls`) whose dock/undock button sits in the exact same top-right corner a close button would occupy.
- Fix: switched all three panels (`WithMainContainer`'s single panel, and Panel A/Panel B in `MultiplePanelsSingleDock`) to the `renderHeaderControls` inline pattern already used by every real consumer of `Draggable` (`ai-panel.tsx`, `agent-notifications.tsx`, `draggable-panel.tsx`) instead of relying on the default overlay: `ContainerHeader` is now rendered directly inside `renderHeaderControls`, with the grip icon passed via its `icon` slot (float mode only, matching `AiPanel`'s exact approach) and the dock/undock button plus the new close button both passed via `actions` — all genuinely part of `ContainerHeader`'s own header row, so there's no absolute-overlay collision to begin with. `onClose` closes each panel the same way its page-header toggle button does (`setPanelOpen(false)`/`setAOpen(false)`/`setBOpen(false)`).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Toggle panel buttons now show a selected state while their panel is open
**Request**: "well done. now when the panels are open, toggle the buttons into a selected state."
- "Toggle right panel" (`WithMainContainer`) and "Toggle panel A"/"Toggle panel B" (`MultiplePanelsSingleDock`) now get `bg-lyra-bg-active-moderate text-lyra-fg-active-strong hover:bg-lyra-bg-active-moderate` (held through hover so it doesn't flicker back to the plain outline color) whenever their panel is open, plus `aria-pressed` for accessibility — the exact same selected/pressed idiom `PanelPinButton` already uses for "currently open" (itself matching `LeftNav`'s active-item treatment), rather than inventing a new one.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Float panels now default below their own toggle button, and remember where they were dragged
**Request**: "let's address the toggle buttons later - I would like you to make all of the undocked draggables appear below the button they toggle with unless they are moved, then keep them in their last position when toggled open/closed, currently they appear over the buttons which is not desireable." Clarified with a screenshot: with both panels toggled open in `MultiplePanelsSingleDock`, Panel A should float directly below "Toggle panel A" (not overlapping the header), while Panel B stays docked full-height on the right.
- Root cause: the float anchor (`floatLeft`, and `top` computed fresh every render) was always based on `containerRef`'s own width/position — landing panels flush against the content area's top-right corner, on top of the `PageHeader` and its toggle buttons, with no relationship to which button actually opened them.
- Fix: each panel's toggle button now has its own ref (`panelButtonRef` in `WithMainContainer`; `buttonARef`/`buttonBRef` in `MultiplePanelsSingleDock`). A new `ensureFloatAnchor`/`ensureAFloatAnchor`/`ensureBFloatAnchor` helper sets `floatLeft`/`floatTop` from that button's own `getBoundingClientRect()` (left-aligned under the button, 8px gap below it) — but ONLY the first time a panel ever floats (guarded by the existing `floatLeft.current === null` check). Once set — whether from this default or from the user dragging the panel — the position is never recomputed again by this code; dragging itself is `Draggable`'s own internal `offset` transform, which already survives both open/close and dock/undock (only reset by `toggleVariant`, which these helpers don't touch), so anchoring once is sufficient for "keep them in their last position" to hold everywhere.
- Broadened slightly beyond the literal "toggled open/closed" ask: previously, docking a panel reset its `floatLeft` to `null`, discarding any drag position the moment it round-tripped through the dock slot. Removed that reset (`handleVariantChange`/`handleAVariantChange`/`handleBVariantChange` now call `ensureFloatAnchor` instead of unconditionally recomputing), so a dragged position now also survives a dock → undock cycle, not just open → close.
- `MultiplePanelsSingleDock`'s old "stagger B's default anchor 32px further left and 40px lower than A's" hack is gone — now unnecessary, since each panel anchors to its OWN button and the two buttons are naturally apart from each other.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Added an "App Header" control to MultiplePanelsSingleDock
**Request**: "add a control to the story that says 'App Header' True/False. When True, display the App Header and put the toggle buttons as icons in the app header (remove them from the page header) and have them start in drag mode instead of pinned. Start with them both closed."
- Extracted the story's render body into a standalone `MultiplePanelsSingleDockDemo({ appHeader })` component (previously an inline arrow function passed to `render`), following the same `argTypes`/`args`/`render: (args) => <XDemo {...args} />` pattern already established in `Table.stories.tsx`'s `Toolbar`/`Footer` stories and `DataManagement.stories.tsx`. The `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` story object now just declares a single boolean control (`appHeader`, labeled "App Header", default `false`) and renders the demo component keyed by `String(appHeader)` — the key forces a full remount when the control is flipped, since the two modes genuinely need different STARTING state (`useState`'s initial value only applies once, at first mount, not on a later prop change).
- When `appHeader` is `true`: an `AppHeader` (with a plain `AppName`/`LayoutGrid` icon) renders above the existing content row, holding the two panel toggles as `ActionIconButton`s (`PanelLeft`/`PanelRight` icons, size `xl`, the AppHeader standard) instead of the PageHeader's outline `Button`s — the PageHeader's own `actions` prop is `undefined` in this mode, so the buttons only ever live in one place at a time. Both toggle-button flavors share the same `selectedClass` (`bg-lyra-bg-active-moderate`/`text-lyra-fg-active-strong`) established in the earlier "selected state" change.
- Also in `appHeader` mode: both panels start closed (`aOpen`/`bOpen` both `false`) and their dock preference starts as `"float"` instead of `"docked"` ("start in drag mode instead of pinned") — in the default (non-`appHeader`) mode, Panel A still starts docked+open and Panel B starts closed, unchanged from the previous entry.
- The same `buttonARef`/`buttonBRef` (used by the existing "anchor float position below the button" logic) are shared across both toggle-button locations — only one location ever renders at a time, so the ref always points at whichever button is actually on screen.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### AppHeader background reverted to match the real pattern + cross-referenced with the templates
**Request**: "why is the background white?" followed by "I think I'd like to match the real pattern because I want you to reference the story properly when I ask for prototypes with draggable in the app header and I'd like this component to be referenced in the other stories in storybook (particularly the templates)."
- Root cause of the white background: `app-header.tsx`'s `AppHeader` has no background of its own by design (fully transparent, just `flex h-14 items-center justify-between`) — every real usage (`AdminShell.stories.tsx`'s `AdminShellDemo`, `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx`'s own top-level wrapper) leaves it that way and relies on whatever's behind it. The previous entry's `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` had explicitly added `bg-lyra-bg-surface-base` (white) + `border-b` directly on its own `<AppHeader>`, which is what produced the white bar — not something the component does by default.
- Fix: removed that `className` from `<AppHeader>` entirely. The demo's outer wrapper already carries `bg-lyra-bg-surface-shell` (added when the App Header control was first built), which is the exact same convention `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` uses (`bg-lyra-bg-surface-shell` on its own outermost wrapper, `AppHeader` itself unstyled) — so no other structural change was needed, just deleting the override.
- Cross-referenced all three files so this pattern is discoverable both directions: `Draggable.stories.tsx` now has a doc comment directly above `MultiplePanelsSingleDockDemoProps` naming this as the canonical place for "multiple Draggable panels + AppHeader" prototypes and pointing to `AdminShell.stories.tsx`/`AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` as the real-app patterns it mirrors. Both of those files got a matching doc comment pointing back — `AdminShellWithAiDemo`'s existing comment in `AdminShell.stories.tsx`, and a new comment above `AgentNextGenTemplate`'s template component in `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` — so a future request for a Draggable-in-AppHeader prototype has an explicit trail to follow in either direction.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of `Draggable.stories.tsx`, `AdminShell.stories.tsx`, and `AgentNextGenTemplate.stories.tsx` — clean.

- Swapped the `ActionIconButton`s' `PanelLeft`/`PanelRight` icon children for plain `lyra-label-emphasis` text spans reading "A" and "B" (matching the demo's panel names directly, rather than a generic panel glyph). Removed the now-unused `PanelLeft`/`PanelRight` imports.

### Removed top padding on the content row when App Header is showing
**Request**: "remove the padding-top on the main container when App Header is true."
- The content row's `p-4` (padding on all four sides) only needed its top inset to keep content off the bare viewport edge when there's no `AppHeader` above it — with the `AppHeader` showing, its own `h-14` already provides that separation, so the extra top padding just added a redundant gap. Changed to `px-4 pb-4` (no top padding) when `appHeader` is true, keeping the original `p-4` for the default (no App Header) mode.

### Opening a panel now brings it to the front (z-index)
**Request**: "per the functionality of the latest clicked drag panels, when a panel is opened it should be the highest z-index of the panels that are already open - currently: open panel B -> open panel A (it appears below panel B)."
- Root cause: `topPanel` (which drives each float wrapper's `zIndex`, 10000 for the top one vs 9999 for the other) was only ever updated by `onInteract` — fired on a direct mousedown/click/drag inside a panel. Simply opening a panel via its toggle button never touched `topPanel`, so a panel opened while another was already open stayed at the same `9999` as whatever was last interacted with (or both at `9999` if neither had ever been clicked), leaving stacking order to fall back to DOM paint order — which put the newly-opened Panel A visually behind the already-open Panel B.
- Fix: both `aOpen`/`bOpen` effects now call `setTopPanel("a")`/`setTopPanel("b")` in their "open" branch, in addition to the existing `onInteract` handlers — so a panel comes to the front both when it's opened AND when it's subsequently clicked/dragged, matching "the latest clicked [or opened] drag panel" should be on top.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Panel A now also starts closed in MultiplePanelsSingleDock's default mode
**Request**: "start the with main container - two panels with panels closed."
- Panel A previously still started docked+open by default (only the App Header mode started it closed); Panel B already started closed in both modes. Changed Panel A's initial `aOpen`/`aMounted`/`aState` to always be closed/`false`/`"closed"` regardless of the `appHeader` control, matching Panel B. Its dock PREFERENCE once opened is unchanged — still "docked" by default, "float" when `appHeader` is on.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Added a "Panel Buttons" (1-5) control to MultiplePanelsSingleDock
**Request**: "now add controls to set the number of panel buttons (1, 2, 3, 4, 5)."
- Generalized `MultiplePanelsSingleDockDemo` from two hardcoded panels ("A"/"B", each with its own separately-named `aOpen`/`bOpen`/`aVariant`/`bVariant`/etc. state) to a configurable 1-5 panel count. React's Rules of Hooks forbid calling a variable number of hooks, so the fix always calls a fixed 5 `usePanelSlot("A"..."E", ...)` hook invocations (a new self-contained hook holding one panel's open/mounted/state/variant/width/resizing state plus its own open/close animation effect) and then slices the resulting 5-element array down to `panelCount` for actual rendering — `const slots = allSlots.slice(0, panelCount)`. Hidden slots beyond `panelCount` keep running in the background harmlessly, so shrinking then growing the count preserves each panel's state instead of resetting it.
- Cross-panel logic that needs sibling awareness (float-anchor defaults, the single-dock rule) deliberately stays out of the hook and lives as plain closures in the parent component, now parametrized over `slots` instead of duplicated per-letter: `ensureFloatAnchor(slot)`, `handleToggleOpen(slot)`, `handleVariantChange(slot)`, `getFloatStyle(slot)`, and `renderPanel(slot)`. The persistent-wrapper JSX (critical for not reintroducing the earlier remount-resets-size bug) and both toggle-button locations (PageHeader outline buttons, AppHeader icon buttons) became `.map()` loops over `slots`, each keyed by `slot.letter`. `topPanel` changed from a `"a" | "b" | null` union to `PanelLetter | null` (`"A" | "B" | "C" | "D" | "E"`).
- Added a new "Panel Buttons" Storybook control (`panelCount`, `control: { type: "number", min: 1, max: 5, step: 1 }`, default `2` to preserve the existing default). It's intentionally NOT included in the story's remount `key` (only `appHeader` still is) — `panelCount` just changes the slice length of an always-fully-mounted 5-slot array, so adjusting it live shows/hides panels without remounting or resetting anyone's state, whereas `appHeader` genuinely changes each slot's initial dock-preference variant, which only takes effect on first mount.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Added a "Start Docked" control to MultiplePanelsSingleDock, decoupled from App Header
**Request**: "we should have a control that allows 'start docked' true/false."
- Previously each panel's dock preference (docked vs. float, once opened) was implicitly tied to the "App Header" control — `appHeader ? "float" : "docked"` — so there was no way to see, say, panels docked-by-default *with* the AppHeader showing, or floating-by-default without it. Added a new independent `startDocked` boolean prop/control: all 5 panel slots now derive their initial variant from `startDocked ? "docked" : "float"` instead of from `appHeader`, so the two controls can be combined in any of the four ways.
- Updated the `MultiplePanelsSingleDockDemoProps` doc comments accordingly — `appHeader` is now documented as purely a layout/chrome choice (AppHeader present or not), with dock preference solely owned by `startDocked`.
- Added a "Start Docked" boolean Storybook control (default `true`, matching the previous default behavior). Included it in the story's remount `key` alongside `appHeader` (now `` `${appHeader}-${startDocked}` ``) since, like `appHeader`, it feeds a `useState` initializer that only applies on first mount — `panelCount` remains excluded from the key for the reasons logged in the previous entry.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Removed WithMainContainer story; renamed MultiplePanelsSingleDock to "Main Container - Single Dock"
**Request**: "let's remove with main container variant b/c we have controls for it and rename With main container - two panels to just Main Container - Single Dock."
- Deleted the `WithMainContainer` story (the original single-panel demo) entirely — everything it demonstrated (docked/float toggle, close button, selected-state toggle button, persistent-wrapper remount fix, float-anchor-below-button behavior) is now a strict subset of what `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` covers with `panelCount` set to 1 and `startDocked` toggled, so the dedicated story was redundant now that those controls exist.
- Updated the handful of comments elsewhere in the file that referenced `WithMainContainer` for rationale (the `Float` story's ghost-hit-area note, and several comments inside `MultiplePanelsSingleDockDemo`/`usePanelSlot` that said "identical to WithMainContainer's ...") so they either point at `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` instead or restate the rationale inline, rather than leaving dangling references to a deleted story.
- Renamed `MultiplePanelsSingleDock`'s Storybook display name from "With main container — two panels (single-dock rule)" to "Main Container - Single Dock" (the exported `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` identifier/component names are unchanged — only the sidebar-facing `name` field).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Single-dock rule no longer displaces closed panels' stale "docked" preference
**Request**: "we need to update the logic of the start docked: IF we are set to start docked, and a container is opened then closed, then when another container is opened it starts docked - only replace to floating if multiple containers are open."
- Root cause: each slot's `variant` field doubles as "current dock state" AND "remembered preference for next time it's opened" — closing a panel doesn't reset its `variant`, by design (so reopening the same panel returns it to wherever it was). But `handleToggleOpen`'s and `handleVariantChange`'s single-dock-rule loops were checking `other.variant === "docked"` alone, so a panel that had been docked and was later closed still counted as "docked" and got forcibly flipped to `"float"` the moment any other panel opened — even though nothing was actually on screen to conflict with.
- Fix: both loops now also require `other.open` before displacing it (`other !== slot && other.open && other.variant === "docked"`). A closed panel's stale `"docked"` value is left untouched, so it starts docked again next time it's opened, and floating is only forced when panels are actually docked AND open at the same time (i.e. genuinely competing for the one docked slot).
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Added a "Multiple Containers" control to MultiplePanelsSingleDock
**Request**: Following a discussion comparing this story's single-dock behavior (float the displaced panel) against Outbound-Campaigns' real Online Help/AI Assist panels (close the displaced panel outright) — "can we add a control then: multiple containers (true/false) and if false then the container content is replaced with the selected button?"
- Added a new `allowMultiple` boolean prop/control (default `true`, preserving all prior behavior). When true, the existing single-dock rule applies unchanged: opening a panel while another is docked+open floats the other one instead of closing it. When false, `handleToggleOpen` first closes every OTHER currently-open panel (regardless of docked/float) before opening the newly clicked one — generalizing Outbound-Campaigns' `App.tsx` `openPanel` rule 1 ("replace outright") from its fixed 2-panel case to this story's N-panel (`panelCount`) case. With `allowMultiple` off, at most one panel is ever open, so the existing float-eviction loop naturally never has anything to evict.
- Added a "Multiple Containers" boolean Storybook control. Not included in the remount `key` (joins `panelCount` there) — it only changes what a toggle-button click does at runtime, not any `useState` initializer.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### "Multiple Containers: false" now swaps content in ONE container instead of closing/reopening a differently-sized one
**Request**: "for multiple containers: false, do not allow the containers to resize based on the button, just change the content of the container."
- Root cause: with `allowMultiple` off, each button still opened/closed its OWN slot (just closing whichever other slot was open first) — so switching from a button whose panel had been resized to 500px to a button whose panel was still at its 320px default visibly resized/repositioned the docked or floating box, because two genuinely different `Draggable` instances (each with their own remembered width) were trading places in the docked slot.
- Fix: when `allowMultiple` is false, every toggle button now drives one shared `singleContainer` (always slot "A"'s `Draggable`/`width`/`variant`/position) instead of its own slot, via a new `handleSingleToggle`/`activeLetter` pair — clicking a different button just updates `activeLetter` and re-renders `renderPanel`'s title/body text (now parametrized with an optional `displayLetter`, defaulting to the slot's own letter for the normal `allowMultiple` case). The underlying container itself never unmounts, resizes, or repositions when switching which letter is showing — only when the user manually drags to resize it, or clicks its own already-active button to close it.
- Added `isSlotSelected`/`handleSlotClick` helpers shared by both toggle-button locations (PageHeader outline buttons, AppHeader icon buttons) so they show the right "selected" state and dispatch to the right handler in either mode without duplicating the branch twice.
- `allowMultiple` is now included in the story's remount `key` (`` `${appHeader}-${startDocked}-${allowMultiple}` ``) — it doesn't feed a `useState` initializer, but it does switch which slots actually render (shared single container vs. independent per-slot), so flipping it live without a remount would otherwise orphan whichever slots were open in the previous mode.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Added "Main Container - Multi Dock" story — Main Container gets its own drag/float ability
**Request**: "we should add a new variant called Multi Dock and add the drag / float ability to the Main Container" — followed by three clarifying answers: it's a new, separate story (not a control on the existing one); the Main Container's dock/float state is independent of the side panels' single-dock rule (not a sixth competitor for their one slot), with a slightly darker "canvas" placeholder shown in the content area when nothing is docked there; and the Main Container is always present with no open/close toggle, just draggable/dockable — followed by "keep the same controls available as Single Dock."
- Added a new exported story, `MultiDock` ("Main Container - Multi Dock"), alongside the existing `MultiplePanelsSingleDock` ("Main Container - Single Dock"). Its demo component, `MultiDockDemo`, is a deliberate near-duplicate of `MultiplePanelsSingleDockDemo` (same side panels A-E, same single-dock rule, same `allowMultiple`/`startDocked` behavior) rather than a shared/parametrized component, so the existing, already-verified Single Dock story's behavior stays completely untouched.
- Refactored the four controls (App Header, Panel Buttons, Start Docked, Multiple Containers) into shared `sharedPanelArgs`/`sharedPanelArgTypes` constants, used by BOTH stories — guarantees they're identical rather than just visually similar, directly satisfying "keep the same controls available as Single Dock."
- The Main Container itself: independent `mainVariant`/`mainWidth` state, not part of the side panels' single-dock rule. Its DOCKED representation is a plain flex-1 box (not a `Draggable` instance) with its own manual "Undock" icon button, since `Draggable`'s docked mode always renders at an explicit pixel width (`draggable.tsx`'s `style={{ width, minWidth }}`) — there's no "fill remaining flex space" mode, which the primary content area needs. Its FLOATING representation is a real `Draggable` (defaultWidth 640/defaultHeight 420, drag handle, corner resize, dock-back button via `renderHeaderControls`), anchored on first undock to the content box's own current position (so it "detaches in place") and competing for front-most z-index (`topPanel`, now typed `PanelLetter | "Main"`) alongside the lettered side panels. `mainWidth` is lifted out to component state so a float resize survives a dock-then-refloat round trip even though the underlying `Draggable` instance itself unmounts/remounts across that transition (no docked-mode instance exists to keep alive in the first place, unlike the side panels).
- When the Main Container is floating, the content area shows a dashed-border `bg-lyra-bg-surface-shell` placeholder ("Main content is floating — drag to redock") instead of collapsing, so the layout doesn't jump.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Multi Dock: Main Container's floating placeholder uses Container Subtle, not a background swap
**Request**: "make the background subtle-neutral when all panels are floating" — corrected on the next turn: "no you misunderstood - keep the main container background the same (Shell) but make the inner container Container-subtle."
- Reverted the previous attempt (an `allFloating` check swapping the OUTER wrapper's background to a raw Tailwind `bg-neutral-200` whenever nothing was docked anywhere) — the outer wrapper's `bg-lyra-bg-surface-shell` is unconditional again, unchanged from before this whole sub-thread.
- Instead, the placeholder that fills the content area when the Main Container is floating (previously `bg-lyra-bg-surface-shell`, which blended invisibly into the identically-colored outer wrapper behind it) now uses `bg-lyra-bg-surface-container-subtle` — a real lyra surface token, applied directly to that one inner box rather than conditionally to the whole page background.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Multi Dock: docked side panel takes over the whole screen when Main floats
**Request**: "if the main container is in float mode and a panel is docked it should take over the whole screen" (screenshot: Main floating near top-left, Panel A docked as a narrow sidebar, with a large empty placeholder area wasted between them).
- Added a `dockedSideSlot`/`takeover` computation in `MultiDockDemo`: `takeover` is true once the Main Container is floating AND one side panel is docked+open (single-dock rule guarantees at most one). When true: `containerRef`'s own flex-basis collapses to `0 0 0px` and renders nothing (no docked Main box, no floating placeholder — there's nothing left to show there); the one docked side panel's two wrapper divs switch from their normal fixed pixel width (`slot.width + 16`) to `flex: 1 1 0%` / `width: 100%`, filling the entire vacated row instead of sitting in its narrow sidebar slot next to empty space.
- The tricky part: `Draggable`'s own docked mode always renders at an explicit inline pixel `width` (`draggable.tsx`'s `style={{ width, minWidth }}`), which a plain `className` can't override under normal CSS cascade rules (a non-`!important` class never beats an inline style). `renderPanel` now accepts an optional `fillWidth` param that adds a `!w-full` Tailwind "important" modifier to the `Draggable`'s `className` specifically for the takeover slot — `!important` is the one thing that DOES win over a non-important inline style, so this stretches the panel to fill its now-full-width wrapper without touching `draggable.tsx` itself or forcing a remount (which would reset that panel's actual resize memory the instant takeover toggles off again).
- This only applies to the one previously-docked side panel — closed panels, other open floating panels, and the Main Container's own floating window are all unaffected.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Full-screen docked panel loses its left-edge resize handle
**Request**: "now write some logic so when a panel is docked and full screen it does not have the left draggability that it normally would when docked b/c there is no main panel to be next to it."
- Added a new `dockedResizable` prop to `Draggable` itself (`draggable.tsx`, default `true`) — when `false`, the docked-mode left-edge resize handle (and its `onMouseDown={onLeftEdgeResizeDown}` hookup) simply isn't rendered at all. This is a genuine core-component change, not a story-only workaround, unlike the earlier `!w-full` width override: unlike that case, there's a clean, minimal, backward-compatible prop for "don't render this one interactive element" — no existing consumer passes it, so every other `Draggable` usage (`ai-panel.tsx`, `agent-notifications.tsx`, `draggable-panel.tsx`, every other story) is completely unaffected by the new default.
- In `MultiDockDemo`, the takeover slot's `<Draggable>` now gets `dockedResizable={!fillWidth}` — while it's taking over the full content row (nothing to its left to grow into or shrink back out of), the handle disappears; it's back the moment Main re-docks and the panel returns to its normal narrow sidebar slot.
- Also dropped that slot's `pl-4` left padding in takeover mode — that gap only ever existed to keep the resize handle clear of the main content beside it, and now there's neither a handle nor a main content area to keep clear of.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and `esbuild` bundles of both `draggable.tsx` and `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Full-screen docked panel drops the content row's left padding too — then reverted
**Request**: "now just remove the padding left when the panels are full screen so they align properly with the app header" — corrected on the next turn, with a screenshot: "you removed the full padding - I want the shell to still have the padding - see how it doesn't align now?" (the panel's own rounded top-left corner sitting flush against the viewport edge, with nothing above/beside it to visually justify the corner radius, looked worse/misaligned against the AppHeader's square edge than the original padded inset did).
- First made the content row's own `px-4`/`p-4` padding conditional on `takeover`, dropping the left side. Reverted this per the follow-up correction — the row's className is back to its original unconditional `appHeader ? "px-4 pb-4" : "p-4"`, regardless of `takeover`.
- The individual takeover panel's own `pl-4`→`""` change and its `dockedResizable={false}` (both from the previous entry) are unaffected by this revert — only the outer shell/row-level padding went back to normal.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Full-screen docked panels use PageHeader instead of ContainerHeader
**Request**: "when the panels are full screen their container headers should become Page Headers."
- `renderPanel`'s `renderHeaderControls` callback in `MultiDockDemo` now branches on `fillWidth`: while taking over the full content row, it renders the same `PageHeader` component the Main Container's own docked state uses (title `Panel {letter}`), instead of `ContainerHeader`. Normal (non-takeover) panels are completely unchanged, still `ContainerHeader` with its grip/dock/close treatment.
- `PageHeader` has no built-in equivalent to `ContainerHeader`'s `onClose` prop or grip handle, so its dock-toggle and close controls are rebuilt manually in its `actions` slot: the same `dockButtonProps`/`dockIcon` Draggable already provides (identical button markup to the normal case), plus a new manual close button (`lucide-react`'s `X` icon, newly imported) that calls the same `slot.setOpen(false)` / `setActiveLetter(null)` the `ContainerHeader` branch's `onClose` already did. No grip icon in either branch here, since `fillWidth` only ever applies to a docked panel — float mode never takes over, so there's nothing to drag.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

### Floating Main Container (App Header off) keeps the panel toggle buttons visible
**Request**: "also - when an Appheader: false control Main container is in drag mode the buttons should still be visible in the floating container."
- Root cause: with `appHeader` off, the "Toggle panel {letter}" buttons only ever lived in the docked Main Container's own `PageHeader` actions — the moment Main undocked (float/"drag mode"), that docked box (and its buttons) stopped rendering entirely, replaced by the container-subtle placeholder, leaving no way to open/close any side panel at all until Main was redocked.
- Fixed by adding the same `!appHeader && slots.map(...)` button list to the floating Main window's own header actions (its `ContainerHeader`, alongside the existing dock-to-redock button) — reusing the same `slot.buttonRef`/`isSlotSelected`/`handleSlotClick` helpers already shared between the AppHeader and docked-PageHeader locations elsewhere in this file (same precedent: only one of the two — now three — locations is ever mounted at a time, so sharing the ref is safe). Still hidden when `appHeader` is true either way, matching the docked case.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` (standing 2-error baseline, unaffected) and an `esbuild` bundle of `Draggable.stories.tsx` — clean.

## Planned Future Work
- Empty template
- Edit template (settings/configure)

**Note**: "Dashboard template (with widgets)" used to be listed here too — stale. It was already built and promoted as `AgentDashboard` (agent-dashboard.tsx, Storybook "Templates/Dashboards") well before this note was corrected, and this list simply wasn't updated when that happened. See `AgentDashboard`'s own doc comment for what it actually is: a specific, fully-baked composition for an individual agent's personal Home tab (greeting, that agent's own queue widgets, their Contact History + Redial, their Performance/Productivity/CSAT) — not a generic, swap-in-any-content dashboard shell. It was surfaced (and this stale note caught) when asked "why didn't you use the dashboard template when creating [Outbound-Campaigns' Monitor] page" — the honest answer is that `AgentDashboard`'s actual content (an agent's personal queue/contact-history/productivity data) has nothing in common with Monitor's (a supervisor-level, tree-scoped, call-center-wide rate-metric dashboard), so composing it would have meant gutting every piece of its content and keeping only the wrapping div — at that point the correct move is what was already done: compose `DashboardCard`/`Metric` directly, the same atoms `AgentDashboard` itself is built from. If a future page's actual content DOES resemble an agent's personal home screen, reach for `AgentDashboard` (or its exported pieces — `AgentDashboardQueueDrilldown`, `AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_ITEMS`, `AGENT_DASHBOARD_QUEUE_SUB_ITEMS`) instead of re-deriving that composition by hand. **Update**: `AgentDashboard` itself was subsequently confirmed to not be the generic template either — see the "`AgentDashboard` shouldn't be the template" entry above. `DashboardTemplate` (dashboard-template.tsx) is now the actual answer to "what's the dashboard template" — the container/width/breakpoint shell, reusable with any card content.

## Known Debt
- **`AppName`/`AppHeader` don't own their own click-to-open menu behavior — on hold, ask before building.** Every place that wants clicking the app name to open an `AppMenu` popover (`AppHeader.stories.tsx`'s `Default`/`AppName Only`/`With Background`, `Agent Next Gen Header`, `AdminShell.stories.tsx`'s demo chrome, `lyra-ux-templates`' `Header.tsx`, `AgentNextGenPage.tsx`) currently hand-wires its own copy of the same Radix `Popover.Root`/`Trigger`/`Portal`/`Content` boilerplate around a plain `AppName`, each with its own locally-duplicated menu-groups data — that's why fixing one copy doesn't fix the others. User confirmed (after this was flagged) that making this automatic via a shared `menuGroups` prop on `AppName`/`AppHeader` is on hold for now — not worth the API-surface change across every consumer just yet. **New apps/prototypes scaffolded from lyra-ux-templates going forward: ask the user whether they want a working `AppMenu` (click-to-open, real menu groups) in the new app's header, or just a static app name — don't default to wiring one in, and don't assume the shared-prop refactor above is wanted either.** Revisit the shared-prop idea only if this keeps coming up across multiple new prototypes.
- **Menu/Popover width scale not yet fully retrofitted.** `CONTRIBUTING.md` now documents a `sm`/`md`/`lg` → `200px`/`256px`/`320px` width scale for `Menu`/`Popover`-based item-list panels (added when `create-new.tsx` and `agent-profile.tsx` were confirmed to already match `lg`/`md` respectively). Components still using an unreviewed one-off width and not yet checked against the scale: `select.tsx` (`w-[240px]`), `connected-apps.tsx` (`w-72`), `ai-input.tsx`/`profile-menu.tsx` (`min-w-[220px]`). `app-menu.tsx` is a bigger case — it's `w-[320px]` (already matches `lg`) but also hand-rolls its own `role="menu"` markup instead of composing the real `Menu` component (the same composition-over-reimplementation issue `create-new.tsx` had before it was fixed to render `<Menu>` internally). Fix `app-menu.tsx`'s composition first, then re-check its width against the scale as part of that pass — don't just resize it in place.
